P·L LIBER ISAIAE PROPHETAE 113

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 113 ·

LIBER ISAIAE PROPHETAE

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 113, coll. 1231–1316  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

PROLOGUS S. HIERONYMI IN ISAIAM PROPHETAM.

( Vide Hieron. in Isa., Operum tom. IV. )

PROLOGUE OF ST. JEROME ON THE PROPHET ISAIAH.

( Vide Hieron. in Isa., Operum tom. IV. )

VITA ISAIAE(Ex Epiphanio, lib. de Vitis prophetarum.)

1231BIsaias in Jerusalem nobili genere ortus est, ibique prophetavit: sub Manasse rege, sectus in duas partes, occubuit. Sepultusque est sub quercu Rogel, juxta transitum aquarum, quas olim Ezechias rex jacto obstruxerat pulvere. Fontem quippe Deus Siloam causa Prophetae efficit, quoniam priusquam moreretur, pusillum precatus est, uti undas illic effingere dignaretur. Et confestim demisit illi coelitus aquam viventem, unde sortitus est locus ille appellationem Siloam, quod si quis interpretetur, sonat demissum. Sub Ezechia quoque rege, antequam stagnum piscinasque fabricasset, precibus Isaiae effectum, 1232But exigua lympha scaturiret, quod populus in obsidione hostium teneretur, et ne ipsa periret undis destituta civitas. In cujus rei perennem memoriam, populus accurate Prophetam isthic solo cum gloria et honore reddidit, ut per ejusdem intercessiones in consummationem usque saeculi, aquae ejusmodi fructus impetretur, cui scilicet usum referebant acceptum. Exstat autem monumentum Isaiae, finitimum sepulturae principum, post sepulcra sacerdotum versus Nothum partemque meridionalem. Salomon enim Jerosolymam construens, regum excitavit mausolea, prout illa David descripserat.

THE LIFE OF ISAIAH(Ex Epiphanio, lib. de Vitis prophetarum.)

1231BIsaiah was born at Jerusalem of noble stock, and there he prophesied: under king Manasseh, cut into two parts, he met his death. And he was buried beneath the oak of Rogel, beside the crossing of the waters which king Hezekiah had once blocked up with cast dust. For God makes the spring Siloam for the Prophet's sake, since before he died he prayed a little while that he might deign to fashion waves there. And at once he sent down to him from heaven living water, whence that place obtained the name Siloam, which, if one interpret it, sounds "sent down." Under king Hezekiah also, before he had built the pool and the fish-ponds, it was brought about by Isaiah's prayers 1232Bthat a scanty water should well up, because the people was being held in a siege of enemies, and lest the city itself should perish, forsaken of waters. In everlasting memory of which thing the people carefully gave the Prophet back to the soil in that place with glory and honor, so that through his intercessions, even to the consummation of the age, the fruit of water of this kind might be obtained, to whom, that is, they credited the use they had received. And the monument of Isaiah stands next to the burial-place of the princes, behind the tombs of the priests, toward Notus and the southern quarter. For Solomon, in building Jerusalem, raised up the mausolea of the kings, according as David had prescribed them.

PRAEFATIO IN ISAIAM.

  • 1231

1231C(HIERON. in Isa. ) Isaias, vir nobilis et urbanae eloquentiae, nihil in eloquio rusticitatis habens, non tam propheta dicendus est quam evangelista: Ita enim liquide Christi et Ecclesiae mysteria prosequitur, ut de praeteritis putetur historiam texere. Prophetavit autem in Hierusalem et Juda, nondum decem tribubus captivatis: et de utroque regno nunc mixtim, nunc separatim, texuit oracula. Et cum interdum ad praesentem respicit historiam, et post Babylonicam captivitatem reditum populi significet in Judaeam, in advocationem tamen gentium et Christi adventum omnem intendit curam.

PREFACE ON ISAIAH.

  • 1231

1231C(HIERON. in Isa. ) Isaiah, a man noble and of urbane eloquence, having nothing of rusticity in his speech, is to be called not so much a prophet as an evangelist: for he pursues the mysteries of Christ and of the Church so clearly that he might be thought to be weaving a history of things past. Now he prophesied in Jerusalem and in Judah, the ten tribes not yet being led captive; and of both kingdoms he wove his oracles, now mingled together, now apart. And although at times he looks to the history then present, and signifies the return of the people into Judaea after the Babylonian captivity, yet he bends all his care upon the calling of the nations and the coming of Christ.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

  • 1231D

VERS. 1.-- Visio Isaiae, etc. Tria sunt genera visionis: primum corporale, quo videmus coelum et terram et hujusmodi; secundum spirituale, quo videmus, sicut in somnis, imagines hominum et hujusmodi; tertium intellectuale, quod est in ratione, per quod Creatorem cognoscimus, et, Spiritu sancto 1232Crevelante, intelligimus quodlibet. Videre quoque saepe pro intelligere ponitur: unde saepe interrogamus, dicentes: Vides, id est, intelligis? Prophetae quoque hinc videntes dicuntur, id est, intelligentes. Hanc visionem vidit Isaias Spiritu sancto, scilicet intimante, quae superventura erant intellexit. Unde in principio non ponit visiones sicut Daniel, neque dicit quid viderit; sed statim dicit: Audite, coeli, et auribus percipe, terra.

(HIER. in Isa., tom. IV). Non solum Isaias, etc., usque ad non ergo ignorabant, secundum Montanum, qui sic videbant.

(ID., Ibid. ) Isaias interpretatur, etc., usque ad et 1232Dcum omnia fecerit, dicit: Servus inutilis sum; quod debui facere, feci.

Filii Amos. (ID., ibid. ) Amos, pater Isaiae, non duodecim Prophetarum tertius est, etc., usque ad Prophetas fuisse contendunt.

Super Judam et Jerusalem. (ID., ibid. ) Isaias principaliter 1233Ade duabus tribubus, etc., usque ad ad correctionem duarum tribuum haec praedicta esse.

Visio Isaiae, etc. (HIER., ibid.) Pro Juda, in quo signantur duae tribus, etc., usque ad de qua: Surge, illuminare, Jerusalem, quia venit lumen tuum.

In Diebus Oziae. (ID., ibid.) Ozias ipse est qui et Azarias, etc., usque ad ut nostrae historiae appareant antiquiores quam gentiles.

VERS. 2.-- Audite, coeli, etc. (ID., ibid.) Supra quis Propheta, etc., usque ad cum hoc potestatis Dei sit, nec terrenae intelligentiae.

Dominus locutus est. (ID., ibid.) Non vobis, sed mihi, ut quod in spiritu audivi referam vobis, qui ipsum audire non meremini.

Filios enutrivi. (ID., ibid.) Quod dicit: Ego eos 1233Badoptavi in filios, etc., usque ad unde: Dedit eis potestatem filios Dei fieri.

VERS. 3.-- Cognovit bos, etc. (ID., ibid.) Unde: Non arabis in vitulo simul et asino (Deut. XXII). Sicut Ebion, etc., usque ad cui dicitur: Venite ad me, qui laboratis et onerati estis.

Asinus. Gentilis vel conversus lascivus, ante immundus, peccatis oneratus, qui incarnationem Christi credidit, qui natus statim reclinatus est in praesepio, ut carnis suae freno reficeret universos.

Israel. (HIER., uti supra. ) Scribae et Pharisaei clavem scientiae et legis habentes. Unde Israel, Deum videntes, appellati sunt.

Non cognovit me. Cujus scilicet diem vidit Abraham et laetatus est (Joan. VIII); cujus adventum 1233Cchorus Prophetarum exspectando vaticinatus est.

VERS. 4.-- Vae genti, etc. (HIER., ibid.) In hoc titulo, personam, causam, tempusque commemorat; secundo, attentos facit; tertio, narrat quid locutus Dominus; quarto, quasi in excessibus gentem increpat peccatricem. Vae genti. etc. Aeternum minatur interitum, quia respuunt Filium, qui salutem attulit.

Iniquitate. (ID. ibid.) Unde: Iniquitas sedet super talentum plumbi (ZACH. V); unde alibi: Quasi onus grave, gravatae sunt super me (Psal. XXXVII).

Nequam. Alii, pessimorum, ut non seminis sit vitium, sed malitiae eorum, qui voluntate propria Deum dereliquerunt. Non enim boni et mali diversa putanda est natura.

1233DBlasphemaverunt. Dicentes: Daemonium habes, et Samaritanus es tu (Joan. VIII); et Non habemus regem nisi Caesarem; et Nonne hic est Filius fabri?

Abalienati sunt. (HIER., ibid.) Qui dicebantur pars et filii Dei (Joan. XIX; Matth. XIII). Dicitur de eis: Filii alieni mentiti sunt mihi (Psal. XVII), unde: Nemo mittens manum suam ad aratrum, etc. (Luc IX), unde Apostolus: Ad priora se extendens, posteriorum oblitus (Philip. III).

VERS. 5.-- Super quo percutiam vos, etc. (ID. ibid.). Quasi dicat, etc., usque ad quod autem jam non sit locus medicinae aptus ostendit sic: Omne caput languidum etc.

Omne caput languidum. (ID., ibid.) Sicut laetitia 1234Aanimi aliquando dolores corporis mitigat, etc., usque ad unde: A planta pedis, etc.

VERS. 6.-- Vulnus et livor. (ID., ibid.) Quasi dicat, etc., usque ad et pium medicum ad se missum occiderunt.

Nec curata medicamine. (ID., ibid.) Unde: Curavimus Babylonem et non est curata, urbem, scilicet confusionis et vitiorum, etc., usque ad et irremediabilem Judaeorum captivitatem expressit.

VERS. 7.-- Terra vestra deserta, etc. (ID., ibid.) Haec sub Babyloniis partim completa sunt, etc., usque ad unde: Nisi Dominus custodierit civitatem, frustra vigilat, etc.

VERS. 8.-- Filia Sion, etc., (ID., ibid.) Affectu piissimi patris. Et: Omnes enim natura filii Dei sumus 1234B(Joan. XIV), sed nostro vitio alieni efficimur. Unde Babylon saepe filia nuncupatur.

VERS. 9.-- Nisi Dominus. (ID.) Hoc ex persona Prophetae, etc, usque ad sic Omnipotens, Filius Omnipotentis est.

VERS. 10.-- Audite verbum. (ID.) Salvatis reliquiis, etc., usque ad et nunc mente cernitur, quando invisibilis creditur.

VERS. 11.-- Plenus sum. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Putatis me indigere his vel delectari? Ego autem plenus sum, id est, nullo indigeo. Mea enim est terra et plenitudo ejus. Vel plenus sum, id est, taedio affectus, quia non bene offertis.

Holocausta arietum, etc., usque: Judicare pupillo, etc. Sacrificia Judaeorum respuit, obedientiam 1234CEvangelii commendat: Holocausta arietum, etc. Ultimam templi destructionem indicat, quae in perpetuum perseverat. Post Babylonicam enim captivitatem, a Zorobabel templum aedificatum est, et in eo diu sacrificatum est.

(ID.) Holocausta arietum. Potest et de illis accipi, qui, cum Dei praecepta despiciant, volunt illum multitudine sacrificiorum placare, et peccandi licentiam emere. Vel qui rapinam et avaritiam altari offerunt, et pauperibus tribuunt.

Nolui. Praeterito utens, ostendit se nunquam Judaeorum sacrificia amasse, sed propter significatum ad tempus sustinuisse: et vero sacrificio impleto, pro nihilo reputasse.

VERS. 12.-- Cum veniretis. (ID.) Haec sacrificia non 1234Dquaesivit Deus, quasi indigeret; sed ne daemonibus immolarent, et ut per carnalia et typica ad spirituale et verum sacrificium facilius pervenirent.

Quis quaesivit? Audiant hoc Ebionitae, qui post Christi passionem putant legem esse servandam : audiant hoc eorum socii, qui Israelitico tantum generi custodiendam decernunt.

VERS. 13.-- Neomeniam, etc, Festum novae lunae, quod celebrabant in principio mensium: Neos, novum, mene, luna; unde: Buccinate Domino in neomenia tuba (Psal. LXXX).

Iniqui sunt coetus vestri, etc. (ID.) Quia spirituales hostias non offerunt; unde: Sacrificium Deo spiritus contribulatus (Psalm. L).

1235AVers. 14.-- Odivit anima mea. (ID.) Ανθρωποσπαθῶς. Non quod animam habeat, sed nostro loquitur more; quasi, omnino odivi.

Laboravi, etc. (HIER.) Symmachus. Defeci miserans, etc., usque ad sed meo tantum salvaris auxilio.

VERS. 15.-- Manus enim vestrae plenae sunt sanguine, etc. (ID.) Prophetarum et tandem Domini eorum, etc., usque ad in oratione dies noctesque invigilant.

VERS. 16.-- Lavamini, etc. Quasi dicat: Hoc odivi: placet veritas Evangelii: Lavamini, sanguine Christi, aqua baptismi. Mundi estote, manentes in innocentia vitae. Baptismus enim solum potest in remissionem peccati, unde: Nisi quis renatus (Joan. III), etc. Et 1235Balibi: Ite, docete omnes gentes, baptizantes eos in nomine Patris, etc.

(HIER., ubi supra ). Ordo praecipuus, etc., usque ad sed etiam in conspectu Dei, quem nihil potest celari.

Quiescite agere perverse. (ID.) Illud Evangelii sonat, etc., usque ad quem sequuntur in Apocalypsi qui splendent candore virginitatis.

VERS. 17.-- Discite benefacere. (ID.) Non confisi naturae, etc., usque ad magistrorum terenda sunt limina.

Quaerite judicium, etc. (ID.) Victimis Judaeorum Evangelica succedunt mandata. Pupillis et viduis providetur, ut securi de conjugibus et liberis martyres ad bella procedant.

VERS. 18.-- Ut coccinum, etc. (ID.) Idem est coccus 1235Cet coccinum, et vermiculus, grande scilicet peccatum, et homicidium et adulterium, etc. Quod autem per nivem, hoc per lanam significatur purgatio, scilicet et ablutio peccatorum.

Quasi nix. Quia frigidi erunt contra incentiva vitiorum: Et velut lana, quia vestiti contra frigus perfidiae.

Lana alba, etc. (ID.) De qua fiat vestis Christi.

VERS. 19.-- Si volueritis et audieritis, etc. (ID.) Liberum arbitrium reservatur, etc., usque ad ad laudem Dei transiret religio.

(ID.) Moraliter; anima fidelis et plena judicii, dum Deo obedit, Deus in ea habitat, qui est justificans justitia; si autem a Deo recedit non loco, sed merito, habitatur a malignis spiritibus, qui sunt homicidae 1235Danimarum.

VERS. 21.-- Justitia. (ID.) Justitia, in Hebraeo sedek צדק , quod magis justum significat, ut intelligamus Dominum commoratum ea, de quo: Justus autem, quid fecit? quod in Ezechiele per Oolibam significatur, quod interpretatur tabernaculum, vel tabernaculum meum in ea.

VERS. 22.-- Argentum, etc. Clari scilicet et lucidi ad vitia sunt reversi.

In scoriam. (ID.) Scoria, Hebraice siggim (סגים) : quod est rubigo metallorum, vel purgamenta et sordes quae igne excoquuntur.

Vinum tuum, etc. Alii: Caupones tui miscent vinum aqua. Symmachus: Vinum tuum mixtum est 1236Aaqua. Moraliter: Vinum tuum mixtum est aqua: quia praecepta sanctae Scripturae, quibus corrigere debent auditores, ad illorum voluntatem emollit, sensu suo admisto vinum corrumpit. Vinum tuum, etc. Haeretici quoque Evangelium corrumpunt, pessimi caupones, de vino aquam facientes, cum aquam in vinum verterit Dominus, quod miratur architriclinus: Et regina Saba, in convivio Salomonis collaudans pincernas et ministros vini.

VERS. 23.-- Socii furum. (ID.) Accipientes a Scribis et Pharisaeis munera, quae pauperibus auferebant. In passione quoque Domini, infideles fuerunt, nolentes credere in Christum; et socii furum, Scribarum scilicet et Pharisaeorum et Judae proditoris, Socii furum, etc. Nos quoque debemus cavere ne ab 1236Billis accipiamus munera, qui de lacrymis pauperum congregant divitias, ne simus socii furum, et ne dicatur de nobis: Si videbas furem, currebas cum eo, etc.

Sequuntur retributiones. (ID.) Quia amicos non putant, etc., usque ad id est, patienter tolera.

VERS. 24.-- Fortis Israel. (ID.) LXX: Vae fortes Israel, etc., usque ad vel moribus.

Heu ego. (ID.) Affectu pii patris eos (licet inimicos) plangit et percutit, quia pereunt et poenitere nolunt.

Consolabor, etc. (ID.) Ultione consolatur, etc., usque ad in quo significat eos tormenta passuros.

VERS. 25.-- Stannum tuum. (ID.) Falsam doctrinam Scribarum et Pharisaeorum, quae est similis 1236Cverae, sicut stannum argento. Et dabo puram et perfectam legis et Evangelii scientiam.

VERS. 26.-- Et restituam judices, etc. (ID.) Non solum post captivitatem, ut Zorobabel, Esdram, et alios, qui usque ad Hircanum praefuerunt populo; sed et post adventum Domini, Apostolos et alios Ecclesiae doctores.

Civitas justi, urbs fidelis Sion, in judicio redimetur. Ecclesia in qua revocantur ad juste vivendum. Est enim civitas congregatio hominum ad juste vivendum.

VERS. 30.-- Velut quercus defluentibus. (ID.) Sicut a quercu ariditate concussa, etc., usque ad nec olera quidem affert, quae infirmus manducet.

CHAPTER ONE.

  • 1231D

VERS. 1.-- The vision of Isaiah, etc. There are three kinds of vision: the first bodily, by which we see heaven and earth and things of this sort; the second spiritual, by which we see, as in dreams, the images of men and things of this sort; the third intellectual, which is in the reason, by which we know the Creator, and, the Holy Spirit 1232Crevealing it, understand anything at all. "To see" is also often put for "to understand": whence we often ask, saying: Seest thou, that is, understandest thou? Prophets too are hence called seers, that is, understanders. This vision Isaiah saw by the Holy Spirit, that is, at his intimation, he understood the things that were to come upon them. Whence at the beginning he does not set down visions as Daniel does, nor does he say what he saw; but at once he says: Hear, O heavens, and give ear, O earth.

(HIER. in Isa., tom. IV). Not only Isaiah, etc., as far as they were not therefore ignorant, according to Montanus, who saw in this way.

(ID., Ibid. ) "Isaiah" is interpreted, etc., as far as and 1232Dwhen he shall have done all things, he says: I am an unprofitable servant; what I ought to have done, I have done. [Vulg. Lc 17:10 has the plural, "We are unprofitable servants: what we ought to have done, we have done."]

The son of Amos. (ID., ibid. ) Amos, the father of Isaiah, is not the third of the twelve Prophets, etc., as far as they contend that they were Prophets.

Upon Judah and Jerusalem. (ID., ibid. ) Isaiah principally 1233Aconcerning the two tribes, etc., as far as that these things were foretold for the correction of the two tribes.

The vision of Isaiah, etc. (HIER., ibid.) For Judah, in whom the two tribes are signified, etc., as far as of which: Arise, be enlightened, O Jerusalem, for thy light is come.

In the days of Ozias. (ID., ibid.) Ozias is himself the man who is also Azarias, etc., as far as so that our histories may appear more ancient than those of the Gentiles.

VERS. 2.-- Hear, O heavens, etc. (ID., ibid.) Above, who the Prophet is, etc., as far as since this belongs to the power of God, and not to earthly understanding.

The Lord hath spoken. (ID., ibid.) Not to you, but to me, that what I have heard in the spirit I may report to you, who do not deserve to hear him yourselves.

I have brought up sons. (ID., ibid.) In that he says: I 1233Badopted them as sons, etc., as far as whence: He gave them power to be made the sons of God.

VERS. 3.-- The ox hath known, etc. (ID., ibid.) Whence: Thou shalt not plow with a calf and an ass together (Deut. XXII) [Vulg. Dt 22:10 reads in bove, "with an ox"]. Like Ebion, etc., as far as to whom it is said: Come to me, you that labor and are burdened.

The ass. The Gentile, or the wanton man converted, unclean before, burdened with sins, who has believed the incarnation of Christ, who as soon as he was born was laid in a manger, that by the bridle of his own flesh he might feed all men.

Israel. (HIER., uti supra. ) The Scribes and Pharisees, having the key of knowledge and of the law. Whence they were called Israel, "those who see God."

He hath not known me. Whose day, that is, Abraham saw and was glad (Joan. VIII); whose coming 1233Cthe choir of the Prophets, in awaiting it, foretold.

VERS. 4.-- Woe to the sinful nation, etc. (HIER., ibid.) In this title he records the person, the cause, and the time; secondly, he makes them attentive; thirdly, he tells what the Lord has spoken; fourthly, as though in transports, he rebukes the sinful nation. Woe to the nation. etc. He threatens everlasting destruction, because they reject the Son, who brought salvation.

With iniquity. (ID. ibid.) Whence: Iniquity sitteth upon a talent of lead (ZACH. V); whence elsewhere: As a heavy burden they are become heavy upon me (Psal. XXXVII).

Wicked. Others, "of the most wicked," so that the fault should not be of the seed, but of the malice of those who by their own will have forsaken God. For the nature of the good and of the evil is not to be thought different.

1233DThey have blasphemed. Saying: Thou hast a devil, and Thou art a Samaritan (Joan. VIII); and We have no king but Caesar; and Is not this the son of the carpenter?

They are gone away estranged. (HIER., ibid.) They who were called the portion and the sons of God (Joan. XIX; Matth. XIII). It is said of them: The strange children have lied to me (Psal. XVII), whence: No man putting his hand to the plow, etc. (Luc IX), whence the Apostle: Stretching himself out to the things that are before, forgetful of the things behind (Philip. III).

VERS. 5.-- For what shall I strike you, etc. (ID. ibid.). As if he should say, etc., as far as but that there is now no place fit for healing he shows thus: Every head is sick etc.

Every head is sick. (ID., ibid.) Just as gladness 1234Aof mind sometimes softens the pains of the body, etc., as far as whence: From the sole of the foot, etc.

VERS. 6.-- A wound and a bruise. (ID., ibid.) As if he should say, etc., as far as and they slew the merciful physician who had been sent to them.

Nor dressed with medicine. (ID., ibid.) Whence: We have healed Babylon, and she is not healed, the city, that is, of confusion and of vices, etc., as far as and he expressed the irremediable captivity of the Jews.

VERS. 7.-- Your land is desolate, etc. (ID., ibid.) These things were fulfilled in part under the Babylonians, etc., as far as whence: Unless the Lord keep the city, he watcheth in vain, etc.

VERS. 8.-- The daughter of Sion, etc., (ID., ibid.) With the affection of a most loving father. And: For we are all by nature the sons of God 1234B(Joan. XIV), but by our own fault we are made strangers. Whence Babylon is often called daughter.

VERS. 9.-- Unless the Lord. (ID.) This from the person of the Prophet, etc, as far as so the Almighty, [he] is the Son of the Almighty.

VERS. 10.-- Hear the word. (ID.) The remnant being saved, etc., as far as and now he is discerned by the mind, when he is believed to be invisible.

VERS. 11.-- I am full. (ID.) As if he should say: Do you think that I have need of these things, or take delight in them? But I am full, that is, I have need of nothing. For mine is the earth and the fulness thereof. Or, I am full, that is, affected with weariness, because you do not offer rightly.

The holocausts of rams, etc., as far as: To judge for the fatherless, etc. He rejects the sacrifices of the Jews, he commends the obedience 1234Cof the Gospel: The holocausts of rams, etc. He points to the last destruction of the temple, which endures for ever. For after the Babylonian captivity the temple was built by Zorobabel, and sacrifice was offered in it for a long time.

(ID.) The holocausts of rams. It can also be taken of those who, while they despise the precepts of God, wish to appease him by a multitude of sacrifices, and to buy themselves licence to sin. Or of those who offer robbery and avarice upon the altar, and bestow them on the poor.

I would not. Using the past tense, he shows that he never loved the sacrifices of the Jews, but bore with them for a time on account of what they signified; and that, the true sacrifice being fulfilled, he reckoned them as nothing.

VERS. 12.-- When you came. (ID.) God did not 1234Dseek these sacrifices as though he were in need; but that they might not sacrifice to demons, and that through carnal and typical things they might come more easily to the spiritual and true sacrifice.

Who required? Let the Ebionites hear this, who after the passion of Christ think that the law is to be kept: let their fellows hear this, who decree that it is to be kept only by the Israelite race.

VERS. 13.-- The new moon, etc, The feast of the new moon, which they used to celebrate at the beginning of the months: Neos, new, mene, moon; whence: Blow up the trumpet to the Lord on the new moon (Psal. LXXX).

Your assemblies are wicked, etc. (ID.) Because they do not offer spiritual victims; whence: A sacrifice to God is an afflicted spirit (Psalm. L).

1235AVers. 14.-- My soul hath hated. (ID.) Ανθρωποσπαθῶς. Not that he has a soul, but he speaks after our manner; as if to say, I have utterly hated.

I have labored, etc. (HIER.) Symmachus. I have failed in pitying, etc., as far as but thou art saved by my help alone.

VERS. 15.-- For your hands are full of blood, etc. (ID.) Of the Prophets, and at last of their Lord, etc., as far as they keep watch in prayer by day and by night.

VERS. 16.-- Be washed, etc. As if he should say: This I have hated: the truth of the Gospel pleases me: Be washed, in the blood of Christ, in the water of baptism. Be clean, abiding in innocence of life. For baptism alone avails unto the remission of sin, whence: Unless a man be born again (Joan. III), etc. And 1235Belsewhere: Go, teach all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father, etc.

(HIER., ubi supra ). The chief order, etc., as far as but also in the sight of God, from whom nothing can be hidden.

Cease to do perversely. (ID.) It sounds that saying of the Gospel, etc., as far as whom they follow in the Apocalypse who shine with the whiteness of virginity.

VERS. 17.-- Learn to do well. (ID.) Not trusting in nature, etc., as far as the thresholds of masters must be worn away.

Seek judgment, etc. (ID.) To the victims of the Jews the commandments of the Gospel succeed. Provision is made for orphans and widows, so that the martyrs, secure concerning their wives and children, may go forth to their battles.

VERS. 18.-- As scarlet, etc. (ID.) The same thing is the scarlet grain 1235Cand the scarlet, and the little worm, namely a great sin, and murder and adultery, etc. But what is signified by snow, this is signified by wool: the purging, that is, and the washing away of sins.

As snow. Because they shall be cold against the incitements of vices: And as wool, because they are clothed against the cold of faithlessness.

White wool, etc. (ID.) Of which the garment of Christ is made.

VERS. 19.-- If you be willing and will hear, etc. (ID.) Free will is preserved, etc., as far as religion might pass over to the praise of God.

(ID.) Morally; the faithful soul, and full of judgment, while it obeys God, God dwells in it, who is the justifying justice; but if it withdraws from God, not by place but by desert, it is inhabited by malignant spirits, who are the murderers 1235Dof souls.

VERS. 21.-- Justice. (ID.) Justice, in the Hebrew sedek צדק, which rather signifies "the just," so that we may understand that the Lord tarried in her, of whom: But the just man, what hath he done? which in Ezechiel is signified by Ooliba, which is interpreted tabernacle, or my tabernacle in her.

VERS. 22.-- Silver, etc. The bright, that is, and the shining have turned back to vices.

Into dross. (ID.) Dross, in Hebrew siggim (סגים) : which is the rust of metals, or the refuse and filth that are burned off by fire.

Thy wine, etc. Others: Thy vintners mingle wine with water. Symmachus: Thy wine is mingled 1236Awith water. Morally: Thy wine is mingled with water: because the precepts of Holy Scripture, by which they ought to correct their hearers, he softens to the will of those hearers, and, his own meaning being mixed in, he corrupts the wine. Thy wine, etc. Heretics too corrupt the Gospel, worst of vintners, making water out of wine, whereas the Lord turned water into wine, at which the steward of the feast marvels: and the queen of Saba, at Solomon's banquet, praising his cupbearers and the servers of the wine.

VERS. 23.-- Companions of thieves. (ID.) Receiving from the Scribes and Pharisees the gifts which they were taking away from the poor. In the passion of the Lord also they were unbelieving, unwilling to believe in Christ; and companions of thieves, that is, of the Scribes and Pharisees and of Judas the traitor, Companions of thieves, etc. We too ought to beware that we do not receive gifts from 1236Bthose who gather riches out of the tears of the poor, lest we be companions of thieves, and lest it be said of us: If thou didst see a thief, thou didst run with him, etc.

They follow after rewards. (ID.) Because they do not count them friends, etc., as far as that is, bear it patiently.

VERS. 24.-- The Strong One of Israel. (ID.) LXX: Woe, ye strong ones of Israel, etc., as far as or in character.

Ah, I. (ID.) With the affection of a loving father he laments and strikes them (though they be enemies), because they perish and will not repent.

I will comfort myself, etc. (ID.) He comforts himself by vengeance, etc., as far as wherein he signifies that they shall suffer torments.

VERS. 25.-- Thy tin. (ID.) The false doctrine of the Scribes and Pharisees, which is like 1236Cthe true, as tin is like silver. And I will give the pure and perfect knowledge of the law and of the Gospel.

VERS. 26.-- And I will restore thy judges, etc. (ID.) Not only after the captivity, as Zorobabel, Esdras, and the others who ruled over the people down to Hircanus; but also after the coming of the Lord, the Apostles and the other doctors of the Church.

The city of the just, the faithful city Sion, shall be redeemed in judgment. [Vulg. divides otherwise, closing Is 1:26 with "the faithful city" and opening Is 1:27 with "Sion shall be redeemed in judgment."] The Church, in which they are called back to living justly. For a city is a gathering of men for living justly.

VERS. 30.-- As an oak with the leaves falling. (ID.) As from an oak shaken by drought, etc., as far as it does not bring forth even herbs, which a sick man might eat.

CAPUT II.

  • 1236D

VERS. 2.-- In novissimis. (HIER.) De quibus Jacob, etc., usque ad: crevit in montem magnum.

Mons. Montes, angeli, prophetae, patriarchae, apostoli, martyres, et alii qui miraculis effulserunt. Colles, minores, a majoribus lumen accipientes.

Et elevabitur. (ID.) Super omnes sanctos, quia de plenitudine ejus omnes accipiunt (de quibus: Montes in circumitu ejus ) qui a monte Christo montes dicuntur.

VERS. 3.-- Domum. (ID.) Quod ideo dicitur, ut Vetus Testamentum suscipiamus, non secundum Manichaeum aliam quam domum Dei Jacob quaeramus.

1237AVias suas, quas alii triverunt. Dominus quoque ascendens in montem docebat discipulos suos. Prius ergo discendae viae, post ambulandum in semitis.

De Sion. (HIER.) Non de Sina petra, sed de monte Sion, in quo Jerusalem, in qua templum et religio Dei. Dominus quoque in templo docuit quod non oportuit Propheta mori extra Jerusalem.

Lex et verbum. Sicut supra, verbum et legem simul posuit, ut principes audirent verbum, legem populus aure perciperet. Qui enim fecerit legem, perveniet ad verbum Dei; exibit lex de verbo quasi de fonte, ut alios irriget.

VERS. 4.-- Et constabunt gladios suos. (ID.) Omne studium bellandi vertetur in pacem, etc., usque ad manducemus in tempore messionis, scilicet 1237Bmortis.

VERS. 5.-- Domus Jacob, etc. (ID.) Post vocationem gentium et ostensionem montis Dei, invitat populum suum, qui est de Jacob, ut, exiens de tenebris infidelitatis, suscipiat lumen Christi.

In lumine. (ID.) Dum lucem habetis, credite in lucem. Accedite ad eum, et illuminamini, etc. Quasi dicet: Qui male agit, odit lucem: sed venite mecum, ambulemus in luce Domini, qui dicit: Ego sum lux mundi, etc.

Vers. 6.-- Projecisti enim. Postquam exhortatus est, vidit in spiritu duritiam eorum, et subito oculos mentis ad Dominum convertens, ait: Projecisti enim, etc., quasi dicat, et ideo hortandi, quia projecisti, etc.

1237CQuia repleti sunt, ut olim, etc. (ID.) Romanis, scilicet ejicientibus, Judaeos in Judaea habitaverunt alienigenae, undecunque adducti a Tito et Vespasiano, vel caeteris principibus. Vel priusquam a Babyloniis vastarentur, haec commiserunt sub impiis regibus.

Ut Philisthiim. (ID.) Philisthiim qui hodie Palaestini, quia p. litteram sermo Hebraeus non habet, sed pro ea φ Graeco utitur.

Pueris, etc. Victorum significat libidinem in Judaeam gentem. Graeci enim et Latini diu hoc vitio laboraverunt: sed sub Constantino imperatore, et coruscante Evangelio, cum caeteris delata est turpitudo haec.

VERS. 7.-- Repleta est terra. (ID.) Et Romanorum et Judaeorum avaritia suggillatur; Graecae enim 1237Det Latinae testantur historiae nihil utraque gente avarius esse: inter idola, avaritia condemnatur.

VERS. 8.-- Equis et innumerabiles. (ID.) Et hoc contra Dominum, qui multiplicari currus prohibuit et equos: haec enim animalia data sunt ad adjuvandum, non ad superbiendum, unde et jumenta dicuntur.

VERS. 9.-- Incurvavit se. Ubi quondam templum et religio Dei, ibi statua Adriani et idolum Jovis.

Non ergo. Non maledicit Isaias, sed benedicit, etc., usque ad et repleti sunt idolis, etc.

A facie timoris Domini. Ab ira judicii.

Et a gloria majestatis ejus. Quia veniet cum potestate magna et majestate.

1238AVERS 11.-- Oculi sublimis, etc. (Hier.) Nec divitiae, nec potentia, nec nobilitas generis quemquam salvabit, omnibus superveniet una captivitas. Possunt et de judicii die haec accipi, cum omnis creatura in conspectu Dei humiliabitur, et nihil se esse sentiet comparatione ejus.

Exaltabitur autem solus Dominus in die illa.

VERS. 12.-- Domini exercituum. Ubicunque de victoria, vel vindicta Domini loquitur; Dominum exercituum eum commemorat, ne dubitemus de ejus victoria, cujus tanta est potentia.

VERS. 14.-- Montes excelsos. (HIER.) Pro varietate virtutum, alii bonorum montes, alii colles appellantur. Pro diversitate vitiorum, alii impiorum montes, allii colles, quibus dicitur: Ego inducam super 1238Bvos gladium, etc.

Et super omnem turrim. (ID.) Ad litteram: venientibus Romanis, et turres et muri funditus eversi sunt, etc., usque ad ne dogma perversum praevaleat veritati.

VERS. 16.-- Naves. (ID.) Habuit Josaphat naves quas mittebat in Tharsis, etc., usque ad sunt et naves bonae de quibus dicitur: Qui descendunt mare in navi, etc.

Tharsis. (ID.) Cujuslibet maris, etc., usque ad si aliter dicitur, Syrum est.

VERS. 17.-- Incurvabitur omnis. (ID.) Allegorice: humanus sermo rationabilis videtur, etc., usque ad ab eo quoque quo conficta erant penitus conterentur.

1238CVERS. 18.-- Et idola penitus. Hoc in captivitate Chaldaeorum, etc., usque ad hoc specialiter ad priorem sensum referendum est.

(BED.) Aedificavit Salomon rem in Jerusalem Astaroth idolum Sydoniorum, etc., ubi (ni fallor) ostenditur quia Salomon nunquam perfecte poenituit, quia idola quae aedificaverat non delevit.

VERS. 20.-- Ut adoraret talpas. (HIER.) Non in re, sed sicut talpae, etc., usque ad quibus ad veritatem pervenitur.

VERS. 22.-- Quiescite ergo. Quia haec evenient nisi quiescatis. Quiescite ergo ne saeviatis in Christum, qui purus homo videtur: quia spirat naribus et vivit sicut alii homines.

Excelsus. Excelsus super omnes gentes Dominus. 1238DEt ipse fundavit eam Altissimus (Psal. CXII, LXXXIX). Hebraeum tamen excelsum significat, non excelsus. Ipse est enim ipsa altitudo, non tantummodo altus.

CHAPTER II.

  • 1236D

VERS. 2.-- In the last days. (HIER.) Of which Jacob, etc., as far as: it grew into a great mountain.

A mountain. Mountains, the angels, the prophets, the patriarchs, the apostles, the martyrs, and the rest who shone forth with miracles. Hills, the lesser, receiving their light from the greater.

And it shall be exalted. (ID.) Above all the saints, because of his fulness all receive (of whom: The mountains round about it ), who are called mountains from Christ the mountain.

VERS. 3.-- The house. (ID.) Which is said for this reason, that we may receive the Old Testament, and may not, after Manichaeus, seek another house than the house of the God of Jacob.

1237AHis ways, which others have trodden. The Lord too, going up into the mountain, taught his disciples. First, then, the ways must be learned; afterward one must walk in the paths.

Out of Sion. (HIER.) Not from the rock Sina, but from mount Sion, in which is Jerusalem, in which is the temple and the religion of God. The Lord also taught in the temple, because it was not fitting that a Prophet should die outside Jerusalem.

The law and the word. As above, he set the word and the law together, so that the princes might hear the word, and the people take in the law with the ear. For he who shall have kept the law will come through to the word of God; the law will go forth from the word as from a fountain, that it may water others.

VERS. 4.-- And they shall stand fast their swords. [Vulg. Is 2:4 conflabunt, "they shall beat (their swords into ploughshares)"] (ID.) All zeal for warring shall be turned into peace, etc., as far as let us eat at the time of harvest, that is, 1237Bof death.

VERS. 5.-- O house of Jacob, etc. (ID.) After the calling of the nations and the showing of the mountain of God, he invites his own people, who are of Jacob, that, going out from the darkness of unbelief, they may receive the light of Christ.

In the light. (ID.) Whilst you have the light, believe in the light. Come ye to him and be enlightened, etc. As if he shall say: He that doth evil hateth the light: but come with me, let us walk in the light of the Lord, who saith: I am the light of the world, etc.

Vers. 6.-- For thou hast cast off. After he had exhorted them, he saw in spirit their hardness, and suddenly turning the eyes of his mind to the Lord, he says: For thou hast cast off, etc., as if he should say, and therefore they are to be exhorted, because thou hast cast off, etc.

1237CBecause they are filled up, as of old, etc. (ID.) The Romans, that is, casting out, the Jews: foreigners dwelt in Judaea, brought in from every quarter by Titus and Vespasian, or by the other princes. Or, before they were laid waste by the Babylonians, they committed these things under impious kings.

As the Philistines. (ID.) The Philisthiim, who are today the Palestinians, because the Hebrew tongue has not the letter p., but in place of it uses the Greek φ.

With boys, etc. He signifies the lust of the conquerors against the Jewish nation. For the Greeks and the Latins labored long under this vice: but under the emperor Constantine, and with the Gospel flashing forth, this baseness was carried away together with the rest.

VERS. 7.-- The land is filled. (ID.) Both the avarice of the Romans and that of the Jews is censured; for the Greek 1237Dand Latin histories bear witness that nothing is more avaricious than either nation: among the idols, avarice is condemned.

VERS. 8.-- With horses, and innumerable. (ID.) And this against the Lord, who forbade that chariots and horses should be multiplied: for these animals were given for helping, not for growing proud; whence they are also called beasts of burden.

VERS. 9.-- He hath bowed himself down. Where once was the temple and the religion of God, there is the statue of Hadrian and the idol of Jupiter.

Therefore not. Isaiah does not curse, but blesses, etc., as far as and they are filled with idols, etc.

From the face of the fear of the Lord. From the wrath of judgment.

And from the glory of his majesty. Because he shall come with great power and majesty.

1238AVERS 11.-- The lofty eyes, etc. (Hier.) Neither riches, nor power, nor nobility of birth shall save any man; one captivity shall come upon them all. These things can also be taken of the day of judgment, when every creature shall be humbled in the sight of God, and shall perceive that it is nothing in comparison with him.

But the Lord alone shall be exalted in that day.

VERS. 12.-- Of the Lord of hosts. Wherever he speaks of the victory or of the vengeance of the Lord, he records him as the Lord of hosts, that we may not doubt of the victory of him whose power is so great.

VERS. 14.-- The high mountains. (HIER.) According to the variety of virtues, some are called the mountains of the good, others hills. According to the diversity of vices, some are the mountains of the impious, others allii hills, to whom it is said: I will bring upon 1238Byou the sword, etc.

And upon every tower. (ID.) To the letter: when the Romans came, both the towers and the walls were overthrown from their foundations, etc., as far as lest a perverse teaching prevail over the truth.

VERS. 16.-- Ships. (ID.) Josaphat had ships which he used to send to Tharsis, etc., as far as there are also good ships, of which it is said: They that go down to the sea in a ship, etc.

Tharsis. (ID.) Of any sea whatever, etc., as far as if it is said otherwise, it is Syriac.

VERS. 17.-- Every one shall be bowed down. (ID.) Allegorically: human speech seems reasonable, etc., as far as by that also, by which they had been framed, they shall be utterly crushed.

1238CVERS. 18.-- And the idols utterly. This in the captivity of the Chaldeans, etc., as far as this is to be referred especially to the former sense.

(BED.) Solomon built a thing in Jerusalem, Astaroth the idol of the Sidonians, etc., where (if I am not mistaken) it is shown that Solomon never perfectly repented, because he did not destroy the idols which he had built.

VERS. 20.-- To adore moles. (HIER.) Not in fact, but like moles, etc., as far as by which one comes to the truth.

VERS. 22.-- Cease ye therefore. Because these things shall come to pass unless you cease. Cease therefore, that you may not rage against Christ, who seems a mere man: because he breathes through his nostrils and lives like other men.

High. The Lord is high above all nations. 1238DAnd the Most High himself hath founded her (Psal. CXII, LXXXIX). Yet the Hebrew signifies excelsum, not excelsus. For he is height itself, not merely high.

CAPUT III.

VERS. 1.-- Ecce enim. Hucusque de judicio dici putant, etc., usque ad postquam omnes gratiae donationes a Judaeis ablatae sunt.

VERS. 2.-- Virum bellatorem. (HIER.) Non est apud Judaeos qui pro lege bellet qui secundum eam judicet: omnes enim vani et stulti sunt.

Et ariolum. Quia aliquando vera ariolantur ut Balaam, et divini quinque urbium Palaestinorum, quorum consilio arca Domini remissa est, quasi diceret: 1239AEt veri prophetae et falsi auferentur a Judaeis.

Senem. Quem LXX presbyterum transtulerunt, etc., usque ad quia presbyteros audire noluit.

VERS. 3.-- Prudentem. Doctorem vel auditorem, etc., usque ad qui discernerent quo spiritu prophetae loquerentur.

De architectis. Ipse Christus fabri Filius dicitur: et in Paralipomenonis vallem artificum legimus. Ibi quippe erant artifices quos non inflabat scientia, sed humilem Christum sequebantur.

VERS. 4.-- Et dabo. pueros. (HIER.) Cum caetera in persona, etc., usque ad prohibet Apostolus episcopum eligi neophytum, ne superbiat sicut diabolus.

Effeminati. LXX, illusores, etc., usque ad sanctus Ecclesiae status confunditur.

1239BVERS. 6.-- Apprehendet. (ID.) Ad litteram, etc., usque ad et ruina nostra tuo sustentetur auxilio.

VERS. 7.-- Respondebit. (ID.) Hoc in Hebraeo non est, sed de LXX hoc additur. Secundum Hebraeum enim ita jungitur: In die illa dicet:

Non sum medicus. Non statim multitudinis judicio acquiescamus, etc., usque ad: Ne quaeras judex fieri, ne forte non possis offerre iniquitates (Eccl. VII).

VERS. 8.-- Ruit enim Jerusalem. Ex persona Prophetae. Quasi dicat: Ideo non invenient qui velit eis subvenire, quia Dominum blasphemaverunt, dicentes: Daemonium habes (Joan. VIII), et hujusmodi.

Quia lingua. Quamvis Romani quantum ad se pro tributo quod negabatur, in obsidionem venerunt, tamen praecipue mors Domini causa fuit.

1239CVERS. 9.-- Agnitio. Agnoscent propter peccata sua hoc contigisse, vel ante oculos mentis semper peccata sua habebunt.

Vae animae eorum. (ID.) LXX: Vae animae eorum, quoniam cogitaverunt consilium pessimum contra semetipsos, dicentes: Alligemus justum, quoniam inutilis est nobis (Sap. I).

(ID.) Caveamus ergo ne simus exactores in populo; ne juxta impium Porphyrium matronae et mulieres sint noster senatus, quae dominentur in Ecclesiis, et de sacerdotali gradu favor judicet feminarum.

VERS. 12.-- Et viam gressuum, etc. (ID.) Ille est ergo doctor ecclesiasticus, etc., usque ad de qua: Ego sum via, veritas, et vita (Joan. XIV).

VERS. 13.-- Stat ad judicandum. (ID.) Jam quasi 1239Diratus, etc., usque ad contra principes et presbyteros venit ut judicet, dans eis locum defensionis.

VERS. 14.-- Vos enim depasti. (ID.) Reor hoc fuisse inter senes et principes veteris populi, quod inter presbyteros et episcopos nostri temporis.

Vineam meam. (ID.) Vineam de Aegypto transtulisti. Vinea Domini Sabaoth domus Israel est (Psal. LXXIX).

VERS. 15.-- Quare atteritis populum meum? Nostri quoque principes, etc., usque ad suas vel suorum divitias esse faciunt.

VERS. 16.-- Pro eo quod elevatae. Hoc ad uxores Scribarum et Pharisaeorum praecipue, et ad alias quoque potest referri: ob quarum delicias et luxum viri earum pauperes exspoliabant. Ne se ergo immunes 1240Aexistimarent a peccato, dicit Dominus: Pro eo, etc.

VERS. 17.-- Decalvabit. Quod occultum est patebit, et foeda calvities apparebit. Nihil enim opertum est quod non reveletur.

VERS. 18.-- In die illa, scilicet Babylonicae captivitatis, vel verius quando capti sunt a Romanis, et omnia ornamenta perdiderunt.

Calceamentorum. Quae in solitudine non sunt attrita, etc., usque ad ut pro munditia matronali, terrae spurcitia vertatur.

VERS. 21.-- Annulos. Quibus signamur ad malitiam Christi, quem Pater signavit. Filius quoque prodigus cum stola annulum recepit, et calceamenta.

1240BGemmas in fronte. Quibus ora nostra decorantur et caput.

VERS. 22.-- Linteamina. (HIER.) Ut latis in Domino perfruamur deliciis.

VERS. 23.-- Vittas. Quae animum huc illucque fluentem constringunt.

Theristra. Vestis muliebris tenuissima, et visui pervia, aestati idonea, et inde nominatur: theria enim aestas dicitur.

VERS. 24.-- Fascia pectorali cilicium. Qua castigantur ubera, ne exuberent.

VERS. 26.-- Et moerebunt atque. Quicunque locum suum diabolo dederit, et cor suum omni custodia non servaverit, lugebunt portae ejus, et absente sponso, semper in luctu erit, et corruens in pulvere 1240Csedebit.

Lugebunt portae ejus, et desolata in terra sedebit. Metonomicos, judices, reges, principes qui solebant sedere in porta.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 1.-- For behold. Thus far they think it is spoken of the judgment, etc., as far as after all the gifts of grace were taken away from the Jews.

VERS. 2.-- The valiant man. (HIER.) There is not among the Jews one who wars for the law, one who judges according to it: for they are all empty and foolish.

And the diviner. Because sometimes they divine true things, as Balaam, and the soothsayers of the five cities of the Palestinians, by whose counsel the ark of the Lord was sent back; as though he should say: 1239ABoth the true prophets and the false shall be taken away from the Jews.

The ancient. Whom the LXX have rendered "presbyter," etc., as far as because he would not hear the presbyters.

VERS. 3.-- The prudent man. A teacher or a hearer, etc., as far as who might discern by what spirit the prophets were speaking.

Concerning architects. Christ himself is called the Son of the carpenter: and in Paralipomenon we read of the valley of craftsmen. For there were craftsmen there whom knowledge did not puff up, but they followed the humble Christ.

VERS. 4.-- And I will give. children. (HIER.) Whereas the rest in the person, etc., as far as the Apostle forbids that a neophyte be chosen bishop, lest he grow proud like the devil.

The effeminate. The LXX, "mockers," etc., as far as the holy state of the Church is thrown into confusion.

1239BVERS. 6.-- He shall take hold. (ID.) To the letter, etc., as far as and let our ruin be propped up by thy help.

VERS. 7.-- He shall answer. (ID.) This is not in the Hebrew, but is added from the LXX. For according to the Hebrew it is joined thus: In that day he shall say:

I am no healer. Let us not at once acquiesce in the judgment of the multitude, etc., as far as: Seek not to be made a judge, lest perchance thou be not able to offer iniquities (Eccl. VII). [Vulg. Sir 7:6 nisi valeas virtute irrumpere iniquitates, "unless thou art strong enough to break through iniquities"]

VERS. 8.-- For Jerusalem is ruined. From the person of the Prophet. As if he should say: Therefore they shall not find one who is willing to come to their help, because they blasphemed the Lord, saying: Thou hast a devil (Joan. VIII), and the like.

Because the tongue. Although the Romans, so far as concerned themselves, came to the siege on account of the tribute that was being refused, yet the death of the Lord was chiefly the cause.

1239CVERS. 9.-- The acknowledging. They shall acknowledge that this has befallen them because of their sins, or they shall always have their sins before the eyes of their mind.

Woe to their soul. (ID.) The LXX: Woe to their soul, for they have devised a most wicked counsel against themselves, saying: Let us bind the just man, for he is unprofitable to us (Sap. I).

(ID.) Let us beware, then, lest we be exactors among the people; lest, after the impious Porphyry, matrons and women be our senate, who should bear rule in the Churches, and the favor of women pass judgment upon the priestly rank.

VERS. 12.-- And the way of thy steps, etc. (ID.) He, then, is the ecclesiastical teacher, etc., as far as of which: I am the way, the truth, and the life (Joan. XIV).

VERS. 13.-- He standeth up to judge. (ID.) Now as it were 1239Dangry, etc., as far as he comes against the princes and the presbyters that he may judge them, giving them room for defense.

VERS. 14.-- For you have eaten up. (ID.) I judge that there was between the elders and the princes of the old people what there is between the presbyters and the bishops of our own time.

My vineyard. (ID.) Thou hast brought a vineyard out of Egypt. The vineyard of the Lord Sabaoth is the house of Israel (Psal. LXXIX).

VERS. 15.-- Why do you grind my people? Our own princes too, etc., as far as they make them to be the riches of themselves or of their own.

VERS. 16.-- Because they are lifted up. This can be referred chiefly to the wives of the Scribes and Pharisees, and to others as well: for whose delicacies and luxury their husbands used to despoil the poor. Lest, then, they should think themselves 1240Afree from sin, the Lord says: Because, etc.

VERS. 17.-- He will make bald. What is hidden shall lie open, and the foul baldness shall appear. For nothing is covered that shall not be revealed.

VERS. 18.-- In that day, that is, of the Babylonian captivity, or more truly when they were taken captive by the Romans, and lost all their ornaments.

Of the shoes. Which in the wilderness were not worn away, etc., as far as so that in place of matronly cleanness there may be a turning to the filth of the earth.

VERS. 21.-- Rings. By which we are sealed unto the malice [malitiam; read militiam, "the warfare"] of Christ, whom the Father hath sealed. The prodigal Son too received a ring together with the robe, and shoes.

1240BJewels on the forehead. By which our faces and our head are adorned.

VERS. 22.-- Linens. (HIER.) That we may fully enjoy broad delights in the Lord.

VERS. 23.-- Fillets. Which bind fast the mind that flows hither and thither.

Theristra. A woman's garment, most fine, and transparent to the sight, suited to summer, and from that it is named: for theria is what summer is called.

VERS. 24.-- For a stomacher, haircloth. By which the breasts are chastened, lest they grow rank.

VERS. 26.-- And they shall mourn and. Whosoever shall have given his place to the devil, and shall not have kept his heart with all watchfulness, his gates shall mourn, and, the bridegroom being absent, he shall be always in grief, and falling down he shall sit 1240Cin the dust.

Her gates shall mourn, and she shall sit desolate on the ground. Metonymically: the judges, the kings, the princes who used to sit in the gate.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- Et apprehendent septem. (HIER.) Cum Nabuchodonosor Sedeciam regem, etc., usque ad hoc loco plures possumus intelligere.

Septem mulieres. (ID.) Septem dona Spiritus sancti: Spiritus sapientiae et intellectus, spiritus consilii et fortitudinis, spiritus scientiae et pietatis, et replebit eum Spiritus timoris Domini. Quae mulieres dicuntur, quia nemo generatur Deo, nisi per Spiritum: et pluraliter, quia plura sunt dona, cum Spiritus unus.

1240DPanem nostrum. Panis noster Christus est, virga de radice Jesse, et flos; mulierum panis Pater est, sicut et Christi, qui ait: Meus cibus est, ut faciam voluntatem Patris mei (Joan. IV). Panem nostrum comedemus, corpus Christi.

Vestimentis nostris operiemur. Ornamentis virtutum.

Invocetur nomen tuum. A Christo dicamur Christiani.

Aufer opprobrium. Quod patimur a Judaeis tanquam sine Deo, etc., usque ad saepe cogamur mutare hospitium.

Aufer. Quia consurgent contra sapientiam falsae doctrinae, et contra caeteras mulieres, id est, virtutes: unicuique suum insurgit contrarium, cum nomen earum falsae virtutes subripiunt.

1241AVERS. 5.-- Nubem, id est, obumbrationem Spiritus, in prosperis ne eleventur, sicut filiis Israel nubes in die erat, ignis in nocte.

Et fumum, etc. (HIER.) Unde: Domus impleta est fumo (Exod. XIII); et alibi: Qui tangit montes, et fumigant (Psal. CIII).

Super omnem enim. Plurimi Judaeorum, etc., usque ad nos autem ad primum Christi adventum.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- And seven shall take hold. (HIER.) When Nabuchodonosor [took] Sedecias the king, etc., as far as in this place we can understand more of them.

Seven women. (ID.) The seven gifts of the Holy Spirit: The spirit of wisdom and of understanding, the spirit of counsel and of fortitude, the spirit of knowledge and of godliness, and the Spirit of the fear of the Lord shall fill him. Which are called women, because no one is begotten unto God save through the Spirit: and in the plural, because the gifts are many, though the Spirit is one.

1240DOur bread. Our bread is Christ, the rod from the root of Jesse, and the flower; the bread of the women is the Father, as he is also of Christ, who says: My food is to do the will of my Father (Joan. IV) [Vulg. Jo 4:34 voluntatem ejus qui misit me, "the will of him that sent me"]. We will eat our own bread, the body of Christ.

We will be covered with our own garments. With the ornaments of the virtues.

Let thy name be called upon. Let us be called Christians from Christ.

Take away our reproach. Which we suffer from the Jews as though we were without God, etc., as far as we are often compelled to change our lodging.

Take away. Because false doctrines shall rise up against wisdom, and against the rest of the women, that is, the virtues: to each one its own contrary rises up, when false virtues steal away their name.

1241AVERS. 5.-- A cloud, that is, the overshadowing of the Spirit, that in prosperity they may not be lifted up, just as for the children of Israel there was a cloud by day, a fire by night.

And smoke, etc. (HIER.) Whence: The house was filled with smoke (Exod. XIII); and elsewhere: He toucheth the mountains, and they smoke (Psal. CIII).

For over all. Very many of the Jews, etc., as far as but we to the first coming of Christ.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1.-- Cantabo dilecto, etc. (HIER.) Canticum aliquando in laetitia, aliquando in tristitia, ut hoc, scilicet quod ille composuit, qui videns civitatem flevit, dicens: Quia si cognovisses et tu, etc. (Luc. XIX).

VERS. 2.-- Vineam electam. (ID.) Alia editio, vineam 1241BSorec, quod est genus vitis optimae, uberrimae perpetuis fructibus; cuncta quae de vinea dicuntur, ad statum animae referuntur, quae a Deo plantata non attulit uvas, sed labruscas: et tradita est bestiis conculcanda, nec imbrem doctrinae suscepit, quia dona praeterita contempsit.

VERS. 3.-- Nunc ergo habitatores, etc. Similiter Nathan David interrogat, ut de se tanquam de alio sententiam proferat.

VERS. 4.-- An quod exspectavi, etc.? Hoc secundum quosdam supra apud se dixit, hic vero quod cogitaverat, alios interrogat.

VERS. 5.-- Et nunc ostendam vobis. Quasi diceret: Quia non vultis respondere, ergo respondebo pro vobis.

1241CAuferam, etc. Ut scilicet qui Deum non senserunt per beneficia, sentiant per supplicia, et ideo minatur poenam, ut convertantur ad poenitentiam.

VERS. 6.-- Et nubibus. (ID.) Quae sub Elia, etc., usque ad quando nec prophetas habuerunt, et apostolos repulerunt.

VERS. 7.-- Domus Israel, etc. (ID.) Ex quo divisum est regnum, etc., usque ad quia Christus ex eis natus est.

Ecce clamor. Quo vociferatus est contra Dominum et clamavit, dicens: Tolle, tolle, crucifige; vel, clamorem pro justitia fecerunt, quia Christum interfecerunt, cujus sanguis clamat ad Dominum: Vox sanguis fratris tui Abel clamat ad me de terra (Gen. IV).

1241DVERS. 8.-- Vae qui conjungitis. Hoc specialiter ad Judaeos qui avarissimi, etc., usque ad donec deficiat terra, sensus scilicet, et sermo humanus.

VERS. 9.-- In auribus. (ID.) Quasi diceret propheta: Haec verba interioribus auribus audivi, quae sunt Domini exercituum.

Nisi domus, etc. Pompa scilicet, et structura haereticorum, usque ad qui ad comparationem multiplicis numeri unione constringitur.

VERS. 10.-- Decem enim jugera. (ID.) Sterilitas terrae, quae captivitatem secutura est, ostenditur.

VERS. 11.-- Vae qui consurgitis. Quasi diceret: Captivitas imminet, etc., usque ad non intelligunt in se opera Domini.

1242AVERS. 17.-- Et pascentur agni. (ID.) Juxta historiam idem est quod supra, regionem vestram alieni devorant coram vobis.

Advenae: Auferetur a vobis regnum Dei, etc. De toto enim orbe congregata, etc., usque ad ut loca taurorum occuparent agni.

VERS. 18.-- Vae qui trahitis. Haec ad principes Judaeorum, etc., usque ad anathematizant vocabulum Christianorum.

In funiculis vanitatis. (ID.) In quibus erant, etc., usque ad tanquam solidissimis vinculis tenemur.

VERS. 19.-- Vae qui dicitis. (ID.) Hoc specialiter contra Judaeos qui Barrabam elegerunt, Christum condemnaverunt; generaliter ad omnes qui injustum justificant, justum condemnant. Malum, tenebras, 1242Bamarum, in simili significatione et figura ponuntur. In Barraba diabolum intelligimus, de quo: Libera nos a malo.

VERS. 21.-- Vae qui sapientes estis. Despecta Dei sapientia, etc., usque ad nec intrant ad Christum, nec permittunt intrare.

VERS. 22.-- Ebrietatem. Vinum continet omne vitium. Ebrietas (quae Hebraice sicera ) significat omnia quae statum mentis evertunt. Ex abundantia panis et vini peccatum Sodomorum crevit.

VERS. 23.-- Qui justificatis impium pro muneribus. Supra dictum est, quia pro uvis attulerunt spinas, et fenum, et ligna, et stipulas, et vepres arsuros, quia non tantum ista fecerunt, sed et legem 1242Cabjecerunt, et eloquium Dei blasphemaverunt. De quibus: De Sion exibit lex, et verbum Domini de Jerusalem.

VERS. 24.-- Radix eorum. (ID.) Prava cogitatio, ex qua procedit opus tanquam germen.

VERS. 25.-- Ideo iratus. (ID.) Notandum quod non exprobrat idololatriam et caetera peccata, sed quod abjecerunt legem Evangelii, et blasphemaverunt sanctum sermonem Domini: qui iratus fuerat contra principes et potentes, qui erant sapientes sibi et munera sequentes; quos devoravit lingua ignis, ut potentes potenter tormenta patiantur.

Iratus est. (ID.) Non quod humanis subjaceat passionibus; sed qui delinquimus, nisi Dominum irascentem audierimus, non timemus.

1242DEt percussit, etc. Futura quasi praeterita dicit prophetali certitudine.

VERS. 26.-- Et sibilabit ad eum de finibus terrae, etc. (ID.) Hebraei hunc locum de Babyloniis intelligunt, etc., usque ad affectu se jungit populo, et ait:

VERS. 30.-- Aspiciemus. Non ausi respicere coelum, cujus habitatorem Deum offendimus, et lux nostra, quam semper habebamus in Deo, tribulationum caligine obscurata est.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- I will sing to my beloved, etc. (HIER.) A canticle is sometimes in gladness, sometimes in sorrow, as this one is, namely the one which he composed who, seeing the city, wept, saying: For if thou also hadst known, etc. (Luc. XIX).

VERS. 2.-- A chosen vineyard. (ID.) Another edition, the vineyard 1241BSorec, which is a kind of the best vine, most fruitful with unfailing fruits; all the things that are said of the vineyard are referred to the state of the soul, which, planted by God, brought forth not grapes but wild grapes: and it was handed over to the beasts to be trodden down, nor did it receive the rain of doctrine, because it despised the gifts that had gone before.

VERS. 3.-- Now therefore, O inhabitants, etc. In like manner Nathan questions David, that he may pronounce sentence upon himself as though upon another.

VERS. 4.-- Is it that I looked for, etc.? This, according to some, he said above within himself; but here he asks others what he had been thinking.

VERS. 5.-- And now I will show you. As if he should say: Because you will not answer, therefore I will answer for you.

1241CI will take away, etc. That is, that they who have not felt God through benefits may feel him through punishments; and therefore he threatens a penalty, that they may be turned to repentance.

VERS. 6.-- And the clouds. (ID.) Which under Elias, etc., as far as when they had not the prophets, and drove the apostles away.

VERS. 7.-- The house of Israel, etc. (ID.) From the time the kingdom was divided, etc., as far as because Christ was born of them.

Behold a cry. Wherewith it cried out against the Lord and shouted, saying: Away with him, away with him, crucify him; or, they made a cry in place of justice, because they slew Christ, whose blood cries to the Lord: The voice of the blood sanguis of thy brother Abel crieth to me from the earth (Gen. IV).

1241DVERS. 8.-- Woe to you that join. This especially to the Jews, who were most avaricious, etc., as far as until the earth fail, that is, human sense and speech.

VERS. 9.-- In the ears. (ID.) As if the prophet should say: These words I have heard with my inward ears, which are of the Lord of hosts.

Unless the houses, etc. The pomp, that is, and the structure of heretics, as far as which is bound together by unity in comparison with a manifold number.

VERS. 10.-- For ten acres. (ID.) The barrenness of the land, which is to follow upon the captivity, is shown.

VERS. 11.-- Woe to you that rise up. As if he should say: Captivity is at hand, etc., as far as they do not understand the works of the Lord in themselves.

1242AVERS. 17.-- And the lambs shall feed. (ID.) According to the history it is the same as above: strangers devour your country before your face.

Strangers: The kingdom of God shall be taken away from you, etc. For gathered out of the whole world, etc., as far as so that the lambs should take the places of the bulls.

VERS. 18.-- Woe to you that draw. These things to the princes of the Jews, etc., as far as they anathematize the name of Christians.

With cords of vanity. (ID.) In which they were, etc., as far as we are held as though by the strongest chains.

VERS. 19.-- Woe to you that call. (ID.) This especially against the Jews who chose Barabbas and condemned Christ; generally, to all who justify the unjust and condemn the just. Evil, darkness, 1242Bbitter, are set down in a like signification and figure. In Barabbas we understand the devil, of whom: Deliver us from evil.

VERS. 21.-- Woe to you that are wise. The wisdom of God being despised, etc., as far as they neither enter in unto Christ, nor permit [others] to enter.

VERS. 22.-- Drunkenness. Wine contains every vice. Drunkenness (which in Hebrew is sicera ) signifies all the things that overturn the state of the mind. Out of the abundance of bread and wine the sin of the men of Sodom grew.

VERS. 23.-- You that justify the wicked for gifts. It was said above that instead of grapes they brought forth thorns, and hay, and wood, and stubble, and briars that were to be burned, because they did not only these things, but also cast away the law 1242Cand blasphemed the word of God. Of which: Out of Sion shall go forth the law, and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem.

VERS. 24.-- Their root. (ID.) A crooked thought, out of which the work proceeds like a bud.

VERS. 25.-- Therefore was he angry. (ID.) It is to be noted that he does not reproach them with idolatry and the other sins, but with this, that they cast away the law of the Gospel and blasphemed the holy word of the Lord: who had been angry against the princes and the mighty, who were wise in their own eyes and followers after gifts; whom the tongue of fire devoured, that the mighty might mightily suffer torments.

He was angry. (ID.) Not that he lies subject to human passions; but we who transgress, unless we have heard the Lord being angry, do not fear.

1242DAnd he struck, etc. He speaks things to come as though past, with a prophetic certainty.

VERS. 26.-- And he shall whistle to him from the ends of the earth, etc. (ID.) The Hebrews understand this place of the Babylonians, etc., as far as he joins himself to the people in affection, and says:

VERS. 30.-- We shall look. Not daring to look back upon heaven, whose inhabitant, God, we have offended; and our light, which we always had in God, is darkened with the gloom of tribulations.

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 1.-- In anno, etc. (HIER.) Ozias ipse est Azarias, etc., usque ad cadens in faciem, excelsa voce clamat ad Dominum.

1243ARex Ozias. Rex, scilicet leprosus, qui sacerdotium usurpavit.

Vidi Dominum. Christum scilicet. Visus est Filius in habitu regnantis; locutus est Spiritus sanctus propter consortium majestatis, unitatemque substantiae.

Super solium excelsum, etc. (HIER.) Qui videt Dominum regnantem in coelestibus virtutibus, videt eum super solium excelsum et elevatum, quae sunt Throni et Dominationes, etc.

Quidam dicunt seraphim virtutes in coelo Deo assistentes et laudantes, quae in ministeria mittuntur maxime ad eos qui purgatione indigent, et propter peccata aliqua ex parte suppliciis.

(ID.) Cherubim, multitudo scientiae, super quos Dominus 1243Bin modum aurigae sedere ostenditur, unde: Qui sedes super cherubim, etc. Seraphim autem praeter hunc locum in Scripturis canonicis me legisse non memini. Errant ergo qui in precibus solent scribere: Qui sedes super cherubim et seraphim.

VERS. 2.-- Stabant. (ID.) Id est, stant assistentia Deo; volant demonstrantia Deum; nudant quasi medietatem; non manifestant quae velata sunt; non velant, quae nota sunt.

Sex alae uni. (ID.) Victorinus duodecim apostolos intelligit. Nos autem duodecim lapides altaris quos ferrum non tetigit, et duodecim gemmas in diademate sacerdotis.

(ID.) Alas habere dicuntur angeli, quia in superioribus 1243Ccommorantur, vel propter velocem in omnibus discursum, sicut et venti alati dicuntur.

Duabus velabant faciem ejus. Ante haec quae fuerunt nescio, etc., usque ad angeli quoque et praedicatores nihil inde docent; similiter:

Velabant pedes. (HIER.) Quid post hunc mundum futurum sit ignoramus: media tamen, quae sex diebus vel aetatibus facta sunt, vel fiunt; cognoscimus tantum Duabus velabant, quia de praesenti tantummodo notitiam habemus.

(ID.) Hebraeus meus longe alia via ingressus est, etc., usque ad et tunc vere purgatus labiis, dixi ad Dominum: Ecce ego, mitte me.

VERS. 3.-- Et clamabant. (ID.) Non leviter dicunt, sed salutarem omnibus confessionem clamore pronuntiant: 1243DSanctus, sanctus, sanctus, quia et ipsa, scilicet seraphim sancta: sanctius enim his nihil est: mirantur autem quod Filius tantum se humiliaverit et crucem subierit, et terrenis innotuerit.

Sanctus, sanctus, etc. Mysterium Trinitatis. In utroque Testamento Trinitas praedicatur, Dominus Christus, scilicet qui hic quatuor litterarum est, quod proprie de Deo ponitur: Iod, He, Iod, He; id est, duo ia, quae duplicata ineffabile nomen Dei faciunt.

Plena est omnis terra. (ID.) Et implebitur, etc., usque ad post duos et quadraginta annos a passione Domini.

VERS. 4.-- Superliminaria. In Veteri Testamento locutus est Dominus, etc., usque ad quem capientes 1244Adicimus: Ex parte cognoscimus, et ex parte prophetamus (I Cor. XIII).

VERS. 5.-- Pollutus. (ID.) Hoc dicit pro societate populi, cum quo cogebatur loqui. Noxium est cum peccatoribus vivere.

Ego habito. Nota cum peccatoribus non esse morandum.

Et regem Dominum exercituum vidi oculis meis: et volavit ad me unus. Sic Abraham postquam vidit Dominum et vocem ejus audivit, se cinerem et pulverem esse confitetur, quia quantacunque sit hominum dignitas, vilescit in comparatione ejus.

VERS. 6.-- De seraphim. (ID.) Seraphim per m, in masculino genere per n, in neutro genere plurali dicimus. Seraphim tamen singulariter dici potest, sicut 1244Bcherubim et cherub.

Et in manu ejus. Id est, operatione ejus, id est Christi. Calculus, id est, charitas, quem forcipe, id est doctrina duorum Testamentorum, de altari tulerat, et Isaiae in exemplum posuerat.

Calculus. (ID.) LXX: carbunculus, lapis ignitus, qui propter flammeum colorem igneus dicitur.

Forcipe. (ID.) Haec est scala Jacob gladius bis acutus, etc., usque ad Dei sermonem duplici acie praeceptorum comprehendens.

De altari. Quod Joannes vidit in Apocalypsi, quod est plenum calculis ignitis, quibus possunt peccata purgari.

VERS. 7.-- Ecce tetigi labia tua: unde: Quid detur tibi aut quid adjiciatur tibi ad linguam dolosam? 1244CSagittae potentis acutae cum carbonibus desolatoriis (Psal. CXIX). Hic est sermo Dei, de quo dicitur: Habes carbones ignis, sedebis super eos, hi erunt tibi in adjutorium.

Et auferetur iniquitas. (ID.) Jeremias, qui puer erat, ideo pene immunis a peccato solius manus attactu purgatur. Ad Isaiam vero qui dicit: Vir pollutus labiis ego sum, angelus cum calculo mittitur.

VERS. 8.-- Quem mittam? Non cogit, sed voluntatem quaerit: voluntas enim sola praemium meretur. Isaias autem, tanquam purgatus, offert se spontaneus.

Mitte me. (ID.) Putat se bonum nuntiaturum, etc., usque ad non temeritatis esse, sed obedientiae, quod se obtulerit.

1244DVERS. 9.-- Audite audientes. (ID.) Lucas secundum LXX in Actibus apostolorum: Aure audietis et non intelligetis, etc., usque ad et magis Graecis quam Hebraicis utitur testimoniis.

VERS. 10.-- Excaeca, etc. (ID.) Cui simile, etc., usque ad nec est Dei crudelitas, sed misericordia, infidelibus excaecatis, humiles illuminare.

Et convertatur. Quamvis grave peccatum sit, si convertatur quis, potest salvari: sed hi pro magnitudine sceleris, poenitentia sunt indigni.

VERS. 13.-- Et adhuc. Et pro, id est, adhuc ex ea gente decimatio, quasi adhuc decima pars illius gentis remanebit.

Et erit. Sub Adriano, Judaea iterum vastata est, 1245Aquinquagesimo vero anno post captivitatem Romanorum. Prohibiti enim sunt publicis legibus Judaeam intrare.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 1.-- In the year, etc. (HIER.) Ozias himself is Azarias, etc., as far as falling upon his face, he cries out with a loud voice to the Lord.

1243AKing Ozias. The king, namely the leper, who usurped the priesthood.

I saw the Lord. Christ, namely. The Son was seen in the habit of one reigning; the Holy Spirit spoke, because of the fellowship of majesty and the unity of substance.

Upon a throne high, etc. (HIER.) He who sees the Lord reigning in the heavenly powers sees him upon a throne high and lifted up, which are the Thrones and the Dominations, etc.

Some say that the seraphim are powers in heaven attending upon God and praising him, which are sent upon ministries chiefly to those who have need of purgation, and, for certain sins, in part to punishments.

(ID.) Cherubim, a multitude of knowledge, upon whom the Lord 1243Bis shown to sit after the manner of a charioteer, whence: Thou that sittest upon the cherubim, etc. But the seraphim, apart from this place, I do not remember to have read in the canonical Scriptures. They err therefore who are wont to write in their prayers: Thou that sittest upon the cherubim and seraphim.

VERS. 2.-- They stood. (ID.) That is, they stand attending upon God; they fly, showing forth God; they lay bare as it were the middle part; they do not make manifest the things that are veiled; they do not veil the things that are known.

Six wings to one. (ID.) Victorinus understands the twelve apostles. But we, the twelve stones of the altar which iron did not touch, and the twelve gems in the diadem of the priest.

(ID.) The angels are said to have wings, because they dwell in the higher places, 1243Cor because of their swift running to and fro in all things, as the winds also are called winged.

With two they veiled his face. What things were before these I know not, etc., as far as the angels also and the preachers teach nothing thereof; likewise:

They veiled the feet. (HIER.) What is to be after this world we know not: the middle things, however, which were made in six days or ages, or are made; we know only With two they veiled, because we have knowledge of the present alone.

(ID.) My Hebrew has entered by a far other way, etc., as far as and then, being truly purged in the lips, I said to the Lord: Behold I, send me.

VERS. 3.-- And they cried. (ID.) They do not say it lightly, but pronounce with a cry a confession salutary for all: 1243DHoly, holy, holy, because they themselves also, namely the seraphim, are holy: for nothing is holier than these: but they marvel that the Son so humbled himself and underwent the cross, and became known to earthly things.

Holy, holy, etc. The mystery of the Trinity. In both Testaments the Trinity is preached, the Lord Christ, namely, who here is of four letters, which is set down properly of God: Iod, He, Iod, He; that is, two ia, which doubled make the ineffable name of God.

The whole earth is full. (ID.) And it shall be filled, etc., as far as after two and forty years from the passion of the Lord.

VERS. 4.-- The lintels. In the Old Testament the Lord spoke, etc., as far as which taking up 1244Awe say: We know in part, and we prophesy in part (I Cor. XIII).

VERS. 5.-- Polluted. (ID.) This he says because of the fellowship of the people, with whom he was constrained to speak. It is harmful to live with sinners.

I dwell. Note that one must not tarry with sinners.

And I have seen with my eyes the King, the Lord of hosts: and one flew to me. So Abraham, after he had seen the Lord and heard his voice, confesses himself to be ashes and dust, because however great the dignity of men may be, it grows cheap in comparison of him.

VERS. 6.-- Of the seraphim. (ID.) Seraphim with m we say in the masculine gender, with n in the neuter gender plural. Seraphim, however, can be said in the singular, as 1244Bcherubim and cherub.

And in his hand. That is, by his working, that is, of Christ. The coal, that is, charity, which with the tongs, that is, the teaching of the two Testaments, he had taken from the altar, and had set before Isaias for an example.

A coal. (ID.) The Seventy: a carbuncle, a fiery stone, which because of its flame-like colour is called igneous.

With tongs. (ID.) This is the ladder of Jacob, the sword twice sharpened, etc., as far as comprehending the word of God with the double edge of the precepts.

From the altar. Which John saw in the Apocalypse, which is full of fiery coals, wherewith sins can be purged.

VERS. 7.-- Behold I have touched thy lips: whence: What may be given to thee, or what may be added to thee for a deceitful tongue? 1244CThe sharp arrows of the mighty, with coals that lay waste (Psal. CXIX). [Vulg. Ps 119:3 apponatur, "may be set beside"] This is the word of God, of which it is said: Thou hast coals of fire, thou shalt sit upon them, these shall be to thee for a help.

And the iniquity shall be taken away. (ID.) Jeremias, who was a boy, and therefore almost free from sin, is purged by the touch of a hand alone. But to Isaias, who says: I am a man polluted in the lips, an angel is sent with a coal.

VERS. 8.-- Whom shall I send? He does not compel, but seeks the will: for the will alone deserves the reward. But Isaias, as one purged, offers himself of his own accord.

Send me. (ID.) He thinks that he is to announce good, etc., as far as that it was not of rashness but of obedience that he offered himself.

1244DVERS. 9.-- Hear ye that hear. (ID.) Luke according to the Seventy in the Acts of the Apostles: With the ear you shall hear, and shall not understand, etc., as far as and he uses Greek testimonies rather than Hebrew.

VERS. 10.-- Blind, etc. (ID.) To which is like, etc., as far as nor is it cruelty of God, but mercy, the unbelieving being blinded, to enlighten the humble.

And he be converted. Although the sin be grievous, if anyone be converted, he can be saved: but these, for the greatness of their crime, are unworthy of penance.

VERS. 13.-- And still. And in place of, that is, still out of that nation a tithing, as if still a tenth part of that nation shall remain.

And it shall be. Under Hadrian Judaea was laid waste again, 1245Ain the fiftieth year indeed after the captivity of the Romans. For they were forbidden by public laws to enter Judaea.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1.-- Et factum est in diebus Achaz, etc. (HIER.) Quae scripta sunt usque ad hunc locum sub Ozia et Joathan filio ejus, visa sunt; sequuntur sub Achaz rege impiissimo, qui a Deo derelictus ab inimicis expugnatur.

Achaz. Qui clausit januas templi et Baalim adoravit, filium suum idolo consecravit, altare aeneum abstulit, et altare idolorum in templo posuit. Jure ergo a Domino derelictus est: Et surrexerunt adversus eum inimici (II Par. XXVIII).

Ascendit, secundo. Primo enim Assyrii de tribu 1245BJuda, etc., usque ad facti sunt amici Herodes et Pilatus in passione Christi.

VERS. 3.-- Derelictus est. A fratre suo Rabsace, qui ad Assyrios transierat, ut tradunt Hebraei.

Jasub filius tuus. (ID.) Qui interpretatur reliquus comes et convertens, qui populus Juda de manibus duorum regum liberandus erat.

In via. Ibi Rabsaces, postea ex praecepto Sennacherib, Dei populum blasphemavit.

VERS. 4.-- Duabus caudis. Duae caudae, etc., usque ad et ductus cum decem tribubus in montes Medorum et Persarum.

VERS. 6.-- Tabeel. (ID.) Tabeel bonus Deus, quasi dicat: Ibi ponamus idolum Dei nostri, vel ad litteram, Filium hominis sic vocat.

1245CVERS. 8.-- Adhuc sexaginta, etc. Quasi diceret: Interim contenti erunt finibus suis, et Damasco tanquam metropoli Syriae imperabit Rasim, et caput civitatum Ephraim Samaria erit, et in ea interim regnabit filius Romeliae.

Sexaginta quinque anni. A vicesimo sexto anno regni Oziae, quando lepra percussus est, ex tunc Joathan filius ejus regnavit septem et viginti annis, patre adhuc vivente. Post mortem ejus annis sexdecim, post quem Achaz aliis sexdecim, postea regnante Ezechia sex annis. Ephraim, id est, decem tribus in captivitatem ductae, cessarunt esse populus.

VERS. 11.-- Pete tibi signum. (ID.) Ut qui prophetae non credidit, Domini auctoritate terreatur; 1245Dquasi diceret: Difficile videtur tibi quod regna potentissima brevi finiantur, et tu libereris et populus tuus, sed pete tibi signum.

Pete tibi signum. Moyses accepit signum de terra, etc., usque ad utrumque mortem Domini et ascensionem respicit.

Non petam. Non humiliter, sed superbe. Multi enim leguntur a Domino signa petivisse et accepisse, sed Achaz ita respondit, tanquam auctoritatem habens: Non tentabis Dominum Deum tuum. Vel, timuit quod in signo Deus glorificaretur et idola vilificarentur.

VERS. 14.-- Ecce virgo concipiet. (ID.) Hebraice alma, Latine quoque alma dicitur sancta, etc., usque 1246Aad quod violenter esse exortum etiam stultis patet.

Emmanuel. (ID.) Quidam Isaiam duos filios habuisse dicunt, etc., usque ad propter quam Verbum caro factum est.

VERS. 15.-- Reprobare malum. (ID.) Quasi diceret: In pannis infantiae positus habebit boni malique discretionem. His verbis percipimus Salvatoris, infantiam divinam in eo non minuisse sapientiam, quamvis dicatur: Puer autem proficiebat aetate et sapientia, ut veritas humani corporis probaretur: infantia enim non praejudicat divinae sapientiae.
