VERS. 16.-- Derelinquetur, etc. (ID.) quasi diceret: Terra Syriae et Samariae ab invocatione nominis ejus vastabitur, et domus Juda a duobus regibus liberabitur.

1246BVERS. 18.-- Muscae. (ID.) Id est Aegyptiis, populo imbelli et idololatria sordenti. Legimus ab Aegyptiis Josiam occisum et populum Israel subjugatum, nec multo post tempore Nabuchodonosor cum innumerabili exercitu cepit Jerusalem, templum succendit, et habitatores Assyrios in Judaea posuit.

VERS. 20.-- Novacula. (ID.) Rex Assyriorum (qui in Jeremia columba Dei vocatur) in eo tanquam in novacula acutissima et in his qui trans flumen, scilicet Assyriis, sunt, radet Dominus in Judaea caput et pilos pedum, scilicet omnem fortitudinem regum et principum, et subjectorum.

VERS. 22.-- Comedet butyrum. Delectabitur in 1246Cpassione Christi vel sua. Butyrum enim ex multis confectionibus efficitur.

VERS. 25.-- Et omnes montes qui in sarculo sarrientur. (ID.) Relictis campestribus metu hostium ad montana confugient, aspera montium manu propria excolentes, vitam miseram sustentabunt. Caetera erunt in pascua.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass in the days of Achaz, etc. (HIER.) The things that are written up to this place were seen under Ozias and Joathan his son; those that follow, under Achaz, a most impious king, who, being forsaken by God, is overcome by his enemies.

Achaz. Who shut the doors of the temple and adored the Baalim, consecrated his son to an idol, took away the brazen altar, and set an altar of idols in the temple. Rightly therefore was he forsaken by the Lord: And his enemies rose up against him (II Par. XXVIII).

He went up, a second time. For first the Assyrians of the tribe of 1245BJuda, etc., as far as Herod and Pilate were made friends in the passion of Christ.

VERS. 3.-- He was forsaken. By his own brother Rabsaces, who had gone over to the Assyrians, as the Hebrews hand down.

Jasub thy son. (ID.) Who is interpreted a remaining companion and one that turns back, who was the people of Juda that was to be delivered out of the hands of the two kings.

In the way. There Rabsaces, afterward at the command of Sennacherib, blasphemed the people of God.

VERS. 4.-- Of two tails. Two tails, etc., as far as and he was led with the ten tribes into the mountains of the Medes and the Persians.

VERS. 6.-- Tabeel. (ID.) Tabeel, the good God, as if he should say: There let us set the idol of our God; or, to the letter, he so calls the Son of man.

1245CVERS. 8.-- Yet sixty, etc. As if he should say: In the meanwhile they shall be content with their own borders, and Rasim shall rule over Damascus as over the metropolis of Syria, and Samaria shall be the head of the cities of Ephraim, and in it in the meanwhile the son of Romelia shall reign.

Sixty-five years. From the twenty-sixth year of the reign of Ozias, when he was struck with leprosy, from that time Joathan his son reigned seven and twenty years, his father being yet alive. After his death, sixteen years; after whom Achaz other sixteen; afterward, Ezechias reigning, six years. Ephraim, that is, the ten tribes led into captivity, ceased to be a people.

VERS. 11.-- Ask thee a sign. (ID.) That he who did not believe the prophet might be terrified by the authority of the Lord; 1245Das if he should say: It seems hard to thee that most powerful kingdoms should be brought to an end in a short time, and that thou shouldst be delivered, and thy people; but ask thee a sign.

Ask thee a sign. Moses received a sign from the earth, etc., as far as it looks to both the death of the Lord and his ascension.

I will not ask. Not humbly, but proudly. For many are read to have asked signs of the Lord and to have received them; but Achaz answered thus, as having authority: Thou shalt not tempt the Lord thy God. Or, he feared that in the sign God should be glorified and the idols made cheap.

VERS. 14.-- Behold a virgin shall conceive. (ID.) In Hebrew alma; in Latin also alma is called holy, etc., as far 1246Aas that it arose by violence is plain even to fools.

Emmanuel. (ID.) Some say that Isaias had two sons, etc., as far as for the sake of which the Word was made flesh.

VERS. 15.-- To reprobate the evil. (ID.) As if he should say: Set in the swaddling-bands of infancy he shall have the discernment of good and evil. By these words we perceive that the infancy of the Saviour did not diminish the divine wisdom in him, although it be said: But the child advanced in age and wisdom, [Vulg. Lc 2:52 Et Jesus proficiebat sapientia, et aetate, et gratia, "And Jesus advanced in wisdom, and age, and grace"] that the truth of the human body might be proved: for infancy is no prejudice to the divine wisdom.

VERS. 16.-- He shall be forsaken, etc. (ID.) as if he should say: The land of Syria and of Samaria shall be laid waste from the invocation of his name, and the house of Juda shall be delivered from the two kings.

1246BVERS. 18.-- The fly. (ID.) That is, the Egyptians, an unwarlike people and foul with idolatry. We read that Josias was slain by the Egyptians and the people of Israel subjugated; and not long after in time Nabuchodonosor with an innumerable army took Jerusalem, burned the temple, and set Assyrian inhabitants in Judaea.

VERS. 20.-- A razor. (ID.) The king of the Assyrians (who in Jeremias is called the dove of God), in him as in a most sharp razor, and in those who are beyond the river, namely the Assyrians, the Lord shall shave in Judaea the head and the hairs of the feet, namely all the strength of kings and of princes and of their subjects.

VERS. 22.-- He shall eat butter. He shall be delighted in 1246Cthe passion of Christ or in his own. For butter is made out of many mixings.

VERS. 25.-- And all the hills that shall be raked with the hoe. (ID.) The plains being left for fear of enemies, they shall flee to the mountain places, tilling the rough parts of the mountains with their own hand; they shall sustain a wretched life. The rest shall be for pastures.

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 1.-- Et dixit Dominus ad me: Sume tibi. (HIER.) Primum propheta praedicit Achaz futura, etc., usque ad unde rursum partus virginalis describitur.

Velociter spolia. (ID.) Hoc nomen pueri, quasi diceret: Ne patiaris ultra diabolum regnare; et non angelos, non Prophetas mitte, sed ipse descende.

1246DVERS. 2.-- Uriam, etc. (ID.) Urias, lux Domini; Zacharias, memoria Domini; Barachias, benedictio Domini; his testibus nativitas Domini comprobatur. Unde duobus testibus euntibus in Emmaus incipiens a Moyse et prophetis, quae de se dicta erant, disseruit.

VERS. 3.-- Ad prophetissam. (ID.) Ad Spiritum sanctum a quo omnis prophetia, quae Graece neutri generis pneuma, Hebraice feminini Ruach, Latine masculini generis est, et ideo diversis positionibus sortitur, nullius est enim. Vel ad Mariam, quam prophetissam fuisse nemo dubitat.

VERS. 4.-- Puer. (ID.) Praecipitur itaque Isaias, etc., usque ad domum David sola invocatione salvaret.

1247APro eo quod abjecit populus aquas Siole. (HIER.) In quibus caecus se lavit, etc., usque ad terram ipsius Judam conatus sit possidere.

VERS. 7.-- Aquas fluminis. (ID.) Exercitum Sennacherib, cujus potentia occupans terram Samariae inundationi fluminis comparatur.

VERS. 9.-- Congregamini populi. (ID.) Quasi: Contra Emmanuel nihil potestis, etc., usque ad quia perversitas non potest rectum superare.

VERS. 12.-- Ne dicatis. (ID.) Quasi diceret: Non timeatis duorum regum adversus Judam conjurationem: conjuratio est consensus multorum in malum.

Ne timeatis neque paveatis. Tu et qui tecum sunt populi, quia videntur timendi; quia principium sapientiae 1247Btimor Domini.

VERS. 14.-- In lapidem. (ID.) Quem reprobaverunt aedificantes, etc., usque ad peccata peccatis sociantes et vincti laqueis delictorum.

Duabus domibus. Vel in duabus familiis, etc., usque ad per traditiones suas legis praecepta dissipaverunt et maculaverunt.

VERS. 16.-- Signa legem. Quasi diceret: Aperi eam, Domine, discipulis meis, etc., usque ad possumus etiam doctrinas intelligere.

VERS. 18.-- Pueri mei, ex Deo nati. Alii Prophetae et eorum discipuli. Legunt hoc quidam de duobus filiis Isaiae, etc., usque ad qui in portentum saecularis sapientiae et superbiae Judaeorum fuerunt.

VERS. 20.-- Non eris eis. (ID.) Hoc est si populus 1247Ciste noluerit veritatem a Domino quaerere, non habebit Christum, scilicet lumen veritatis, quam negligit.

VERS. 21.-- Regi suo. Diabolo et paternis traditionibus, id est, antiquis erroribus.

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 1.-- And the Lord said to me: Take thee. (HIER.) First the prophet foretells to Achaz the things to come, etc., as far as whence again the virginal birth is described.

Quickly the spoils. (ID.) This is the name of the child, as if he should say: Suffer not the devil to reign any longer; and send not angels, send not Prophets, but come down thyself.

1246DVERS. 2.-- Urias, etc. (ID.) Urias, the light of the Lord; Zacharias, the memory of the Lord; Barachias, the blessing of the Lord: by these witnesses the nativity of the Lord is proved. Whence, to two witnesses going to Emmaus, beginning from Moses and the prophets, he discoursed of the things which had been said of himself.

VERS. 3.-- To the prophetess. (ID.) To the Holy Spirit, from whom is all prophecy, which in Greek is of the neuter gender, pneuma; in Hebrew of the feminine, Ruach; in Latin it is of the masculine gender, and therefore it is allotted by different positions, for it is of none. Or to Mary, whom no one doubts to have been a prophetess.

VERS. 4.-- The child. (ID.) Isaias therefore is commanded, etc., as far as he should save the house of David by invocation alone.

1247ABecause the people hath cast away the waters of Siole. (HIER.) In which the blind man washed himself, etc., as far as he attempted to possess Juda, his own land. [Vulg. Is 8:6 populus iste aquas Siloe, "this people the waters of Siloe"]

VERS. 7.-- The waters of the river. (ID.) The army of Sennacherib, whose power, seizing the land of Samaria, is compared to the flooding of a river.

VERS. 9.-- Gather yourselves together, O ye people. (ID.) As if: Against Emmanuel you can do nothing, etc., as far as because perversity cannot overcome what is right.

VERS. 12.-- Say ye not. (ID.) As if he should say: Fear not the conspiracy of the two kings against Juda: a conspiracy is the consent of many unto evil.

Fear ye not, neither be afraid. Thou and the peoples that are with thee, because they seem to be feared; because the beginning of wisdom 1247Bis the fear of the Lord.

VERS. 14.-- For a stone. (ID.) Which the builders rejected, etc., as far as joining sins to sins and bound with the snares of their offences.

To the two houses. Or in the two families, etc., as far as through their own traditions they scattered and defiled the precepts of the law.

VERS. 16.-- Seal the law. As if he should say: Open it, Lord, to my disciples, etc., as far as we can also understand doctrines.

VERS. 18.-- My children, born of God. The other Prophets and their disciples. Some read this of the two sons of Isaias, etc., as far as who were for a portent of worldly wisdom and of the pride of the Jews. [Vulg. Is 8:18 Ecce ego et pueri mei quos dedit mihi Dominus, "Behold I and my children whom the Lord hath given me"]

VERS. 20.-- Thou shalt not be to them. (ID.) That is, if this people 1247Cwill not seek the truth from the Lord, it shall not have Christ, namely the light of truth, which it neglects. [Vulg. Is 8:20 non erit eis matutina lux, "there shall not be for them the morning light"]

VERS. 21.-- To his king. To the devil and to the traditions of the fathers, that is, to ancient errors.

CAPUT IX.

VERS. 1.-- Primo tempore, (HIER.) Hae duae tribus ab Assyriis in captivitatem ductae sunt, etc., usque ad vidit prius lucem praedicantis Christi.

Terra Zabulon. (ID.) In qua Christus primum praedicavit et miracula fecit: ibi enim aquas in vinum convertit, apostolos elegit.

Via maris. Mare in hoc loco lacus Genezareth, qui Jordane influente efficitur. In cujus littore est Capharnaum, 1247DTiberias, Bethsaida et Corozain, in quibus Dominus maxime commoratus est, et sic: Populus qui ambulabat in tenebris vidit lucem magnam.

Galilaeae gentium. Galilaeae duae sunt: una gentium, vicina Tyriis in tribu Nephtali; altera circa Tiberiadem et stagnum Genesareth, in tribu Zabulon.

VERS. 2.-- In regione umbrae mortis. (ID.) In umbra mortis sunt, qui in peccatis adhuc vivunt: In regione umbrae mortis, qui jam mortuis operibus ad inferna descenderunt.

VERS. 3.-- Non magnificasti laetitiam. Nobis de salute Judaeorum; quamvis enim gaudeant de gentium conversione, dolent tamen de Judaeorum repulsione.

1248ALaetabuntur coram te, etc. Propheta ad Christum de discipulis.

VERS. 4.-- Jugum enim. (ID.) Habet Christus jugum, sed suave; et diabolus, sed importabile onere. Et Christus principatum super humerum, in quo crucem portavit, et diabolus in humero virgam, qua superbit.

Exactoris. Ipsius, scilicet exigentis. Quotidie enim nummum peccati quem primo homini commisit, a posteris cum usura exigit.

VERS. 5.-- Quia omnis violenta praedatio. (ID.) Acsi dicat: Genus humanum quod diabolus depraedatus est, auferet ei Dominus violenter, id est, potenter, et ipsum cum tumultu detrudet in infernum. Unde tam de ipso quam de membris ejus dicitur: 1248BPeriit memoria eorum cum sonitu (Psal. IX).

Vestimentum mistum. (ID.) Diabolus qui plenus omnium sanguine, qui homicida ab initio; vel homines, quibus diabolus tanquam veste circumdatur, quorum manus plenae sunt sanguine.

VERS. 6.-- Puer natus est nobis. Non Judaeis, nobis, etc., usque ad sed oriri non videntibus non videtur.

Principatus super humerum. (ID.) Dum duceretur ad patibulum, portavit crucem, in qua meruit principatum.

(ID.) Septuaginta: Magni consilii angelus. Quod in Hebraeo non habetur, sed nominum majestate perterriti, non ausi sunt de puero dicere, quod aperte Deus appellandus sit. Ideo pro his sex nominibus 1248Choc posuerunt, magni consilii angelus.

VERS. 7.-- Zelus Domini. (ID.) Coepit ab Emmanuel prophetia, ubi dicitur: Pete tibi signum a Deo. Hic finitur.

VERS. 8.-- Verbum misit Dominus. (ID.) Verbum Hebraicum (דבר) Dabar, etc., usque ad despiciunt Ecclesiam, et simplicitatem ejus putant imperitiam.

VERS. 10.-- Lateres ceciderunt. (ID.) Dicebant superius, etc., usque ad ex fortibus et bellicosis principibus populum muniemus.

Cedros. Id est, invincibiles ex nobis principes praeficiemus eis.

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 1.-- In the first time, (HIER.) These two tribes were led into captivity by the Assyrians, etc., as far as it first saw the light of Christ preaching.

The land of Zabulon. (ID.) In which Christ first preached and wrought miracles: for there he turned water into wine, and chose the apostles.

The way of the sea. The sea in this place is the lake of Genezareth, which is made by the inflowing of the Jordan. Upon whose shore is Capharnaum, 1247DTiberias, Bethsaida and Corozain, in which the Lord chiefly abode; and so: The people that walked in darkness saw a great light.

Of the Galilee of the Gentiles. There are two Galilees: one of the Gentiles, near to the Tyrians in the tribe of Nephtali; the other about Tiberias and the pool of Genesareth, in the tribe of Zabulon.

VERS. 2.-- In the region of the shadow of death. (ID.) They are in the shadow of death who still live in sins: in the region of the shadow of death, they who have already gone down to the lower regions with dead works.

VERS. 3.-- Thou hast not magnified the joy. To us, concerning the salvation of the Jews; for although they rejoice over the conversion of the Gentiles, yet they grieve over the rejection of the Jews.

1248AThey shall rejoice before thee, etc. The prophet to Christ, concerning the disciples.

VERS. 4.-- For the yoke. (ID.) Christ has a yoke, but a sweet one; the devil also, but one unbearable by its burden. And Christ has the princedom upon his shoulder, upon which he bore the cross; and the devil upon his shoulder a rod, wherewith he is proud.

Of the exactor. Of him, namely, that exacts. For daily he exacts from posterity, with usury, the coin of sin which he committed to the first man.

VERS. 5.-- For every violent plundering. (ID.) As if he should say: The human race which the devil has plundered, the Lord shall take away from him violently, that is, powerfully, and shall thrust him down with tumult into hell. Whence both of him and of his members it is said: 1248BTheir memory hath perished with a noise (Psal. IX).

The garment mingled. (ID.) The devil, who is full of the blood of all, who is a murderer from the beginning; or men, with whom the devil is clothed about as with a garment, whose hands are full of blood.

VERS. 6.-- A child is born to us. Not to the Jews, to us, etc., as far as but to them that see not, he seems not to rise. [Vulg. Is 9:6 Parvulus enim natus est nobis, "For a little one is born to us"]

The princedom upon his shoulder. (ID.) While he was being led to the gibbet, he bore the cross, upon which he merited the princedom.

(ID.) The Seventy: The angel of great counsel. Which is not had in the Hebrew; but, terrified by the majesty of the names, they did not dare to say of the child that he was openly to be called God. Therefore for these six names 1248Cthey put this, the angel of great counsel.

VERS. 7.-- The zeal of the Lord. (ID.) The prophecy began from Emmanuel, where it is said: Ask thee a sign from God. Here it is ended.

VERS. 8.-- The Lord sent a word. (ID.) The Hebrew word (דבר) Dabar, etc., as far as they despise the Church, and think her simplicity to be unskilfulness.

VERS. 10.-- The bricks are fallen. (ID.) They said above, etc., as far as we will fortify the people out of strong and warlike princes.

The cedars. That is, we will set over them unconquerable princes out of ourselves.

CAPUT X.

1248DVERS. 1.-- Vae qui condunt. (HIER.) Haec plerique adhuc contra decem tribus dici arbitrantur, etc., usque ad cum captivitatis, vel judicii dies advenerit.

VERS. 5.-- Vae Assur. (ID.) Hucusque contra decem et duas tribus, etc., usque ad et non solum captivavit, sed partim delevit.

VERS. 7.-- Ipse autem. (ID.) Sic philosophi, sic haeretici, et sic persecutores Christiani nominis: cum Deus aliquos eis verberandos permittit, in omnes saeviunt, nec verberare, sed occidere cupiunt: et paucis superatis in omnes audaciam sumunt.

VERS. 9.-- Nunquid non principes mei simul. Ordo: Reges sunt mei principes, id est, mihi servientes: 1249Amultos enim reges secum adduxit, quos subjugaverat.

Damascus. Damascus est metropolis in Syria: hanc condidit Eliezer, servus Abrahae; in hac regnavit Rasin.

VERS. 10.-- Quomodo invenit manus. Vox Sennacherib admirantis potentiam suam. Plene dicta complevit. Omnes enim civitates decem tribuum, et Juda et Benjamin praeter Jerusalem depraedatus est, et ipsius partem inferiorem, quam tradidit ei Sobna scriba.

VERS. 12.-- Cum impleverit Dominus. Subversiones, scilicet urbis, etiam obsidionem quando venit Rabsaces, et fecit ea quae in sequentibus commemorantur.

1249BVERS. 14.-- Ganniret. Gannire est vulpium, vel, ut alii, avium.

VERS. 16.-- Et subtus gloriam. Dicit Hieronymus secundum Hebraeos, corpora interfectorum Assyriorum coelesti igne exusta, et in pulverem redacta, vestimenta illaesa; et venientes Judaei, vestium summitate arrepta, pulverem excutiebant.

(HIER.) Quidam volunt hunc regem significare contrariam fortitudinem, etc., usque ad tradunt enim Hebraei decem tantum de ejus exercitu remansisse.

VERS. 17.-- Et erit lumen. Dicunt Hebraei quod Michael archangelus fuerit, cui commissa est custodia illius gentis, sicut Daniel dicit.

1249CVERS. 18.-- Et gloria saltus. Carmelus mons magnus, fecundus arboribus. Quasi exercitus innumerus (sicut ligna sylvarum), et corpore et anima peribit.

Ad carnem. (ID.) Haeretici quoque, cum ecclesiastica doctrina illuxerit, et eorum decipula patuerit, ad tantam venient solitudinem, ut de saltu et infructuosis arboribus, et innumerabili multitudine, vix pauci resideant, qui eorum sequantur errorem.

VERS. 20.-- Et erit in die illa. (HIER.) Cum Phacee et Rasin vellent venire, etc., usque ad sed in Deo spem ponant.

VERS. 21.-- Reliquiae convertentur. Secundum Josephum innumerabili Judaeorum multitudine occisa, 1249Dintelligimus paucos in apostolis et apostolicis viris esse salvatos.

VERS. 22.-- Consummatio abbreviata. Perfectio eorum quae de Christo erant tam longo tempore ante praedicta tribus annis et dimidio completa.

Justitiam, quia injuste occisus, mortem et diabolum juste vicit, et mundum redemit morte.

VERS. 23.-- Consummationem enim. (ID.) Ad litteram. Fecit, quia tantam multitudinem praeter decem, occidit brevi spatio unius noctis.

VERS. 24.-- Super te in via Aegypti. Captivitatis decem tribubus, etc., usque ad cepit et subvertit.

VERS. 25.-- Paululum modicumque. Hoc longo tempore post completum est; sed omnia saecula 1250Aaeternitati (in quo omnia praesentia sunt) comparata momentanea sunt.

VERS. 26.-- Virgam suam in mare. (ID.) Quasi: Antequam veniat super te, aggredietur Tharacam, regem Aethiopiae, et transibit mare Rubrum, et levabit virgam super Aegyptum, et sic cum multo exercitu revertetur contra Jerusalem.

VERS. 29.-- Transierunt cursim. Gaba sedes, etc. Praeteritum pro futuro more prophetico.

VERS. 31, 32.-- Confortamini adhuc dies est. (ID.) Postquam Sennacherib tetendit in Nobe tentoria, volens mane expugnare Jerusalem, non cessavit usque in mediam noctem minari desolationem Jerusalem, dicens: En, ista sola civitas liberari de manu mea poterit?

1250BVERS. 33.-- Ecce dominator Dominus exercituum, etc. Dixit pueri nomen de virgine nascituri, etc., usque ad et Libanus cum suis cedris succidatur.

CHAPTER X.

1248DVERS. 1.-- Woe to them that make. (HIER.) Very many think that these things are still said against the ten tribes, etc., as far as when the day of the captivity, or of the judgment, shall have come.

VERS. 5.-- Woe to Assur. (ID.) Thus far against the ten and the two tribes, etc., as far as and he not only led captive, but in part destroyed.

VERS. 7.-- But he. (ID.) So the philosophers, so the heretics, and so the persecutors of the Christian name: when God permits some to be scourged by them, they rage against all, and desire not to scourge but to kill; and when a few are overcome they take boldness against all.

VERS. 9.-- Are not my princes together. The order is: Kings are my princes, that is, serving me: 1249Afor he brought many kings with him, whom he had subjugated.

Damascus. Damascus is the metropolis in Syria: Eliezer, the servant of Abraham, founded it; in it Rasin reigned.

VERS. 10.-- As my hand hath found. The voice of Sennacherib marvelling at his own power. He fulfilled fully the things he had said. For he plundered all the cities of the ten tribes, and of Juda and Benjamin, except Jerusalem, and the lower part of it, which Sobna the scribe delivered to him.

VERS. 12.-- When the Lord shall have accomplished. The overthrows, namely of the city, and also the siege, when Rabsaces came and did the things which are recounted in what follows.

1249BVERS. 14.-- That should yelp. To yelp belongs to foxes, or, as others say, to birds.

VERS. 16.-- And under his glory. Jerome says, according to the Hebrews, that the bodies of the slain Assyrians were burnt up by heavenly fire and reduced to dust, their garments unhurt; and that the Jews, coming and seizing the top of the garments, shook out the dust.

(HIER.) Some will have it that this king signifies the contrary fortitude, etc., as far as for the Hebrews hand down that only ten of his army remained.

VERS. 17.-- And there shall be a light. The Hebrews say that it was Michael the archangel, to whom the custody of that nation was committed, as Daniel says.

1249CVERS. 18.-- And the glory of his forest. Carmel is a great mountain, fruitful in trees. As it were an innumerable army (like the timber of the woods), it shall perish both in body and in soul.

Unto the flesh. (ID.) The heretics also, when the ecclesiastical doctrine has shone forth and their snare has been laid open, shall come to so great a solitude that, out of a forest and unfruitful trees and an innumerable multitude, scarcely a few shall be left who follow their error.

VERS. 20.-- And it shall be in that day. (HIER.) When Phacee and Rasin were minded to come, etc., as far as but let them put their hope in God.

VERS. 21.-- The remnant shall be converted. According to Josephus, an innumerable multitude of the Jews being slain, 1249Dwe understand that a few were saved in the apostles and in apostolic men.

VERS. 22.-- The consumption abridged. The perfecting of the things which had been foretold of Christ so long a time before, completed in three years and a half.

Justice, because, being unjustly slain, he justly overcame death and the devil, and redeemed the world by his death.

VERS. 23.-- For the consummation. (ID.) To the letter. He did it, because so great a multitude, except ten, he slew in the short space of one night.

VERS. 24.-- Upon thee in the way of Egypt. Of the captivity of the ten tribes, etc., as far as he took and overthrew.

VERS. 25.-- A very little and a moment. This was fulfilled a long time after; but all ages 1250Acompared to eternity (in which all things are present) are momentary.

VERS. 26.-- His rod into the sea. (ID.) As if: Before he come upon thee, he shall set upon Tharaca, king of Ethiopia, and shall cross the Red Sea, and shall lift up his rod over Egypt, and so with a great army shall return against Jerusalem. [Vulg. Is 10:26 virgam suam super mare, "his rod over the sea"]

VERS. 29.-- They have passed over in haste. Gaba the seat, etc. The past for the future, after the prophetic manner.

VERS. 31, 32.-- Be strengthened; it is yet day. (ID.) After Sennacherib had pitched his tents in Nobe, wishing to storm Jerusalem in the morning, he ceased not until the middle of the night to threaten the desolation of Jerusalem, saying: Behold, shall this city alone be able to be delivered out of my hand?

1250BVERS. 33.-- Behold the sovereign Lord of hosts, etc. He said the name of the child that was to be born of a virgin, etc., as far as and let Libanus with its cedars be cut down.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 1.-- Et egredietur virga. (HIER.) Usque ad principium visionis Babylonis, prophetia est de Christo, quam per partes explanamus, ne lectoris memoriam confundamus.

Egredietur. Virgam et florem Judaei ipsum Christum interpretantur, etc., usque ad et tu es Filius meus primogenitus, qui regnas in sempiternum.

VERS. 2.-- Et requiescet. (ID.) Quia in ipso habitat omnis plenitudo Divinitatis.

Spiritus. Sicut sermo Domini, lux, vita et resurrectio 1250Cvocatur, etc., usque ad hi sunt septem oculi in lapide uno secundum Zachariam.

VERS. 3.-- Et replebit eum spiritus. (ID.) Spiritualiter propter parvulos qui timore indigent, quem foras mittit perfecta charitas. Qui enim timet, poenam habet, et non est perfectus in charitate.

Non secundum. Non accipiet personam, quia non est personarum acceptor Deus, sed in omni gente; qui timet Deum acceptus est illi.

Arguet. (ID.) Pharisaeos et reprehendentes apotolos, quando in sabbato ambulantes per sata, spicas vellentes manibus confricabant et manducabant.

VERS. 4.-- Virga oris sui. (ID.) Potest per virgam sententia judicii intelligi. In principio enim praedicationis 1250Ddixit: Poenitentiam agite, appropinquabit enim regnum coelorum; et subjunxit: Omnis arbor quae non facit fructus bonos eradicetur et in ignem mittetur.

VERS. 5.-- Interficiet impium. Si enim Michael interficiet (ut quidam dicunt), ipse Deus tamen in Michaele hoc faciet.

Fides. (HIER.) Fideles bene operando adhaerebunt ei qui factus est sapientia, justitia et sanctificatio, qui ait: Ego lux et vita.

VERS. 6.-- Lupus cum agno. (ID.) Paulus cum Petro, unde: Benjamin lupus rapax. Et: Pasce oves meas. Habitabit lupus cum agno. Non agnus et haedus habitabant cum lupo et pardo, sed lupus et 1251Apardus, agni et haedi imitantur innocentiam, scilicet Christum.

Habitabit lupus. (HIER.) Judaei nostri judaizantes, etc., usque ad nihil malum, nisi turpitudinem.

(ID.) Vitulus et ovis humiles et innocentes, etc., usque ad hoc praestet Ecclesia in mundo.

VERS. 7.-- Leo quasi bos, etc. (ID.) Principes enim hujus saeculi et simplices alii historiae superficie sunt contenti; triticum enim et medullam, id est, interiorem sensum non intelligunt.

VERS. 8.-- Infans. Christus, vel apostoli, malitia parvuli, sensu perfecti: qui daemones ejiciebant de corporibus obsessis.

Reguli. Regulus, rex omnium serpentium, etc., usque ad de obsessis corporibus scilicet.

1251BManum. Id est, divinam potentiam in comprehensum exinde aspidem vel regulum, id est, diabolum, tradet captivum.

VERS. 9.-- Quia repleta. More suo, in fine prophetalia verba panduntur, ne ad litteram accipiantur.

VERS. 10.-- Qui stat in signum, unde: Ecce positus est hic in ruinam, et in resurrectionem multorum in Israel, et in signum cui contradicetur (Luc. II). LXX: Qui consurget ut sit princeps, etc., usque ad idem significat sepulcrum. Pro dormitione et requie, altero verbo, sed eodem sensu, sepulcrum vertimus.

VERS. 11.-- Residuum. (ID.) Apostolus: Reliquiae salvae fient. Tradunt enim historiae quod apostoli 1251Ctoto orbe diffusi praedicaverunt Evangelium: et quidem Persas, Indosque penetraverunt, ut Aethiopia manum daret Deo.

VERS. 12.-- Signum. Crucis, in qua est victoria, ut sciant omnes in quo diabolus sit victus.

A quatuor plagis. (ID.) Hoc in Pentecosten videtur impletum, etc., usque ad ut totius mundi vocatio demonstretur.

VERS. 13.-- Et auferetur, etc. (ID.) Quando Isaias hoc praedicebat, inter duas et decem tribus discordia inexorabilis erat, credentes autem pacificati sunt in fide Christi et unitate charitatis.

VERS. 14.-- Praeceptum manus, etc. Quod praecipiunt operantur; et verbo docent, et exemplo.

VERS. 15.-- Linguam maris. (ID.) Quae idola laudabat. 1251DMare, id est, populus Aegypti, (qui super omnes idola colit,) ut Deum laudet et timeat, et idola condemnet et abijciat.

Et levabit. Ut Idumaea, et Moab, et filii Ammon tradent manus apostolis, sic et Aegyptii.

Et percutiet. Id est, minuet pro uno septem principes ponendo, etc., usque ad ut siccis pedibus transirent.

Ut transeant per eum, etc. Sub metaphora, Nilus divisus et in partes caesus dicitur, etc., usque ad Domino Evangelio viam praeparante.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 1.-- And there shall come forth a rod. (HIER.) Up to the beginning of the vision of Babylon the prophecy is of Christ, which we expound by parts, lest we confound the memory of the reader.

Shall come forth. The Jews interpret the rod and the flower to be Christ himself, etc., as far as and thou art my firstborn Son, who reignest for ever.

VERS. 2.-- And shall rest. (ID.) Because in him dwells all the fulness of the Godhead.

The Spirit. As the word of the Lord is called light, life and resurrection, etc., 1250Cas far as these are the seven eyes upon one stone, according to Zacharias.

VERS. 3.-- And the spirit shall fill him. (ID.) Spiritually, for the sake of the little ones who have need of fear, which perfect charity casts out. For he that fears has punishment, and is not perfect in charity.

Not according to. He shall not accept the person, because God is not an accepter of persons, but in every nation he that fears God is acceptable to him.

He shall reprove. (ID.) The Pharisees, and them that reproved the apostles apotolos, when, walking on the sabbath through the corn, they plucked the ears with their hands and rubbed them and ate.

VERS. 4.-- The rod of his mouth. (ID.) By the rod the sentence of judgment can be understood. For in the beginning of his preaching 1250Dhe said: Do penance, for the kingdom of heaven shall draw near; [Vulg. Mt 3:2 appropinquavit, "hath drawn near"] and he subjoined: Every tree that doth not bring forth good fruits shall be rooted up and cast into the fire. [Vulg. Mt 3:10 Omnis ergo arbor quae non facit fructum bonum, excidetur, "Every tree therefore that doth not bring forth good fruit shall be cut down"]

VERS. 5.-- He shall slay the wicked one. For if Michael shall slay him (as some say), yet God himself shall do this in Michael.

Faith. (HIER.) The faithful, by working well, shall cleave to him who was made wisdom, justice and sanctification, who says: I am light and life.

VERS. 6.-- The wolf with the lamb. (ID.) Paul with Peter, whence: Benjamin a ravening wolf. And: Feed my sheep. The wolf shall dwell with the lamb. It was not the lamb and the kid that dwelt with the wolf and the leopard, but the wolf and 1251Athe leopard imitate the innocence of the lambs and the kids, namely Christ.

The wolf shall dwell. (HIER.) Our judaizing Jews, etc., as far as nothing evil, save baseness.

(ID.) The calf and the sheep, the humble and the innocent, etc., as far as let the Church furnish this in the world.

VERS. 7.-- The lion as the ox, etc. (ID.) For the princes of this world, and other simple folk, are content with the surface of the history; for they do not understand the wheat and the marrow, that is, the inner sense.

VERS. 8.-- The infant. Christ, or the apostles, children in malice, perfect in understanding: who cast out demons from possessed bodies.

Of the basilisk. The basilisk, the king of all serpents, etc., as far as from possessed bodies, namely.

1251BThe hand. That is, the divine power: he shall deliver up captive the asp or the basilisk thereupon seized, that is, the devil.

VERS. 9.-- For it is filled. After his manner, at the end the prophetic words are laid open, lest they be taken to the letter.

VERS. 10.-- Who standeth for a sign, whence: Behold this child is set for the ruin and for the resurrection of many in Israel, and for a sign which shall be contradicted (Luc. II). The Seventy: Who shall rise up to be a prince, etc., as far as the same signifies a sepulchre. For a sleeping and a rest, with another word but the same sense, we have rendered sepulchre.

VERS. 11.-- The remnant. (ID.) The Apostle: The remnant shall be saved. For the histories relate that the apostles, 1251Cspread abroad through the whole world, preached the Gospel: and indeed they penetrated the Persians and the Indians, so that Ethiopia gave her hand to God.

VERS. 12.-- A sign. Of the cross, in which is the victory, that all may know in what the devil was overcome.

From the four quarters. (ID.) This seems to have been fulfilled at Pentecost, etc., as far as that the calling of the whole world might be shown.

VERS. 13.-- And it shall be taken away, etc. (ID.) When Isaias foretold this, between the two and the ten tribes there was an implacable discord; but they that believed were made peaceable in the faith of Christ and the unity of charity.

VERS. 14.-- The precept of the hand, etc. What they command, they do; and they teach both by word and by example.

VERS. 15.-- The tongue of the sea. (ID.) Which praised idols. 1251DThe sea, that is, the people of Egypt (which above all others worships idols), that it may praise and fear God, and condemn and cast away idols.

And he shall lift up. As Idumaea, and Moab, and the children of Ammon shall give their hands to the apostles, so also the Egyptians.

And he shall strike. That is, he shall lessen it by setting seven princes for one, etc., as far as that they might pass over dry-shod.

That they may pass through it, etc. Under a metaphor the Nile is said to be divided and cut into parts, etc., as far as the Lord preparing the way by the Gospel.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- Confitebor tibi. (HIER.) Sicut transito 1252Amari Rubro, dixerunt: Cantemus Domino, etc. Sic regno mundi destructo, dicit Ecclesia: Confitebor tibi.

VERS. 3.-- De fontibus Salvatoris. (ID.) Unde: Benedicite Domino de fontibus Israel, etc., usque ad ut prophetia cum Evangelio conveniat.

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- I will give thanks to thee. (HIER.) As, the Red Sea being passed, 1252Athey said: Let us sing to the Lord, etc. So, the kingdom of the world being destroyed, the Church says: I will give thanks to thee.

VERS. 3.-- Out of the fountains of the Saviour. (ID.) Whence: Bless ye the Lord from the fountains of Israel, etc., as far as that the prophecy may agree with the Gospel.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- Onus Babylonis. (HIER.) Babylon, metropolis Chaldaeorum, etc., usque ad qui dicit: Haec omnia tibi dabo, si cadens adoraveris me.

VERS. 7.-- Dissolventur, etc. Quia nullum opus Dei justitia dignum reperietur.

VERS. 8.-- Combustae. Quasi carbones nimio dolore, vel in re. Multi enim fuere combusti. Combustae igne. Igne quem sibi succenderunt, quia dicere 1252Bnon possunt: Signatum est super nos lumen vultus tui, Domine (Psal. IV), et: Nos autem revelata facie gloriam Domini contemplantes (II Cor. III).

VERS. 10.-- Obtenebratus est sol. Possunt haec ad diem judicii referri, quando omnia luminaria obtenebrari videbuntur comparata splendori Christi.

VERS. 11.-- Et visitabo super orbis mala. Vindicabo ut corrigam, etc., usque ad: Misericordia autem mea non auferetur ab eis.

Et requiscere. Quasi diceret: Hoc faciam, ut qui superbit humilietur.

VERS. 12.-- Pretiosior erit vir auro, etc. (ID.) Omnia rara pretiosa, id est, vir Babylonis rarius invenietur auro. Similiter dicitur sermo Domini pretiosus, id est, raro inveniebantur. In die quoque 1252Cjudicii visitabit Dominus mala orbis, et quiescere faciet arrogantiam daemonum, et pauci invenientur electi propter persecutionem Antichristi.

VERS. 13.-- Coelum turbabo, etc. Non quod animata sunt, sed propter indignationem Domini, qui respicit terram, et facit eam tremere. Forsitan imminente captivitate, coelum tonando, et terra concutiendo mota est. Potest de die judicii legi.

VERS. 14.-- Damula fugiens. Damula, diabolus est vel prava doctrina, cujus magistri in damula significantur, quae Graece dicitur δορκὰς.

VERS. 17.-- Ecce ego suscitabo, etc. Legimus in Genesi X, quod et Madai filius Noe auctor Medorum fuit: qui interpretatur mensura, sive a potente vel forti. Igitur contra Babylonios qui mente confusi 1252Dsunt, suscitantur a potente et forti Deo fortitudines ad puniendum, quae reddant unicuique secundum opera sua.

VERS. 19.-- Et erit Babylon illa civitas, etc. (ID.) Non postea reaedificata est, etc., usque ad a lupis et canibus devorandas.

VERS. 21.-- Bestiae, etc. Per has omnes bestias lucis refugas, daemonum vel angelorum fortitudines intelligimus, quibus ad puniendum traduntur.

Struthiones. De quibus in Job satis tractatum est, quae de terra altius non elevantur, cum pennas habere videantur.

Pilosi. Sylvestres homines hispidi, qui et incubones, vel satyri, vel genera daemonum.

1253AVERS. 22.-- Ululae in aedibus. Magnitudinis corvinae, sed maculis respersae, quae rostro in palude sito horrendum stridunt.

Sirenes. Sunt serpentes cristati et alati velut alii pisces marini in specie muliebri, vel portenta diaboli, quae dulci cantilena decipiunt homines, hujus saeculi naufragium, non clausis auribus transeuntes.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- The burden of Babylon. (HIER.) Babylon, the metropolis of the Chaldeans, etc., as far as who says: All these will I give thee, if falling down thou wilt adore me.

VERS. 7.-- They shall be dissolved, etc. Because no work worthy of the justice of God shall be found.

VERS. 8.-- Burnt. As it were coals, by excessive grief, or in very fact. For many were burnt. Burnt with fire. With the fire which they kindled for themselves, because they cannot say: 1252BThe light of thy countenance, O Lord, is signed upon us (Psal. IV), and: But we, beholding with open face the glory of the Lord (II Cor. III). [Vulg. 2 Cor 3:18 Nos vero omnes, revelata facie gloriam Domini speculantes, "But we all, with open face beholding the glory of the Lord"]

VERS. 10.-- The sun is darkened. These things can be referred to the day of judgment, when all the lights shall be seen to be darkened in comparison of the brightness of Christ.

VERS. 11.-- And I will visit the evils of the world. I will take vengeance that I may correct, etc., as far as: But my mercy shall not be taken away from them.

And to rest. As if he should say: This I will do, that he who is proud may be humbled.

VERS. 12.-- A man shall be more precious than gold, etc. (ID.) All rare things are precious, that is, a man of Babylon shall be found more rarely than gold. Likewise the word of the Lord is called precious, that is, they were rarely found. In the day of 1252Cjudgment also the Lord shall visit the evils of the world, and shall make the arrogance of the demons to be still, and few elect shall be found because of the persecution of Antichrist.

VERS. 13.-- I will trouble the heaven, etc. Not that they are animate, but because of the indignation of the Lord, who looks upon the earth and makes it tremble. Perhaps, the captivity being at hand, the heaven was moved by thundering and the earth by shaking. It can be read of the day of judgment.

VERS. 14.-- The fleeing doe. The doe is the devil, or perverse doctrine, whose masters are signified in the doe, which in Greek is called δορκὰς.

VERS. 17.-- Behold I will stir up, etc. We read in Genesis X that Madai also, the son of Noe, was the author of the Medes: who is interpreted measure, or from a powerful or a strong one. Therefore against the Babylonians, who are confounded in mind, 1252Dthere are stirred up by the powerful and strong God fortitudes for punishing, which shall render to every one according to his works.

VERS. 19.-- And Babylon shall be that city, etc. (ID.) It was not afterward rebuilt, etc., as far as to be devoured by wolves and dogs.

VERS. 21.-- Beasts, etc. By all these beasts that shun the light we understand the fortitudes of demons or of angels, to whom they are delivered up for punishment.

Ostriches. Of which there has been treatment enough in Job: which are not raised higher from the earth, although they seem to have wings.

Hairy ones. Wild shaggy men, who are also incubi, or satyrs, or kinds of demons.

1253AVERS. 22.-- Owls in the houses. Of the bigness of a raven, but sprinkled with spots, which with their beak set in the marsh make a horrid screeching.

Sirens. They are serpents crested and winged, like other sea-fishes, in the shape of a woman; or portents of the devil, which by sweet singing deceive men, the shipwreck of this world, as they pass by with ears not closed.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- Jacob. Nota proprietatem verborum. Jacob qui adhuc in lucta est, miseretur: Israel, qui post victoriam nomen accepit, elegit.

VERS. 4.-- Quomodo, etc. Admirando quomodo vastator terrarum, etc., usque ad novissima autem inimica destruetur mors.

Exactor. (HIER.) Qui exigit usque ad novissimum 1253Bquadrantem a debitoribus suis, qui dicunt illud: Dimitte nobis debita (Matth. V). Huic tradentur debitores, huic tradidit Paulus Corinthum fornicantem.

VERS. 5.-- Baculum. Vocat Nabuchodonosor vel diabolum, quia sicut per illum flagellantur a Deo impii, sic per hunc in poenis inferni vel quotidie in peccato suggerendo, insidiando.

VERS. 8.-- Abietes quoque. Principes diversarum gentium ab illo afflicti; vel filii, quorum patres interfecit.

Ex quo dormisti. Quia victo forti et direptis vasis, ejus satellites corruerunt, unde: Ite in ignem aeternum, qui paratus est diabolo, et angelis ejus (Matth. 1253CXXV). Nota mortis mors dormitio dicitur.

Qui succidat. Unde psalmus: Operuit montes umbra ejus, et arbusta ejus cedros Dei (Psal. LXXIX.) Has aliquando peccantes tradit Dominus supplicio conterendas: unde dicitur: Conteret Dominus cedros Libani (Psal. XXVIII).

VERS. 9.-- Infernus. Habitatores vel principes ejus inferni subter te. Sub terra enim est infernus, in quo visus est dives ille a Lazaro, unde Dominus vinctos suos eruit, vinctum diabolum dimisit.

Omnes principes. Etsi haec facta non sunt, tamen fieri potuerunt. Solatium est malorum inimicos habere socios poenarum.

VERS. 10.-- Vulneratus es. Qui immortalis et Deus videbaris. Nos pro imbecillitate sensuum putabamus 1253Dnon posse resistere potentiae Dei, et te solum in altitudine tua permanere.

VERS. 11.-- Subter. (ID.) Putredo tinea et operimentum vermium, etc., usque ad qui dicebat Christo: Haec omnia tibi dabo, si cadens adoraveris me (Matth. IV).

VERS. 12.-- Qui mane oriebaris. (HIER.) Hi sunt falsi apostoli, etc., usque ad et in lateribus aquilonis, in frigidis.

VERS. 13.-- In coelum conscendam. (ID.) In firmamento erat, in coelum ubi est Domini solium cupiebat ascendere. Haec omnia ad haereticos sunt referenda, qui cum deorsum sint cum principe suo, jactant se excelsos esse.

1254AIn lateribus aquilonis. Unde: Ab aquilone exardescunt mala, et succenditur olla Jeremiae. De quo: Dicam aquiloni, Da: id est, redde captivos.

Nubium. Id est prophetarum, de quibus: Mandabo nubibus meis ne pluant super eam imbrem (Psal. XXXV). Et veritas tua usque ad nubes. Judas nubes fuit cum aliis apostolis, sed vitio suo diabolum ascensorem suscepit.

VERS. 15.-- Verumtamen ad infernum detraheris. Qui per virtutes potuisti ascendere, per vitia cades. Sancti habent pennas columbae et aquilae ut volent et requiescant: impii quasi plumbum merguntur in aquis vehementibus, et descendunt in profundum quas lapis (Zach. V). Iniquitas sedet super talentum plumbi.

1254BDetraheris. Invitus Christus; descendit voluntarius.

Profundum. Ad infimas et pessimas poenas. Quanto enim gradus altior, tanto casus gravior.

Laci. Id est, inferni, etc., usque ad sed per teporem et simulationem fidei a Domino rejiciuntur.

VERS. 16.-- Est vir. (ID.) Non Deus; unde: Inimicus homo superseminavit zizaniam (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 17.-- Urbes. (ID.) Ecclesias, ut faceret synagogas diaboli, et haeretica pravitate pollueret munditiam fidei.

Vinctis ejus non. Quos solvunt apostoli, imitatores magistri, etc., usque ad sed in tenebris recludebat, ut nec Evilmerodach filio suo aperiret.

VERS. 18.-- Omnes reges. (ID.) Reges gentium, 1254Cetc., usque ad nec in morte quiescit a cruciatibus.

VERS. 19.-- Tu autem. Videtur fabulae consentire, etc., usque ad reviviscet pater tuus.

Quasi cadaver. (ID.) Multitudine peccatorum putruerunt, et corruptae sunt cicatrices meae (Psal. XXXVII). Virtus suavis odoris est.

VERS. 20.-- Consortium. Scilicet sepulturae. Altera est enim poena auctoris, altera ejus qui ab auctoritate compulsus: Hic est gladius quo vulnerati sunt plurimi, et interfecti.

Tu enim terram. Quasi: In superbia tua regnum Chaldaeorum destructum est, quod adhuc mansisset, nisi superbia tua non fuisset.

1254DPopulum. Quem tibi Deus commiserat corrigendum et regendum.

Occidisti. Non Deo reservans vivos, sed poenae tuae praeparans socios.

Non vocabitur. Ideo non vocabitur, in aeternum, etc., usque ad deleta est omnis progenies, pro superbia et impietate patris.

VERS. 21.-- Iniquitate: (ID.) LXX. Pro iniquitate patris tui. Quod quidam non intelligentes, draconem, qui regnat in mari, quem Leviathan appellant, Hebraei volunt esse patrem diaboli; secundum illud: Quia mendax est et pater ejus (Joan. VIII), quod male intelligendo depravant.

VERS. 23.-- Et ponam. Nihil est in ea nisi venatio 1255Aregia, et coctilibus muris post annos plurimos refectis inclusa.

In possessionem ericii. Mundus quoque ab ericio et paludibus possidetur modo, id est, ab immundis spiritibus; sed in fine mundi scopabit, id est, purgabit eum Dominus igne purgatorio.

VERS. 24.-- Juravit Dominus. Hucusque de Babylone et regibus, etc., usque ad et hoc in terra Juda et in montibus ejus, sicut Dominus praedixerat.

VERS. 25.-- In montibus. Nota proprietatem, etc., usque ad sed et eos qui virtutibus profecerunt.

VERS. 26.-- Hoc consilium, id est, auferatur jugum ejus, etc., usque ad generaliter ad orbem terrarum pertinere.

1255BVERS. 28.-- In anno quo mortuus. A principio libri hucusque sub Ozia, Joatham, Achaz, vaticinatus est Isaias, usque ad finem sub Ezechia praedicta et partim completa.

Achaz. Quo vivente, regnante in peccatoribus: nec pondus contra alienigenas, nec sermo Dei (juxta Septuaginta) fieri poterat ad Prophetam.

VERS. 29.-- Ne laeteris. Rege mortuo, inimici laetantur, quasi aliquid lucri facturi novis rebus: hinc Philisthaei, quos Achaz afflixit, de morte ejus laetantur et Judaeis insultant, quia maturo rege perdito, subjaceant ejus filio, quo regnante, se putant fines eorum posse invadere.

Virga percussoris. (HIER.) Abjecto jugo Dei, etc., usque ad et de eis serpentes volantes, vel qui absorbeant 1255Cvolucres.

VERS. 31.-- Ulula. (ID.) Quasi: Philisthaei, plorate miseriam vestram, quae venit per Sennacherib.

Ulula, porta; clama, civitas. Philisthaei potione cadentes, etc., usque ad anima malis cogitationibus repleta.

Ab aquilone enim fumus. Exardescentibus sagittis diaboli, qui noxius oculis et contrarius lumini. Ab aquilone olla Jeremiae succenditur.

Non est qui effugiat agmen ejus. Nullus praeter Jerusalem, etc., usque ad et sola Jerusalem sit liberanda.

VERS. 32.-- Nuntiis gentis. Angelis qui praesunt civitatibus et gentibus: singulis mirantibus, cur sola Sion amaritudinem fumi vitaverit, vel evaserit.

1255DFundavit Sion. Super fundamentum prudentiae, justitiae, fortitudinis, temperantiae, quae sunt Christus.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- Jacob. Note the propriety of the words. Jacob, who is still in the wrestling, he has mercy on: Israel, who received the name after the victory, he chose.

VERS. 4.-- How, etc. Marvelling how the waster of lands, etc., as far as but the last enemy, death, shall be destroyed.

The exactor. (HIER.) Who exacts to the last 1253Bfarthing from his debtors, who say that word: Forgive us our debts (Matth. V). To him shall the debtors be delivered; to him Paul delivered the fornicator of Corinth.

VERS. 5.-- The staff. He calls Nabuchodonosor, or the devil, because as through the one the impious are scourged by God, so through the other in the punishments of hell, or daily by suggesting sin, by lying in wait.

VERS. 8.-- The fir trees also. The princes of divers nations afflicted by him; or the sons whose fathers he slew.

Since thou hast slept. Because, the strong man being overcome and his vessels plundered, his satellites fell down; whence: Go into everlasting fire, which was prepared for the devil and his angels (Matth. 1253CXXV). [Vulg. Mt 25:41 Discedite a me maledicti in ignem aeternum, "Depart from me, you cursed, into everlasting fire"] Note that the death of death is called a sleeping.

Who should cut down. Whence the psalm: The shadow of it covered the mountains, and its branches the cedars of God (Psal. LXXIX.) These, sinning at some time, the Lord delivers over to punishment to be bruised: whence it is said: The Lord shall crush the cedars of Libanus (Psal. XXVIII). [Vulg. Ps 28:5 confringet Dominus cedros Libani, "the Lord shall break in pieces the cedars of Libanus"]

VERS. 9.-- Hell. The inhabitants or the princes of that hell beneath thee. For hell is under the earth, in which that rich man was seen by Lazarus; whence the Lord drew out his own bound ones, and left the devil bound.

All the princes. Even though these things were not done, yet they could have been done. It is a solace of the wicked to have their enemies as companions of their punishments.

VERS. 10.-- Thou art wounded. Thou who didst seem immortal and God. We, by reason of the weakness of our senses, thought 1253Dthat it was not possible to resist the power of God, and that thou alone didst remain in thy height.

VERS. 11.-- Beneath. (ID.) Rottenness, the moth and the covering of worms, etc., as far as who said to Christ: All these will I give thee, if falling down thou wilt adore me (Matth. IV).

VERS. 12.-- Who didst rise in the morning. (HIER.) These are the false apostles, etc., as far as and in the sides of the north, in the cold places.

VERS. 13.-- I will ascend into heaven. (ID.) He was in the firmament; he desired to ascend into heaven, where is the throne of the Lord. All these things are to be referred to the heretics, who, though they be below with their prince, boast themselves to be on high.

1254AIn the sides of the north. Whence: From the north evils break forth, and the pot of Jeremias is kindled. Of which: I will say to the north: Give up: that is, restore the captives.

Of the clouds. That is, of the prophets, of whom: I will command my clouds that they rain no rain upon it (Psal. XXXV). And thy truth even unto the clouds. Judas was a cloud with the other apostles, but by his own fault he received the devil for his rider.

VERS. 15.-- But yet thou shalt be drawn down to hell. Thou who couldst have ascended by virtues shalt fall by vices. The saints have the wings of a dove and of an eagle, that they may fly and rest: the wicked are sunk as lead in the mighty waters, and go down into the deep as quas a stone (Zach. V). Iniquity sits upon a talent of lead.

1254BThou shalt be drawn down. Christ unwilling; he came down willingly.

The deep. To the lowest and worst punishments. For the higher the step, the heavier the fall.

Of the pit. That is, of hell, etc., as far as but through lukewarmness and the pretence of faith they are rejected by the Lord.

VERS. 16.-- Is this the man. (ID.) Not God; whence: An enemy man hath oversown cockle (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 17.-- The cities. (ID.) The churches, that he might make them synagogues of the devil, and might pollute the cleanness of the faith with heretical perversity.

To his prisoners not. Whom the apostles loose, imitators of their master, etc., as far as but he shut them up in darkness, so that he did not open even to Evilmerodach his son.

VERS. 18.-- All the kings. (ID.) The kings of the nations, 1254Cetc., as far as nor does he rest in death from torments.

VERS. 19.-- But thou. He seems to agree with the fable, etc., as far as thy father shall live again.

As a carcass. (ID.) By the multitude of sins my sores are putrefied and corrupted (Psal. XXXVII). Virtue is of a sweet odour.

VERS. 20.-- Fellowship. Of burial, namely. For one is the punishment of the author, another of him who was compelled by the authority: This is the sword wherewith very many were wounded and slain.

For thou the land. As if: In thy pride the kingdom of the Chaldeans has been destroyed, which would still have remained, unless thy pride had not been.

1254DThe people. Which God had committed to thee to be corrected and ruled.

Thou hast slain. Not reserving the living for God, but preparing companions for thy own punishment.

He shall not be called. Therefore he shall not be called, for ever, etc., as far as all the offspring is destroyed, for the pride and impiety of the father.

VERS. 21.-- For the iniquity: (ID.) The Seventy: For the iniquity of thy father. Which some not understanding, the Hebrews will have the dragon, who reigns in the sea, whom they call Leviathan, to be the father of the devil; according to that: Because he is a liar and his father (Joan. VIII), which by understanding ill they deprave.

VERS. 23.-- And I will make. There is nothing in it but the royal 1255Ahunting, and it is shut in with brick walls repaired after very many years.

For a possession for the hedgehog. The world also is possessed now by the hedgehog and by marshes, that is, by unclean spirits; but at the end of the world the Lord shall sweep it, that is, shall purge it with the fire of purgatory.

VERS. 24.-- The Lord hath sworn. Thus far of Babylon and of the kings, etc., as far as and this in the land of Juda and in his mountains, as the Lord had foretold.

VERS. 25.-- In the mountains. Note the propriety, etc., as far as but also those who have advanced in virtues.

VERS. 26.-- This counsel, that is, let his yoke be taken away, etc., as far as to pertain generally to the whole world.

1255BVERS. 28.-- In the year in which he died. From the beginning of the book up to this place Isaias prophesied under Ozias, Joatham, Achaz; up to the end, under Ezechias, the things foretold and in part fulfilled.

Achaz. While he was living, reigning among sinners: neither the burden against the aliens, nor the word of God (according to the Septuagint) could come to the Prophet.

VERS. 29.-- Rejoice not. The king being dead, his enemies rejoice, as though they were to make some gain out of the new state of things: hence the Philistines, whom Achaz afflicted, rejoice at his death and insult the Jews, because, the ripened king being lost, they are subject to his son, in whose reign they think that they can invade their borders.

The rod of the striker. (HIER.) The yoke of God being cast off, etc., as far as and out of them flying serpents, or such as should swallow up 1255Cbirds.

VERS. 31.-- Howl. (ID.) As if: O Philistines, bewail your misery, which comes through Sennacherib.

Howl, O gate; cry, O city. The Philistines falling by a draught, etc., as far as a soul filled with evil thoughts.

For from the north a smoke. The arrows of the devil blazing up, who is hurtful to the eyes and contrary to the light. From the north the pot of Jeremias is kindled.

There is none that escapeth his troop. None but Jerusalem, etc., as far as and that Jerusalem alone is to be delivered.

VERS. 32.-- To the messengers of the nation. To the angels who are set over cities and nations: each one marvelling why Sion alone has avoided, or escaped, the bitterness of the smoke.

1255DHe hath founded Sion. Upon the foundation of prudence, justice, fortitude, temperance, which are Christ.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 1.-- Onus Moab. Haec est visio Isaiae habita de onere, id est, de destructione Moab. Moab, filius Loth, cujus progenies et provincia Moab vocata est: in ipsa est Ar, quae nunc Ariopolis metropolis.

Onus Moab. (HIER.) Est circumcisio carnalis et spiritualis, etc., usque ad Loth quoque, Sodoma per eunte, venit in Segor, et ortus est sol.

1256AAr, id est adversarius, etc., usque ad post a Chaldaeis vastati sunt.

VERS. 2.-- Ascendit domus. Ad excelsa, in quibus superbierat, ut non immolet, sed ut plangat ubi peccaverat.

Dibon. (ID.) Fluxus eorum: Mendacium transit et fluit, et nunquam in eodem statu permanet; sermo autem Domini est compactus et stabilis. Unde manna videtur quasi glacies: non fluit, sed consistit.

Super Nabo. (ID.) Quia et omnis prophetiae hujus unus est sensus, etc., usque ad cum anima eorum ululaverit sibi.

VERS. 3.-- Omnis ululatus. Alia littera: Omnis ululat, Moab scilicet, et sic descendet in fletum. 1256BQuasi: Ascendit ad excelsa, ut ante idola ploret. Sed cum ibi nullum auxilium reperiret, descendit ad fletum in terram vel in domum suam.

VERS. 4.-- Clamabit Hesebon. A principio regni quod est in Hesebon, usque Jasa civitatem, ubi finitur, ubique clamor, ubique luctus.

Expediti Moab ululabunt. Ab uxoribus, fortes bellatores. Inde dicitur Abraham vernaculos domus suae elegisse expeditos, id est, sine uxoribus.

VERS. 5.-- Cor meum ad Moab. Ut scilicet provocem eos ad poenitentiam, etc., usque ad quod interpretatur leo Dei.

Ad Segor. (ID.) Segor est ex quinque urbibus Sodomorum, etc., usque ad si post liberationem non peccasset.

1256CVERS. 6.-- Nemrim. Nemrim oppidum est super mare, etc., usque ad et ideo arescet herba.

VERS. 7.-- Et visitatio eorum. Visitatio in malo: Visitabo in virga iniquitates eorum (Psal. LXXXVIII); visitatio in bono: Visitavit nos oriens ex alto (Luc. I).

Ad torrentem salicum. Hactenus de captivitate Moabitarum per Assyrios, modo de illa quae per Chaldaeos.

VERS. 8.-- Ululatus ejus. Quasi, ideo ululabit Moab, quia ubi luxus prius, et delectatio propter irrigua et fontium amoena, ibi modo rivi sanguinis.

VERS. 9.-- Aqua Dibon. Dibon, silentium, Dibon flens, et tamen una est civitas.

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- The burden of Moab. This is the vision of Isaiah had concerning the burden, that is, concerning the destruction of Moab. Moab, the son of Lot, whose offspring and province was called Moab: in it is Ar, which is now Ariopolis the metropolis.

The burden of Moab. (HIER.) There is a circumcision carnal and spiritual, etc., as far as Lot too, when Sodom was perishing, came into Segor, and the sun rose.

1256AAr, that is, adversary, etc., as far as afterward they were laid waste by the Chaldeans.

VERS. 2.-- The house is gone up. To the high places, in which it had been proud, that it might not sacrifice, but that it might bewail where it had sinned.

Dibon. (ID.) Their flowing: A lie passes and flows away, and never remains in the same state; but the word of the Lord is compact and stable. Whence manna is seen as it were ice: it does not flow, but stands firm.

Upon Nabo. (ID.) Because the sense of all this prophecy also is one, etc., as far as when their soul shall have howled to itself.

VERS. 3.-- Every howling. Another reading: Every one howls, Moab namely, and so it shall go down into weeping. 1256BAs if: It goes up to the high places, that it may weep before the idols. But when it found no help there, it went down to weeping into the land or into its own house.

VERS. 4.-- Hesebon shall cry out. From the beginning of the kingdom, which is in Hesebon, as far as Jasa the city, where it ends, everywhere clamor, everywhere mourning.

The ready men of Moab shall howl. Away from their wives, brave warriors. Hence Abraham is said to have chosen the home-born servants of his house ready, that is, without wives.

VERS. 5.-- My heart to Moab. That is, that I may provoke them to repentance, etc., as far as which is interpreted the lion of God.

Unto Segor. (ID.) Segor is one of the five cities of the Sodomites, etc., as far as if after the deliverance she had not sinned.

1256CVERS. 6.-- Nemrim. Nemrim is a town upon the sea, etc., as far as and therefore the grass shall wither.

VERS. 7.-- And their visitation. Visitation in evil: I will visit their iniquities with a rod (Psal. LXXXVIII); visitation in good: The Orient from on high hath visited us (Luc. I).

To the torrent of the willows. Thus far concerning the captivity of the Moabites by the Assyrians, now concerning that which was by the Chaldeans.

VERS. 8.-- Its howling. As if: therefore shall Moab howl, because where there was riot before, and delight by reason of the watered places and the pleasantness of the springs, there now are streams of blood.

VERS. 9.-- The water of Dibon. Dibon, silence; Dibon, weeping; and yet it is one city.

CAPUT XVI.

  • 1256D

VERS.--1. Emitte agnum. (HIER.) Idcirco prophetae vix intelliguntur, etc., usque ad qui dominetur in toto orbe.

Petra deserti. (ID.) Ruth scilicet, quia juxta praeceptum Domini, etc., usque ad sicut enim Christus pro utroque populo venit, sic de utroque nasci voluit.

Ad montem filiae Sion. Jerusalem, vel potius ad Ecclesiam, quae est in contemplatione tanquam speculationis filia. In Jerusalem quoque maxime praedicavit, miracula fecit, passus est, mortuus est, et resurrexit, et genus humanum redemit.

VERS. 2.-- Sic erunt. Sic in captivitatem transibunt 1257Aper Arnon: qui est terminus Moabitarum et Amorrhaeorum.

(HIER.) Arnon (quae interpretatur illuminatio) eorum qui, derelictis erroribus, transcendunt ad scientiam veritatis.

VERS. 3.-- Ini consilium. Nihil agas sine consilio, nec circumferaris omni vento doctrinae, sequere magni consilii angelum.

Coge consilium. De vagis scilicet et errantibus Ecclesiam faciendo.

Pone quasi noctem. Tabernaculum scilicet, in quo quiescere te putabas, etc., usque ad ut fugientem populum Dei apud se recipiat et abscondat.

VERS. 4.-- Profugi mei. Qui me fugerunt, qui de Ecclesia egressi sunt, qui sensum suum sunt secuti, 1257Bnon doctrinam Spiritus sancti.

Finitus est enim pulvis. Subito transit ad interfectionem Sennacherib, qui pulvis et miser, quia gloria ejus tanquam pulvis, quem projicit ventus a facie terrae, et miserabiliter ab hac luce migravit, occisus a filiis suis in templo Dei sui.

VERS. 5.-- Et praeparabit. Judaei exponunt hoc de Ezechia, qui, post mortem Sennacherib, in pace regnavit. Sed melius ad Christum referuntur, et quaedam ad primum, quaedam ad secundum referuntur adventum.

VERS. 6.-- Sedebit super illud. Quiescens et judicans, super corda bonorum.

Audivimus superbiam Moab. More Scripturae, quia laeta nuntiaverat, ne negligentes faceret, comminatione 1257Cterret.

Et indignatio. (ID.) Contra Deum in coelum posuit os suum prophetis detrahens. Illud evangelicum apponens: Omnes qui venerunt ad te ante me fures fuerunt et latrones. Moysen et David homicidas vocat, Josue sanguinarium.

VERS. 7.-- Ululabit. Alter ad alterum, omnes scilicet haereticorum et philosophorum diversitates contra se in tormentis mugient.

Super muros. Non aedificatos quadro lapide, sicut templum Dei sunt, in quo sic lapides politi, ut nec malleus auditus sit, nec securis.

VERS. 8.-- Suburbana Hesebon. Omnes cogitationes eorum (quod significat Hesebon) non pertinent ad civitatem Dei; de qua: Fluminis impetus laetificat 1257Dcivitatem Dei (Psal. XLV); sed suburbana sunt, ut ad urbem Domini pertinere credantur.

Sabama. Attolens altitudinem, etc., usque ad Domini ergo gentium apostoli et apostolici viri vineae Sabama exciderunt flagella.

Erraverunt. Lustrantes universam terram Moab.

In deserto. Ut tandem quos interficerent, non haberent.

Propagines. Metaphoram vineae prosequitur.

Transierunt mare. (ID.) Ut mirabilia Domini viderent in profundo tentationum liberati ex eis.

VERS. 9.-- Vineam: Vinea Moab, ex vicinia loci talis est, qualis Sodoma.

Sabama. Qui se extollit contra sententiam Dei.

1258AVERS. 10.-- De carmelo. Carmelus mons est inter Phoenicem et Palaestinam imminens Ptolomaidi, in quo Elias oravit. Et ponitur saepe pro qualibet fertilitate; unde et hic: Auferetur laetitia et exsultatio de Carmelo (III Reg. XVIII), id est, fertilitas in qua laetabantur Moabitae.

Exsultabit. Quia spiritualis circumcisionis notitiam se habere jactant haeretici.

Vocem. Ne draconis venena inde exprimantur, et bibentes interficiantur.

VERS. 11.-- Venter meus. Propheta, qui tanquam cithara, musica arte compositus, et de Dei timore concipiens, multos liberos generat, ut omnis chorda sonum suum reddat.

Et viscera. Sicut cithara sonum compositum non 1258Bemittit, etc., usque ad et Jacobus, cui una deest, desunt omnes.

VERS. 12.-- Sancta sua. Ad sanctam, scilicet Ecclesiae, quae conabitur sua facere, non praevalebit: non enim statim perfecte virtutem capimus.

Non valebit. In eis scilicet non invenient auxilium, in quibus sperabat, et sic apparuit, quia incassum colebat.

VERS. 13.-- Hoc verbum. In prooemio, etc., usque ad. Quod enim tibi coeperat, hic est completum.

VERS. 14.-- In tribus annis: quasi diceret: Sicut pauci racemi remanent, etc., usque ad sed relicto errore conversus, tribus annis tantum parvus et modicus et inglorius relinquitur.

Mercenari. (ID.) Falsa doctrina omnia lucri causa 1258Cfacit, etc., usque ad levius peccatum est aperte peccare quam sanctitatem simulare.

CHAPTER XVI.

  • 1256D

VERS.--1. Send forth the lamb. (HIER.) For this reason the prophets are scarcely understood, etc., as far as who may bear rule in the whole world.

The rock of the desert. (ID.) Ruth, namely, because according to the precept of the Lord, etc., as far as for as Christ came for either people, so he willed to be born of either.

To the mount of the daughter of Sion. Jerusalem, or rather the Church, which is in contemplation as it were the daughter of watching. In Jerusalem also he chiefly preached, wrought miracles, suffered, died, and rose again, and redeemed the human race.

VERS. 2.-- So shall they be. So shall they pass over into captivity 1257Aby Arnon: which is the boundary of the Moabites and of the Amorrhites.

(HIER.) Arnon (which is interpreted illumination) of those who, their errors forsaken, pass over to the knowledge of the truth.

VERS. 3.-- Take counsel. Do nothing without counsel, and be not carried about with every wind of doctrine; follow the angel of great counsel.

Gather counsel. [Vulg. Is 16:3 coge concilium, "call the assembly together"] Out of the straying, namely, and the erring, by making a Church.

Set as night. Thy tabernacle, namely, in which thou didst think to rest, etc., as far as that it may receive and hide with itself the fleeing people of God.

VERS. 4.-- My fugitives. Who have fled from me, who have gone out from the Church, who have followed their own sense, 1257Bnot the doctrine of the Holy Spirit.

For the dust is at an end. He passes suddenly to the slaying of Sennacherib, who is dust and wretched, because his glory is as dust which the wind casts forth from the face of the earth, and he passed wretchedly from this light, slain by his own sons in the temple of his god.

VERS. 5.-- And he shall prepare. [Vulg. Is 16:5 Et praeparabitur, passive, "and a throne shall be prepared"] The Jews expound this of Ezechias, who, after the death of Sennacherib, reigned in peace. But they are better referred to Christ, and some things are referred to the first, some to the second advent.

VERS. 6.-- He shall sit upon it. Resting and judging, upon the hearts of the good.

We have heard the pride of Moab. After the manner of Scripture, because it had announced glad things, lest it should make them negligent, it terrifies 1257Cwith a threatening.

And the indignation. (ID.) Against God he set his mouth in heaven, disparaging the prophets. Adding that word of the Gospel: All who came to thee before me were thieves and robbers. [Vulg. Jo. 10:8 Omnes quotquot venerunt, fures sunt, et latrones, "All, as many as came, are thieves and robbers"] He calls Moses and David murderers, Josue a man of blood.

VERS. 7.-- He shall howl. One to another, that is, all the diversities of heretics and of philosophers shall bellow against one another in torments.

Upon the walls. Not built of squared stone, as they are the temple of God, in which the stones are so polished that neither hammer was heard, nor axe.

VERS. 8.-- The suburbs of Hesebon. All their thoughts (which is what Hesebon signifies) do not belong to the city of God; of which: The stream of the river maketh joyful the 1257Dcity of God (Psal. XLV); but they are suburbs, that they may be believed to belong to the city of the Lord.

Sabama. Lifting up height, etc., as far as the lords of the nations, therefore, the apostles and apostolic men, cut off the shoots of the vine of Sabama.

They wandered. Ranging over the whole land of Moab.

In the desert. That at last they might not have those whom they should slay.

The branches. He pursues the metaphor of the vine.

They passed over the sea. (ID.) That they might see the wonders of the Lord in the deep of temptations, delivered out of them.

VERS. 9.-- The vine: The vine of Moab, from the neighbourhood of the place, is such as Sodom.

Sabama. He who lifts himself up against the sentence of God.

1258AVERS. 10.-- From Carmel. Carmel is a mountain between Phoenicia and Palestine overhanging Ptolemais, in which Elias prayed. And it is often put for any fertility whatever; whence here also: Gladness and rejoicing shall be taken away from Carmel (III Reg. XVIII), that is, the fertility in which the Moabites rejoiced.

He shall rejoice. Because heretics boast that they have the knowledge of the spiritual circumcision.

The voice. Lest the poisons of the dragon be pressed out thence, and the drinkers be slain.

VERS. 11.-- My belly. The prophet, who as a harp, composed by the musician's art, and conceiving from the fear of God, begets many children, that every string may render its own sound.

And the bowels. As a harp does not 1258Bsend forth a composed sound, etc., as far as and James, to whom one is lacking, to him all are lacking.

VERS. 12.-- Her holy things. To the holy thing, namely, of the Church, which she shall endeavour to make her own, she shall not prevail: for we do not at once perfectly take hold of virtue.

She shall not prevail. In them, namely, they shall not find the help in which she hoped, and so it appeared, because she worshipped in vain.

VERS. 13.-- This word. In the preface, etc., as far as. For what had begun for thee, here is completed.

VERS. 14.-- In three years: as if he should say: As few clusters remain, etc., as far as but, his error forsaken, converted, for three years only he is left small and slight and inglorious.

Of a hireling. (ID.) False doctrine does all things for the sake of gain, 1258Cetc., as far as it is a lighter sin to sin openly than to feign sanctity.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1.-- Onus Damasci. Ecce Damascus. (ID.) Damascus metropolis Syriae, etc., usque ad sed quia non egerunt poenitentiam captivati sunt.

VERS. 2.-- Aroer gregibus. Myrica, quae est arbor infructuosa, nascens in desertis, et saxosis locis, quasi diceret: Omnes Syri infructuosi erunt et desolati. Alii dicunt ex hac arbore maleficis artibus odia concitari.

VERS. 3.-- Et regnum. Ut peccatum non regnet in ea, quae prius sanguinem diligebat.

Reliquiae Syriae. Ironice, pro ignominia, etc., 1258Dusque ad ambo ergo captivati ignominiose.

VERS. 4.-- Et erit in die illa. (ID.) Cum Syria vastata fuerit ab Assyriis, etc., usque ad alii in Cyrenem translati, quae est regio ultra Aegyptum.

Emarcescet. (ID.) Non habebunt prophetas, nec signa, nec virtutes, nec regnum, nec sacerdotium; sed omne corpus gentis marcescet.

VERS. 5.-- In messe. De gentibus dicitur: Messis quidem multa operarii, autem pauci (Matth. IX). Illi vero pauperes, messis reliquias quae per apostolos salvatae sunt, et rarissimas spicas legunt. Nec in montibus, sed in valle Raphaim, id est, in humilitate, litterae veritate. Raphaim enim gigantes sonat.

VERS. 6.-- Sicut excussio. (ID.) Percusso populo 1259AJudaeorum, oliva quae sub Moyse sexcenta millia armatorum habuerat, et sub David numerante Joab innumerum populum, vix paucos fructus Salvatori obtulit.

Duarum aut trium. Pauli et Barnabae. Et trium Petri, et Jacobi et Joannis, qui in monte transfiguratum Dominum conspexerunt, et archisynagogi filiam suscitari viderunt.

Sive quatuor. (HIER.) Hi sunt reliqui novem, in quibus Judae proditoris Matthias locum obtinuit. Hi pro diversitate gratiarum nobis ignoti, in quatuor aut in quinque separati sunt, ut Evangeliorum numerum et legis in se volumina demonstrarent, tanquam praedicatores utriusque Testamenti.

VERS. 7.-- In die illa inclinabitur. Quasi diceret: 1259BDominus esuriens, vix paucas, etc., usque ad sicut legitur in Paralipomen., et idola abjecerunt.

VERS. 9.-- In die illa erunt. Irruentibus Assyriis, et vastantibus non solum Damascum, sed et terram decem tribuum.

Aratra. (ID.) Amorrhaeorum. Hoc contra decem tribus, quasi diceret: Sicut olim venientibus filiis Israel, relictis aratris fugerunt Amorrhaei, sic modo decem tribus venientibus Assyriis.

VERS. 10.-- Plantationem fidelem. Plantatio fidelis est, quando quis plantat et colit, et fructum colligit. Plantatio infidelis et germen alienum, quando alienus colligit: Omnis plantatio quam non plantavit Pater meus eradicabitur (Matth. XV).

Germen alienum. Non recipiens Patrem, quia non 1259Crecipis Filium; qui enim credit in Patrem, credit in Filium. Germen alienum, blasphemiae contra Christum.

VERS. 11.-- Mane semen tuum. (ID.) Cum scilicet visum fuerit germen, etc., usque ad omnes sancti cum ramis oleastri radicem salvari.

VERS. 12.-- Vae multitudini. Sennacherib et exercitui ejus, etc., usque ad cuncta opprimere et occupare cupienti.

VERS. 13.-- Rapietur. Raptus est a Jerusalem, quando cum paucis fugit in Ninivem. De mundo raptus est, quando a filiis suis cito occisus est, ut patet.

VERS. 14.-- Haec est pars eorum qui vastaverunt. Propheta ex persona Israel, quasi diceret: Sic solet 1259DDeus beare inimicos nostros.

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1.-- The burden of Damascus. Behold Damascus. (ID.) Damascus, the metropolis of Syria, etc., as far as but because they did not do penance they were led captive.

VERS. 2.-- Aroer for the flocks. The tamarisk, which is a fruitless tree, springing up in deserts and in rocky places, as if he should say: All the Syrians shall be fruitless and desolate. Others say that from this tree hatreds are stirred up by evil arts.

VERS. 3.-- And the kingdom. That sin may not reign in her who before loved blood.

The remnant of Syria. Ironically, for a disgrace, etc., 1258Das far as both therefore were led captive in disgrace.

VERS. 4.-- And it shall be in that day. (ID.) When Syria shall have been laid waste by the Assyrians, etc., as far as others were carried over into Cyrene, which is a region beyond Egypt.

It shall wither away. (ID.) They shall not have prophets, nor signs, nor powers, nor kingdom, nor priesthood; but the whole body of the nation shall wither.

VERS. 5.-- In the harvest. Of the nations it is said: The harvest indeed is great, the labourers, however, few (Matth. IX). But those poor men gather the leavings of the harvest, which have been saved by the apostles, and the very rare ears. Nor on the mountains, but in the valley of Raphaim, that is, in humility, in the truth of the letter. For Raphaim sounds giants.

VERS. 6.-- As the shaking. (ID.) When the people 1259Aof the Jews had been struck down, the olive tree, which under Moses had had six hundred thousand armed men, and under David, when Joab numbered them, a people beyond counting, offered scarcely a few fruits to the Saviour.

Of two or three. Of Paul and Barnabas. And of three, Peter, and James and John, who beheld the Lord transfigured on the mountain, and saw the daughter of the ruler of the synagogue raised up.

Or four. (HIER.) These are the remaining nine, among whom Matthias obtained the place of Judas the traitor. These, unknown to us by reason of the diversity of graces, were parted into four or into five, that they might show forth in themselves the number of the Gospels and the volumes of the law, as preachers of either Testament.

VERS. 7.-- In that day he shall bow down. As if he should say: 1259BThe Lord hungering, scarcely a few, etc., as far as as is read in Paralipomen., and they cast away the idols.

VERS. 9.-- In that day they shall be. When the Assyrians rushed in, and laid waste not only Damascus, but also the land of the ten tribes.

Ploughs. (ID.) Of the Amorrhites. This against the ten tribes, as if he should say: As of old, when the children of Israel came, the Amorrhites fled and left their ploughs, so now the ten tribes, when the Assyrians come.

VERS. 10.-- A faithful planting. A planting is faithful when one plants and tills, and gathers the fruit. The planting is unfaithful, and the seed a stranger's, when a stranger gathers: Every planting which my Father hath not planted shall be rooted up (Matth. XV). [Vulg. Mt 15:13 Pater meus caelestis, "my heavenly Father"]

A stranger's seed. Not receiving the Father, because thou dost not receive 1259Cthe Son; for he who believes in the Father, believes in the Son. A stranger's seed, of blasphemy against Christ.

VERS. 11.-- In the morning thy seed. (ID.) When, namely, the shoot shall have been seen, etc., as far as that all the saints are saved with the branches of the wild olive, the root.

VERS. 12.-- Woe to the multitude. Sennacherib and his army, etc., as far as desiring to crush and to seize upon all things.

VERS. 13.-- He shall be caught away. He was caught away from Jerusalem, when he fled with a few into Ninive. From the world he was caught away, when he was quickly slain by his own sons, as is plain.

VERS. 14.-- This is the portion of them that have laid waste. The prophet in the person of Israel, as if he should say: Thus is 1259DGod wont to bless our enemies.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Vae terrae. (ID.) Quia de caeteris persecutoribus Ecclesiae dixerat, etc., usque ad quasi errando inutilia fecerit.

Vae terrae cymbalae alarum. Convertitur iterum ad Aegyptum, et vocat eum, etc., usque ad timorem Domini abstulerunt eis.

VERS. 2.-- In vasis papyri, id est in navibus de papyro factis, etc., usque ad pertransit et labitur.

VERS. 3.-- Gentem exspectantem et conculcatam. Omnes enim haeretici sibi promittunt ingentia, etc., usque ad id est doctrinam eorum audiunt.
