1260AVERS. 4.-- Nubes roris. Grata messoribus fatigatis, quibus refrigerium praestat, et stipulam arentem facit humectando secabilem: sic sermo meus contra Aegyptum, gratus omnibus erit in me credentibus, et Assyriis a me eis data victoria.

VERS. 5.-- Ramusculi. (ID.) Inutiles: unde: Ego sum vitis vera, et Pater meus agricola est: omnem palmitem in me non ferentem fructum tollet eum, et omnem qui fert fructum purgabit eum.

VERS. 6.-- Et omnes bestiae terrae. Hyperbolicos. Neque enim omnes bestiae terrae super eum hyemabunt. Sed significat, et occisionem immensam, et bestiarum multitudinem maximam superventuram, quibus aestate et hieme cadavera sufficiant in escam.

1260BVERS. 7.-- In tempore. Revertitur Propheta ad populum Israel, et promittit ei bona, dicens: In tempore, quasi dicat: Vastata Aegypto, non sperabit ultra Juda in umbra ejus; sed fidei et bonorum operum Domino offerret munus. Quod impletum est septuagesimo anno post captivitatem sub Zorobabel et aliis ducibus.

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Woe to the land. (ID.) Because he had spoken of the rest of the persecutors of the Church, etc., as far as as though by straying he had done useless things.

Woe to the land of the cymbal of wings. [Vulg. Is 18:1 cymbalo alarum, ablative] He turns again to Egypt, and calls it, etc., as far as they took away from them the fear of the Lord.

VERS. 2.-- In vessels of papyrus, that is, in ships made of papyrus, etc., as far as it passes by and slips away.

VERS. 3.-- A nation waiting and trodden underfoot. For all heretics promise themselves great things, etc., as far as that is, they hear their doctrine.

1260AVERS. 4.-- A cloud of dew. Welcome to the wearied reapers, to whom it affords refreshment, and by moistening makes the dry stubble fit for cutting: so my word against Egypt shall be welcome to all that believe in me, and to the Assyrians the victory given them by me.

VERS. 5.-- Little branches. (ID.) Useless: whence: I am the true vine, and my Father is the husbandman: every branch in me not bearing fruit he will take it away, and every one that beareth fruit he will purge it.

VERS. 6.-- And all the beasts of the earth. Hyperbolically. For not all the beasts of the earth shall winter upon him. But he signifies both a slaughter beyond measure, and a very great multitude of beasts about to come upon them, for whom the corpses shall suffice for food in summer and in winter.

1260BVERS. 7.-- In the time. The Prophet returns to the people of Israel, and promises them good things, saying: In the time, as if he should say: When Egypt has been laid waste, Juda shall hope no more in its shadow; but he would offer to the Lord the gift of faith and of good works. Which was fulfilled in the seventieth year after the captivity, under Zorobabel and the other leaders.

CAPUT XIX.

VERS. 1.-- Onus Aegypti, etc. (ID.) Sciendum multa quae hic dicuntur ad Aegyptum historialiter pertinere; sed, et hic et in aliis locis, Scripturarum multa ponuntur quae non possunt juxta historiam stare, ut rerum necessitate cogamur altiorem intelligentiam quaerere.

Ecce Dominus ascendet, etc. (ID.) Aegyptus, tenebrae 1260Cvel moeror, etc., usque ad et corda sapientium Aegypti pavore tabuerunt.

Simulacra Aegypti. Idololatria fractam se sentiens, ut etiam magi a daemonibus docti, vel juxta prophetiam Balaam intelligentes natum Filium Dei, qui magicam destrueret artem, in Bethleem venerunt et Puerum adoraverunt.

VERS. 2.-- Et concurrere faciam. (ID.) Unde in Evangelio: Non veni pacem mittere in terram, sed gladium: veni enim separare hominem a patre suo, etc., (Matth. X).

VERS. 3.-- Et dirumpetur, etc. (ID.) Dividetur in contraria voluntas Aegypti, ut non magna sentiant sicut ante, sed spirituali gladio separati, consilium suum cognoscant praecipitatum esse.

1260DVERS. 4.-- Dominorum crudelium. Multis dominis Aegyptus tradita est. Primum Assyriis, post Nabuchodonosor, tertio Alexandro, ad ultimum Romanis, Antonio et Cleopatra ab Augusto superatis. Chaldaei crudeliores, Romani potentiores.

VERS. 5.-- Et arescet. Fieri solet, ut cum ira Dei captivitas venit, ut indignationem ejus pestilentia subsequatur, et contra peccatores elementa desaeviant. Veniente ergo captivitate per Chaldaeos vel Romanos, forsitan et Nilus, et fontes siccati sunt. Hyperbolicos quoque propter nimiam tribulationem flumina possunt dici defecisse, et fontes aruisse et rivuli.

VERS. 11.-- Stulti. Adveniente captivitate Babylonica 1261Avel Romana, omnia magorum defecerunt consilia.

Principes Thaneos. (HIER.) Humilis mandati; omnes enim haeretici contrariam altitudini humilitatem docent, et ad inferos detrahunt, et humilis mandati et dejecti sunt principes.

Filius sapientium. Haec dicebat unusquisque Pharaoni, etc., usque ad et deos magnos auctores suae gentis fuisse jactant Isim, et Osirim, et Typhonem.

Regum antiquorum. Quasi dicat: Habeo scientiam Scripturarum cum sapientia saeculari.

VERS. 14.-- Ebrius et vomens. (ID.) Furore draconum, ut tandem post vomitum intelligant ebrietatem suam. Quandiu enim ebrii fuerint, nec principium habebunt, nec finem.

1261BVERS. 15.-- Refrenantem. (HAYMO. in Isa.) Pro eo quod Hieronymus debuit transferre, etc., usque ad alii prae lascivia quid faciant, nesciunt.

VERS. 16.-- Mulieres. Quas amat Aegyptus, quas solas vult Pharao vivere, necatis viris. Diabolus virtutes interficit, vitia conservat.

VERS. 17.-- Terra Juda Aegypto. Scientia Scripturarum lex, et prophetae; Evangelia erunt Aegypto in festivitatem cum se cognoverit, vel in timorem cum se mendacia habuisse intellexerit.

In pavorem. Alii in festivitatem, etc., usque ad promittendo auxilium eis.

VERS. 18.-- Quinque civitates. Quidam quinque libros Moysi intelligunt, etc., usque ad quia de vitiis ad virtutes commutantur.

1261CLingua Chananaea. Quaeritur cur non lingua Hebraea, etc., usque ad et qui tactu immundo delectantur.

VERS. 19.-- In die illa. (ID.) Hoc non intelligens Onias sacerdos, etc., usque ad et eos usque hodie linguam propriam tenuisse.

Et titulus. Titulus Domini evangelica doctrina et apostolica, quae est usque ad terminos terrae, unde: In omnem terram exivit sonus eorum (Psal. XVIII). Vel titulus juxta terminum fidei, operatio bona, quae quantum et fides extendi debet, vel imitatio passionis Christi.

VERS. 20.-- Clamabunt enim. Cum evangelica doctrina creverit, et persecutio ingruerit, tunc qui crediderint, clamabunt ad Deum patrem.

1261DVERS. 23.-- In die illa. Ante adventum Domini, singulae nationes, etc., usque ad qui de regno in regnum discurrunt.

Et servient Aegyptii. Non quod postea illi fuissent subjugati, etc., usque ad ejusdem cum ipsis conditionis.

In die illa erit. (ID.) Non vicissim ab Assyriis Aegyptiisque medius vexabitur, sed erit tertius cum ipsis serviens, scilicet Romanis.

VERS. 25.-- Benedictus. Hae regiones prae omnibus aliis monarchis abundaverant, et ideo benedici meruerunt.

CHAPTER XIX.

VERS. 1.-- The burden of Egypt, etc. (ID.) It is to be known that many things which are here said belong to Egypt historically; but, both here and in other places, many things of the Scriptures are set down which cannot stand according to history, so that by the necessity of the matter we are compelled to seek a higher understanding.

Behold the Lord shall ascend, etc. (ID.) Egypt, darkness 1260Cor sorrow, etc., as far as and the hearts of the wise men of Egypt melted with dread.

The idols of Egypt. Idolatry, feeling itself broken, so that even the magi, taught by the demons, or, according to the prophecy of Balaam, understanding that the Son of God was born, who should destroy the magic art, came into Bethlehem and adored the Child.

VERS. 2.-- And I will make to run together. (ID.) Whence in the Gospel: I came not to send peace upon the earth, but the sword: for I came to separate a man from his father, etc., (Matth. X). [Vulg. Mt 10:35 adversus patrem suum, "against his father"]

VERS. 3.-- And it shall be broken asunder, etc. (ID.) The will of Egypt shall be divided into contrary things, so that they shall not be great in their own sense as before, but, sundered by the spiritual sword, shall know that their counsel has been cast headlong.

1260DVERS. 4.-- Of cruel lords. Egypt was delivered up to many lords. First to the Assyrians, afterward to Nabuchodonosor, thirdly to Alexander, at the last to the Romans, when Antony and Cleopatra were overcome by Augustus. The Chaldeans were the crueller, the Romans the mightier.

VERS. 5.-- And it shall be dried up. It is wont to happen, when captivity comes by the wrath of God, that pestilence follows upon his indignation, and that the elements rage against sinners. When therefore the captivity came by the Chaldeans or the Romans, perhaps both the Nile and the springs were dried up. Hyperbolically also, by reason of the exceeding tribulation, the rivers can be said to have failed, and the springs and the brooks to have gone dry.

VERS. 11.-- Foolish. When the Babylonian 1261Aor the Roman captivity came, all the counsels of the magi failed.

The princes of Thaneos. (HIER.) Of a lowly commandment; for all heretics teach a lowliness contrary to loftiness, and drag men down to the lower regions, and the princes are of a lowly commandment and cast down.

The son of the wise. Each one said these things to Pharao, etc., as far as and they boast that the great gods, Isis and Osiris and Typhon, were the founders of their nation.

Of ancient kings. As if he should say: I have the knowledge of the Scriptures together with worldly wisdom.

VERS. 14.-- Drunken and vomiting. (ID.) With the madness of dragons, that at last after the vomiting they may understand their drunkenness. For as long as they shall have been drunken, they shall have neither beginning nor end.

1261BVERS. 15.-- That refraineth. (HAYMO. in Isa.) In place of what Jerome ought to have rendered, etc., as far as others, out of wantonness, know not what they do.

VERS. 16.-- Women. Whom Egypt loves, whom alone Pharao wills to live, the men being slain. The devil kills the virtues, and preserves the vices.

VERS. 17.-- The land of Juda to Egypt. The knowledge of the Scriptures, the law and the prophets; the Gospels shall be to Egypt for a festival when she shall have known herself, or for a terror when she shall have understood that she held lies.

For a dread. Others, for a festival, etc., as far as by promising them help.

VERS. 18.-- Five cities. Some understand the five books of Moses, etc., as far as because they are changed from vices to virtues.

1261CThe tongue of Chanaan. [Vulg. Is 19:18 lingua Chanaan, the bare place-name; Migne prints an adjectival form] It is asked why not the Hebrew tongue, etc., as far as and those who delight in an unclean touch.

VERS. 19.-- In that day. (ID.) Onias the priest, not understanding this, etc., as far as and that they have kept their own tongue until this day.

And a title. The title of the Lord is the evangelical and apostolic doctrine, which is unto the ends of the earth, whence: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth (Psal. XVIII). Or the title beside the boundary of faith is good work, which ought to be extended as far as faith also, or the imitation of the passion of Christ.

VERS. 20.-- For they shall cry out. When the evangelical doctrine shall have grown, and persecution shall have pressed in, then they that have believed shall cry out to God the Father.

1261DVERS. 23.-- In that day. Before the coming of the Lord, the several nations, etc., as far as who run to and fro from kingdom to kingdom.

And the Egyptians shall serve. Not that they were afterward subjugated, etc., as far as of the same condition with them.

In that day it shall be. (ID.) He shall not be vexed in the midst by the Assyrians and the Egyptians in turn, but he shall be a third with them, serving, namely, the Romans.

VERS. 25.-- Blessed. These regions had abounded above all other monarchies, and therefore they deserved to be blessed.

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 1.-- In anno. (ID.) Assyria nomina sunt 1262Ahaec, etc., usque ad expugnat eos qui serviunt generationi et libidini.

In anno. Azotus, urbs Palaestinorum potentissima de quinque, etc., usque ad quae per eum loquebatur Dominus.

Cum misisset. Pulchre rex Assyriorum Sargon, id est princeps hortorum, dicitur, cui similis est Achab: Qui vineam Naboth in hortum vertere cupiebat; quod ille tropologice intelligens, maluit mori quam facere, ne paterna haereditas et possessio antiqua in regis impii mutaretur delicias.

VERS. 2.-- Vade et solve. Praecipitur prophetae ut sacco calceamentisque depositis, etc., usque ad nihil enim occultum quod non reveletur.

Et fecit sic. Mira obedientia, solo cilicio induebatur 1262Bvir nobilissimus, ut Hebraei tradunt. Cujus filiam Rex Manasses accepit in conjugium, non erubuit nudus incedere, nihil honestius judicans quam Domino obedire.

VERS. 3.-- Discalceatus. (ID.) Notandum quod antequam capiatur Azotus, etc., usque ad ad quam festinat, vel stare, vel ambulare.

VERS. 4.-- Ad ignominiam. Quia pascha Domini comedere non poterant, quod comedit lumbis accinctus, baculum tenens manu, calceatis pedibus, ne, per hujus saeculi solitudinem transiens, a serpentibus mordeatur.

VERS. 6.-- Haeccine erat. Admiranda Dei providentia, etc., usque ad in fine ex toto peribit, cum regnum Christi illuxerit.

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 1.-- In the year. (ID.) These are Assyrian 1262Anames, etc., as far as he overcomes those who are in bondage to generation and to lust.

In the year. Azotus, the mightiest city of the Philistines out of the five, etc., as far as which the Lord spoke through him.

When he had sent. Beautifully is the king of the Assyrians called Sargon, that is, prince of gardens, to whom Achab is like: who desired to turn the vineyard of Naboth into a garden; which he, understanding it tropologically, chose rather to die than to do, lest the paternal inheritance and the ancient possession should be turned into the delights of an impious king.

VERS. 2.-- Go and loose. The prophet is commanded that, his sackcloth and his shoes laid aside, etc., as far as for there is nothing hidden that shall not be revealed.

And he did so. A wondrous obedience: a man most noble was clothed 1262Bin sackcloth alone, as the Hebrews hand down. Whose daughter King Manasses took in marriage; he did not blush to walk naked, judging nothing more honourable than to obey the Lord.

VERS. 3.-- Unshod. (ID.) It is to be noted that before Azotus is taken, etc., as far as to which it hastens, either to stand or to walk.

VERS. 4.-- To the disgrace. Because they could not eat the pasch of the Lord, which he ate with his loins girded, holding a staff in his hand, with shoes on his feet, lest, passing through the wilderness of this world, he be bitten by serpents.

VERS. 6.-- Was this then. [Vulg. Is 20:6 Ecce haec erat spes nostra, "Behold, this was our hope"] The providence of God is to be marvelled at, etc., as far as in the end it shall perish utterly, when the kingdom of Christ shall have shone forth.

CAPUT XXI.

  • 1262C

VERS. 1.-- Onus deserti maris. Babylonis, pro multitudine populi dicta mare.

De deserto venit. In quo Dominus tentatus, in quo Israelitae percussi a serpentibus.

VERS. 2.-- Qui incredulus. Generalis sententia, quasi culpa singulorum est quod in tempestate opprimuntur.

Ascende Aelam. LXX: Aelamitae et legati Persarum. Aelamitae, despicientes. Ascende, quasi diceret: O Persae et Medi, nolite timere, equos ascendite, Babylonem obsidete. Ascende. Dei fortitudine confisus, audacter, contra adversarios loquitur.

Omnem gemitum. Quo te et alias gentes gemere 1262Dfaciebat; vel, ut nullus jam gemat pressus potentia Babylonis; vel, tantis malis sit afflicta, ut contra te gemere non audeat.

VERS. 5.-- Pone mensam. Quasi diceret: O Persae et Medi, sumite cibum, etc., usque ad in quo ignea tela diaboli restringatis.

Contemplare, etc. Quid post mensam ingruat ei, vel, contemplare, o Balthasar, quid scripserit in pariete: Mane, Tethel, Phares.

VERS. 6.-- Speculatorem. Speculantes dicuntur prophetae.

VERS. 7.-- Ascensorem. Cyrus princeps imperabat Medis et Persis, etc., usque ad litterae scilicet et spiritus ad duo referunt Testamenta.

1263AVERS. 8.-- Leo. Idem leo pro virtute animi, qui speculator praerogativa contemplationis.

Super speculam Domini. Id est, per diem in contemplatione positus, diebus ac noctibus paratus audire et loqui quae praeceperit.

Stans totis noctibus. Ut me custodiam ab omni peccato, ut sim dignus audire verbum Domini.

VERS. 9.-- Ecce iste venit vir. Quod sequitur, se vidisse significat, venire, scilicet Salvatorem super bigam sedentem, et duobus animalibus, id est, asino et camelo, unum currum jungentem.

Cecidit, cecidit. Cyro et Dario Babylonem vastantibus, etiam idola sunt confracta, templo Beli subverso et igne concremato.

VERS. 10.-- Tritura mea. Quasi dicat: O popule, 1263Bqui recondendus es in horrea mea, quem trivi variis angustiis, ut excuterem paleas et triticum purum, conferrem in horrea, quae audivi a Domino ex Deo Israel, etc.

VERS. 11.-- Onus Duma. LXX: Idumaeae, etc., usque ad onus Duma ad me clamat.

Ad me clamat. (HIER.) Deus, scilicet custos meus, etc., usque ad loquitur deinde gentibus: Si me quaeritis, studiosius quaerite (Joan. IX).

VERS. 12.-- Venit mane, etc. Quasi populo meo dabo lumen laetitiae, etc., usque ad operatione, et sic audiam vos.

VERS. 13.-- Onus in Arabia. Arabes sunt Saraceni, etc., usque ad inde alloquitur Deus Judaeos fugientes sic:

1263CIn saltu ad vesperam. O Judaei, qui obsidionem Babyloniam fugietis in saltu, id est in silvis Saracenorum dormietis, fugientes Chaldaeos in semitis Dodanim, quod interpretatur in propinquos vel cognatos, id est, in via quae ducit propinquos vestros Saracenos.

Vesperam. (ID.) Quae est principium noctis. Qui peccare incipit, in vespera est; qui ad summum venit, in media nocte. Media nocte primogenita Aegypti occiduntur. Petrus negat, luce jam vicina poenitet.

In semitis. (ID.) Quia vespera principium malorum, etc., usque ad quando contra patrem rebellavit.

VERS. 14.-- Occurentes sitienti ferte. Hoc contra eos potest dici, qui otio et desidiae se tradiderunt et propria salute contenti sunt, nec poenitentibus et conversis manum porrigunt.

1263DSitienti ferte aquam. Fatigatis fratribus ardore solis et sitis ferte aquam, ne in solitudine pereant.

VERS. 16.-- Adhuc in uno, etc. Quasi diceret: Quia non habuistis in eos misericordiam, prope sustinebitis captivitatis miseriam. Post annum a Judaica captivitate et Jerusalem subversione, hi quoque vastati sunt.

Quasi in anno mercenarii: (ID.) quod est: Quomodo mercenarius annum suum, etc., usque ad panemque coelestem comederint.

CHAPTER XXI.

  • 1262C

VERS. 1.-- The burden of the desert of the sea. Of Babylon, called a sea for the multitude of its people.

It cometh from the desert. In which the Lord was tempted, in which the Israelites were struck by serpents.

VERS. 2.-- He that is unbelieving. A general sentence, as though it were the fault of individuals that they are overwhelmed in the storm.

Go up, Aelam. The LXX: The Aelamites and the legates of the Persians. Aelamites, despisers. Go up, as if he should say: O Persians and Medes, fear not, mount your horses, besiege Babylon. Go up. Trusting in the strength of God, he speaks boldly against his adversaries.

All the mourning. Whereby it made thee and other nations to mourn; 1262Dor, that none may now mourn, pressed by the power of Babylon; or, that it may be afflicted with so great evils that it dare not mourn against thee.

VERS. 5.-- Prepare the table. As if he should say: O Persians and Medes, take food, etc., as far as wherein ye may quench the fiery darts of the devil.

Behold, etc. What may fall upon him after the table; or, behold, O Balthasar, what he has written on the wall: Mane, Tethel, Phares. [Vulg. Dn 5:25 Mane, Thecel, Phares]

VERS. 6.-- A watchman. Those who watch are called prophets.

VERS. 7.-- A rider. Cyrus the prince was ruling over the Medes and the Persians, etc., as far as the letter, namely, and the spirit, refer to the two Testaments.

1263AVERS. 8.-- A lion. The same is a lion for the strength of his mind, who is a watchman by the prerogative of contemplation.

Upon the watchtower of the Lord. That is, set by day in contemplation, ready by days and by nights to hear and to speak the things which he shall have commanded.

Standing all the nights. That I may keep myself from all sin, that I may be worthy to hear the word of the Lord.

VERS. 9.-- Behold this man cometh. What follows, he signifies that he has seen, namely that the Saviour comes, sitting upon a two-horse chariot, and joining two animals, that is, an ass and a camel, to one chariot.

It is fallen, it is fallen. When Cyrus and Darius were laying Babylon waste, the idols also were broken in pieces, the temple of Bel being overthrown and burned with fire.

VERS. 10.-- My threshing. As if he should say: O people, 1263Bwho art to be stored up in my barns, whom I have threshed with divers straits, that I might beat out the chaff and gather the pure wheat into the barns, the things which I have heard from the Lord, from the God of Israel, etc.

VERS. 11.-- The burden of Duma. The LXX: Of Idumaea, etc., as far as the burden of Duma crieth to me.

He crieth to me. (HIER.) God, namely, my keeper, etc., as far as he speaks thereafter to the nations: If ye seek me, seek more earnestly (Joan. IX). [Vulg. Is 21:12 si quaeritis, quaerite, "if you seek, seek"]

VERS. 12.-- The morning cometh, etc. As if: to my people I will give the light of gladness, etc., as far as by working, and so I will hear you.

VERS. 13.-- The burden in Arabia. The Arabs are the Saracens, etc., as far as thence God addresses the fleeing Jews thus:

1263CIn the thicket at evening. O ye Jews, who shall flee the Babylonian siege in the thicket, that is, ye shall sleep in the woods of the Saracens, fleeing the Chaldeans in the paths of Dodanim, which is interpreted among the near of kin or the kindred, that is, in the way which leads to your kinsmen the Saracens. [Vulg. Is 21:13 in semitis Dedanim]

Evening. (ID.) Which is the beginning of night. He who begins to sin is in the evening; he who has come to the height is in the middle of the night. In the middle of the night the firstborn of Egypt are slain. Peter denies; when the light is now near, he repents.

In the paths. (ID.) Because evening is the beginning of evils, etc., as far as when he rebelled against his father.

VERS. 14.-- Meeting him that is thirsty, bring. This can be said against those who have given themselves over to idleness and sloth and are content with their own salvation, and reach out no hand to the penitent and the converted.

1263DBring water to him that is thirsty. To your brethren, wearied with the burning of the sun and with thirst, bring water, lest they perish in the wilderness.

VERS. 16.-- Yet in one, etc. As if he should say: Because ye have not had mercy upon them, ye shall soon endure the misery of captivity. A year after the captivity of the Jews and the overthrow of Jerusalem, these also were laid waste.

As in the year of a hireling: (ID.) which is: As a hireling his year, etc., as far as and shall have eaten the heavenly bread.

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 1.-- Onus vallis. Jerusalem quondam domina gentium, etc., usque ad qui de sublimi sensu Scripturarum ad infima corruerunt.

1264AVallis visionis. LXX: Sion, id est speculae, scilicet Ecclesiae.

Quidnam tibi quoque. Quasi diceret: Cum philosophia et sapientia saecularis, de sublimibus disputans, simplicitatem Ecclesiae contemnat, cur, tu quoque sectaris excelsa?

VERS. 2.-- Plena. Doctrina haereticorum plena est multiloquio et clamore.

Interfecti tui. (HIER.) Vallis, scilicet Sion, voluntate propria, ad haereticos scilicet transierunt. Comparatione eorum qui post praelium victi sunt, infelicius vulnerantur qui sponte se tradunt: quomodo in martyrio, qui post poenas negat, levius punitur quam qui sine necessitate Christum abnegat.

VERS. 3.-- Cuncti principes, etc. Sicut in Jeremia 1264Blegitur, Sedechias nocte per muri foramen, etc., usque ad et in Babylonem ducti sunt.

Fugerunt. In fuga concordes, quorum princeps est draco fugiens, coluber tortuosus.

VERS. 5.-- Et fletuum. (ID.) LXX: Erroris. Non quod fletus et error a Domino sit, sed occasione Scripturarum quas dedit eis legendas, nascitur erroris occasio, et possunt dicere: Quare nos errare fecisti a via tua? (Isa. LXIII.)

VERS. 6.-- Elam. Civitas in Perside provincia, in qua optimi sagittarii: Elam sumpsit pharetram. Elam, ascensus eorum, id est superbia haereticorum, sumpsit pharetram, ut sagittet in obscuro rectos corde.

Parietem. Ecclesiae firmamenta, clypeo suo, id est impugnatione nudavit.

1264CVERS. 8.-- Operimentum. Templum quod gentibus et pollutis ante opertum et clausum erat.

Armamentarium. Juxta templum erat domus quam aedificavit David ad reponenda arma, in cujus circuitu arbores multae; unde dicitur domus saltus. Ipsa quoque non lapidea, sed lignea fuit: ex humore enim caementi et lapidum (ut aiunt) eruginat ferrum.

VERS. 9.-- Et congregastis. Terra promissionis, quamvis uberrima, etc., usque ad inferior quae ab hostibus capi poterat, superior quae non poterat.

VERS. 11.-- Et lacum. (ID.) Non puteum qui aquam vitalem habet et aeternam, sed lacus contritos qui continere non valent aquam.

Inter duos muros. Scilicet et Novum et Vetus 1264DTestamentum, fecerunt sibi novas aquas, quas interiores putant et majora continere mysteria quam Vetus Testamentum.

VERS. 12.-- Et vocabit Dominus Deus exercituum. Quotidie provocat Dominus haereticos ad poenitentiam, etc., usque ad sed vilipendentes et desperantes dicebant: Comedamus et bibamus, cras enim moriemur (Jerem. XXXIV).

VERS. 14.-- Moriamini. (ID.) Vitiis, aut cum eis ad inferos descendatis, etc., usque ad quae hic dicuntur non pertinent ad captivitatem factam tempore Ezechiae.

VERS. 15.-- Sobnam. (HIER.) Convertere nunc, etc., usque ad unde ei Dominus haec mala minatur

1265AVERS. 16.-- Quid tu hic? Ac si diceret: Cur tibi vis aedificare domum? etc., usque ad quam sepulcrum et memoria appellandum.

VERS. 18.-- Coronans coronabit te. Qui scilicet quondam habebas coronam pontificis, et sanctificationem in lamina auri (in qua scriptum erat nomen Dei), tribulatione et angustia coronaberis.

Tribulatione. Pro lamina aurea in qua scriptum erat nomen Domini, quam in fronte portabat sacerdos semel in anno.

Quasi pilam. Sicut sphaera in loco declivi et spacioso stare non potet, sic populus tuus in orbis terminos dispergetur.

VERS. 20.-- Servum meum Eliacim. Per Sobnam superbum, etc., usque ad Filium Helciae, id est patris 1265BDomini.

VERS. 22.-- Clavem. Per clavem quam super humerum portat, sceptrum regni et signum victoriae, id est crucem possumus intelligere quam Christus ipse in suo proprio portavit humero.

Domus David. Domum David vocat domum Domini, in qua erat aurea clavis.

Et aperiet. Unde: Qui aperit et nemo claudit, claudit et nemo aperit. Quod in passione reseravit claudi non poterit; quod in caeremoniis clausit, nemo aperiet.

VERS. 24.-- Et suspendam super. Unde in Evangelio: Omnis populus pendebat ex eo. Hodie quoque ad verbum sapientiae ejus pendent.

VERS. 25.-- Auferetur paxillus. Quae dicuntur, etc., 1265Cusque ad Judaei convertentur ad fidem.

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 1.-- The burden of the valley. Jerusalem, once the mistress of the nations, etc., as far as who have fallen from the lofty sense of the Scriptures to the lowest things.

1264AThe valley of vision. The LXX: Sion, that is, of the watchtower, namely of the Church.

What aileth thee also. As if he should say: Since philosophy and worldly wisdom, disputing of lofty things, despises the simplicity of the Church, why dost thou also follow after high things?

VERS. 2.-- Full. The doctrine of the heretics is full of much talking and of clamor.

Thy slain. (HIER.) Of the valley, namely of Sion, by their own will, that is, they went over to the heretics. In comparison with those who have been conquered after a battle, they are more unhappily wounded who give themselves up of their own accord: as in martyrdom, he who denies after torments is more lightly punished than he who denies Christ without compulsion.

VERS. 3.-- All the princes, etc. As is read in Jeremias, 1264BSedechias by night through a hole in the wall, etc., as far as and they were led into Babylon.

They fled. In flight of one mind, whose prince is the fleeing dragon, the crooked serpent.

VERS. 5.-- And of weepings. (ID.) The LXX: Of error. Not that weeping and error are from the Lord, but from the occasion of the Scriptures which he gave them to read there arises an occasion of error, and they can say: Why hast thou made us to err from thy way? (Isa. LXIII.) [Vulg. Is 63:17 Quare errare nos fecisti, Domine, de viis tuis, "Why hast thou made us to err, O Lord, from thy ways?"]

VERS. 6.-- Elam. A city in the province of Persia, in which are the best archers: Elam took the quiver. Elam, their ascent, that is, the pride of the heretics, took the quiver, that he may shoot in the dark at the upright of heart. [Vulg. Ps 10:3 ut sagittent, plural, "that they may shoot"]

The wall. He stripped bare the bulwarks of the Church with his shield, that is, by his assault.

1264CVERS. 8.-- The covering. The temple, which before was covered and shut against the nations and the polluted.

The armoury. Beside the temple was a house which David built for the storing of arms, about which were many trees; whence it is called the house of the forest. It too was not of stone, but of wood: for from the moisture of the mortar and of the stones (as they say) iron rusts.

VERS. 9.-- And you gathered together. The land of promise, although most rich, etc., as far as the lower, which could be taken by the enemy, the upper, which could not.

VERS. 11.-- And a pool. (ID.) Not a well which has living and eternal water, but broken pools which cannot hold water.

Between two walls. That is, both the New and the Old 1264DTestament: they made for themselves new waters, which they think are more inward and contain greater mysteries than the Old Testament.

VERS. 12.-- And the Lord God of hosts shall call. Daily the Lord provokes the heretics to repentance, etc., as far as but, making light of it and despairing, they said: Let us eat and drink, for to-morrow we shall die (Jerem. XXXIV).

VERS. 14.-- Ye shall die. (ID.) To your vices, or ye shall go down with them to the lower regions, etc., as far as the things which are here said do not belong to the captivity made in the time of Ezechias.

VERS. 15.-- Sobna. (HIER.) Turn thee now, etc., as far as whence the Lord threatens him with these evils

1265AVERS. 16.-- What dost thou here? As if he should say: Why dost thou wish to build thee a house? etc., as far as than which it is to be called a sepulchre and a memorial.

VERS. 18.-- Crowning he will crown thee. Thou, namely, who once hadst the crown of the pontiff, and the sanctification in a plate of gold (in which the name of God was written), shalt be crowned with tribulation and anguish.

With tribulation. In place of the golden plate in which the name of the Lord was written, which the priest bore on his forehead once in the year.

As a ball. As a sphere cannot stand in a sloping and spacious place, so thy people shall be scattered to the ends of the world.

VERS. 20.-- My servant Eliacim. By Sobna the proud, etc., as far as the son of Helcias, that is, of the father 1265Bof the Lord.

VERS. 22.-- The key. By the key which he bears upon his shoulder, the sceptre of the kingdom and the sign of victory, that is, the cross we can understand, which Christ himself bore upon his own shoulder.

The house of David. He calls the house of David the house of the Lord, in which was the golden key.

And he shall open. Whence: He that openeth and no man shutteth, shutteth and no man openeth. What he unlocked in his passion cannot be shut; what he shut in the ceremonies, no man shall open.

VERS. 24.-- And I will hang upon. [Vulg. Is 22:24 Et suspendent super eum, "And they shall hang upon him"] Whence in the Gospel: All the people hung upon him. [Vulg. Lc 19:48 omnis populus suspensus erat, audiens illum, "all the people was in suspense, hearing him"] To-day also they hang upon the word of his wisdom.

VERS. 25.-- The peg shall be taken away. The things which are said, etc., 1265Cas far as the Jews shall be converted to the faith.

CAPUT XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Onus Tyri. Tyrus nobilissima civitas Chananaeorum, etc., usque ad hanc miseriam praedicit Isaias: Onus Tyri, etc.

Ululate. In Graeco apertius: Ululate, naves Carthaginis: vos, scilicet, qui Tyrum venire consuevistis causa negotiationis. Carthago colonia fuit Tyriis, ab eis constructa et culta.

Naves maris. Hebraice Tharsis, id est exploratio gaudii vel contemplatio, quia gaudii contemplatio et laetitiae vertetur in lacrymas.

VERS. 3.-- Negotiatio. (HIER.) Negotiatores, etc., usque ad et de aliis vitiis ad alia transeunt.

Semen Nili messis fluminis fruges, etc. Sic construitur: 1265Dsemen Nili fluminis erat messis et fruges ejus, scilicet Tyri. Terra Chananaeorum, in qua Tyrus est, infertilis est: Aegyptus, quam irrigat Nilus, fertilis frugibus, quas Tyrus ab Aegyptiis commutabat.

VERS. 4.-- Mare. Saeculi scilicet in quo parva animalia cum magnis, draco (quem Dominus formavit ad illudendum ei) videns eos a venatoribus Christi capi, qui a daemonibus fuerunt capti, dicit contrariae fortitudini venatorum (quam significat Sidon): Erubesce, Sidon.

Non parturivi. Quasi diceret: Nihil profuit parturisse, peperisse et enutrisse juvenes, quia Tyrus sine defensione capta est.

VERS. 5.-- Cum auditum fuerit in Aegypto, etc. 1266ATum de omnibus vitiis, tum de haeresibus hoc sentitur, quia una capta alia pertimescit, et in alterius confusione altera se capiendam sentit.

VERS. 6.-- Transite, ululate, qui habitatis. Sicut supra dictum est, cum non possent Nabuchodonosor resistere, festinaverunt qui potuerunt naves conscendere: alii remanserunt in civitate.

VERS. 7.-- Nunquid non haec vestra est, quae gloriabatur a diebus pristinis in antiquitate, etc. Quasi diceret: Haec ideo praecipio, quia urbs vestra Tyrus, deposito errore veteri, transibit ad veritatem.

VERS. 8.-- Principes institores ejus inclyti terrae, etc. (ID.) Inter alios negotiatores, vel etiam in regnis.

VERS. 9.-- Ad ignominiam deduceret, etc. Non Dominus ignominiae auctor est, sed quod ignominiosum 1266Best ostendit, secundum illud: Contaminatione contaminabit eum, non quod sacerdos contaminationis auctor sit, sed quod ostendit contaminatum eum quem mundum putabant multi.

VERS. 10.-- Flumen. Babyloniorum. Super flumina Babylonis illic sedimus et flevimus, dum recordaremur Sion (Psal. CXXXVI).

Cingulum ultra tibi. Fortitudo resistendi, cingulo enim lumbos restringimus: in lumbis fortitudinem habere dicimur.

VERS. 11.-- Conturbavit regna. Scilicet Aegyptiorum et Moabitarum, et aliarum in circuitu. Tyro enim capta et subversa, alia regna conturbata sunt timore. Regnum quoque Aegypti pro subversa Tyro datum est Nabuchodonosor.

1266CVERS. 12.-- Filia Sidonis. (ID.) In terra Chanaan sunt Tyrus et Sidon; unde: Mulier Chananaea exivit de finibus Tyri et Sidonis occurrens Salvatori (Matth. XV).

Transfreta. Saepe videmus quosdam a proposito suo transire ad aliud, a militia ad negotiationem: a causis ad arma mutant industriam, ut mutent infelicitatem. Sed, Deo dispensante, omnia eveniunt contraria, ut per miserias coacti non in se, sed in Domino creatore confidant.

VERS. 13.-- Talis populus non fuit. Usque ad mortem Nabuchodonosor fortissimi super omnes Chaldaei et Assyrii; postea populus Graecorum et Romanorum fortior fuit.

Suffoderunt. Quando Nabuchodonosor Tyrum invasit 1266Dnavigio, non solum murum, sed et domos subvertit; et quae erat negotiatio civitatum, facta est per septuaginta annos siccatio sagenarum.

VERS. 14.-- Naves maris, etc. Hebraice Tharsis, etc., usque ad vir ecclesiasticus destruit et subjicit sibi.

VERS. 15.-- Septuaginta annis. (HIER.) Tyrus quondam angustiata, etc., usque ad quis ergo non habeat spem salutis, si bene cecinerit, si chordas, id est virtutes, bene composuerit?

(ID.) Septenarius et septuagesimus, qui vel singulis diebus, vel septem decadibus perficitur, perfectam significat poenitentiam, ut, tempore poenitentiae expleto, jure Tyrus ad antiquum statum restituatur.

1267ACanticum meretricis. Quae ab amatoribus derelicta solet carmina in consolationem componere, et praeterita commemorare, sic et Tyrus reversa.

VERS. 16.-- Sume tibi citharam. Quae de hoc onere sequuntur ad reversionem Tyri spectant.

Bene cane, frequenter, etc. Admonet eam canticum miseriarum suarum semper cantare, ne iterum eadem patiatur. Dicunt enim hanc esse musicae naturam: si laetum invenerit, laetiorem facit; si tristem, tristiorem reddit.

VERS. 18.-- Et erunt negotiationes ejus, et mercedes ejus. Sicut dicit Hieronymus in Jeremia, etc., usque ad mercedes Domino sacrificaverunt.

CHAPTER XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- The burden of Tyre. Tyre, the most noble city of the Chananites, etc., as far as this misery Isaias foretells: The burden of Tyre, etc.

Howl ye. In the Greek more openly: Howl, ye ships of Carthage: ye, namely, who were wont to come to Tyre for the sake of trading. Carthage was a colony to the Tyrians, built and tilled by them.

Ships of the sea. In Hebrew Tharsis, that is, the exploring of joy or contemplation, because the contemplation of joy and of gladness shall be turned into tears.

VERS. 3.-- Trading. (HIER.) Traders, etc., as far as and they pass over from other vices to others.

The seed of the Nile, the harvest of the river, the fruits, etc. It is so construed: 1265Dthe seed of the river Nile was her harvest and her fruits, namely Tyre's. The land of the Chananites, in which Tyre is, is unfruitful: Egypt, which the Nile waters, is fruitful in fruits, which Tyre used to exchange with the Egyptians.

VERS. 4.-- The sea. Of the world, namely, in which are small living things with the great; the dragon (whom the Lord formed to make sport of him), seeing those taken by the hunters of Christ who had been taken by the demons, says to the strength contrary to the hunters (which Sidon signifies): Blush, O Sidon.

I have not been in labour. As if he should say: It has profited nothing to have been in labour, to have brought forth and nourished up young men, because Tyre was taken without defence.

VERS. 5.-- When it shall have been heard in Egypt, etc. 1266AThis is understood both of all the vices and of the heresies, because when one is taken another is greatly afraid, and in the confusion of the one the other feels that it is to be taken.

VERS. 6.-- Pass over, howl, ye that dwell. As has been said above, when they could not resist Nabuchodonosor, those hastened who were able to go on board the ships: the rest remained in the city.

VERS. 7.-- Is not this your city, which gloried from the days of old in its antiquity, etc. As if he should say: This I therefore command, because your city Tyre, its old error laid aside, shall pass over to the truth.

VERS. 8.-- Her princes, her traffickers, the nobles of the earth, etc. (ID.) Among other traders, or even in kingdoms.

VERS. 9.-- That he might bring down to disgrace, etc. The Lord is not the author of disgrace, but he shows what 1266Bis disgraceful, according to that word: He shall defile him with defilement, not that the priest is the author of the defilement, but that he shows him defiled whom many thought clean.

VERS. 10.-- The river. Of the Babylonians. Upon the rivers of Babylon there we sat and wept, while we remembered Sion (Psal. CXXXVI).

No girdle any more for thee. The strength of resisting, for with a girdle we bind up the loins: in the loins we are said to have strength.

VERS. 11.-- He hath troubled the kingdoms. Namely of the Egyptians and of the Moabites, and of the others round about. For when Tyre had been taken and overthrown, the other kingdoms were troubled with fear. The kingdom of Egypt also was given to Nabuchodonosor in place of Tyre overthrown.

1266CVERS. 12.-- The daughter of Sidon. (ID.) In the land of Chanaan are Tyre and Sidon; whence: The Chananite woman went out of the borders of Tyre and Sidon, meeting the Saviour (Matth. XV).

Pass over. Often we see certain men pass over from their own purpose to another, from soldiering to trading: from lawsuits to arms they change their industry, that they may change their ill fortune. But, by God's dispensation, all things fall out contrary, that, constrained by miseries, they may trust not in themselves, but in the Lord the Creator.

VERS. 13.-- There was no such people. Until the death of Nabuchodonosor the Chaldeans and the Assyrians were the mightiest above all; afterward the people of the Greeks and of the Romans was mightier.

They dug through. When Nabuchodonosor invaded Tyre 1266Dwith a fleet, he overthrew not only the wall, but the houses also; and what was the trafficking of the cities became for seventy years the drying of nets.

VERS. 14.-- Ships of the sea, etc. In Hebrew Tharsis, etc., as far as the churchman destroys and subjects it to himself.

VERS. 15.-- Seventy years. (HIER.) Tyre, once straitened, etc., as far as who then may not have hope of salvation, if he shall have sung well, if he shall have well composed the strings, that is, the virtues?

(ID.) The seventh and the seventieth, which is completed either by single days or by seven decades, signifies perfect penance, so that, the time of penance being fulfilled, Tyre is by right restored to her ancient state.

1267AThe song of a harlot. Who, forsaken by her lovers, is wont to compose songs for her consolation, and to call to mind things past; so also Tyre, returned.

VERS. 16.-- Take thee a harp. [Vulg. Is 23:16 Sume citharam, without tibi] The things which follow concerning this burden look to the return of Tyre.

Sing well, often, etc. [Vulg. Is 23:16 frequenta canticum, "repeat the song"] He admonishes her always to sing the song of her miseries, lest she suffer the same again. For they say that this is the nature of music: if it find a man glad, it makes him gladder; if sad, it renders him sadder.

VERS. 18.-- And her trafficking shall be, and her hire. As Jerome says on Jeremias, etc., as far as they sacrificed their hire to the Lord.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- Ecce Dominus nudabit, etc. (HIER.) 1267BPost specialem gentium singularum correptionem, etc., usque ad Aegyptiorum et multorum aliorum ad generale onus judicii extremi transit.

VERS. 2.-- Erit. (ID.) Omnes similiter vel pro bonis remunerabuntur, vel pro malis damnabuntur. Nulla diversitas erit inter nobilem et ignobilem, sacerdotem et laicum, servum et dominum, etc. Omnes enim exaequo stabunt ante tribunal Christi; nec erit acceptio personarum apud Deum.

Sic sacerdos. Bonus populus cum bono sacerdote percipiet coronam vitae; mali simul sustinebunt tormenta gehennae sine acceptione personae.

Terra. (ID.) Terrena opera ad nihilum redigentur, ut abolita imagine terreni hominis permaneat imago coelestis. Caro enim et sanguis regnum Dei 1267Cnon possidebunt, quia mortale hoc induet immortalitatem.

VERS. 6.-- Maledictio vorabit terram. Sicut in principio benedixit Deus omni creaturae, sic in fine maledicit his qui terreni sunt; nec peregrini, sed habitatores terrae: et qui in ea peccant, obliti conditionis suae contra se mutuo debacchantes. Et pauci remanebunt, qui habeant imaginem coelestis.

VERS. 7.-- Luxit vindemia, infirmata vitis est. (ID.) In consummatione, mundi praeteritarum deliciarum recordatio erit materia cruciatuum; unde, purpuratus dives qui receperat bona in vita sua, elevans oculos de inferno, cernit Lazarum in requie (Luc. XIX); unde: Vae vobis qui saturati estis, quia esurietis (Luc. VI).

Ingemuerunt omnes qui laetabuntur, etc. Hoc testimonium 1267Dingerimus eis qui in conviviis non solum gula et ebrietate, sed auribus luxuriantur, ut per omnes sensus animae fortitudo mollescat.

VERS. 10.-- Attrita est civitas vanitatis. Mundus in quo omnia vana, vanitas vanitatum et omnia vanitas.

VERS. 11.-- Clamor erit super vino. Per vinum, errorem; ebrii enim errant. Per plateas (quae Graece a latitudine dicuntur) spatiosam et latam viam, quae ducit ad mortem, accipimus. Super his ergo qui erraverunt in lata et spatiosa via hujus saeculi, in die judicii clamor doloris erit.

VERS. 13.-- In medio terrae. Jerusalem, quae est (ut aiunt) umbilicus terrae. Et, autumant multi, quia Dominus judicaturus super montem Sion, 1268Aex quo ascendit, in aere sedebit, in nube veniet.

Quomodo si paucae olivae, etc. Paucis relictis, cum persecutio voraverit terram, etc., usque ad collectis quoque racemis vix pauci remanent in vitibus vacuis.

VERS. 14.-- Atque laudabunt: cum glorificatus fuerit Dominus. (ID.) Laudabunt qui poterunt remanere et pressuram Antichristi fugere, vel ardorem imminentis gehennae.

Hinnient. Equorum similitudine, laetitiae gestientes magnitudinem.

VERS. 16.-- A finibus terrae, laudes audivimus, gloriam justi. In Hebraeo, alis terrae. Alae autem vel fines terrae, prophetae sunt et apostoli, qui de terrenis ad coelestia volant, et quibusdam praeceptorum finibus vivendi modum terminant. Dicunt ergo sancti: 1268BSicut a prophetis et apostolis audivimus, sic modo impletum videmus.

Secretum meum mihi. (ID.) Nec potest plene enarrare praemia bonorum, nec poenas malorum, quae praevidet per spiritum; et inde dolet, dicens: Vae mihi.

VERS. 17.-- Formido et fovea. Possunt haec tam ad tempora martyrum, etc., usque ad sic etiam fiet in tempore Antichristi.

VERS. 18.-- Apertae sunt, etc. Ut videat Deus iniquitates Antichristi et judicet. Dum enim non punit peccata, se videre ea dissimulat.

Fundamenta terrae. Principes saeculi, terrena opera.

VERS. 19.-- Confractione confringetur. Non quod terra redigatur in pulverem, vel nihilum, sed quia omnia terrena transibunt. Et quomodo ebrius nescit 1268Cquid agat, sic omnes qui versantur in terra malorum magnitudine ebrii erunt et stupebunt ad omnia.

VERS. 20.-- Tabernaculum unius noctis. (ID.) Quod a viatore transfertur, ut nullum remaneat vestigium, sic transibit figura hujus mundi (I Cor. VII), et erit terra deserta ut non sit habitatio amplius hominum in ea.

VERS. 21.-- Et erit, etc. Quasi diceret: Fenestrae coeli apertae sunt, ut prospiciente Domino terrena peccata, omnis figura terrenorum operum praetereat et corruat, neque in pristina statura resurgat. Visitabit ergo Dominus in die illa super militiam coeli, ut non solum terrena, sed coelestia judicet.

Super militiam. (ID.) Inebriatus est in coelo gladius meus. Astra non sunt munda in conspectu ejus. Et in angelis suis reperit pravitatem (Isa. XXXIV, Job. XV, IV).

1268DReges terrae. (HIER.) Rectores tenebrarum, contra spiritualia nequitiae in coelestibus (Ephes. VI); unde in Daniel: Exiit in occursum mihi princeps regni Persarum, et princeps regni Medorum (Dan. X)

VERS. 22.-- Et congregabuntur. (ID.) Origenes dicit quod, post hanc sententiam: Ite, maledicti, in ignem aeternum (Matth. XXV), post multos dies, id est post mille annos visitandi et liberandi sunt. Sed hoc Ecclesia non recipit. Dictum est enim: Ibunt impii in supplicium aeternum, justi autem in vitam aeternam (Matth. XIII). Quod autem aeternum est omni fine caret, et sicut gaudium aeternum, ita et supplicium.

Unus fascis in lacum. Unde: Alligate ea per fasciculos ad comburendum.

1269AEt post multos. Notandum quod non addit: a Domino vel ab angelis, etc., usque ad quod Domini arbitrio derelictum est.

VERS. 23.-- Et erubescet. Unde creatura ingemiscit et parturit, etc., usque ad qualis fuit Abraham mortuus in senectute bona.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- Behold the Lord shall make naked, etc. (HIER.) 1267BAfter the particular rebuke of the several nations, etc., as far as he passes over from the Egyptians and many others to the general burden of the last judgment.

VERS. 2.-- It shall be. (ID.) All alike shall either be rewarded for their good deeds or damned for their evil ones. There shall be no difference between the noble and the base-born, the priest and the layman, the servant and the lord, etc. For all shall stand on equal terms before the tribunal of Christ; nor shall there be any respect of persons with God.

So the priest. A good people with a good priest shall receive the crown of life; the wicked together shall bear the torments of hell without respect of person.

The earth. (ID.) Earthly works shall be brought to nothing, so that, the image of the earthly man being abolished, the image of the heavenly may remain. For flesh and blood shall 1267Cnot possess the kingdom of God, because this mortal shall put on immortality.

VERS. 6.-- A curse shall devour the earth. As in the beginning God blessed every creature, so in the end he curses those who are earthly; and not sojourners, but dwellers in the earth: those also who sin in it, forgetful of their condition, raging against one another. And few shall remain, who have the image of the heavenly.

VERS. 7.-- The vintage hath mourned, the vine hath been made weak. (ID.) At the consummation, the remembrance of the world's past delights shall be matter of torment; whence the rich man clothed in purple, who had received good things in his lifetime, lifting up his eyes out of hell, beholds Lazarus at rest (Luc. XIX); whence: Woe to you that are filled, for you shall hunger (Luc. VI).

All have groaned who shall rejoice, [Vulg. Is 24:7 qui laetabantur corde, "who rejoiced in heart"] etc. This testimony 1267Dwe press upon those who at banquets riot not only in gluttony and drunkenness, but in the ears, so that through all the senses the strength of the soul is softened.

VERS. 10.-- The city of vanity is broken down. The world, in which all things are vain, vanity of vanities and all is vanity.

VERS. 11.-- There shall be a crying over wine. By wine, error; for drunken men go astray. By the streets (which in Greek are named from breadth) we understand the spacious and broad way which leads to death. Over these, then, who have gone astray in the broad and spacious way of this world, there shall be a cry of grief in the day of judgment.

VERS. 13.-- In the midst of the earth. Jerusalem, which is (as they say) the navel of the earth. And many suppose that the Lord, being about to judge upon Mount Sion, 1268Afrom which he ascended, shall sit in the air, shall come in a cloud.

As if a few olives, etc. Few being left, when persecution shall have devoured the earth, etc., as far as the clusters also being gathered, scarcely a few remain on the empty vines.

VERS. 14.-- And they shall praise: when the Lord shall have been glorified. (ID.) They shall praise who shall be able to remain and to flee the oppression of Antichrist, or the burning of the hell that hangs over them.

They shall neigh. By the likeness of horses, showing forth the greatness of their gladness.

VERS. 16.-- From the ends of the earth we have heard praises, the glory of the just one. In Hebrew, the wings of the earth. Now the wings or the ends of the earth are the prophets and the apostles, who fly from earthly things to heavenly, and bound the manner of living by certain ends of precepts. The saints therefore say: 1268BAs from the prophets and the apostles we have heard, so now do we see it fulfilled.

My secret to myself. (ID.) He can neither fully declare the rewards of the good, nor the punishments of the wicked, which he foresees in the spirit; and thence he grieves, saying: Woe is me.

VERS. 17.-- Fear and the pit. These things may be referred both to the times of the martyrs, etc., as far as so also shall it be in the time of Antichrist.

VERS. 18.-- They are opened, etc. That God may see the iniquities of Antichrist and judge them. For while he does not punish sins, he makes as though he did not see them.

The foundations of the earth. The princes of the world, earthly works.

VERS. 19.-- With breaking shall it be broken. Not that the earth is reduced to dust, or to nothing, but because all earthly things shall pass away. And as a drunken man knows not 1268Cwhat he does, so all who dwell in the earth shall be drunk with the greatness of their evils and shall be astonished at all things.

VERS. 20.-- A tent of one night. (ID.) Which is carried off by the traveller, so that no trace remains, so shall the figure of this world pass away (I Cor. VII), and the earth shall be deserted so that there be no dwelling of men in it any more.

VERS. 21.-- And it shall be, etc. As if he should say: The windows of heaven are opened, so that, the Lord looking upon the sins of the earth, every figure of earthly works may pass away and fall, and not rise again in its former stature. The Lord therefore shall visit in that day upon the host of heaven, that he may judge not only earthly things, but heavenly.

Upon the host. (ID.) My sword is inebriated in heaven. The stars are not clean in his sight. And in his angels he found depravity (Isa. XXXIV, Job. XV, IV). [Vulg. Job 15:15 caeli non sunt mundi, "the heavens are not clean"]

1268DThe kings of the earth. (HIER.) The rulers of darkness, against the spirits of wickedness in the high places (Ephes. VI); whence in Daniel: The prince of the kingdom of the Persians went out to meet me, and the prince of the kingdom of the Medes (Dan. X) [Vulg. Dan. 10 has neither this sentence nor any prince of the kingdom of the Medes; 10:13 sets princeps regni Persarum and 10:20 princeps Graecorum]

VERS. 22.-- And they shall be gathered together. (ID.) Origen says that, after this sentence: Go, ye cursed, into everlasting fire (Matth. XXV), after many days, that is after a thousand years, they are to be visited and set free. But the Church does not receive this. For it has been said: The wicked shall go into everlasting punishment, but the just into life everlasting (Matth. XIII). [Vulg. Matt. 25:46 Et ibunt hi, "and these shall go"] Now what is everlasting lacks all end, and as the joy is everlasting, so also is the punishment.

One bundle into the pit. [Vulg. Is 24:22 unius fascis, "of one bundle"] Whence: Bind them in bundles to burn.

1269AAnd after many. It is to be noted that he does not add: by the Lord or by the angels, etc., as far as which has been left to the Lord's judgment.

VERS. 23.-- And it shall blush. Whence the creature groaneth and travaileth, etc., as far as such as was Abraham, dead in a good old age.

CAPUT XXV.

VERS. 1.-- Amen. (HIER.) Hebraeum, quo saepe Dominus utitur in Evangelio: Amen amen, id est vere vere dico vobis.

VERS. 2.-- Civitas. Eversa a Nabuchodonosor, restituta est a Neemia, et a Jesu summo sacerdote. Eversa a Romanis, restituta est ab Aelio Adriano; sed non in eodem loco, nec in statum pristinum.

Et in sempiternum. (ID.) Ut mille annorum regnum 1269Bet aureae Jerusalem et gemmatae fabula quiescat.

VERS. 10.-- Et triturabitur Moab, etc. (ID.) Licet generaliter de consummatione mundi loqueretur, etc., usque ad per Moab igitur recte intelligimus diabolum filiis Dei semper adversarium.

Paleae in plaustro. Ferratis plaustris teruntur paleae, ut eas facilius animalia et pascant et ruminent.

CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1.-- Amen. (HIER.) A Hebrew word, which the Lord often uses in the Gospel: Amen, amen, that is, truly, truly, I say to you.

VERS. 2.-- A city. Overthrown by Nabuchodonosor, it was restored by Nehemias and by Jesus the high priest. Overthrown by the Romans, it was restored by Aelius Hadrian; but not in the same place, nor to its former state.

And for ever. (ID.) That the fable of the thousand years' reign 1269Band of the golden and jewelled Jerusalem may rest.

VERS. 10.-- And Moab shall be threshed, etc. (ID.) Although he was speaking generally of the consummation of the world, etc., as far as by Moab therefore we rightly understand the devil, always the adversary of the sons of God.

Straw in a wain. Straw is threshed by iron-shod wains, that the beasts may the more easily both feed upon it and chew the cud.

CAPUT XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- In die illa, etc. (ID.) Hoc ad praesentem Ecclesiam potest referri, et ad diem judicii. Nunc Ecclesia canticum novum cantat: Christi nativitatem, passionem, resurrectionem, ascensionem. Vetus namque est antiquum hominem ad inferna descendere; novum, ad coelum ascendere.

Urbs fortitudinis. Vox justorum, et nunc, et in 1269Cfuturo.

VERS. 2.-- Aperite portas. Hoc canticum repente mutat personas.

Portas. Justitiae; unde: Aperite mihi portas justitiae. Portae vitae et justitiae, etc., usque ad et per multas portas ad eum qui dicit: Nemo venit ad Patrem nisi per me (Joan. XIV).

VERS. 3.-- Pacem. (ID.) Repetitio confirmatio est. Hanc habet ille de quo dicitur: Homo homo filiorum Israel (Ezech. XIV); et alibi: Vir vir cujus uxor cubile maculaverit (Num. V), ut duplex homo et duplex vir pacem consequatur duplicem.

VERS. 6.-- Conculcabit eam pes, etc. Quia Omnis qui se exaltat humiliabitur (Luc. I). Etiam ad contrarias fortitudines haec referre possumus.

1269DEgenorum. (HIER.) Apostolorum, quos expulerunt Judaei de finibus suis, qui, Domini imitantes paupertatem privilegium virtutis, ejus consecuti sunt.

VERS. 9.-- Anima mea desideravit, etc. Non caro; unde: Sitivit anima mea ad Deum fontem vivum (Psal. XLI). Caro concupiscit adversus spiritum (Gal. V).

VERS. 10.-- Misereamur. (ID.) Dominus ad prophetam: An misereamur impio, ita ut discat, scilicet clementiam meam, dum ipse salvatur? quasi, hoc melius est.

Et non discet justus, etc. Quasi diceret: Quomodo poterit nosse tuam justitiam, expertus tuam clementiam?

Et non videbit. Dominus ad prophetam, quasi melius 1270Aest, ut videat, et erubescat de malis suis. Et non videbit, quasi diceret: ad poenam sufficit, quia non videbit me regnantem in coelo cum sanctis meis. LXX: Auferetur impius, ut non videat claritatem Dei.

VERS. 11.-- Confundantur zelantes. LXX: Zelus apprehendet populum ineruditum.

VERS. 12.-- Domine, dabis. (ID.) Notandum quod postquam opera nostra reddiderit nobis, pacem etiam daturus sit, et quod ideo postulent pacem, quia operatus sit in eis. Dicunt, se, scilicet tormenta, sustinuisse, ut post misericordiam consequantur.

VERS. 13.-- Domine, possederunt. (ID.) Daemones, quos in idolis coluimus. LXX: Domine, extra te nescimus alium, non absolute alium, sed extra te: Pater enim in Filio, et Filius in Patre.

1270BTantum in te recordemur. (ID.) Non aliud petimus, nisi ut post errores digni simus, qui nominis tui recordemur.

Perdidisti omnem. (ID.) LXX: Masculinum eorum, etc., usque ad memoria masculinum interpretatus est contra illud: Nolite errare, Deus non irridetur.

VERS. 15.-- Nunquid. Non poenitentiam egit, et hoc est: Nunquid, etc., quasi diceret: Securitas negligentiam, negligentia contemptum parit.

VERS. 18.-- Et peperimus. Alii distingunt sic: Et peperimus spiritum, salutem non fecimus in terra.

Spiritum: salute, etc. Ut tota mente credamus, et per quem beneficia non sensimus, per tormenta discamus.

VERS. 21.-- Revelabit terra. (ID.) Terreni tunc 1270Cnon poterunt celare quos occiderunt, quia tunc omnia aperta; vel, terra quae suscepit sanguinem martyrum et operuit, tunc revelabitur quando resurgent.

CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- In that day, etc. (ID.) This may be referred both to the present Church and to the day of judgment. Now the Church sings a new song: the nativity of Christ, his passion, his resurrection, his ascension. For it is old that the old man should go down to the lower regions; new, that he should ascend to heaven.

The city of strength. The voice of the just, both now and in the 1269Ctime to come.

VERS. 2.-- Open ye the gates. This song suddenly changes the persons.

The gates. Of justice; whence: Open to me the gates of justice. The gates of life and of justice, etc., as far as and through many gates to him who says: No man cometh to the Father but by me (Joan. XIV).

VERS. 3.-- Peace. (ID.) The repetition is a confirmation. He has this of whom it is said: Man, man of the children of Israel (Ezech. XIV); [Vulg. Ezech. 14:4 Homo, homo de domo Israel, "man, man of the house of Israel"] and elsewhere: A man, a man whose wife shall have defiled the bed (Num. V), [Vulg. Num. 5:12 Vir cujus uxor erraverit, "a man whose wife shall have gone astray," without the doubled Vir] that a twofold man and a twofold husband may obtain a twofold peace.

VERS. 6.-- The foot shall tread it down, etc. Because Every one that exalteth himself shall be humbled (Luc. I). We may refer these things also to the contrary powers.

1269DOf the needy. (HIER.) Of the apostles, whom the Jews drove out of their borders, who, imitating the poverty of the Lord, obtained the privilege of his virtue.

VERS. 9.-- My soul hath desired, etc. Not the flesh; whence: My soul hath thirsted after God, the living fountain (Psal. XLI). [Vulg. Ps 41:3 ad Deum fortem, vivum, "after God, the strong, the living"] The flesh lusteth against the spirit (Gal. V).

VERS. 10.-- Let us have mercy. (ID.) The Lord to the prophet: Shall we have mercy on the wicked man, so that he may learn, namely my clemency, while he himself is saved? as if to say, this is better.

And the just man shall not learn, etc. As if he should say: How shall he be able to know thy justice, having experienced thy clemency?

And he shall not see. The Lord to the prophet, as though it were better 1270Athat he should see, and blush for his own evils. And he shall not see, as if he should say: it suffices for his punishment, that he shall not see me reigning in heaven with my saints. The Septuagint: The wicked man shall be taken away, that he may not see the brightness of God.

VERS. 11.-- Let them be confounded that are envious. The Septuagint: Zeal shall lay hold on the untaught people.

VERS. 12.-- Lord, thou wilt give. (ID.) It is to be noted that after he has rendered our works to us, he is also about to give peace, and that they therefore ask for peace because he has wrought in them. They say that they, namely, have borne torments, that afterwards they may obtain mercy.

VERS. 13.-- Lord, they have possessed. (ID.) The demons, whom we worshipped in idols. The Septuagint: Lord, apart from thee we know no other, not absolutely another, but apart from thee: for the Father is in the Son, and the Son in the Father.

1270BOnly in thee let us remember. (ID.) We ask nothing else, save that after our errors we may be worthy to remember thy name.

Thou hast destroyed all. (ID.) The Septuagint: Their male, etc., as far as he interpreted the memory to be the male, against that word: Be not deceived, God is not mocked.

VERS. 15.-- Hast thou. He did not do penance, and this is: Hast thou, etc., as if he should say: Security begets negligence, negligence contempt.

VERS. 18.-- And we have brought forth. Others punctuate thus: And we have brought forth the spirit, we have not wrought salvation in the earth. [Vulg. Is 26:18 Salutes non fecimus, plural, "we have not wrought deliverances"]

Spirit: salvation, etc. That we may believe with the whole mind, and that we may learn through torments through him by whom we have not felt benefits.

VERS. 21.-- The earth shall disclose. (ID.) Then earthly men 1270Cshall not be able to hide those whom they killed, because then all things are open; or, the earth which received the blood of the martyrs and covered it, shall then be disclosed when they rise again.

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 1.-- In die illa, etc. (ID.) Tradunt Hebraei diabolum, etc., usque ad ut inducatur gladius super eum.

Vectem. (HIER.) LXX: Fugitivum. Tunc enim fugiet, etc., usque ad serpens autem, quia insidias molliter infundit.

Cetum. Hebraei, Tanniu; unde Hebraei fabulabantur Leviathan habitare sub terra et in aethere, Tanniu vero in mari.

VERS. 2.-- Vinea meri cantabit. Cui Jeremias propinat calicem meracissimum. Merum Graece, purum Latine.

1270DVERS. 5.-- An potius? Vox Patris deliberative, etc., usque ad qui non sunt salvati auctoritate legis.

VERS. 6.-- Israel, id est populus Judaeorum conversus, videns doctrina filiorum suorum orbem floruisse.

VERS. 9.-- Idcirco. Quasi diceret: Ideo dimittetur iniquitas domui Jacob: et caetera propter merita scilicet apostolorum, qui per mundum idolorum altaria comminuerunt.

VERS. 11.-- Conterentur. (ID.) Sicut Delbora ad bellum ivit et populum ad ignominiam Judaeorum judicavit; et imminente Chaldaeorum captivitate, viris tacentibus Olda prophetavit: sic imminente captivitate Romanorum, veniebant forte mulieres et docebant eos.

1271AMulieres. Diversae Synagogae; vel aperte mulieres, quae nudatis pectoribus sanguinantes feriunt lacertos.

Docentes eam. (HIER.) Magna infelicitas populi et reprehensio, a mulieribus lamentationis carmina discere.

VERS. 12.-- Ab alveo. LXX: ab Euphrate, etc., usque ad quia habet turbidas aquas, et non perpetuas.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 1.-- In that day, etc. (ID.) The Hebrews hand down that the devil, etc., as far as that the sword may be brought upon him.

The bar. (HIER.) The Septuagint: The fugitive. For then he shall flee, etc., as far as but the serpent, because he pours in his snares softly.

The whale. The Hebrews, Tanniu; whence the Hebrews used to tell in fables that Leviathan dwelt under the earth and in the air, but Tanniu in the sea.

VERS. 2.-- The vineyard of pure wine shall sing. To which Jeremias offers a cup of the purest wine. Merum in Greek, purum in Latin.

1270DVERS. 5.-- Or rather? The voice of the Father deliberating, etc., as far as who are not saved by the authority of the law.

VERS. 6.-- Israel, that is the people of the Jews converted, seeing that the world has flourished by the teaching of their sons.

VERS. 9.-- Therefore. As if he should say: Therefore shall the iniquity be forgiven to the house of Jacob: and the rest, on account, namely, of the merits of the apostles, who throughout the world broke to pieces the altars of the idols.

VERS. 11.-- They shall be broken. (ID.) As Delbora went to war and judged the people to the shame of the Jews; and when the captivity of the Chaldeans was at hand, Olda prophesied while the men were silent: so, when the captivity of the Romans was at hand, women perhaps came and taught them.

1271AWomen. The diverse Synagogues; or plainly women, who with bared breasts strike their bleeding arms.

Teaching her. (HIER.) It is a great unhappiness for a people, and a reproach, to learn songs of lamentation from women.

VERS. 12.-- From the channel. The Septuagint: from the Euphrates, etc., as far as because it has turbid waters, and not perpetual.

CAPUT XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Vae coronae superbiae. (ID.) Huc usque ad consummationem mundi, etc., usque ad nec poenitentiae fructum salutis habuit.

Vallis pinguissimae. Gethsemani fertilitate frugum, per eam erat eis ascensus in civitatem, et ibi figebant aliqui, ludi causa, tentoria dum in civitate morabantur.

1271BVERS. 5.-- In die illa. (ID.) Secundum prophetiam, cum Dominus, etc., usque ad Scribae enim et Pharisaei vino et sicera inebriati sunt.

VERS. 6.-- Fortitudo revertentibus. (ID.) Non leguntur bella Ezechiae, sed Dominus pro eo bellum gessit, interfectis centum et octoginta quinque millibus Assyriorum, quorum Judaei spolia collegerunt: et ad portas Jerusalem cum gaudio reversi sunt.

VERS. 8.-- Omnes enim. Dum Scripturarum non digerunt cibos, nec corpori faciunt vitales, sed immaturos et fetentes egerunt, ut in eis Dominus nullum inveniat locum.

VERS. 9.-- Ablactatos a lacte, avulsos. Alios non, quia portare non possunt. Hi enim sunt qui jam solido cibo uti possunt; aliis dicit Apostolus: Lac 1271Cdedi vobis potum, non escam (I Cor. III).

VERS. 10.-- Quia manda, remanda, manda, remanda; exspecta, reexspecta, exspecta, reexspecta, modicum ibi, modicum ibi. (ID.) Quasi dicat. Vos non potestis doceri, quia irridentes prophetas (per quos praedicit Dominus vobis mala superventura, ut poenitentiam agatis et liberemini); opponitis, manda, remanda; quasi dicat: O Propheta, praecipe quod vis, minare quae vis; haec tamen non eveniunt quae minaris.

VERS. 11.-- In loquela enim. Quasi dicat: Malo vestro haec dicitis, quia non praenuntiabit vobis Dominus amodo mala, sed statim inferet. Et hoc est: In loquela enim labii et lingua altera loquentur ad populum istum, etc.

1271DVERS. 16.-- Lapidem. Lapidem quem aedificantes reprobaverunt, hic factus est in caput anguli.

VERS. 19.-- Sola vexatio intellectum dabit auditui.

VERS. 21.-- Sicut enim. Philisthaeos congregatos superveniens David auxilio Domini, divisit vivos a mortuis et perversa loca dispersit; unde locus ille Baalphrasim nomen accepit.

Alienum opus. (ID.) Opus proprium Dei est misereri, parcere, condonare. Non suum, sed alienum est, irasci, punire.

(GREG.) In mundum veniet, ut faciat opus suum, scilicet ut redimat genus humanum; sed ab ipso est alienum opus ejus; non convenit enim Deitati conspui, flagellari, crucifigi.

VERS. 24.-- Nunquid tota. (HIER.) Pius et misericors 1272ADeus genus humanum, ut fructum boni operis afferat, arat ut agricola vomere praedicationis, proscindit verbere tribulationis, nunc peste, nunc captivitate corrigit; et hoc est: Nunquid tota die, etc.

VERS. 25.-- Gith et cyminum. Gith et cyminum quae in virga excutiuntur, etc., usque ad clavem scientiae habentes.

VERS. 29.-- Et hoc a Domino Deo exivit.

CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Woe to the crown of pride. (ID.) Up to this point unto the consummation of the world, etc., as far as nor had he the fruit of penance unto salvation.

Of the most fat valley. Gethsemani, by its fruitfulness of produce; through it lay their ascent into the city, and there some pitched tents, for sport's sake, while they tarried in the city.

1271BVERS. 5.-- In that day. (ID.) According to the prophecy, when the Lord, etc., as far as for the Scribes and Pharisees were drunk with wine and strong drink.

VERS. 6.-- Strength to them that return. (ID.) The wars of Ezechias are not read of, but the Lord waged war for him, a hundred and eighty-five thousand of the Assyrians being slain, whose spoils the Jews gathered: and they returned with joy to the gates of Jerusalem.

VERS. 8.-- For all. While they do not digest the food of the Scriptures, nor make it life-giving to the body, but cast it out unripe and stinking, so that the Lord finds no place in them.

VERS. 9.-- Them that are weaned from the milk, that are drawn away. Not others, because they cannot bear it. For these are they who are now able to use solid food; to others the Apostle says: I gave you 1271Cmilk to drink, not meat (I Cor. III).

VERS. 10.-- For command, command again, command, command again; expect, expect again, expect, expect again, a little there, a little there. (ID.) As if he should say: You cannot be taught, because, mocking the prophets (through whom the Lord foretells to you the evils that are to come upon you, that you may do penance and be delivered), you object, command, command again; as if to say: O Prophet, enjoin what thou wilt, threaten what thou wilt; yet these things which thou threatenest do not come to pass.

VERS. 11.-- For in the speech. As if he should say: You say these things to your own hurt, because the Lord shall no longer foretell evils to you, but shall bring them at once. And this is: For in the speech of the lip and with another tongue they shall speak to this people, [Vulg. Is 28:11 loquetur, singular, "shall he speak"] etc.

1271DVERS. 16.-- A stone. The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner.

VERS. 19.-- Vexation alone shall give understanding to the hearing.

VERS. 21.-- For as. David, coming upon the Philistines gathered together by the help of the Lord, divided the living from the dead and scattered the perverse places; whence that place received the name Baalphrasim.

A strange work. (ID.) The proper work of God is to have mercy, to spare, to pardon. Not his own, but strange, is it to be angry, to punish.

(GREG.) He shall come into the world, that he may do his own work, namely that he may redeem the human race; but that work of his is alien to him: for it does not befit the Godhead to be spat upon, to be scourged, to be crucified.

VERS. 24.-- Shall all the day. (HIER.) God, kind and merciful, 1272Aploughs the human race like a husbandman with the ploughshare of preaching, that it may bring forth the fruit of good work; he breaks it up with the lash of tribulation, and corrects it now with pestilence, now with captivity; and this is: Shall all the day, etc.

VERS. 25.-- Gith and cummin. Gith and cummin, which are beaten out with a rod, etc., as far as having the key of knowledge.

VERS. 29.-- And this hath come forth from the Lord God. [Vulg. Is 28:29 a Domino Deo exercituum exivit, "from the Lord God of hosts"]

CAPUT XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- Vae Ariel. (ID.) Ad Jerusalem compatientis affectu: Quia si cognovisses et tu (Luc. XIX); Ariel, leo Dei; leo enim Dei Jerusalem potentissima et fortissima fuit.

Expugnavit. (ID.) Cum potentissima esset, repugnantibus caecis et claudis, quando Joab primus domatum 1272Bexcelsa conscendit.

Additus est annus. Cum captivitati essent a Nabuchodonosor, neque templum habebant, neque sacrificium, et hoc per multos annos: et hoc est annus ad annum additus.

VERS. 3.-- Quasi sphaeram in circumitu tuo. Templi similitudinem retinentis antiquam, non magnificentiam.

VERS. 4.-- Pythonis, etc. (ID.) Phytonissa quae erat in Endor per necromantiam Samuelem, imo daemonem suscitavit: et hujusmodi futura praedixit in populo illo. Mania, insania, unde manichaeos, manthos, divinatio.

VERS. 6.-- In tonitruo. Visi sunt Romani irruere cum tonitruo, et terra moveri eorum adventu, et 1272Ccum flamma ignis, quia et civitatem succenderunt et templum.

VERS. 9.-- Obstupescite et admiramini. (HIER.) Post subversionem Jerusalem et templi, id est Ariel contra Scribas et Pharisaeos prophetia dirigitur.

VERS. 11.-- Signatus est enim. Haec impleta sunt, quando et dignitatem et sacerdotium amiserunt, et civitas destructa est et templum a principibus Romanorum.

VERS. 13.-- Eo quod appropinquat populus iste. (ID.) Dominus in Evangelio Matthaei utitur, etc., usque ad verbis propriis usos.

Labiis suis. (ID.) LXX. Labiis suis me honorat, cor autem ejus longe est a me: frustra autem colunt me, docentes praecepta hominum atque doctrinas.

1272DVERS. 14.-- Peribit enim. (ID.) Ab loco hoc Apostolus testimonium ponit: Perdam sapientiam sapientium, et intellectum prudentium reprobabo: aliis verbis eumdem sensum: Vere grande miraculum fecit Deus in populo ut eodem tempore civitas arderet, et templum corrueret, et omnis magistrorum scientia deperiret.

VERS. 15.-- A Domino abscondatis. (ID.) Quasi non videat Deus. Hoc errore Adam et Eva absconderunt se: Cain quoque dixit: A facie tua abscondar.

VERS. 16.-- Perversa est, etc. (ID.) Ut Creator ignoret creaturam; unde Apostolus: O homo tu quis es, qui respondeas Deo? nunquid dicit figmentum factori suo: Quare me fecisti? (Rom. IX.)

1273AVERS. 17.-- In modico et in brevi. (HIER.) Modicum et breve ex quo hoc praedictum est usque ad ascensionem Domini: omnia enim tempora, aeternitati comparata, brevia sunt et momentanea.

Libanus in Carmel. Libanus mons in Phoenicia, etc., usque ad si esset cum adultero viro.

VERS. 21.-- Et declinaverunt frustra a justo. Daemones conati sunt, etc., usque ad Scribae et Pharisaei declinaverunt frustra a justo, dicentes: Non habemus regem nisi Caesarem (Joan. XIX).

VERS. 22.-- Redemit Abraham. De igne Chaldaeorum, etc., usque ad ignis enim lingua eorum ur dicitur.

CHAPTER XXIX.

VERS. 1.-- Woe to Ariel. (ID.) To Jerusalem, with the affection of one that suffers with her: If thou also hadst known (Luc. XIX); Ariel, the lion of God; for Jerusalem, the lion of God, was most powerful and most strong.

He took by storm. (ID.) Since she was most powerful, the blind and the lame resisting, when Joab first climbed the heights 1272Bof the roofs.

A year is added. When they were in captivity under Nabuchodonosor, they had neither temple nor sacrifice, and this for many years: and this is, a year added to a year.

VERS. 3.-- As a ball round about thee. Retaining the ancient likeness of the temple, not its magnificence.

VERS. 4.-- Of the python, etc. (ID.) The pythoness who was at Endor raised up Samuel by necromancy, nay rather a demon: and she foretold things to come of this kind among that people. Mania, madness, whence the Manichees; manthos, divination.

VERS. 6.-- In thunder. The Romans were seen to rush in with thunder, and the earth to be moved at their coming, and 1272Cwith the flame of fire, because they burned both the city and the temple.

VERS. 9.-- Be astonished and wonder. (HIER.) After the overthrow of Jerusalem and of the temple, that is of Ariel, the prophecy is directed against the Scribes and Pharisees.

VERS. 11.-- For it is sealed. These things were fulfilled when they lost both their dignity and their priesthood, and the city was destroyed and the temple, by the princes of the Romans.

VERS. 13.-- Forasmuch as this people draw near. (ID.) The Lord uses it in the Gospel of Matthew, etc., as far as having used his own words.

With their lips. (ID.) The Septuagint. With their lips they honour me, but their heart is far from me: and in vain do they worship me, teaching the precepts of men and doctrines.

1272DVERS. 14.-- For shall perish. (ID.) From this place the Apostle takes a testimony: I will destroy the wisdom of the wise, and the understanding of the prudent I will reject: [Vulg. 1 Cor. 1:19 prudentiam prudentium reprobabo, "the prudence of the prudent I will reject"] the same sense in other words: Truly God wrought a great miracle among the people, that at one and the same time the city should burn, and the temple fall down, and all the knowledge of the masters perish.

VERS. 15.-- That you may hide from the Lord. (ID.) As though God did not see. By this error Adam and Eve hid themselves: Cain also said: From thy face I shall be hidden.

VERS. 16.-- Perverse is, etc. (ID.) That the Creator should be ignorant of the creature; whence the Apostle: O man, who art thou that repliest to God? doth the thing formed say to its maker: Why hast thou made me? (Rom. IX.) [Vulg. Rom. 9:20 figmentum ei qui se finxit: Quid me fecisti sic?, "to him that formed it: Why hast thou made me thus?"]

1273AVERS. 17.-- In a little and a short while. (HIER.) A little and short is the time from when this was foretold up to the ascension of the Lord: for all times, compared with eternity, are short and of a moment.

Libanus into Carmel. Libanus, a mountain in Phoenicia, etc., as far as if she were with an adulterous husband.

VERS. 21.-- And they have turned aside in vain from the just man. The demons attempted, etc., as far as the Scribes and Pharisees turned aside in vain from the just man, saying: We have no king but Caesar (Joan. XIX).

VERS. 22.-- He redeemed Abraham. From the fire of the Chaldeans, etc., as far as for in their tongue fire is called ur.

CAPUT XXX.

VERS. 1.-- Vae, filii desertores, dicit Dominus. 1273B(ID.) Post prophetiam contra Ariel et caetera, etc., usque ad fugerunt in Aegyptum: et hoc est: vae, filii desertores.

VERS. 2.-- Qui ambulatis. Ut dicit Hieronymus: Jeremias cum eis ivit, etc., usque ad: ut habitaretis in terra vestra.

Erant enim in Thaphnis. Quasi dicat: An ideo recessistis a me, ut fugeretis usque in Thaphnis et Anes? Thaphnis civitas in qua magi contra Moysen signa fecerunt; Anes in finibus Aegypti sita est: forsitan timore turbati non ausi sunt in Thaphnis manere, et fugerunt usque ad Anes.

VERS. 6.-- Onus jumentorum. (ID.) Post decem onera diversarum gentium, undecimum onus reliquarum duarum tribuum ponit, quae remanserunt 1273Cducto Sedechia et aliis principibus in Babylonem.

In terra. (ID.) Eremus per quam pontifex Joanna filius Chareae et alii duces traduxerunt reliquias in Aegyptum contra praeceptum Domini; vel, ipsa Aegyptus in qua multas tribulationes et angustias sustinuerunt.
