VERS. 8.-- Nunc ergo. (ID.) Dominus ad Jeremiam, vel ad Isaiam secundum Hebraeos, etc., usque ad quia bene feci eis, bene docui, et ipsi spreverunt.

VERS. 12.-- Pro eo quod reprobastis verbum. (ID.) Haeretici quoque et omne dogma contrarium veritati, etc., usque ad et saltem coenosam et parvulam aquam sitientibus praebere.

VERS. 13.-- Sicut interruptio cadens. Quasi: Quomodo interruptio muri altissimi qui longam traxit 1273Druinam difficile instaurari, et pristinum decorem recipere potest, sic veniet vobis repentina contritio.

VERS. 19.-- Plorans nequaquam plorabit. (ID.) Judaei referunt hoc ad tempus illud quando sub Zorobabel, etc., usque ad in specula scilicet et visione pacis, id est Ecclesia.

VERS. 21.-- Post tergum. Prima conditione quasi in facie Adam audivit a Domino, etc., usque ad et hoc est: Aures tuae audient verbum monentis post tergum.

Neque ad dexteram. In utraque parte quidquid supra modum est, in vitio est. Neque ad dexteram, etc. declinandum est, sed via publica gradiendum, id est regia, quae non fallit.

VERS. 22.-- Et contaminabis laminas. (ID.) Idola 1274Ascilicet quae ad similitudinem veritatis artifex lingua composuerit, in splendore eloquentiae (quod significat argentum) et in ratione sapientiae (quod significat aurum) comminues, et disperges, et immunda judicabis.

VERS. 23.-- Et dabitur pluvia. (ID.) In Regum volumine scriptum est, etc., usque ad nisi enim vitia abjecerint, virtutes non subeunt.

Pascetur. Quidam haec hyperbolicos Cyri temporibus, qui captivitatem populi relaxavit, in Judaea completa, contendunt.

VERS. 24.-- Commixtum migma. Isaac hordeum sevit, etc., usque et ad per paleas et hordeum paulatim proficiunt, et transeunt ad frumentum.

VERS. 25.-- Et erunt. In die judicii super montes 1274Bet colles erunt rivi, etc., usque ad qui enim biberit de aquis ejus non sitiet in aeternum.

Interfectionis multorum. (ID.) Multi enim sunt vocati, etc., usque ad quorum linguae in Babylone confusae, et super quos cecidit turris in Siloe.

VERS. 26.-- Et erit lux. Cum praemium laboris percipient, quia creatura liberabitur a servitute corruptionis. Non mirum si luna solis fulgorem recipiat: Quia justi fulgebunt sicut sol (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 27.-- Ecce nomen Domini. Filius in nomine Patris, unum enim nomen est Patris et Filii et Spiritus sancti: unde: Baptizantes eos in nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus sancti: non in nominibus, sed in nomine quod est Deus.

VERS. 28.-- Spiritus ejus velut torrens inundans. 1274C(HIER.) Sicut torrens inundans cum pervenerit usque ad collum, animantem suffocat, sic spiritus irae Dei in judicio impios suffocabit. Finem omnium venisse significans, sicut torrens suffocat eum ad cujus collum pervenit; sic Dei judicium neminem transibit impunitum.

Quod erat in maxillis. Ut pro voluntate diaboli circumferrentur, haec perdet Dominus in die judicii, vel in praedicatione apostolorum cum gentes ad fidem venerint, et idola relinquent.

VERS. 29.-- Canticum erit vobis. Post poenam malorum agit de gloria sanctorum. Sicut filii Israel, transito mari Rubro, cum vidissent Pharaonem et Aegyptios submersos, cum laetitia dixerunt: Cantemus Domino, gloriose enim, etc.; sic in die judicii, 1274Dtransito mundo et damnato diabolo et membris suis, sancti cum laetitia exsultabunt in Dei laudibus; eodem modo in conversione gentium.

Sicut qui pergit. Praeceptum est filiis Israel, ut decimam bonorum suorum Domino offerrent, Datorem bonorum glorificantes: sic sancti in judicio offerent manipulos suos exsultantes et Deum laudantes, et gentes conversae similiter.

VERS. 33.-- Sulphuris. In inferno fetor intolerabilis et inexstinguibilis ignis.

CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 1.-- Woe, ye apostate children, saith the Lord. 1273B(ID.) After the prophecy against Ariel and the rest, etc., as far as they fled into Egypt: and this is: woe, ye apostate children.

VERS. 2.-- Who walk. As Jerome says: Jeremias went with them, etc., as far as: that you might dwell in your own land.

For they were in Thaphnis. As if he should say: Have you therefore withdrawn from me, that you might flee as far as Thaphnis and Anes? Thaphnis is a city in which the magicians wrought signs against Moses; Anes is situated in the borders of Egypt: perhaps, troubled with fear, they did not dare to remain in Thaphnis, and fled as far as Anes.

VERS. 6.-- The burden of the beasts of burden. (ID.) After the ten burdens of the various nations, he sets down an eleventh burden, of the two remaining tribes, which were left 1273Cwhen Sedecias and the other princes had been led into Babylon.

In the land. (ID.) The desert through which the pontiff Joanna the son of Chareas and the other leaders led the remnant into Egypt against the precept of the Lord; or, Egypt itself, in which they endured many tribulations and straits.

VERS. 8.-- Now therefore. (ID.) The Lord to Jeremias, or to Isaias according to the Hebrews, etc., as far as because I did them good, I taught them well, and they despised it.

VERS. 12.-- Because you have rejected the word. (ID.) Heretics also and every teaching contrary to the truth, etc., as far as and at least to offer muddy and scanty water to the thirsty.

VERS. 13.-- As a breach falling. As if to say: As a breach in a very high wall which has drawn a long 1273Druin can hardly be repaired and receive its former beauty, so shall a sudden destruction come upon you.

VERS. 19.-- Weeping he shall in no wise weep. [Vulg. Is 30:19 plorabis, second person, "weeping thou shalt not weep"] (ID.) The Jews refer this to that time when under Zorobabel, etc., as far as in the watchtower, namely, and the vision of peace, that is the Church.

VERS. 21.-- Behind the back. In the first condition, as it were in the face, Adam heard from the Lord, etc., as far as and this is: Thy ears shall hear the word of one admonishing behind the back.

Nor to the right hand. On either side, whatever is beyond measure is in fault. Nor to the right hand, etc., is one to turn aside, but one must walk by the public road, that is the royal one, which does not deceive.

VERS. 22.-- And thou shalt defile the plates. (ID.) The idols, 1274Anamely, which the craftsman's tongue shall have fashioned after the likeness of truth, thou shalt break to pieces in the splendour of eloquence (which silver signifies) and in the reason of wisdom (which gold signifies), and shalt scatter them, and shalt judge them unclean.

VERS. 23.-- And rain shall be given. (ID.) In the volume of Kings it is written, etc., as far as for unless they have cast off vices, the virtues do not enter in.

He shall feed. Some contend that these things were fulfilled hyperbolically in Judaea in the times of Cyrus, who released the captivity of the people.

VERS. 24.-- Mingled provender. Isaac sowed barley, etc., as far and as through the chaff and the barley they advance little by little, and pass over to the wheat.

VERS. 25.-- And there shall be. In the day of judgment upon the mountains 1274Band the hills there shall be streams, etc., as far as for he that shall drink of its waters shall not thirst for ever.

Of the slaying of many. (ID.) For many are called, etc., as far as whose tongues were confounded in Babylon, and upon whom the tower in Siloe fell.

VERS. 26.-- And there shall be light. When they shall receive the reward of their labour, because the creature shall be delivered from the servitude of corruption. It is no wonder if the moon receive the brightness of the sun: Because the just shall shine as the sun (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 27.-- Behold the name of the Lord. The Son in the name of the Father, for the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost is one: whence: Baptizing them in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost: not in the names, but in the name which is God.

VERS. 28.-- His spirit as a torrent overflowing. 1274C(HIER.) As an overflowing torrent, when it has come up to the neck, chokes a living creature, so the spirit of the wrath of God shall choke the wicked in the judgment. Signifying that the end of all things has come, as a torrent chokes him to whose neck it has come; so the judgment of God shall pass over no man unpunished.

Which was in the jaws. That they might be carried about at the will of the devil, these things the Lord shall destroy in the day of judgment, or in the preaching of the apostles when the nations shall have come to the faith, and shall leave their idols.

VERS. 29.-- You shall have a song. After the punishment of the wicked he treats of the glory of the saints. As the children of Israel, when the Red Sea had been crossed, when they had seen Pharao and the Egyptians drowned, said with gladness: Let us sing to the Lord, for gloriously, etc.; so in the day of judgment, 1274Dwhen the world has been passed over and the devil condemned with his members, the saints shall exult with gladness in the praises of God; in the same manner also in the conversion of the nations.

As one that goeth. It was commanded the children of Israel that they should offer to the Lord the tenth of their goods, glorifying the Giver of good things: so the saints in the judgment shall offer their sheaves, exulting and praising God, and the converted nations likewise.

VERS. 33.-- Of brimstone. In hell there is an intolerable stench and an inextinguishable fire.

CAPUT XXXI

VERS. 1.-- Vae qui descendunt in Aegyptum. (ID.) Egit de gloria bonorum et de poena reproborum, nunc revertitur ad reliquias quae remanserunt 1275Ain terra Juda, ducto Sedecia et aliis in Babylonem. Sed prius monuit ne irent in Aegyptum: modo quia ierunt minatur malum.

Non sunt confisi super sanctum Israel. Non exquisierunt corde, sed verbis; dixerunt enim ad Jeremiam: Ora pro nobis Dominum quid agere debeamus? et noluerunt obedire. Cui simile: Deum non invocaverunt. Quod de malis sacerdotibus dictum est.

VERS. 2.-- Ipse autem sapiens. Unde Jeremias: Maledictus homo qui confidit in homine, etc., usque ad locus ubi crucifixus est Dominus specialiter vocatur Sodoma et Aegyptus (Apoc. XI).

VERS. 4.-- Quia haec dicit. (HIER.) Hoc de illis, qui cum Sedecia in captivitatem ducti, post septuaginta annos, Domino miserante, reversi sunt, caesis in 1275BAegypto Israelitis et Aegyptiis, in quorum auxilio confidebant.

VERS. 5.-- Sic proteget Dominus. Ita fecit Dominus contra Samaritanos, qui impedire conati sunt aedificationem templi. Et in tempore Machabaeorum in paucis multos fudit.

VERS. 6.-- Convertimini. In prophetia, nec temporis, nec historiae ordinem servat, etc., usque ad sic toto affectu mentis redirent.

VERS. 9.-- Ignis est in Sion. Sion, Ecclesia in qua ignis et caminus qui devoret peccatores, scilicet ligna, fenum, stipulamque consumat.

CHAPTER XXXI

VERS. 1.-- Woe to them that go down into Egypt. (ID.) He has treated of the glory of the good and of the punishment of the reprobate; now he returns to the remnant which was left 1275Ain the land of Juda, when Sedecias and the others had been led into Babylon. But first he warned them not to go into Egypt: now, because they have gone, he threatens evil.

They have not trusted in the Holy One of Israel. They sought not with the heart, but with words; for they said to Jeremias: Pray for us to the Lord, what we ought to do? and they refused to obey. To which is like: They have not called upon God. Which was said of wicked priests.

VERS. 2.-- But he that is wise. Whence Jeremias: Cursed be the man that trusteth in man, etc., as far as the place where the Lord was crucified is specially called Sodom and Egypt (Apoc. XI).

VERS. 4.-- For thus saith. (HIER.) This of those who, led into captivity with Sedecias, after seventy years, by the Lord's mercy, returned, when the Israelites and the Egyptians had been slain in 1275BEgypt, in whose help they trusted.

VERS. 5.-- So shall the Lord protect. Thus did the Lord against the Samaritans, who attempted to hinder the building of the temple. And in the time of the Machabees he routed many by a few.

VERS. 6.-- Turn ye again. In prophecy he keeps neither the order of time nor of history, etc., as far as so with the whole affection of the mind they might return.

VERS. 9.-- Fire is in Sion. Sion, the Church, in which is a fire and a furnace to devour sinners, that is, to consume wood, hay and stubble.

CAPUT XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- Ecce in justitia regnabit. (HIER.) Finita prophetia de his qui in Aegyptum descenderant, incipit 1275Cde adventu Christi et apostolorum ejus.

VERS. 2.-- Et erit vir sicut. (ID.) Quasi diceret: Sic erit tutus in tribulationibus, sicut qui ventum et turbinem fugiens tuto se abscondit loco; et qui fontes purissimos invenit in deserto; qui solis ardorem suum prominenti devitat saxo.

VERS. 9.-- Mulieres. (ID.) Matronas alloquitur captivandas a Romanis ut poeniteant; vel, per mulieres Synagogas notat, vel urbes Judaeae. Mulieres opulentae, surgite: Post vocationem gentium, quibus Christus in justitia imperavit, ad mulieres opulentas sermo dirigitur.

VERS. 10.-- Post dies et annum. Unde: Ecce nunc tempus acceptabile, ecce nunc dies salutis (II Cor. VI); et alibi: Annum placabilem Domino (Isa. LXI).

1275DPost dies. Id est praedicationem Christi. Qui enim impoenitentes fuerunt, etsi non illico captivati, tamen illico conturbati, quia noluerunt recipere praedicationem Domini.

VERS. 12.-- Super ubera plangite. Filiae Jerusalem, nolite flere super me, sed super vos ipsas flete, et super filios vestros.

VERS. 14.-- Onagrorum. (ID.) Quorumlibet sylvestrium animalium et avium, quae ibi longo tempore habitaverunt.

VERS. 15.-- Donec effundatur. Spiritus sanctus, praedicante Elia et Enoch, descendat super nos, sicut In Pentecoste super apostolos. Adjungit autem se propheta compatiendo illis.

1276ADesertum. Solitudo scilicet gentium vertetur in divitias Israel, et Israel reputabitur in gentes.

VERS. 17.-- Et cultus justitiae. Ut in silentio oremus, non in plateis, non clamantes, non in multiloquio.

VERS. 18.-- In tabernaculis. (ID.) Jam in mansionibus coeli confidenter habitat, unde: In domo Patris mei mansiones multae sunt (Joan. XIV); vel, in Ecclesiis quae in Domino fiducialiter sperant.

VERS. 19.-- Grando, quasi diceret: Cum populus Christianus in pulchritudine pacis sederit, tunc ira Dei in saltum saeviet.

VERS. 20.-- Beati qui seminatis. (ID.) In expositione Ecclesiastis. Ille magister dignus beatitudine habetur, qui super irriguum pectus seminat audientium, 1276Btam de Judaeis quam de gentibus populo congregato.

CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- Behold, in justice shall he reign. (HIER.) The prophecy concerning those who had gone down into Egypt being finished, he begins 1275Cconcerning the coming of Christ and of his apostles.

VERS. 2.-- And a man shall be as. (ID.) As if he should say: He shall be as safe in tribulations as one who, fleeing the wind and the whirlwind, hides himself in a safe place; and who finds the purest springs in the desert; who avoids the heat of the sun by an overhanging rock.

VERS. 9.-- Women. (ID.) He addresses the matrons who are to be taken captive by the Romans, that they may do penance; or, by the women he marks the Synagogues, or the cities of Judaea. Rise up, ye rich women: After the calling of the nations, over whom Christ has ruled in justice, the discourse is directed to the rich women.

VERS. 10.-- After days and a year. Whence: Behold, now is the acceptable time, behold, now is the day of salvation (II Cor. VI); and elsewhere: The acceptable year of the Lord (Isa. LXI).

1275DAfter days. That is, the preaching of Christ. For those who were impenitent, although not straightway led captive, yet were straightway troubled, because they refused to receive the preaching of the Lord.

VERS. 12.-- Mourn upon the breasts. Daughters of Jerusalem, weep not over me, but weep for yourselves, and for your children. [Vulg. Is 32:12 continues super regione desiderabili, super vinea fertili; the words Migne sets here are Luke 23:28]

VERS. 14.-- Of the wild asses. (ID.) Of any wild beasts and birds whatsoever, which dwelt there for a long time.

VERS. 15.-- Until there be poured out. Let the Holy Spirit, at the preaching of Elias and Enoch, come down upon us, as In Pentecost upon the apostles. And the prophet joins himself to them by suffering with them.

1276AThe desert. The solitude, namely, of the nations shall be turned into the riches of Israel, and Israel shall be reputed among the nations.

VERS. 17.-- And the service of justice. That we may pray in silence, not in the streets, not crying out, not in much speaking.

VERS. 18.-- In tabernacles. (ID.) Already he dwells confidently in the mansions of heaven, whence: In my Father's house there are many mansions (Joan. XIV); or, in the Churches which hope trustingly in the Lord.

VERS. 19.-- Hail, as if he should say: When the Christian people shall have sat in the beauty of peace, then the wrath of God shall rage against the wood.

VERS. 20.-- Blessed are you that sow. (ID.) In the exposition of Ecclesiastes. That master is held worthy of blessedness who sows upon the watered breast of his hearers, 1276Bthe people being gathered together both from the Jews and from the nations.

CAPUT XXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Vae qui praedaris. (ID.) Contra Sennacherib: possunt haec etiam referri ad quemlibet raptorem et ad ipsum diabolum.

VERS. 2.-- Domine, miserere nostri. (ID.) In persona populi obsessi a Sennacherib, vel potius, apostolorum et omnium fidelium.

Mane. Qui de exercitu Sennacherib remanserunt; socios mortuos viderunt et in fugam versi sunt.

VERS. 3.-- Angeli. Michaelis scilicet, qui princeps Judaeorum, vel Gabrielis, fortitudo et dux praeliorum.

VERS. 4.-- Bruchus. Ex locusta bruchus nascitur, etc., usque ad mortuae etiam bruchos gignant.

1276CVERS. 6.-- Tuis. O Ezechia; vel suis secundum alios, scilicet liberati populi.

Ejus. (HIER.) Scilicet salutis: sine timore enim neque sapientia, neque scientia quidquam prodest, unde: Habebis multa bona si timueris Deum (Tob. IV); et: Initium sapientiae timor Domini (Psal. CX).

VERS. 8.-- Pactum. Quod pepigit Dominus cum Abraham, scilicet: Multiplicabo semen tuum sicut stellae, etc. (Gen. XXII); et alibi: Habitabis in loco isto amodo et usque in sempiternum (Zach. XI).

VERS. 9.-- Libanus. Templum: Basan, Regio Og, quam dedit Moyses trans Jordanem duabus tribubus et dimidiae: haec quoque uberrima. Carmelus mons terrae promissionis fertilissimus. Per haec ergo debemus intelligere Judaeos omnem fertilitatem amisisse.

1276DVERS. 14.-- Quis poterit. Dei magnalia narrat et Deum ignem vocat.

VERS. 17.-- Terram. Viventium. Beati mites, quoniam ipsi possidebunt terram, vel in coelo positi, terrena despiciunt.

VERS. 20.-- Tabernaculum quod. (ID.) Tabernaculum quod habuerunt Judaei per eremum, etc., usque ad Ecclesia autem immobilis fundata est supra firmam petram.

VERS. 22.-- Dominus enim. (ID.) Judicabit orbem terrae in justitia.

VERS. 23.-- Laxati sunt funiculi tui, etc. Redit ad terrenam Jerusalem, et loquitur de ea sub specie navis: superiora dicta sunt de urbe solemnitatis 1277Anostrae quam Dominus fluviis suis circumdat.

CHAPTER XXXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Woe to thee that spoilest. (ID.) Against Sennacherib: these things may also be referred to any plunderer and to the devil himself.

VERS. 2.-- O Lord, have mercy on us. (ID.) In the person of the people besieged by Sennacherib, or rather, of the apostles and of all the faithful.

In the morning. Those who were left of the army of Sennacherib; they saw their companions dead and were turned to flight.

VERS. 3.-- Of the angel. Of Michael, namely, who is the prince of the Jews, or of Gabriel, the strength and leader of battles.

VERS. 4.-- The bruchus. From the locust the bruchus is born, etc., as far as the dead also beget bruchi.

1276CVERS. 6.-- In thy times. O Ezechias; or, in his times, according to others, namely of the people set free.

His. (HIER.) Of salvation, namely: for without fear neither wisdom nor knowledge profits anything, whence: Thou shalt have many good things if thou fear God (Tob. IV); [Vulg. Tob. 4:23 multa bona habebimus si timuerimus Deum, first person plural, "we shall have many good things if we fear God"] and: The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom (Psal. CX).

VERS. 8.-- The covenant. Which the Lord made with Abraham, namely: I will multiply thy seed as the stars, etc. (Gen. XXII); [Vulg. Gen. 22:17 sicut stellas caeli, accusative, "as the stars of heaven"] and elsewhere: Thou shalt dwell in this place from henceforth and for ever (Zach. XI).

VERS. 9.-- Libanus. The temple: Basan, the country of Og, which Moses gave beyond the Jordan to the two tribes and a half: this also most fruitful. Carmel, a mountain of the land of promise, most fertile. By these things therefore we ought to understand that the Jews have lost all fruitfulness.

1276DVERS. 14.-- Which of you shall be able. He recounts the mighty works of God and calls God a fire.

VERS. 17.-- The land. Of the living. Blessed are the meek, for they shall possess the land, or, being placed in heaven, they despise earthly things.

VERS. 20.-- A tabernacle that. (ID.) The tabernacle which the Jews had through the desert, etc., as far as but the Church is founded immovable upon the firm rock.

VERS. 22.-- For the Lord. (ID.) He shall judge the world in justice. [Vulg. Ps 95:13 judicabit orbem terrae in aequitate, "shall judge the world in equity"]

VERS. 23.-- Thy tacklings are loosed, etc. He returns to the earthly Jerusalem, and speaks of her under the figure of a ship: the things above were said of the city of our solemnity 1277Awhich the Lord surrounds with his rivers.

CAPUT XXXIV.

VERS. 4.-- Omnis militia coelorum. (HIER.) Sol, et luna, et stellae obscurari videbuntur, clarescente vera luce, quae est Christus. Spiritus quoque mali, amissa potestate, in inferno recludentur.

Et complicabuntur sicut liber. Non scilicet interior, etc., usque ad nihil enim postea scribetur in eis.

VERS. 6.-- In Bosra. (ID.) In civitate mundi contra Deum munita. Bosra enim munita, vel ex Deo; mundus enim voluntate Domini firmatur; unde: Ipse super maria fundavit eum: et: Ego confirmavi columnas ejus. Bosram et Edom, Hebraeorum magistri, Romam volunt intelligi, et omnia de ipsa dici quae sequenti continentur capitulo.

1277BVERS. 8.-- Quia dies ultionis Domini annus retributionis. Post generalem mundi ultionem, ad Jerusalem revertitur, cui primum loquebatur et destructionem ejus plano sermone describit, quae facta est a Romanis.

VERS. 11.-- Ibis. Avis aquatica serpentibus inimica: has aves dicit Josephus a Moyse delatas in bellum contra Aethiopes; quia serpentes abundant in terra illa per quam aditurus erat.

VERS. 12.-- Nobiles ejus. Apostoli enim jam transierant ad gentes: alii fideles admonitione Spiritus sancti, imminente captivitate, transierunt Jordanem in regnum Agrippae qui pacem habebat cum Romanis.

CHAPTER XXXIV.

VERS. 4.-- All the host of the heavens. (HIER.) The sun, and the moon, and the stars shall seem to be darkened, when the true light, which is Christ, grows bright. The evil spirits also, their power lost, shall be shut up in hell.

And they shall be folded together as a book. Not the inner one, namely, etc., as far as for nothing shall afterwards be written in them.

VERS. 6.-- In Bosra. (ID.) In the city of the world fortified against God. For Bosra means fortified, or from God; for the world is made firm by the will of the Lord; whence: He hath founded it upon the seas: and: I have established the pillars thereof. Bosra and Edom the masters of the Hebrews will have to be understood as Rome, and will have all things to be said of her which are contained in the following chapter.

1277BVERS. 8.-- For it is the day of the vengeance of the Lord, the year of retribution. [Vulg. Is 34:8 annus retributionum judicii Sion, "the year of the recompenses of the judgment of Sion"] After the general vengeance upon the world, he returns to Jerusalem, to whom he was speaking first, and describes her destruction in plain speech, which was wrought by the Romans.

VERS. 11.-- The ibis. A water-bird hostile to serpents: Josephus says that these birds were carried by Moses into the war against the Ethiopians; because serpents abound in that land through which he was to go.

VERS. 12.-- Her nobles. For the apostles had already passed over to the nations: other faithful, by the admonition of the Holy Spirit, when the captivity was at hand, passed over the Jordan into the kingdom of Agrippa, who had peace with the Romans.

CAPUT XXXV.

1277CVERS. 1.-- Laetabitur. Exposita captivitate Judaeorum, agit de conversione gentium.

VERS. 2.-- Saron. Populi Judaeorum; unde: Erit Saron sicut desertum (Isa. XXXII).

VERS. 7.-- Viror calami. Impletio Scripturae, quae per calamum; et intentionis adjutorium, quae per juncum: calamus enim et juncus juxta aquas oriuntur et virent.

CHAPTER XXXV.

1277CVERS. 1.-- Shall rejoice. The captivity of the Jews having been set forth, he treats of the conversion of the nations.

VERS. 2.-- Saron. Of the people of the Jews; whence: Saron shall be as a desert (Isa. XXXII). [Vulg. Is 33:9 factus est Saron sicut desertum, past tense, "Saron is become as a desert"]

VERS. 7.-- The greenness of the reed. The fulfilling of Scripture, which is signified by the reed; and the help of the intention, which by the rush: for the reed and the rush spring up and grow green beside the waters.

CAPUT XXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- Et factum est. (HIER.) Libri Regum et Paralipomenorum consentiunt, etc., usque ad Jerusalem obsedit.

VERS. 3.-- Sobna. (ID.) Sobna scriba, alius, non superbus, qui, ut tradunt Haebraei, comminatione Rabsacis perterritus, inferiorem partem civitatis 1277Dtradidit, arce remanente et templo.

VERS. 4.-- Haec dicit. Sicut forsan a patre audierat, haec dicit Dominus: et ut verba ejus habeant majus pondus, notat arrogantiam: Haec dicit rex magnus, quasi contraria fortitudo.

VERS. 6.-- Ecce confidis. (ID.) Mentitur plane. Nusquam enim legitur Ezechiam in Aegypto spem posuisse.

VERS. 8.-- Nec poteris. Non ex imbecillitate, sed praeceptorum Domini observatione, qui per Moysen de rege Israel praecepit, dicens: Non multiplicabis tibi equos, et currus, nec uxores plurimas.

VERS. 16.-- Facite mecum benedictionem, etc. Laudate me, benedicite me. Vel sicut habet alia 1278Aeditio: Facite quod vobis utile est, ut me recipiatis et pax maneat vobiscum.

CHAPTER XXXVI.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass. (HIER.) The books of Kings and of Paralipomenon agree, etc., as far as he besieged Jerusalem.

VERS. 3.-- Sobna. (ID.) Sobna the scribe, another man, not the proud one, who, as the Hebrews hand down, terrified by the threatening of Rabsaces, delivered up the lower part of the city, 1277Dthe citadel and the temple remaining.

VERS. 4.-- Thus saith. As perhaps he had heard from his father, thus saith the Lord: and that his words might have greater weight, he marks the arrogance: Thus saith the great king, as it were the contrary power.

VERS. 6.-- Behold thou trustest. (ID.) He lies plainly. For nowhere is it read that Ezechias put his hope in Egypt.

VERS. 8.-- Neither wilt thou be able. Not from weakness, but from the keeping of the precepts of the Lord, who through Moses commanded concerning the king of Israel, saying: Thou shalt not multiply to thyself horses, and chariots, nor many wives. [Vulg. Deut. 17:16–17 non multiplicabit sibi equos… Non habebit uxores plurimas, third person, and with no mention of chariots]

VERS. 16.-- Make a blessing with me, etc. Praise me, bless me. Or as another 1278Aedition has it: Do what is useful for you, that you may receive me and peace may remain with you.

CAPUT XXXVII.

VERS. 1.-- Et factum, est cum audivisset rex Ezechias, scidit vestimenta sua. (ID.) Scindunt vestes, quia audierant Rabsacen blasphemantem. Scidit rex, credens ex peccatis suis et populi hoc provenisse.

VERS. 2.-- Isaiam filium Amos. In libro Regum legitur, qui misit ad Isaiam prophetam. Sed hic, quia de se Isaias texit historiam, non prophetam, sed filium prophetae se nominat.

VERS. 4.-- Pro reliquiis. Jam enim decem tribus adductae, civitates Judae et Benjamin vastatae: pars etiam inferior civitatis in manus hostium tradita ab impio et superbo pontifice Sobna. Leva oculos, etc.

1278BVERS. 7.-- Ecce ego dabo ei spiritum, etc. Quasi dicat: Non dicam universa quae Assyrio faciam, ne meam videar jactare potentiam, sed hoc unum: Ecce ego dabo.

VERS. 8.-- Et invenit. Pugnasse Sennacherib regem Assyriorum contra Aegyptios, etc., usque ad Chaldaicae scriptores historiae.

VERS. 15.-- Oravit. (HIER.) Jam securior de liberatione. Ante enim ascendit, sed non legitur orasse, quia pro peccatis suis metuebat haec fore.

VERS. 18.-- Vere enim, Domine: multis probatur historiis venisse reges Persarum in Graeciam, et subvertisse et spoliasse templa.

VERS. 21.-- Et misit. Historia libri Regum et Paralipomenorum admiscetur prophetiae, quia est 1278Cprophetia de liberatione urbis, de interitu Assyrii, de reversione solis, et de annis Ezechiae additis.

VERS. 22.-- Filia Jerusalem. Quae prius non responderat, jam certa de ultione, secura de poena.

VERS. 25.-- Et exsiccavi. Tantus fuit exercitus ut fluenta exsiccarent, et ideo puteos aquae inopia facerent.

VERS. 29.-- Superbia. (ID.) Non ultra sustinebo, ut scias te non viribus haec potuisse, sed arbitrio meo. Merebantur enim impiae gentes et infructuosae arbores ut per te, quasi securim succiderentur.

VERS. 30.-- Tibi autem. (ID.) Omnia haec per nuntios Propheta ad Ezechiam loquitur, quid Sennacherib dixerit, quid ei Dominus responderit: nunc ad ipsum verba convertit, ne dubitet ventura quae 1278Dpraedixit. In hoc enim maxime prophetis credebatur, quia non solum longe post futura, sed in proximo complenda testabantur.

Et in anno. Scilicet imminente messe, irruerunt Assyrii, et vastaverunt messes, et vineta, et oliveta: et ob nimiam obsidionem, quod ex granis repertum est, habuerunt in victum primo anno et secundo; quia enim in anno obsidionis serere non potuerunt, quae repererunt ex pomis et frugibus sumpserunt.

VERS. 33.-- Propterea. (ID.) Revertitur ad propositum, et post futurorum spem metum excutit praesentem. Nec enim tanta erit laetitia de his quae promittebat futura, quanta de imminentibus cura

VERS. 35.-- Propter me. Significat, quia non ex 1279Amerito suo, sed Dei clementia conservantur, imo et patris eorum David memoria. In quo admonentur suae negligentiae, et fidei illius, atque justitiae, quam tantum diligit Deus, ut posteros sanctorum merito eorum tueatur.

VERS. 37.-- Et reversus. Ideo reservatus, ut sciat potentiam Dei et blasphemantia comprimat ora, et praedicet majestatem illius quem ante despexerat.

VERS. 38.-- Filii ejus. Invidentes (secundum traditionem Judaeorum) Asaraddon, etc., usque ad reservatus a filiis in templo Dei sui occidendus.

Ararat. (HIER.) Regio Armeniae campestris, per quam fluit Araxes, incredibilis ubertatis, ad radicem Tauri montis, qui illuc usque tenditur. Arca vero Noe non ad montes generaliter Armeniae delata est, 1279Bquae appellata est Ararat, sed ad montes Tauri altissimos, qui imminent campis Ararat.

CHAPTER XXXVII.

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass, when king Ezechias had heard it, he rent his garments. (ID.) They rend their garments, because they had heard Rabsaces blaspheming. The king rent them, believing that this had come about from his own sins and the people's.

VERS. 2.-- Isaias the son of Amos. In the book of Kings it is read, who sent to Isaias the prophet. But here, because Isaias is weaving the history about himself, he names himself not the prophet, but the son of the prophet.

VERS. 4.-- For the remnant. For the ten tribes had already been led away, the cities of Juda and Benjamin laid waste: the lower part of the city also delivered into the hands of the enemy by the wicked and proud pontiff Sobna. Lift up thine eyes, etc.

1278BVERS. 7.-- Behold I will give him a spirit, etc. As if he should say: I will not tell all the things that I shall do to the Assyrian, lest I seem to boast of my own power, but this one thing: Behold I will give.

VERS. 8.-- And he found. That Sennacherib king of the Assyrians fought against the Egyptians, etc., as far as the writers of Chaldean history.

VERS. 15.-- He prayed. (HIER.) Now more secure of deliverance. For before he went up, but he is not read to have prayed, because for his own sins he feared that these things would come to pass.

VERS. 18.-- For truly, O Lord: it is proved by many histories that the kings of the Persians came into Greece, and overthrew and spoiled the temples.

VERS. 21.-- And he sent. The history of the book of Kings and of Paralipomenon is mingled with the prophecy, because there is 1278Ca prophecy concerning the deliverance of the city, concerning the destruction of the Assyrian, concerning the turning back of the sun, and concerning the years added to Ezechias.

VERS. 22.-- The daughter of Jerusalem. She who before had not answered, is now sure of the vengeance, secure of the punishment.

VERS. 25.-- And I have dried up. So great was the army that they dried up the streams, and therefore made wells by the scarcity of water.

VERS. 29.-- Pride. (ID.) I will bear it no longer, that thou mayest know that thou couldst not do these things by thy own strength, but by my will. For the wicked nations and the unfruitful trees deserved to be cut down by thee, as by an axe.

VERS. 30.-- But to thee. (ID.) All these things the Prophet speaks to Ezechias by messengers, what Sennacherib said, what the Lord answered him: now he turns his words to him himself, lest he doubt of the things to come which he 1278Dforetold. For in this above all were the prophets believed, because they testified not only of things far off to come, but of things to be fulfilled close at hand.

And in the year. That is, when the harvest was at hand, the Assyrians rushed in, and laid waste the harvests, and the vineyards, and the olive groves: and because of the length of the siege, what was found from the grains they had for food in the first year and the second; for since in the year of the siege they could not sow, they took what they found from the fruits and the crops.

VERS. 33.-- Therefore. (ID.) He returns to his purpose, and after the hope of things to come he shakes off the present fear. For the gladness over those things which he was promising as future will not be so great as the care over the things at hand

VERS. 35.-- For my own sake. He signifies that they are preserved not by 1279Atheir own merit, but by God's clemency, nay rather by the memory of David their father. Wherein they are admonished of their own negligence, and of his faith and justice, which God so loves that he protects the posterity of the saints for the merit of the saints.

VERS. 37.-- And he returned. Therefore he was spared, that he might know the power of God and check the blaspheming mouths, and might proclaim the majesty of him whom before he had despised.

VERS. 38.-- His sons. Envying (according to the tradition of the Jews) Asaraddon, etc., as far as spared by his sons to be slain in the temple of his own god.

Ararat. (HIER.) A level region of Armenia, through which flows the Araxes, of incredible fruitfulness, at the foot of Mount Taurus, which stretches as far as there. But the ark of Noe was not carried to the mountains of Armenia generally, 1279Bwhich is called Ararat, but to the highest mountains of Taurus, which overhang the plains of Ararat.

CAPUT XXXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- In diebus illis aegrotavit. (HIER.) Quia de victoria dignas Deo grates non retulit, sed in superbia evanuit, sicut Regum et Paralipomenorum liber aperit; unde: Quem Deus diligit, corripit, ne elevetur in superbiam post incredibiles triumphos.

Morieris tu. Cui simile: Ninive subvertetur: et multa sub comminatione praedicta, nec impleta; non Deo mutante sententiam, sed provocante nos ad sui notitiam. Dominus enim patiens est super malitia.

VERS. 2.-- Ad parietem. (ID.) Templi, scilicet juxta quod Salomon exstruxit palatium.

VERS. 3.-- Obsecro, Domine. Iturus ad Dominum, 1279Cnarrat opera sua. Felix conscientia, quae in tempore malorum potest recordari bonorum.

Obsecro, Domine. Audiens se moriturum, non annos plurimos petit, sed Dei judicio dimittit quid praestare velit: noverat Salomonem Deo placuisse, quia non petierat ampliores annos vitae.

Et quod bonum. Idola patris sui destruxit, ostia domus Domini, quae ille clauserat, aperuit, serpentem aeneum quem in Gabaon Judaei adorabant comminuit, et in ventum sparsit.

Et flevit. Flevit quia moriebatur sine filiis, etc., usque ad et post mortem plurimos resuscitatos legamus.

VERS. 4.-- Et factum est Verbum Domini. Praepostero ordine hic refertur historia, quae in Regum 1279Dvolumine consequentius legitur.

VERS. 5.-- Ecce ego adjiciam super dies. Secundum quod visum est, etc., usque ad et de annis Ezechiae additis.

VERS. 7.-- Hoc autem tibi erit signum a Domino, quia fecit Dominus verbum hoc quod locutus. (ID.) Praesentis scilicet temporis et futuri, ut quomodo sol revertitur ad exordium sui, ita Ezechiae vita ad evolutos redeat annos. Nobis quoque in hebdoade et ogdoade viventibus post resurrectionem Christi vitae spatia protelentur.

VERS. 10.-- Ego dixi. (ID.) Cum Deus Ezechiae sine labore victoriam dedisset, etc., usque ad peccatores in dimidio dierum moriuntur.

In dimidio dierum meorum. Unde: Viri impii et 1280Adolosi non dimidiabunt dies suos, quia non implent opera virtutum, nec poenitendo emendant delicta.

Ad portas inferi. Quaesivi. Ubi constituta est domus omni viventi ante adventum Christi.

VERS. 11.-- Quietis. Quidam codices habent: Quievit generatio mea, quod et Hieronymus aliquando transtulit, deceptus ambiguitate verbi, et postea correxit.

VERS. 15.-- Respondebit mihi. (ID.) Quod erravi emendabo, tu esto fidejussor et protector: Non est enim volentis neque currentis, sed Dei miserentis (Rom. IX).

Cum ipse. Haec mihi juste, quasi diceret: nihil restat nisi patienter sustinere et ipsum fortem exorare qui fecit quod voluit.

1280BRecogitabo. Quod praeteritum est non adjuvat sustinentem; unde stulta est Epicuri sententia, qui asserit recordatione praeteritorum bonorum mala praesentia mitigari.

In amaritudine animae, quasi diceret: et hi etiam in amaritudine, quia praesens tribulatio nihil boni praeteriti sentit, et semper humana felicitas aliquibus aspergitur aversis.

VERS. 18.-- Quia non infernus confitebitur. Sine fine damnati neque in inferno poenitentiam agunt, neque laudes Deo referunt.

VERS. 19.-- Vivens vivens, etc. Non infernus velmors.

Pater filiis. Unde: interroga patrem tuum, et annuntiabit, etc.

VERS. 21.-- Super vulnus ejus. Aiunt Hebraei 1280CSehin (שחין) ulcus sonare, non vulnus, etc., usque ad cum Deus fecerit eam.

CHAPTER XXXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- In those days he was sick. (HIER.) Because he did not render worthy thanks to God for the victory, but vanished away in pride, as the book of Kings and of Paralipomenon discloses; whence: Whom God loves, he chastises, lest he be lifted up into pride after incredible triumphs.

Thou shalt die. To which is like: Ninive shall be overthrown: and many things foretold under a threat, and not fulfilled; God not changing his sentence, but provoking us to the knowledge of himself. For the Lord is patient over wickedness.

VERS. 2.-- To the wall. (ID.) Of the temple, namely, beside which Solomon built his palace.

VERS. 3.-- I beseech thee, O Lord. Being about to go to the Lord, 1279Che recounts his works. Happy the conscience which in the time of evils can remember good things.

I beseech thee, O Lord. Hearing that he was to die, he does not ask for many years, but leaves it to God's judgment what he may be pleased to grant: he knew that Solomon had pleased God, because he had not asked for a greater number of years of life.

And what is good. He destroyed the idols of his father, he opened the doors of the house of the Lord which the other had shut, he broke to pieces the brazen serpent which the Jews worshipped in Gabaon, and scattered it to the wind.

And he wept. He wept because he was dying without children, etc., as far as and that we read of very many raised up after death.

VERS. 4.-- And the word of the Lord came. In a reversed order the history is here related, which in the volume of Kings 1279Dis read in better sequence.

VERS. 5.-- Behold I will add to thy days. According to what was seen, etc., as far as and concerning the years added to Ezechias.

VERS. 7.-- And this shall be a sign to thee from the Lord, that the Lord hath done this word which he hath spoken. [Vulg. Is 38:7 faciet, future, "that the Lord will do"] (ID.) Of the present time, namely, and of the future, that as the sun turns back to its own beginning, so the life of Ezechias may return to the years that have run out. To us also, living in the seventh and the eighth age, may the spaces of life be prolonged after the resurrection of Christ.

VERS. 10.-- I said. (ID.) When God had given Ezechias the victory without toil, etc., as far as sinners die in the midst of their days.

In the midst of my days. Whence: Wicked and deceitful men shall not 1280Alive out half their days, because they do not fulfil the works of the virtues, nor amend their faults by doing penance.

To the gates of hell. I sought. Where the house appointed for every living man was set before the coming of Christ.

VERS. 11.-- Of quiet. Some copies have: My generation is at rest, which Jerome also once translated, deceived by the ambiguity of the word, and afterwards corrected.

VERS. 15.-- He shall answer me. (ID.) What I have done amiss I shall amend, be thou my surety and protector: For it is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that sheweth mercy (Rom. IX).

When he himself. These things justly to me, as if he should say: nothing remains but patiently to endure and to entreat him who is strong, who did what he willed.

1280BI will think over. What is past does not help him that endures; whence the saying of Epicurus is foolish, who asserts that present evils are lessened by the remembrance of past goods.

In the bitterness of the soul, as if he should say: and these too in bitterness, because a present tribulation feels nothing of past good, and human happiness is always sprinkled with some adversities.

VERS. 18.-- For hell shall not confess. The damned without end neither do penance in hell, nor render praises to God.

VERS. 19.-- The living, the living, etc. Not hell nor death velmors.

The father to the children. Whence: ask thy father, and he will declare, etc.

VERS. 21.-- Upon his wound. The Hebrews say 1280Cthat Sehin (שחין) sounds an ulcer, not a wound, etc., as far as when God shall have made it.

CAPUT XXXIX.

VERS. 1.-- In tempore illo. (HIER.) Superius dictum est, quia Asaraddon Sennacherib successit, etc., usque ad et amisso virili robore, in muliebrem redigitur mollitiem.

VERS. 2.-- Et in omni potestate sua. (ID.) In thesauris domus Domini, in quo praecipue offendit. Hoc enim non erat potestatis ejus. Jam tamen de valvis illis templi auri laminas tulerat.

In thesauris ejus. Tacet de thesauris Domini, metuens reprehendi, quod scilicet ostendit eis quodcunque haberet in potestate, templi, scilicet supellectilem; 1280Dunde:

VERS. 7.-- Et erunt eunuchi. (ID.) Tradunt Hebraei hoc impletum esse in Daniel et ejus sociis, qui, capto Joachim filio Josue, cum suo populo sunt capti et in Babylonem ducti et eunuchizati, vel evirati; non tamen putandum quod eunuchi fuerint corpore, sed mente, sicut qui se castraverunt propter regnum coelorum (Matth. XIX).

VERS. 8.-- Fiat tantum pax. Debuit peccatum suum cum David confiteri, et iram Dei removere a posteris, aut compati cum Moyse, qui dicit: Dimitte eis hanc noxam, aut dele me de libro quem scripsisti (Exod. XXXII). Quod quia non fecit, propterea non respondet ei; sed, conversus ad populum, dicit: Consolamini, etc.

CHAPTER XXXIX.

VERS. 1.-- At that time. (HIER.) It has been said above that Asaraddon succeeded Sennacherib, etc., as far as and, his manly strength being lost, he is reduced to a womanish softness.

VERS. 2.-- And in all his power. (ID.) In the treasures of the house of the Lord, in which he chiefly offended. For this was not of his power. Yet already he had taken the plates of gold from those doors of the temple.

In his treasures. He is silent about the treasures of the Lord, fearing to be blamed, because, namely, he showed them whatsoever he had in his power, that is, the furniture of the temple; 1280Dwhence:

VERS. 7.-- And they shall be eunuchs. (ID.) The Hebrews hand down that this was fulfilled in Daniel and his companions, who, when Joachim the son of Josue had been taken, were taken with their people and led into Babylon and made eunuchs, or emasculated; yet it is not to be thought that they were eunuchs in body, but in mind, like those who have made themselves eunuchs for the kingdom of heaven (Matth. XIX).

VERS. 8.-- Let there be peace only. He ought to have confessed his sin with David, and turned away the wrath of God from his posterity, or to have suffered with Moses, who says: Forgive them this trespass, or blot me out of the book which thou hast written (Exod. XXXII). Because he did not do this, therefore he does not answer him; but, turning to the people, he says: Be comforted, etc.

CAPUT XL.

  • 1281A

VERS. 1.-- Consolamini. Quasi dicat: Quia rex vester non compatitur vobis, consolamini Christo. Potest ad gentes referri, de quibus Dominus per Zachariam: Ego congregabo populum orientis, et erunt mihi in populum, et ego ero illis in Deum (Zach. VIII).

Loquimini ad cor. (HIER.) Ad cor loquitur qui moestum consolatur, sicut de Sichen dicitur: Locutus est Sichen ad cor ejus, et consolatus est eam (Gen. XXIV).

Jerusalem. Jerusalem a Chaldaeis et Romanis corpore et anima; Ecclesia a persecutoribus corpore cruciata, animo angustiata.

Malitia ejus. Malitia afflictio sicut illud: Si est malum in civitate quod non faciat Dominus (Amos. III).

Dimissa est. Causa consolationis, remissio peccatorum, 1281Betc., usque ad quia purgata sunt et soluta per poenas.

VERS. 3.-- Vox clamantis. (ID.) Hujus vocis memores, Judaei miserunt ad Joannem ut interrogarent eum: Tu quis es?

VERS. 4.-- Omnis vallis exaltabitur. Qui se exaltat, humiliabitur, et qui se humiliat, etc. Hoc usque hodie in deserto gentilium Ecclesiarum clamant magistri.

VERS. 5.-- Et videbit omnis caro. De primo adventu subito ad secundum raptus dicit: Et videbit omnis caro.

Os Domini. Prophetae qui praedicaverunt futurum Christum, scilicet salutare Dei nostri.

VERS. 6.-- Quid clamabo? (ID.) Non sine causa quaerit: cum enim se sponte obtulit, dicens: Ecce 1281Cego, mitte me: populo suo dura nuntiavit, et nunc idem metuit.

Omnis caro. Qui portat imaginem terreni, etc., usque ad sic homo, per aetatum successiones, amittit decorem et cadit in mortem.

VERS. 8.-- Verbum autem Domini. Verbum Christus cum corpore suo, quod est Ecclesia.

VERS. 9.-- Sion. (ID.) Per Sion et Jerusalem, apostolos et alios doctores intelligit, quos admonet ut quae a Domino acceperunt praedicent, et culmina virtutum ascendentes, verbo et exemplo doceant.

VERS. 11.-- Gregem suum, etc. Nunc contemplatione, in futuro ipsa visione: nunc docendo, postea salvando vel satiando.

Fetas. Doctores filios quotidie Deo parturientes.

1281DVERS. 12.-- Quis mensus est? (ID.) Ne de vocatione gentium dubitetur, magnitudo vocantis describitur.

Pugillo. Pugillus, contractis digitis pugnus, palmus a pollice usque ad minimum digitum extenta manus, ut per palmum et pugillum coelos extentos et globum terrae noverimus.

VERS. 13.-- Spiritum Domini super me, eo quod unxerit me ad annuntiandum mansuetis misit me: Et requiescet super eum Spiritus Domini.

VERS. 15.-- Quasi pulvis exiguus. (ID.) Hebraei hoc verbo tenuissimum pulverem significari putant, etc., usque ad et tam Graecae quam Latinae linguae paupertatem.

VERS. 16.-- Libanus non sufficiet. Libanus mons 1282Amaximus et pascuis uberrimus et arboribus; sed etsi omnia ligna succidantur ad succendendum, et omnia animalia immolentur ad holocaustum, ad implendam vel placandam tamen majestatis potentiam non sufficiunt.

VERS. 19.-- Nunquid. Stultitiam gentium redarguit, ut ab idololatria recedant.

VERS. 20.-- Forte lignum. (ID.) Hebraice mesuchan (מסכן) , quia genus ligni est imputribile, de quo maxime idola fiunt: hoc autem dicit ut, idolis reprobatis, suscipiatur doctrina Evangelii et reveletur gloria Domini, ut videat omnis caro salutare Dei.

VERS. 21.-- Ab initio. Legis Moysi, vel a principio mundi in ipsis creaturis quae omnes suum suo modo praedicant artificem.

1282BVERS. 22.-- Super gyrum. Hinc nonnulli terram quasi punctum et globum esse contendunt.

Quasi locustae. In ejus comparatione, vel considerata terrae magnitudine.

Tabernaculum. Hinc quoque in similitudinem sphaerae coelum esse contendunt, et ejus hemisphaerium terris imminere.

VERS. 23.-- Qui dat secretorum. Stultam fecit Deus sapientiam hujus mundi, et hoc est: Qui dat secretorum scrutatores, quasi non sint (I Cor. I).

VERS. 25.-- Cui assimilastis me, adaequastis me, dicit Sanctus. Quasi diceret: Quare similitudini Deum comparatis, nec ex creaturarum magnitudinem Deum creatorem intelligitis? si non creditis verbis, credite oculis vestris, et elementorum omnium 1282Cservitute omnipotentiam Domini cogitate.

VERS. 26.-- In numero. (ID.) Illi numerata sunt quae Abraham numerare non potuit: et serviunt delegato officio, ut sol cursum coeli uno expleat anno, et caetera suo modo.

Militiam eorum, etc. Angelos scilicet, unde: Millia millium ministrabant ei (Dan. VII). Inde et Dominus Sabaoth dicitur, id est militiae, vel exercituum, vel virtutum.

VERS. 27.-- Quare dicis, etc. (ID.) Cum haec propheta diceret, etc., usque ad sine judice sumus, et immerito affligimur.

Via mea. Bona opera, non pertinent ad Deum terrena, non considerat quid quisque agat. Inde injuste opprimitur, quasi jumenta et pisces, sine rectore.

1282DVERS. 28.-- Creavit. Omnia novit, omnia continet, omnia majestate sua dispensat.

Sapientiae ejus. (ID.) Ex hoc loco aliud apostoli sumptum videtur: Incomprehensibilia judicia ejus, et investigabiles viae ejus: quasi diceret: Solus Deus aeternus, impassibilis, caetera omnia deficient.

VERS. 30.-- Deficient pueri. Qui scilicet gaudent corporis sanitate, et pueritiam et adolescentiam putant esse perpetuam.

VERS. 31.-- Mutabunt. Ut sint fortes in Deo, fient debiles mundo, et post mortem carnis fient immortales.

Sicut aquilae. Sicut aquila secundum physicos, alliso ad silicem rostro, vetustate nimia aduncato 1283Ainnovatur, sic qui vetustatem peccati per petram, quae est Christus, mortificant, mortui mundo innovantur Deo, unde: Renovabitur ut aquilae juventus tua (Psal. CII).

CHAPTER XL.

  • 1281A

VERS. 1.-- Be comforted. As if he should say: Because your king has no compassion upon you, be comforted in Christ. It can be referred to the Gentiles, of whom the Lord says through Zechariah: I will gather together the people of the east, and they shall be to me for a people, and I will be to them for a God (Zach. VIII) [Vulg. Zach. 8:7 has salvabo populum meum de terra orientis, "I will save my people out of the land of the east"].

Speak ye to the heart. (HIER.) He speaks to the heart who comforts one that is sorrowful, as it is said of Sichem: Sichem spoke to her heart, and comforted her (Gen. XXIV) [Vulg. Gen. 34:3 tristemque delinivit blanditiis, "and soothed her sadness with flatteries"; the quoted words stand nowhere in the Vulgate].

Jerusalem. Jerusalem by the Chaldeans and by the Romans, in body and in soul; the Church tormented in body by persecutors, straitened in mind.

Her malice. Malice is affliction, as in that place: If there is evil in a city which the Lord does not do (Amos. III).

It is forgiven. The cause of consolation, the remission of sins, 1281Betc., as far as because they are purged and loosed by punishments.

VERS. 3.-- The voice of one crying. (ID.) Mindful of this voice, the Jews sent to John to ask him: Who art thou?

VERS. 4.-- Every valley shall be exalted. He that exalts himself shall be humbled, and he that humbles himself, etc. This the masters cry even to this day in the desert of the Churches of the Gentiles.

VERS. 5.-- And all flesh shall see. From the first coming, caught up suddenly to the second, he says: And all flesh shall see.

The mouth of the Lord. The prophets who preached the Christ to come, namely the salvation of our God.

VERS. 6.-- What shall I cry? (ID.) Not without cause does he ask: for when he had offered himself of his own accord, saying: Behold 1281CI, send me: he announced hard things to his own people, and now he fears the same.

All flesh. He who bears the image of the earthly, etc., as far as so man, through the successions of his ages, loses his comeliness and falls into death.

VERS. 8.-- But the word of the Lord. The Word is Christ with his body, which is the Church.

VERS. 9.-- Sion. (ID.) By Sion and Jerusalem he understands the apostles and the other teachers, whom he admonishes to preach the things they have received from the Lord, and, ascending the summits of the virtues, to teach by word and by example.

VERS. 11.-- His flock, etc. Now by contemplation, in the time to come by vision itself: now by teaching, afterward by saving or by satisfying.

Them that are with young. Teachers daily bringing forth sons to God.

1281DVERS. 12.-- Who has measured? (ID.) Lest there be any doubt about the calling of the Gentiles, the greatness of him who calls is described.

With his fist. A pugillus is the fist with the fingers drawn in; a palmus is the hand stretched out from the thumb to the least finger, so that by the palm and by the fist we may know the heavens stretched out and the globe of the earth.

VERS. 13.-- The spirit of the Lord upon me, in that he has anointed me: to preach to the meek he has sent me: And the spirit of the Lord shall rest upon him. [Vulg. Is. 61:1 names the agent, eo quod unxerit Dominus me, "because the Lord hath anointed me"; the closing clause is Is. 11:2]

VERS. 15.-- As a little dust. (ID.) The Hebrews think that by this word the finest dust is meant, etc., as far as and the poverty both of the Greek and of the Latin tongue.

VERS. 16.-- Libanus shall not suffice. Libanus is a very great mountain, 1282Amost rich in pastures and in trees; but although all its trees be cut down for the burning, and all its beasts be sacrificed for a holocaust, yet for filling or for appeasing the power of his majesty they do not suffice.

VERS. 19.-- Surely not. He refutes the folly of the Gentiles, that they may withdraw from idolatry.

VERS. 20.-- Strong wood. (ID.) In Hebrew mesuchan (מסכן), because it is a kind of wood that cannot rot, of which idols are chiefly made: and he says this so that, the idols being rejected, the teaching of the Gospel may be received and the glory of the Lord revealed, that all flesh may see the salvation of God.

VERS. 21.-- From the beginning. Of the law of Moses, or from the beginning of the world in the very creatures, which all in their own manner proclaim their maker.

1282BVERS. 22.-- Upon the circle. Hence some contend that the earth is as it were a point and a globe.

As locusts. In comparison with him, or considering the greatness of the earth.

A tabernacle. Hence also they contend that heaven is after the likeness of a sphere, and that its hemisphere overhangs the lands.

VERS. 23.-- Who brings forth the searchers of secrets. God hath made foolish the wisdom of this world, and this is: Who brings forth the searchers of secrets, as though they were not (I Cor. I).

VERS. 25.-- To whom have you likened me, made me equal, says the Holy One. As if he should say: Why do you compare God to a likeness, and do not from the greatness of creatures understand God the creator? If you do not believe words, believe your own eyes, and from the 1282Cservitude of all the elements consider the omnipotence of the Lord.

VERS. 26.-- In number. (ID.) To him are numbered the things which Abraham could not number: and they serve in an assigned office, as the sun fulfils the course of heaven in one year, and the rest in their own manner.

Their host, etc. The angels, namely; whence: Thousands of thousands ministered to him (Dan. VII). Hence too he is called Lord Sabaoth, that is, of the host, or of the armies, or of the powers.

VERS. 27.-- Why sayest thou, etc. (ID.) When the prophet said these things, etc., as far as we are without a judge, and are afflicted undeservedly.

My way. Good works: earthly things do not pertain to God, he does not consider what each man does. Hence a man is unjustly oppressed, like beasts of burden and fishes, without one to rule them.

1282DVERS. 28.-- He created. He knows all things, contains all things, dispenses all things by his majesty.

Of his wisdom. (ID.) From this place another saying of the Apostle seems to be taken: Incomprehensible are his judgments, and his ways unsearchable: as if he should say: God alone is eternal and impassible; all other things shall fail.

VERS. 30.-- The children shall faint. Those, namely, who rejoice in the health of the body, and think that childhood and youth are perpetual.

VERS. 31.-- They shall change. That they may be strong in God, they shall become weak to the world, and after the death of the flesh they shall become immortal.

As eagles. As the eagle, according to the natural philosophers, when its beak, hooked by excessive age, has been dashed against a flint, 1283Ais made new, so they who mortify the oldness of sin by the rock, which is Christ, being dead to the world are made new to God; whence: Thy youth shall be renewed like the eagle's (Psal. CII).

CAPUT XLI.

VERS. 1.-- Taceant insulae. (HIER.) Quia in salo gentes, scilicet quae salsis et amaris fluctibus saeculi tunduntur.I

VERS. 2.-- Quis suscitavit ab oriente. (ID.) Quidam Hebraeorum hoc ad Cyrum regem Persarum referunt, etc., usque ad qui ab initio cognovit haec.

VERS. 3.-- Semita in pedibus. Laborem vel lassitudinem ejus vitae non sentiet: Exsultavit ut gigas ad currendam viam.

VERS. 5.-- Viderunt insulae. (ID.) Id est gentes in 1283Bcircumitu, etc., usque ad et confortavit eum clavis praeceptorum, ut in timore Domini permaneret.

VERS. 8.-- Et tu, Israel, serve meus. (ID.) Dominus ad apostolos affectuose, etc., usque ad postea electi in amicitiam Dei jungimur.

VERS. 9.-- Et a longinquis. Jam usque ad extrema Judaei dispersi, et inde ad finem multi vocati.

VERS. 14.-- Vermis Jacob. (ID.) Vermes dicuntur apostoli, tanquam ejus imitatores qui dicit: Ego sum vermis et non homo (Psal. XXI). Et sicut vermis penetrat terram, sic praedicatio eorum corda audientium.

Qui mortui estis ex Israel. Sicut ille qui dicit: Quotidie morior propter vestram gloriam, fratres (I Cor. XV); et alibi: Vivo autem jam non ego; vivit 1283Cvero in me Christus (Gal. II).

VERS. 15.-- Ego posui te quasi plaustrum. Vir ecclesiasticus habet praedicationis rostra, etc., usque ad ut male congregati bene dividantur.

VERS. 19.-- Dabo in solitudinem cedrum. (ID.) Arborum varietates diversitatem spiritualis gratiae significant, ne saltem una chorda de cithara Domini, et aliqua virtus gratiarum Ecclesiae deesse videatur: quarum sublimitatem ad coelestia festinantem illuminat lignum olivae, fomes lucis, requies laborantis vel lassati.

Spinam, et myrtum, et lignum olivae. LXX: Sethim, quae est arbor albae spinae similis, etc., usque ad et ideo consolatores sanctae Ecclesiae significat.

VERS. 22.-- Annuntiate. (ID.) Significat quia 1283Didola nec audiendi nec respondendi habent facultatem, etc., usque ad patet quod idola post adventum Christi vocem amiserunt.

VERS. 23.-- Si potestis. Neque daemones possunt, nisi Dei permissione, quod assident idolis, quia nec porcos, nec sancti Job jumenta potuerunt disperdere sine Dei permissione. Paulus quoque tradidit Satanae quosdam.

VERS. 25.-- Suscitavi. (ID.) Adhuc contra idola loquitur et contra cultores eorum, etc., usque ad ut credant in eum qui venit ab ortu solis.

VERS. 26.-- Quis annuntiavit ab exordio? quasi diceret: Nullus in gentibus fuit qui annuntiaverit adventum Filii. Si est autem qui annuntiaverit, dicemus, 1284Aquia justus est; in adventu Filii, daemones, qui in idolis ventura nuntiabant, obmutuerant.

Audiens sermones vestros. Quia bruta gentilium corda errorem suum intellexerunt, et vaticinia falsa reliquerunt.

VERS. 27.-- Primus ad Sion dicet. Ecclesiam significant; ecce astiterunt filii, scilicet tui, quos per fidem meam donavi tibi, scilicet apostoli, quorum praedicatione nati sunt tibi filii.

VERS. 29.-- Ecce omnes injusti. Quidquid de idolis vel de gentibus diximus, ad haeresiarchas et pravitatis dogmata referamus, quia, Christo pacem Ecclesiae nuntiante et veritatem monstrante, ipsi errores sequuntur, et vento et turbini comparantur.

CHAPTER XLI.

VERS. 1.-- Let the islands be silent. (HIER.) Because the Gentiles are in the salt sea, namely those who are beaten by the salt and bitter waves of the world.

VERS. 2.-- Who has raised up from the east. (ID.) Certain of the Hebrews refer this to Cyrus king of the Persians, etc., as far as who from the beginning knew these things.

VERS. 3.-- A path under his feet. He shall not feel the labor or the weariness of that life: He rejoiced as a giant to run the way.

VERS. 5.-- The islands saw. (ID.) That is, the nations round 1283Babout, etc., as far as and he strengthened him with the nails of the commandments, that he might abide in the fear of the Lord.

VERS. 8.-- And thou, Israel, my servant. (ID.) The Lord speaks lovingly to the apostles, etc., as far as afterward, being chosen, we are joined into the friendship of God.

VERS. 9.-- And from afar. Already the Jews are scattered even to the ends of the earth, and thence unto the end many are called.

VERS. 14.-- Worm of Jacob. (ID.) The apostles are called worms, as imitators of him who says: I am a worm and no man (Psal. XXI). And as a worm penetrates the earth, so does their preaching the hearts of those who hear.

You that are dead of Israel. Like him who says: I die daily for your glory, brethren (I Cor. XV) [Vulg. 1 Cor. 15:31 per vestram gloriam, "by your glory"]; and elsewhere: And I live, now not I; but Christ lives 1283Cin me (Gal. II).

VERS. 15.-- I have made thee as a threshing wain. The churchman has the teeth of preaching, etc., as far as that those ill gathered together may be well divided.

VERS. 19.-- I will give in the wilderness the cedar. (ID.) The varieties of the trees signify the diversity of spiritual grace, lest even one string be wanting from the harp of the Lord, and any virtue of the graces seem to be lacking to the Church: and the loftiness of these, as it hastens to heavenly things, the olive tree enlightens, the fuel of light, the rest of him who labors or is wearied.

The thorn, and the myrtle, and the olive tree. The Seventy: Sethim, which is a tree like the white thorn, etc., as far as and therefore it signifies the comforters of holy Church.

VERS. 22.-- Declare ye. (ID.) He signifies that 1283Dthe idols have the power neither of hearing nor of answering, etc., as far as it is plain that the idols lost their voice after the coming of Christ.

VERS. 23.-- If you can. Neither can the demons, save by the permission of God, in that they attend upon the idols; for they could destroy neither the swine nor holy Job's beasts without the permission of God. Paul too delivered certain men to Satan.

VERS. 25.-- I have raised up. (ID.) He still speaks against the idols and against their worshippers, etc., as far as that they may believe in him who came from the rising of the sun.

VERS. 26.-- Who has declared from the beginning? as if he should say: There was none among the Gentiles who declared the coming of the Son. But if there is one who has declared it, we shall say 1284Athat he is just; at the coming of the Son, the demons, who in the idols announced things to come, had fallen dumb.

Hearing your words. Because the brute hearts of the Gentiles understood their own error, and forsook the false oracles.

VERS. 27.-- The first shall say to Sion. They signify the Church; behold, sons have stood by, namely thine, whom I have given to thee by my faith, namely the apostles, by whose preaching sons have been born to thee.

VERS. 29.-- Behold, all are unjust. Whatever we have said of the idols or of the Gentiles, let us refer to the heresiarchs and to the doctrines of depravity; because, while Christ announces peace to the Church and shows the truth, they themselves follow errors, and are compared to the wind and to the whirlwind.

CAPUT XLII.

1284BVERS. 1.-- Ecce servus. (HIER.) Notandum quia apostoli Jacob, Israel, et semen Abrahae appellantur, quibus postea quasi servis et hominibus dicitur: Noli timere, vermis Jacob, etc. Christus autem non semen Jacob vel Israel, sed servus dicitur.

Dedi Spiritum. In conceptione; unde: Spiritus sanctus superveniet in te, etc. (Phil. II). Et in baptismo claruit, cum Spiritus in specie columbae super ipsum requievit (Luc. I).

VERS. 3.-- Linum fumigans. Sacerdotium jam pene exstinctum, de lino autem fit sacerdotale vestimentum.

Educet judicium. Judicabit, scilicet non metuens Scribas et Pharisaeos.

VERS. 4.-- Non erit tristis. (ID.) Hoc Matthaeus 1284Cnon posuit, sive inter judicium et judicium media scriptoris errore sublata sunt. Est autem sensus, aequalitatem vultus omni tempore conservabit: Quod de Socrate falso gloriantur philosophi, quod scilicet plus solito non tristis fuerit, nec laetus.

In terra judicium. Unde: In judicium ego veni in hunc mundum, ut qui non vident videant, etc. (Joan. IX).

VERS. 6.-- Justitia. Justum fuit ut transiret per apostolos ad gentes contemptus a Judaeis.

VERS. 7.-- Vinctum. Funibus peccatorum: in Evangelio, quem Satanas vinxerat Dominus solvit.

VERS. 8.-- Alteri. Filius et Spiritus sanctus in Deitate a Patre alter non est, et ideo eamdem et gloriam 1284Det laudem habet.

1284DVERS. 9.-- Vobis faciam. Ut sciatis, quia ego Dominus, non est alius praeter me: et qui priora cernitis impleta, credatis quae polliceor futura.

VERS. 10.-- Novum. In novitate Spiritus, non in vestustate litterae; nec solum in Veteri Testamento, sed etiam in Novo.

Qui descenditis in mare. (ID.). Prius piscatores piscium, post hominum; unde ad litteram: multa maria transierunt.

VERS. 11.-- Cedar. Filius Ismaelis: Saraceni ab eo, qui melius Agareni: sed nomine liberae velaverunt ignominiam ancillae. Ismael enim filius ancillae Agar fuit, ab hac Saraceni. Duodecim enim duces genuit. Nomine quoque ejus ipsa dicitur provincia.

1285ADe vertice montium. (HIER.) Super montem excelsum ascende. De vertice montis. De altitudine virtutis et contemplationis, ut etiam exemplo doceant quae praedicant.

VERS. 13.-- Vociferabitur. Unde: Ipsi me provocaverunt in eo qui non erat Deus (Deut. XXXII); et ego in gente stulta irritabo eos.

VERS. 14.-- Sicut parturiens. Partum consilii mei profundam in lucem sicut mulier infantem, sic proferam dissimulatum dolorem.

VERS. 16.-- In semitis. (ID.) In cognitione prophetarum, qui sunt semitae ad Christum, cum ejus nativitatem, resurrectionem et ascensionem praedicunt.

VERS. 17.-- Confundantur. Cum plenitudo gentium intraverit. Tunc etiam Israel conversus, erroris sui 1285Bpoenitebit, qui idolis ante servivit.

VERS. 22.-- Laqueus juvenum. Revertitur ad praedicationem Domini, increpans Scribas et Pharisaeos: Hi enim erant laquei simplicibus ne ad Dominum converterentur.

VERS. 23.-- Quis est. Hortatur eos propheta, ut saltem aliqui audiant et causam suae captivitatis intelligant et caveant.

VERS. 25.-- Super eum. Ut quod prius tacuerat, jam non taceret nec verbis, sed tormentis corriperet.

Et non cognovit. Sicut historiae tradunt, nec gentes hoc fieri in ultionem filii intellexerunt, nec Judaei malitia sua excaecati.

CHAPTER XLII.

1284BVERS. 1.-- Behold my servant. (HIER.) It is to be noted that the apostles are called Jacob, Israel, and the seed of Abraham, to whom afterward, as to servants and men, it is said: Fear not, worm of Jacob, etc. But Christ is called not the seed of Jacob or of Israel, but the servant.

I have given my Spirit. In the conception; whence: The Holy Spirit shall come upon thee, etc. (Phil. II). And it shone forth in the baptism, when the Spirit in the likeness of a dove rested upon him (Luc. I).

VERS. 3.-- Smoking flax. The priesthood now almost extinguished; and of flax is the priestly vestment made.

He shall bring forth judgment. He shall judge, namely, not fearing the Scribes and the Pharisees.

VERS. 4.-- He shall not be sad. (ID.) This Matthew 1284Cdid not set down; or else the words between the one judgment and the other were taken away by a copyist's error. And the sense is: he shall preserve at all times an evenness of countenance; which the philosophers falsely boast of Socrates, namely that he was neither sadder nor gladder than his wont.

Judgment in the earth. Whence: For judgment I came into this world, that they who do not see may see, etc. (Joan. IX).

VERS. 6.-- Justice. It was just that he who was despised by the Jews should pass over through the apostles to the Gentiles.

VERS. 7.-- The bound. With the cords of sins: in the Gospel, the Lord loosed him whom Satan had bound.

VERS. 8.-- To another. The Son and the Holy Spirit is not another than the Father in the Godhead, and therefore has the same glory 1284Dand the same praise.

1284DVERS. 9.-- I will make known to you. That you may know that I am the Lord, and there is none other beside me: and that you who behold the former things fulfilled may believe the things to come which I promise.

VERS. 10.-- A new thing. In the newness of the Spirit, not in the oldness of the letter; and not only in the Old Testament, but also in the New.

You that go down into the sea. (ID.). First fishers of fishes, afterward of men; whence, according to the letter: they crossed many seas.

VERS. 11.-- Cedar. The son of Ismael: from him the Saracens, who are better called Agarenes: but by the name of the free woman they have veiled the shame of the handmaid. For Ismael was the son of the handmaid Agar, and from her the Saracens. For he begot twelve princes. By his name too the province itself is called.

1285AFrom the top of the mountains. (HIER.) Go up upon a high mountain. From the top of the mountain. [Vulg. Is. 42:11 de vertice montium, plural, as in the lemma just quoted] From the height of virtue and of contemplation, that they may teach by example also the things they preach.

VERS. 13.-- He shall cry out. Whence: They have provoked me with that which was no God (Deut. XXXII); and I will provoke them with a foolish nation.

VERS. 14.-- As a woman in labor. The birth of my counsel I will pour forth into the light as a woman her infant; so will I bring forth the grief I have dissembled.

VERS. 16.-- In the paths. (ID.) In the knowledge of the prophets, who are the paths to Christ, when they foretell his nativity, his resurrection and his ascension.

VERS. 17.-- Let them be confounded. When the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in. Then Israel also, being converted, shall repent of its 1285Berror, who before served idols.

VERS. 22.-- A snare to the young men. He returns to the preaching of the Lord, rebuking the Scribes and the Pharisees: for these were snares to the simple, that they might not be converted to the Lord.

VERS. 23.-- Who is there. The prophet exhorts them, that at least some may hear and understand the cause of their captivity and beware.

VERS. 25.-- Upon him. That what he had before passed over in silence he might now no longer pass over in silence, and might rebuke not with words but with torments.

And he knew it not. As the histories relate, neither did the Gentiles understand that this was done in vengeance for the Son, nor did the Jews, blinded by their own malice.

CAPUT XLIII.

1285CVERS. 1.-- Et nunc haec. Superius locutus est de captivitate Judaeorum qui credere noluerunt, nunc primitivam alloquitur Ecclesiam, quae est de Judaeis, ut Creatorem animae suae et corporis intelligant.

Creans te Jacob. Jacob supplantator, Israel vir videns Deum. Hi sunt apostoli et apostolici viri, qui et vitia supplantaverunt, et Deum videre meruerunt.

VERS. 2.-- Per aquas, etc. (ID.) Secundum Hebraeos: Per aquas Aegyptii, etc., usque ad haec quoque regna praedicando transierunt apostoli, et Dominus ubique cum eis.

VERS. 3.-- Dedi propitiationem. Ut inde acquiras tibi misericordiam et coronam, praedicando, scilicet, Aegyptiis, et Aethiopibus (qui pene ultimi) et Sabaeis: est autem Saba civitas in finibus Aethiopiae sita: In 1285Domnem terram exivit sonus eorum, et in fines, etc.

VERS. 5.-- Ab Oriente. Multi venient ab Oriente et Occidente, et recumbent cum Abraham, Isaac et Jacob, ab omnibus scilicet mundi partibus.

VERS. 8.-- Educ foras. Vox Patris ad Filium de Judaeis mente caecis. Et nota prius esse factum vocationem Judaeorum, deinde gentium.

VERS. 9.-- Omnes gentes. Propheta ad gentes, increpans de idololatria et admirans quod nullus in gentibus hoc praescire potuit; sed tantum populus Dei qui legem Dei accepit, prophetas habuit.

VERS. 10.-- Non est formatus. Quidam codices habent plasmatus; sed non convenit bene, sic tamen potest exponi: Ante me non est formatum 1286Aidolum, quia feci hominem justum a quo injuste formatum est idolum.

VERS. 12.-- Ego annuntiavi et salvavi. Per prophetas nativitatem Filii, vocationem gentium, expulsionem daemonum.

VERS. 14.-- Haec dicit Dominus Salvator, etc. Haec, scilicet quae sequuntur: Pater ad apostolos. Propter vos qui scilicet testes mei estis, qui scilicet Evangelium praedicatis. Quibus dixit: Noli timere. Chaldaeos in navibus. Secundum historiam, Chaldaei abundant fluminibus, et strenui sunt navibus et inde gloriantur.

VERS. 15.-- Ego Dominus sanctus vester. Olim filiis Israel, modo vobis apostoli, in mundo expulsis daemonibus et omnibus victis principibus.

1286BVERS. 19.-- Facio nova. Multa quae reficiunt animas, non corpora: olim de petra unum tantum produxi fontem ad corpora reficienda.

VERS. 20.-- Dracones et struthiones. Maligni, venenosi, qui in solitudine gentium morabantur, idololatriae sanguine morumque feritate bestiales.

Struthio speciem alarum habet, volatu caret. Sic hypocrita speciem sanctitatis habet, rem autem nonhabet.

VERS. 22.-- Non me invocasti. (HIER.) Ad Judaeos loquitur, qui ante adventum Domini idola coluerunt; vel ad eos qui post adventum credere noluerunt.

Nec laborasti. Non feci te laborare post adventum Filii, ut pretiosa quaeque offerres, quia: Sacrificium Deo spiritus contribulatus, etc. (Psal. L.) Vel: Non laborasti in me tantum qui idolis offerebas.

1286CVERS. 23.-- In thure. Per thus et calamum, qui est sarculus arboris aromaticae, omnia aromata accipe.

VERS. 25.-- Ego sum. (ID.) Quasi diceret: Fecisti me laborare sub onere peccati tui; ideo jam non servum meum, non puerum voco, sed simpliciter appello Jacob et Israel, ut ostendam et arguam peccatores.

VERS. 26.-- Reduc me. Bona quae feci tibi. Quid ultra debui facere tibi, et non feci? Vel: Si quid justi habes, ego libenter recipiam.

VERS. 27.-- Pater tuus. Abraham scilicet qui, Domino terram promissionis pollicente, respondit: In quo sciam quia possidebo eam? Vel ipse Abraham qui peccasse dicitur in promissione filii dubitando; vel, populus in deserto contra Deum murmurando, quasi diceret: cognoscet de peccatoribus generatum.

1286DInterpretes. Quasi diceret: inter partes, scilicet pacem ferentes.

CHAPTER XLIII.

1285CVERS. 1.-- And now these things. Above he spoke of the captivity of the Jews who would not believe; now he addresses the primitive Church, which is of the Jews, that they may understand the Creator of their soul and of their body.

Creating thee, Jacob. Jacob is the supplanter, Israel the man seeing God. These are the apostles and apostolic men, who both supplanted vices and deserved to see God.

VERS. 2.-- Through the waters, etc. (ID.) According to the Hebrews: Through the waters the Egyptians, etc., as far as these kingdoms too the apostles passed through by preaching, and the Lord everywhere with them.

VERS. 3.-- I have given a propitiation. That from thence thou mayest gain for thyself mercy and a crown, by preaching, namely, to the Egyptians, and to the Ethiopians (who are almost the last) and to the Sabaeans: and Saba is a city situated on the borders of Ethiopia: Their sound has gone forth into 1285Dall the earth, and unto the ends, etc.

VERS. 5.-- From the East. Many shall come from the East and from the West, and shall sit down with Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, from all the parts of the world, that is. [Vulg. Matt. 8:11 multi ab oriente et occidente venient … in regno caelorum, "in the kingdom of heaven"]

VERS. 8.-- Bring forth. The voice of the Father to the Son concerning the Jews who are blind in mind. And note that the calling of the Jews was made first, then that of the Gentiles.

VERS. 9.-- All the nations. The prophet speaks to the Gentiles, rebuking them for idolatry and marvelling that none among the Gentiles could foreknow this; but only the people of God, who received the law of God, had prophets.

VERS. 10.-- There was not formed. Certain manuscripts have plasmatus; but it does not fit well, though it can be expounded thus: Before me no 1286Aidol was formed, because I made man just, by whom an idol was unjustly formed.

VERS. 12.-- I have declared and have saved. Through the prophets the nativity of the Son, the calling of the Gentiles, the casting out of demons.

VERS. 14.-- Thus says the Lord the Savior, etc. [Vulg. Is. 43:14 redemptor vester, "your redeemer"] These things, namely, which follow: the Father to the apostles. For your sakes, that is, you who are my witnesses, you who preach the Gospel. To whom he said: Fear not. The Chaldeans in the ships. According to the history, the Chaldeans abound in rivers, and are vigorous with ships, and thence take glory.

VERS. 15.-- I am the Lord your Holy One. Formerly to the sons of Israel, now to you, apostles, the demons having been cast out in the world and all the princes conquered.

1286BVERS. 19.-- I do new things. Many things that refresh souls, not bodies: of old I brought forth from the rock one fountain only, to refresh bodies.

VERS. 20.-- Dragons and ostriches. The malignant, the venomous, who abode in the solitude of the Gentiles, brutish through the blood of idolatry and the savagery of their manners.

The ostrich has the appearance of wings, but lacks flight. So the hypocrite has the appearance of holiness, but does not have the thing itself.

VERS. 22.-- Thou hast not called upon me. (HIER.) He speaks to the Jews, who before the coming of the Lord worshipped idols; or to those who after his coming would not believe.

Neither hast thou labored. I have not made thee labor after the coming of the Son, that thou shouldst offer costly things, because: A sacrifice to God is an afflicted spirit, etc. (Psal. L.) Or: Thou hast not labored in me only, thou who didst offer to idols.

1286CVERS. 23.-- In incense. By the incense and the cane, which is a shoot of an aromatic tree, understand all spices.

VERS. 25.-- I am he. (ID.) As if he should say: Thou hast made me labor under the burden of thy sin; therefore I no longer call thee my servant, nor my child, but simply name thee Jacob and Israel, that I may show and convict sinners.

VERS. 26.-- Bring me back to remembrance. The good things I have done for thee. What more ought I to have done for thee, and did not do? [Vulg. Is. 5:4 Quid est quod debui ultra facere vineae meae, et non feci ei, "for my vineyard … for it"] Or: If thou hast anything of justice, I will gladly receive it.

VERS. 27.-- Thy father. Abraham, namely, who, when the Lord promised the land of promise, answered: Whereby shall I know that I shall possess it? [Vulg.
