Gen. 15:8 unde scire possum quod possessurus sim eam] Or Abraham himself, who is said to have sinned by doubting at the promise of a son; or the people in the desert by murmuring against God, as if he should say: he shall recognize one begotten of sinners.

1286DInterpreters. As if he should say: inter partes, that is, those who bear peace between the parties.

CAPUT XLIV.

VERS. 1.-- Et nunc audi, Jacob. Primos fideles de Judaeis aliis obduratis cum quodam privilegio nominis et dignitate honoris alloquitur; apostolorum scilicet chorum. Audi, Jacob serve meus. Prima servitus, secunda electio.

VERS. 2.-- Ab utero auxiliator tuus. In quo fratris plantam arripuisti, vel, in principio Ecclesiae manus persecutorum evasisti.

Et rectissime quem elegi. Israel, secundum Hebraicam veritatem, rectus vel rectissimus. Quod ante, vir 1287Avidens Deum dicitur: non a proprietate, sed a sono vocis habetur.

VERS. 3.-- Effundam Spiritum meum. Scilicet in baptismo per impositionem manuum.

VERS. 4.-- Quasi salices juxta praeterfluentes aquas. Salix arbor infructuosa, etc., usque ad quae cito credidit praedicantibus apostolis.

VERS. 5.-- Iste dicet. Credentium varietatem describit.

Et in nomine Israel assimilabitur. Ut recipiant legem, et prophetas, et Spiritus sancti gratias quae Israeli promissae sunt.

VERS. 6.-- Ego primus et ego novissimus. Post vocationem gentium quae, abjectis idolis, crediderunt, agit contra eos qui converti noluerunt.

1287BAbsque me non est Deus. Non dicit se solum esse, sed praeter virtutem et sapientiam suam nullum externum Deum esse, deorum multorum opinionem et simulacra condemnans.

VERS. 12.-- Esuriet et deficiet. (HIER.) Fame et siti artificis, etc., usque ad quorum Deus venter est, gloria in confusione eorum.

VERS. 23.-- Jubilate, extrema terrae. (ID.) Angeli quasi habitatoribus terrae praesunt; vel apostoli, vel prophetae super quos et alii esse constant; unde Ephesiis dicitur: Super aedificati super fundamentum apostolorum et prophetarum, etc.

VERS. 24.-- Haec dicit Dominus Redemptor, etc. (ID.) Supra locutus est de idolorum subversione, et Israel poenitentis redemptione: nunc autem de potentiae 1287Csuae magnitudine, ne quis dubitet de salvatione quam promittit.

VERS. 26.-- Qui dico. De restauratione quae facta est a Zorobabel et aliis ducibus, Cyro permittente, loquitur: Cum tamen adhuc implenda esset captivitas.

VERS. 27.-- Flumina tua. Sub Dario scilicet et Cyro nepote ejus. Nocte enim qua ab eis Balthasar occisus est, Euphrates fluvius, qui per mediam civitatem fluebat, ab eorum exercitu in rivos divisus est, ut per ejus alveum civitatem ingrederentur.

CHAPTER XLIV.

VERS. 1.-- And now hear, Jacob. He addresses the first believers from among the Jews, the others being hardened, with a certain privilege of name and dignity of honor; the choir of the apostles, namely. Hear, Jacob my servant. The first is servitude, the second election.

VERS. 2.-- From the womb thy helper. In which thou didst seize thy brother's heel; or, in the beginning of the Church thou didst escape the hands of the persecutors.

And most upright, whom I have chosen. Israel, according to the Hebrew verity, is upright or most upright. As for what is said before, a man seeing God: 1287Athat is held not from the proper meaning, but from the sound of the word.

VERS. 3.-- I will pour out my Spirit. Namely in baptism, through the imposition of hands.

VERS. 4.-- As willows beside the running waters. The willow is an unfruitful tree, etc., as far as which quickly believed when the apostles preached.

VERS. 5.-- This one shall say. He describes the variety of them that believe.

And in the name of Israel he shall be likened. That they may receive the law, and the prophets, and the graces of the Holy Spirit which were promised to Israel.

VERS. 6.-- I am the first and I am the last. After the calling of the Gentiles, who, having cast away idols, believed, he acts against those who would not be converted.

1287BBesides me there is no God. He does not say that he alone exists, but that beside his power and his wisdom there is no external God, condemning the opinion of many gods and their images.

VERS. 12.-- He shall hunger and shall faint. (HIER.) With the hunger and thirst of the craftsman, etc., as far as whose God is their belly, whose glory is in their confusion.

VERS. 23.-- Shout, ye ends of the earth. (ID.) The angels preside as it were over the inhabitants of the earth; or the apostles, or the prophets, over whom others also are agreed to be; whence it is said to the Ephesians: Super aedificati Built up upon the foundation of the apostles and prophets, etc.

VERS. 24.-- Thus says the Lord the Redeemer, etc. (ID.) Above he spoke of the overthrow of idols, and of the redemption of a repentant Israel: but now of the greatness of his own 1287Cpower, lest anyone doubt of the salvation he promises.

VERS. 26.-- I who say. He speaks of the restoration which was made by Zorobabel and the other leaders, Cyrus permitting it: though the captivity was still to be fulfilled.

VERS. 27.-- Thy rivers. Under Darius, namely, and Cyrus his grandson. For on the night in which Balthasar was slain by them, the river Euphrates, which flowed through the middle of the city, was divided into streams by their army, so that they might enter the city through its channel.

CAPUT XLV.

VERS. 1.-- Haec dicit Dominus Christo. Apud Romanos diadema, apud Judaeos unctio, et reges facit et sacerdotes, a qua et christi dicuntur. Cyrus christus, 1287Dnon unctione, sed dignitate.

Et dorsa regum. (ID.) Quae civitas illi non patuit? Quis regum terga non vertit? qui muri, ante inexpugnabiles, non sunt ejus obsidione subversi? Haec autem completa plenius invenies in historia Xenophontis.

VERS. 3.-- Thesauros absconditos. Cunctarum urbium, ut abjectis idolis, beneficiis invitatus, unum Deum sentires, qui ante praedixit nomen tuum antequam esses.

VERS. 4.-- Et vocavi te. (ID.) Sicut vocavi Abraham, Isaac et Jacob, etc., usque ad Filius autem Christus oleo laetitiae unctus prae participibus suis.

VERS. 5.-- Extra me. Quia Filius et Spiritus sanctus in Patre; praeter sermonem quippe et rationem 1288Avirtutemque meam et sapientiam quae in me est, non est alius Deus.

VERS. 6.-- Ut sciant. Esdras dicit Cyrum scripsisse omnibus gentibus non esse Deum, nisi Deum Israel. Vel ex captivorum liberatione Dei clementia et potentia omnibus magis nota. Josephus autem in libro Antiquitatum ideo populum fuisse dimissum ait, quia Cyrus legit in libro Isaiae quae de eo Dominus per prophetam dixerat ante.

VERS. 7.-- Tenebras. Noctem vel captivitatem. Quasi poenitentes misertus a captivitate reduco; iratus peccantes captivo.

Malum. (ID.) Bellum, scilicet afflictionem secundum quod dicitur: Sufficit diei malitia sua. Confundantur ergo qui Deum malorum conditorem arbitrantur. 1288BHic enim malum non contrarium bono, sed pro afflictione ponitur et bello.

VERS. 8.-- Rorate coeli. (ID.) De adventu Christi, per Cyrum significati, quia a captivitate diaboli liberabit genus humanum, convenienter adjungit. Vel possunt haec haerere superioribus, ut Cyro captivitatem laxante metonymicos coelum et terra laetentur.

Et nubes. Prophetae corda nostra salutari pluvia irrigantes, et Christi nativitatem et resurrectionem et ascensionem annuntiantes, unde: Mandabo nubibus meis ne pluant super eam imbrem; et alibi, Veritas tua usque ad nubes.

Creavi eum qui: scilicet vermis fuit, et germinatus de terra legitur secundum humanitatem.

VERS. 9.-- Vae qui contradicit: quasi diceret: 1288CCredite quae praedico, Christum scilicet venturum, quia, Vae qui contradicit factori suo (Psal. XXXV); vel Cyrum cui per liberationem populi mei omnia regna substernam.

VERS. 10.-- Mulieri, quid parturis? Vir mulieris potens, mulier viri potens.

VERS. 11.-- Plastes ejus, Filii, secundum humanitatem in utero virginali, unde: Spiritus sanctus superveniet in te, et virtus Altissimi obumbrabit tibi, etc.

VERS. 12.-- Et omni militiae. Hac occasione putant quidam astra esse rationalia; aiunt enim, quia insensatis non mandaret aliquid, quae mandata non intelligerent.

VERS. 13.-- Ego suscitavi. (ID.) Quasi diceret: Qui supradicta feci; quid mirum si et Filium mittam 1288Din mundum ut redimat genus humanum et a mortuis suscitem eum?

Vias ejus dirigam. Qui peccatum non fecit, nec inventus est dolus in ore ejus (I Petr. II).

Ipse aedificabit. (ID.) De quo dictum est: Non cognovisti me. Nec ipse aedificavit civitatem vel templum, sed permisit aedificari: nec in ejus tempore hoc consummatum est, sed Assueri.

VERS. 14.-- Labor Aegypti. Cultus idolorum. Multum enim laborabat in exsequendis idolorum sacrificiis. Sed apostolis praedicantibus conversi sunt, et prae omnibus regnis abundant ibi religiones monachorum, ut ubi abundavit delictum, superabundet et gratia (Rom. V).

1289AVERS. 19.-- Non in abscondito, etc. (ID.) Olim in monte Sinai uni tantum populo dixit: Non erunt tibi dii alieni (Exod. XX). Evangelium autem per apostolos omnibus gentibus praedicavit, unde: In omnem terram exivit sonus eorum (Psal. XVIII).

Non dixi. (HIER.) Primum quidem ad Judaeos veni: et ut umbram legis relinquentes, veritatem sequerentur admonui.

Annuntians recta. Ut creatura Creatorem adoret, ut relictis figuris sequatur veritatem Evangelii.

VERS. 20.-- Nescierunt. Noluerunt scire quidam remanentes in errore, simulacrorum scilicet onere gravati, et sperantes in quibus non est salus.

VERS. 21.-- Consiliamini. Ut omnes per mundum euntes idem annuntietis. Hinc enim simul symbolum 1289Bcomposuerunt de Trinitate et unitate, de communione Ecclesiae, de generali resurrectione.

Praedixi illud. Gentes esse salvandas, sed idolum nihil esse: quando locutus sum Abrahae, vel quando legem dedi.

VERS. 23.-- Egredietur: in mundum visibiliter in carne.

Justitiae verbum, filius donec obedientiam impleverit, etc., usque ad quod Judaei superbia mentis demonstrantes omnino non faciunt.

VERS. 25.-- In Domino dicet. Omnes gentes etiam Barbarae, non in synagogis, sed in Ecclesiis confitentes.

CHAPTER XLV.

VERS. 1.-- Thus says the Lord to his Christ. Among the Romans the diadem, among the Jews the anointing, makes both kings and priests, from which they are also called christs. Cyrus is a christ, 1287Dnot by anointing, but by dignity.

And the backs of kings. (ID.) What city did not lie open to him? Which of the kings did not turn his back? What walls, before impregnable, were not overthrown by his siege? But you will find these things more fully fulfilled in the history of Xenophon.

VERS. 3.-- Hidden treasures. Of all the cities, so that, having cast away idols, and invited by benefits, thou mightest perceive one God, who foretold thy name beforehand, before thou wert.

VERS. 4.-- And I have called thee. (ID.) As I called Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, etc., as far as but the Son, Christ, is anointed with the oil of gladness above his fellows.

VERS. 5.-- Beside me. Because the Son and the Holy Spirit are in the Father; for beside my word and my reason 1288Aand my power and the wisdom that is in me, there is no other God.

VERS. 6.-- That they may know. Esdras says that Cyrus wrote to all the nations that there is no God but the God of Israel. Or, from the freeing of the captives the clemency and power of God became better known to all. Josephus, moreover, in the book of the Antiquities says that the people was released for this reason, that Cyrus read in the book of Isaiah the things which the Lord had said of him before through the prophet.

VERS. 7.-- Darkness. The night, or the captivity. As if: taking pity on the penitent, I bring them back from captivity; being angry, I take sinners captive.

Evil. (ID.) War, that is, affliction, according to what is said: Sufficient for the day is its own evil. Let them therefore be confounded who judge God to be the author of evils. 1288BFor here evil is set down not as the contrary of good, but for affliction and for war.

VERS. 8.-- Drop down dew, ye heavens. (ID.) Of the coming of Christ, signified through Cyrus, because he will free the human race from the captivity of the devil, he fitly adds these things. Or these words may cling to what goes before, so that, as Cyrus loosens the captivity, heaven and earth rejoice by metonymy.

And the clouds. The prophets, watering our hearts with a saving rain, and announcing the nativity and the resurrection and the ascension of Christ; whence: I will command my clouds that they rain no rain upon it; [Vulg. Is. 5:6 et nubibus mandabo, with no meis] and elsewhere, Thy truth unto the clouds.

I have created him who: was, namely, a worm, and is read to have sprung from the earth according to his humanity.

VERS. 9.-- Woe to him that gainsays: as if he should say: 1288CBelieve the things I foretell, namely that Christ is to come, because, Woe to him that gainsays his maker (Psal. XXXV) [Vulg. Is. 45:9 fictori suo, "his fashioner"]; or Cyrus, to whom, through the deliverance of my people, I shall lay all kingdoms under.

VERS. 10.-- To the woman, what dost thou bring forth? The man has power over the woman, the woman power over the man.

VERS. 11.-- His fashioner, that is, of the Son according to his humanity in the virginal womb; whence: The Holy Spirit shall come upon thee, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow thee, etc.

VERS. 12.-- And to all their host. On this occasion some think that the stars are rational; for they say that he would not command anything to senseless things, which would not understand the commands.

VERS. 13.-- I have raised him up. (ID.) As if he should say: I who did the things said above; what wonder if I both send the Son 1288Dinto the world to redeem the human race, and raise him from the dead?

I will direct his ways. Who did no sin, neither was guile found in his mouth (I Petr. II).

He himself shall build. (ID.) Of whom it is said: Thou hast not known me. Nor did he himself build the city or the temple, but permitted it to be built: nor was this completed in his time, but in Ahasuerus's.

VERS. 14.-- The labor of Egypt. The worship of idols. For it labored much in performing the sacrifices of idols. But when the apostles preached they were converted, and above all kingdoms the religious orders of monks abound there, so that where sin abounded, grace also might more abound (Rom. V) [Vulg. Rom. 5:20 Ubi autem abundavit delictum, superabundavit gratia, indicative and without et].

1289AVERS. 19.-- Not in secret, etc. (ID.) Of old on Mount Sinai he said to one people only: Thou shalt not have strange gods (Exod. XX) [Vulg. Exod. 20:3 Non habebis deos alienos coram me]. But the Gospel he preached through the apostles to all nations, whence: Their sound has gone forth into all the earth (Psal. XVIII).

I have not said. (HIER.) First indeed I came to the Jews: and I admonished them that, leaving the shadow of the law, they should follow the truth.

Declaring right things. That the creature may adore the Creator, that, the figures being left behind, it may follow the truth of the Gospel.

VERS. 20.-- They have not known. Certain men, remaining in error, were unwilling to know, weighed down, that is, by the burden of images, and hoping in those in whom there is no salvation.

VERS. 21.-- Take counsel together. That you all, going through the world, may announce the same thing. For from this they together composed 1289Bthe creed concerning the Trinity and the unity, concerning the communion of the Church, concerning the general resurrection.

I have foretold it. That the Gentiles were to be saved, but that the idol is nothing: when I spoke to Abraham, or when I gave the law. [Vulg. Is. 45:21 praedixit illud, third person, "he foretold it"]

VERS. 23.-- Shall go forth: into the world, visibly, in the flesh.

The word of justice, the son until he shall have fulfilled obedience, etc., as far as which the Jews, showing the pride of their mind, do not do at all.

VERS. 25.-- In the Lord, he shall say. All the nations, even the barbarous, confessing not in synagogues but in churches.

CAPUT XLVI.

VERS. 1.-- Confractus est Bel. (HIER.) Post vocationem 1289Cgentium subjicitur destructio idolorum.

Nabo. Nabo est aurum, etc., usque ad et prophetia daemonum, et divinatio magorum ad nihilum devenerunt.

Bestiis et jumentis. (ID.) Bestias enim et jumenta adorabant, et regio eorum plerisque oppidis ex bestiis et jumentis, quae adorabant, nomina imposuit, ut scilicet, Leonata a Leone, et alia hujusmodi.

VERS. 2.-- Et anima eorum. Non quod simulacra habeant animam, etc., usque ad quas diabolo et daemonibus subjugavit.

VERS. 3.-- Domus Jacob. Non dicit Jacob, servi mei, electi mei cum adjectione honoris, sed simpliciter: et alloquitur tanquam carnales et recedentes a mandatis suis.

1289DQui portamini. Qui gestamini tanquam fetus conceptus per desertum usque ad terram promissionis. Diu enim patienter eos exspectavit, et in terra promissionis, et post passionem per duos et quadraginta annos.

VERS. 5.-- Cui assimilastis? In eos praecipue invehitur, qui tempore Achaz et Ezechielis idola coluerunt in angulis platearum, in hortis, in lucis super domos.

VERS. 8.-- Mementote istud. Quia vos genui et usque ad senectam portavi, deserite idola quae fecistis, et ad unius Dei cultum revertimini.

Ad cor. Intellectum, qui quasi bestiae vixistis, simulacra venerantes, ut furiosi in ligna et lapides impingentes.

1290AVERS. 10.-- Annuntians. Per prophetas, ut cum praedicta implevero per divinationem, id est prophetam praedicentem futura, probem divinitatem meam.

Novissimum. Nos sumus in quos fines saeculorum devenerunt (I Cor. X). Et alibi: At ubi venit plenitudo temporis misit Deus Filium suum (Gal. IV). Et Joannes: Filioli, inquit, novissima hora est (I Joan. II).

Consilium: redimendi mundum per Filium, quasi, consilium meum statutum nunc dico, ut cum videritis effectum, sciatis nullum esse Deum nisi qui futura cognovit et fieri praecepit.

VERS. 11.-- Vocans ab oriente avem: (ID.) Secundum Hebraeos Cyrum regem Persarum, etc., usque ad et quem Magi ob oriente venientes adoraverunt.

VERS. 12.-- Duro corde. Qui semper resistitis veritati, 1290Bprimo prophetis, tandem, et Filio.

CHAPTER XLVI.

VERS. 1.-- Bel is broken. (HIER.) After the calling 1289Cof the Gentiles is subjoined the destruction of the idols.

Nabo. Nabo is gold, etc., as far as and the prophecy of the demons and the divination of the magi came to nothing.

To beasts and to cattle. (ID.) For they worshipped beasts and cattle, and their region gave to very many towns the names of the beasts and cattle which they worshipped, as, namely, Leonata from the lion, and others of this kind.

VERS. 2.-- And their soul. Not that the images have a soul, etc., as far as which he subjugated to the devil and to the demons.

VERS. 3.-- House of Jacob. He does not say, Jacob, my servants, my elect, with an addition of honor, but simply: and he addresses them as carnal and as departing from his commandments.

1289DYou that are carried. You who are borne like an offspring conceived, through the desert as far as the land of promise. For he waited for them long and patiently, both in the land of promise and, after the passion, for two and forty years.

VERS. 5.-- To whom have you likened? He inveighs chiefly against those who in the time of Achaz and Ezechiel worshipped idols in the corners of the streets, in gardens, in groves upon the housetops.

VERS. 8.-- Remember this. Because I have begotten you and have carried you even to old age, forsake the idols you have made, and return to the worship of the one God.

To the heart. To understanding, you who have lived as it were like beasts, venerating images, dashing yourselves like madmen against wood and stones.

1290AVERS. 10.-- Declaring. Through the prophets, that when I shall have fulfilled the things foretold through divination, that is, through a prophet foretelling things to come, I may prove my divinity.

The last. We are they upon whom the ends of the ages are come (I Cor. X). And elsewhere: But when the fulness of time came, God sent his Son (Gal. IV). And John: Little children, he says, it is the last hour (I Joan. II).

Counsel: of redeeming the world through the Son; as if: I now declare my counsel established, so that when you shall have seen the effect, you may know that there is no God save him who knew the things to come and commanded them to come to pass.

VERS. 11.-- Calling a bird from the east: (ID.) According to the Hebrews, Cyrus king of the Persians, etc., as far as and him whom the Magi, coming from the east, adored.

VERS. 12.-- Hard of heart. You who always resist the truth, 1290Bfirst the prophets, at last the Son also.

CAPUT XLVII.

VERS. 1.-- Descende, sede, etc. (HIER.) Quia omnes creaturae Dei sumus; nec juxta haereticos, natura Babylonis damnabilis; vel ob luxuriam et ornatum potentissimae urbis, quae jam senex et vicina occasui virgunculam se et puellam jactat.

VERS. 2.-- Farinam, vel coitum praebe Medis et Persis, qui in hoc ferventissimi sunt. Secundum hoc dicunt Hebraei, quod Philisthaei Samsonem erutis oculis molere coegerunt, id est cum mulieribus suis coire, ut ex eo susciperent homines magnae et invincibilis staturae.

VERS. 3.-- Et non resistet, sicut, Moyses Deo volenti delere filios Israel. Significat autem angelum 1290CBabyloni praepositum: qui cum aliis ait: Curavivimus Babylonem, et non est sanata.

VERS. 5.-- Sede. Sermo ad Babylonem refertur, et quasi quondam reginam, modo ancillam alloquitur. Sede humilis et vilis. Tace ut tristis considerans miseriam tuam.

VERS. 6.-- Iratus sum. Causam reddit quare Chaldaeos ita afflixerit, ne sine causa fecisse videretur, qui nihil sine causa operatur; et ut cognoscant Chaldaei, quia non in viribus suis superaverunt populum Dei.

VERS. 9.-- Subito. Ex improviso irruente Cyro et Dario. Non enim metuebat ut a Medis et Persis (qui illius comparatione nullius erant fortitudinis, et ipsi subjecti) posset superari.

VERS. 11.-- Nescies ortum. Ex insperato veniet 1290Dcalamitas, nec a magis nec divinis praedicta, despectum enim et subjectum populum non timebant.

VERS. 13.-- Augures coeli. Astrologi, qui per stellas arbitrantur omnia regi. Quasi dicat: Quasi non providerunt omnia tibi? Hi vulgo appellantur mathematici, qui cursu astrorum res mundanas putant regi, unde: Ab oriente venerunt magi dicentes: Vidimus stellam ejus in oriente (Matth. II). Vel, a vaticinio Balaam provocati, qui ait: Orietur stella ex Jacob (Num. XXIV).

VERS. 14.-- Facti sunt quasi stipula, etc. LXX: Habens carbones ignis, sedebis super eos, hi erunt tibi in adjutorium. Quasi, Gazareni et magi per errorem ducunt te ad superbiam. Incendium autem 1291ABabylonis per supplicia vocat ad poenitentiam.

CHAPTER XLVII.

VERS. 1.-- Come down, sit, etc. (HIER.) Because we are all creatures of God; nor, as the heretics hold, is the nature of Babylon damnable; or else because of the luxury and adornment of that most powerful city, which, already old and near to its setting, boasts itself a maiden and a girl.

VERS. 2.-- Meal, or rather, offer intercourse to the Medes and Persians, who are most ardent in this. According to this the Hebrews say that the Philistines, when they had torn out Samson's eyes, compelled him to grind, that is, to have intercourse with their women, that from him they might get men of great and unconquerable stature.

VERS. 3.-- And he shall not resist, as Moses did to God when he willed to blot out the sons of Israel. But it signifies the angel 1290Cset over Babylon, who says with the others: We have healed Babylon, and she is not healed.

VERS. 5.-- Sit. The speech is referred to Babylon, and he addresses her as one once a queen, now a handmaid. Sit, lowly and vile. Be silent, as one sorrowful, considering thy misery.

VERS. 6.-- I was angry. He renders the reason why he so afflicted the Chaldeans, lest he should seem to have done it without cause, who works nothing without cause; and that the Chaldeans might know that they did not overcome the people of God by their own strength.

VERS. 9.-- Suddenly. By the unforeseen inrush of Cyrus and Darius. For she did not fear that she could be overcome by the Medes and Persians (who in comparison with her were of no strength, and were themselves subject).

VERS. 11.-- Thou shalt not know the rising. Unlooked for the calamity shall come, 1290Dforetold neither by the magi nor by the soothsayers; for they did not fear a despised and subject people.

VERS. 13.-- The augurs of heaven. The astrologers, who judge that all things are ruled by the stars. As if he should say: As though they did not provide all things for thee? These are commonly called mathematicians, who think that worldly affairs are ruled by the course of the stars; whence: From the east came magi saying: We have seen his star in the east (Matth. II). Or else, stirred up by the oracle of Balaam, who said: A star shall rise out of Jacob (Num. XXIV).

VERS. 14.-- They are become as stubble, etc. The Seventy: Having coals of fire, thou shalt sit upon them, these shall be to thee for a help. As if: the Gazareni and the magi lead thee by error into pride. But the burning 1291Aof Babylon calls to repentance through punishments.

CAPUT XLVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Audite haec. (HIER.) Consuetudo est ut quando agitur de carnali Israel, simpliciter et sine nominis adjectione Jacob vel Israel dicatur, sicut in hoc loco, ubi eos alloquitur, qui nomine tenus Jacob vel Israel appellantur.

Et de aquis Juda. Judae facit mentionem, quia decem tribubus captivatis, Juda et Benjamin et Levitae in terra promissionis manebant, et regem de Juda habebant.

VERS. 2.-- De civitate enim sancta. (ID.) Ac si dicat: Habitatores Jerusalem vos esse jactatis, et Domini Sabaoth habere privilegium, cum cassa vobis, Jacob, Israel, et sanctae urbis, Dei omnipotentis assumatis 1291Bvocabula.

VERS. 3.-- Priora extunc. Praedixi Babylonios superandos, et repente faciam quod minatus sum: ut cum venerint quae praedicta sunt, scias nec fortuito nec deorum tuorum nutu accidisse.

Priora miracula, scilicet quae feci in medio eorum ex quo ab Aegypto exierunt, et imminente captivitate a Nabuchodonosor: nunc autem antequam captiventur praedicit eorum liberationem et Chaldaeorum vastationem; unde repente scilicet quae ventura praedico operatus sum.

VERS. 4.-- Nervus ferreus. Hinc Stephanus: dura cervice et incircumcisi cordibus (Act. XVII).

Frons tua aerea: unde Ezechiel: Omnis quippe domus Israel attrita fronte est (Ezech. III), etc. Et 1291CJeremias: Frons mulieris meretricis facta est tibi, noluisti erubescere (Jer. III).

VERS. 6.-- Et conservata sunt. Quasi dicat: Plurima annuntiaturus eram, sed quia non recordaris quae dico, quae dicturus eram reservo.

VERS. 9.-- Propter nomen meum. Scilicet ne violaretur: longe faciam; feci: scilicet furorem meum ne disperderem te, quia adorasti vitulum: et laude mea (ut me scilicet laudares): Infrenabo te lege mea.

VERS. 10.-- In camino paupertatis. (ID.) Purgando per paupertatem, divitias tuas dando hostibus; vel in Aegypto, ubi ostenditur divitiis et paupertate plerosque tentari.

VERS. 12.-- Audi me, Jacob. (HIER.) Eisdem 1291Dadhuc loquitur, etc., usque ad sed vocatos appellat.

1291DVERS. 14.-- Congregamini omnes vos. Quid ergo gloriatur terra et cinis et ignorans fragilitatem suam?

Annuntiavit haec. Quod Cyrus Babylonem destrueret, vel quia Filius meus de virgine nasciturus esset.

Dominus dilexit eum. Haec proprie ad Christum pertinent. Possunt tamen ad Cyrum referri et ad Darium usque ad locum illum: Directa est via ejus.

Suam: quia capiet eam. Vel, Filius in mundo redimendo eum, qui et Chaldaeos (scilicet daemones) ejecit, unde, princeps hujus mundi ejicietur foras.

VERS. 15.-- Vocavi eum. Cyrum vel filium, per Gabrielem dicens: Et vocabis nomen ejus Jesum.

VERS. 16.-- Misit me. (ID.) Totam Trinitatem breviter, plene et aperte distinguit.

1292AVERS. 17.-- Ego Dominus. Qui Israel futura promitto. Reddit causam quare eos prius afflixerit.

VERS. 21.-- Non sitierunt. (ID.) De Aegypto venientibus ad litteram, etc., usque ad satiati aqua sapientiae.

Scidit petram. Petra erat Christus. Scinditur in partes sermo divinus, ut quem totum suscipere non possumus, per partes sumamus.

Et fluxerunt aquae. Unus militum lancea latus ejus aperuit, et continuo exivit sanguis nostrae redemptionis, et aqua baptismi, baptismum et martyrium dedicans nobis.

CHAPTER XLVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Hear these things. (HIER.) It is the custom that when the carnal Israel is treated of, Jacob or Israel should be named simply and without an addition of name, as in this place, where he addresses those who are called Jacob or Israel only in name.

And out of the waters of Juda. He makes mention of Juda, because when the ten tribes had been led captive, Juda and Benjamin and the Levites remained in the land of promise, and had a king of Juda.

VERS. 2.-- For of the holy city. (ID.) As if he should say: You boast that you are the inhabitants of Jerusalem, and that you have the privilege of the Lord of hosts, whereas you take to yourselves, empty as they are for you, the appellations of Jacob, of Israel, and of the holy city, of God 1291Balmighty.

VERS. 3.-- The former things from that time. I foretold that the Babylonians were to be overcome, and suddenly I shall do what I threatened: so that when the things foretold shall have come, thou mayest know that they befell neither by chance nor by the nod of thy gods.

The former wonders, namely those which I did in the midst of them from the time they went out of Egypt, and when the captivity by Nabuchodonosor was at hand: but now, before they are taken captive, he foretells their deliverance and the devastation of the Chaldeans; whence suddenly, namely, the things I foretell as coming, I have wrought.

VERS. 4.-- An iron sinew. Hence Stephen: stiffnecked and uncircumcised in hearts (Act. XVII) [Vulg. Act. 7:51 incircumcisis cordibus et auribus, ablative, "and ears"].

Thy forehead brazen: whence Ezechiel: For all the house of Israel is of a hardened forehead (Ezech. III), etc. And 1291CJeremias: Thou hast had the forehead of a harlot woman, thou wouldst not blush (Jer. III).

VERS. 6.-- And they are kept. As if he should say: I was about to announce very many things, but because thou dost not remember what I say, what I was about to say I keep in reserve.

VERS. 9.-- For my name's sake. Namely, that it be not profaned: I will put far off; I have done it: [Vulg. Is. 48:9 longe faciam furorem meum, "I will put my fury far off"] my fury, that is, that I should not destroy thee, because thou hast adored the calf: and with my praise (that thou mightest praise me, namely): I will bridle thee with my law.

VERS. 10.-- In the furnace of poverty. (ID.) By purging thee through poverty, giving thy riches to the enemies; or in Egypt, where it is shown that very many are tempted by riches and by poverty.

VERS. 12.-- Hear me, Jacob. (HIER.) He is still speaking to the same men, 1291Detc., as far as but he calls them the called.

1291DVERS. 14.-- Assemble yourselves, all of you. Why then does earth and ashes glory, and one ignorant of his own frailty?

He has announced these things. That Cyrus should destroy Babylon, or that my Son was to be born of a virgin.

The Lord has loved him. These things belong properly to Christ. Yet they can be referred to Cyrus and to Darius, as far as that place: His way is made straight.

His own: because he shall take it. Or, the Son, in redeeming the world, who also cast out the Chaldeans (that is, the demons); whence, the prince of this world shall be cast forth.

VERS. 15.-- I have called him. Cyrus, or the Son, saying through Gabriel: And thou shalt call his name Jesus.

VERS. 16.-- He has sent me. (ID.) He distinguishes the whole Trinity briefly, fully and plainly.

1292AVERS. 17.-- I am the Lord. Who promise to Israel the things to come. He renders the reason why he had afflicted them before.

VERS. 21.-- They did not thirst. (ID.) Of those coming from Egypt, according to the letter, etc., as far as filled with the water of wisdom.

He clove the rock. The rock was Christ. The divine word is cloven into parts, so that what we cannot take in whole, we may take by parts.

And the waters flowed out. One of the soldiers opened his side with a lance, and immediately there came forth the blood of our redemption, and the water of baptism, [Vulg. Jo. 19:34 exivit sanguis et aqua, without nostrae redemptionis and baptismi] dedicating to us baptism and martyrdom.

CAPUT XLIX.

VERS. 1.-- Audite, insulae. (HIER.) Post reliquiarum Israel vocationem et infidelium objectionem (de quibus, Non est pax impiis, dicit Dominus ), sub insularum 1292Bnomine alloquitur in persona Filii Ecclesias de gentibus.

VERS. 2.-- Gladium acutum. Non veni pacem mittere, sed gladium (Matth. X), malos scilicet a bonis separans.

VERS. 3.-- Gloriabor. Quod non in aliis servis: Pater, glorificavi nomen tuum (Joan. XVII.) Et alibi: Exsurge, gloria mea (Psal. LVI), etc.

VERS. 4.-- Et ego dixi. (ID.) Quasi dicat: Quomodo te glorificavi, qui magnam partem Judaeorum ad te revocare non potui? Hic autem liberum hominis monstratur arbitrium: Dei enim est vocare, nostrum credere, ipso tamen adjuvante, ut justi voluntatis praemium consequantur.

Ergo judicium, etc. (ID.) Judicabit Pater utrum 1292Comnia fecerim eis quae debui: Manifestavi nomen tuum hominibus quos dedisti mihi. Et alibi: Ego te glorificavi super terram (Joan. XVII).

Opus meum. Alii: Labor et dolor meus: quia flevit civitatem, et ait in Psalmo: Quae utilitas in sanguine meo? (Psal. XXIX.) Juxta Hebraicum, pendens in cruce longe a salute mea verba lamentationum mearum.

VERS. 5.-- Glorificatus. Quasi dicat: Ego Judaeos perdidi, sed glorificatus sum in gentibus, unde, Data est mihi omnis potestas in coelo et in terra. (Matth. XXVIII).

VERS. 7.-- Abominatam. Christum et Nazaraeos ejus, quos quotidie Judaei in synagogis maledicunt, quia propter eos regnum, locum et gentem amiserunt.

Reges. Propheta ad Filium. Reges videbunt te venientem ad judicium: Reges, scilicet apostoli et alii 1292Dqui cum Domino judicabunt.

VERS. 8.-- In tempore placito. Passionis, resurrectionis, in quo placuit Deo redimere genus humanum.

VERS. 16.-- Ecce in manibus, etc. (ID.) Ex hoc discimus Jerusalem in regione Palestinae non esse quaerendam: quae totius provinciae deterrima et saxosis montibus exasperata penuriam fontium patitur.

VERS. 17.-- Structores. Apostoli et alii: Culpa Judaeorum, id est, Scribarum et Pharisaeorum destructa es praedicatione quorumdam Judaeorum, id est apostolorum tam de Judaeis quam de gentibus congregata es.

VERS. 18.-- Omnes isti congregati. Judaei ex omni natione, etc., usque ad quotidie congregantur ad fidem Ecclesiae.

1293AOrnamento. Variis Spiritus sancti gratiis, quibus ornatur sponsae ambitio, de qua dicitur: Astitit regina a dextris tuis in vestitu deaurato, circumdata varietate (Psal. XLIV).

VERS. 21.-- Ego sterilis. Quia post Aggaeum, et Zachariam, et Malachiam, qui et Esdras, non habuerunt prophetas qui eos in lege nutrirent, usque ad Joannem Baptistam.

VERS. 23.-- Reginae. Idem, sed magis affectuose. Unde: Astitit regina, etc. Quae scilicet quotidie Christi parvulos nutrit et ad perfectam aetatem ducit.

Adorabunt. Venerabuntur secundum illud: Adorabunt scabellum pedum ejus (Psal. XCVIII), in quo pede majestas indicatur.

Pulverem pedum tuorum. (HIER.) Omnem scilicet 1293Bterrenitatem verbo et exemplo mundabunt, secundum quod dictum est, Quodcunque solveris super terram, erit solutum (Matth. XVI), etc., unde: Excutite pulverem pedum vestrorum (Matth. X).

VERS. 25.-- Quia haec dicit Dominus. (ID.) Respondet quaestioni, quia haec scilicet in adventu suo compleantur.

VERS. 26.-- Carnibus suis. Non aliena morte, sed propria, etc., usque ad ferocium bestiarum patens morsibus.

Fortis Jacob. Qui dedi fortitudinem Jacob luctandi etc., usque ad quasi et oratione contra Esau quem metuis, praevalebis.

CHAPTER XLIX.

VERS. 1.-- Hear, ye islands. (HIER.) After the calling of the remnant of Israel and the objection of the unbelieving (concerning whom, There is no peace to the wicked, saith the Lord ), under the name of the 1292Bislands he addresses, in the person of the Son, the Churches from among the Gentiles.

VERS. 2.-- A sharp sword. I came not to send peace, but the sword (Matth. X), separating, that is, the evil from the good.

VERS. 3.-- I will be glorified. Which he was not in the other servants: Father, I have glorified thy name (Joan. XVII.) And elsewhere: Arise, my glory (Psal. LVI), etc.

VERS. 4.-- And I said. (ID.) As though he said: How have I glorified thee, I who could not call back to thee a great part of the Jews? But here man's free choice is shown: for it is God's to call, ours to believe, yet with him helping, that the just may obtain the reward of their will.

Therefore my judgment, etc. (ID.) The Father shall judge whether 1292CI have done for them all the things I owed: I have manifested thy name to the men whom thou gavest me. And elsewhere: I have glorified thee upon the earth (Joan. XVII).

My work. Others: My labour and my sorrow: because he wept over the city, and said in the Psalm: What profit is there in my blood? (Psal. XXIX.) According to the Hebrew, hanging on the cross far from my salvation are the words of my lamentations.

VERS. 5.-- Glorified. As though he said: I have lost the Jews, but I am glorified among the Gentiles, whence, All power is given to me in heaven and in earth. (Matth. XXVIII).

VERS. 7.-- Abhorred. Christ and his Nazarenes, whom the Jews daily curse in their synagogues, because on their account they lost their kingdom, their place and their nation.

Kings. The prophet to the Son. Kings shall see thee coming to judgment: Kings, namely the apostles and the others 1292Dwho shall judge with the Lord.

VERS. 8.-- In an acceptable time. Of the Passion, of the Resurrection, in which it pleased God to redeem the human race.

VERS. 16.-- Behold, in my hands, etc. (ID.) From this we learn that Jerusalem is not to be sought in the region of Palestine: which is the worst of the whole province, roughened with rocky mountains, and suffers a want of springs.

VERS. 17.-- Thy builders. The apostles and others: By the fault of the Jews, that is, of the Scribes and Pharisees, thou wast destroyed; by the preaching of certain Jews, that is, of the apostles, thou wast gathered together both from the Jews and from the Gentiles.

VERS. 18.-- All these are gathered together. The Jews out of every nation, etc., as far as daily they are gathered to the faith of the Church.

1293AWith an ornament. With the various graces of the Holy Spirit, with which the bride's array is adorned, of which it is said: The queen stood on thy right hand in gilded clothing, surrounded with variety (Psal. XLIV).

VERS. 21.-- I am barren. Because after Aggeus, and Zacharias, and Malachias, who is also Esdras, they had no prophets to nourish them in the law, until John the Baptist.

VERS. 23.-- Queens. The same, but more affectionately. Whence: The queen stood, etc. She, namely, who daily nourishes the little ones of Christ and leads them to the perfect age.

They shall adore. They shall venerate, according to that: They shall adore the footstool of his feet [Vulg. Ps 98:5 adorate, "adore ye" — an imperative, here made a future, matching the lemma Adorabunt] (Psal. XCVIII), in which foot majesty is signified.

The dust of thy feet. (HIER.) They shall cleanse away, that is, all 1293Bearthliness by word and by example, according to what is said, Whatsoever thou shalt loose upon earth, it shall be loosed (Matth. XVI), etc., whence: Shake off the dust of your feet [Vulg. Matt 10:14 excutite pulverem de pedibus vestris, "shake off the dust from your feet" — the proof-text conformed to the lemma the dust of thy feet] (Matth. X).

VERS. 25.-- For thus saith the Lord. (ID.) He answers the question, namely that these things are fulfilled at his coming.

VERS. 26.-- With their own flesh. Not by another's death, but by his own, etc., as far as lying open to the bites of savage beasts.

The mighty one of Jacob. I who gave Jacob strength to wrestle, etc., as far as thou shalt prevail, as it were by prayer also, against Esau whom thou fearest.

CAPUT L.

VERS. 1.-- Haec dicit Dominus: Quis est? (HIER.) Post vocationem gentium, post captionem fortis, 1293Ccujus praeda divisa est apostolis, loquitur ad populum Judaeorum super Sion quae superius ait: Dereliquit me Dominus, etc.

Vendidi. Ego autem carnalis sum venundatus sub peccato (Rom. VII). Qui facit peccatum servus peccati est (Joan. VIII). Unde: Non regnet peccatum in corpore vestro (Rom. VI).

VERS. 2.-- Quia veni. (ID.) Hoc est ut sciatis me matrem vestram non repudiasse, sed eam voluntate propria recessisse: post multa beneficia carnem assumpsi, nec jam per prophetas, sed praesens ipse locutus sum.

Non erat vir. Herodi propter malitiam, etc., usque 1293Dad de omnibus in commune: Visio quadrupedum in deserto (Isa. XXX.)

Vocavi. Quasi diceret: Rationales, Inclinate aurem vestram in verba oris mei (Psal. LXXVII). Qui sitit, veniat et bibat (Joan. VIII), et hujusmodi.

Nunquid. (ID.) Invehitur contra eos qui putabant, etc., usque ad ut exhibeat mihi plus quam duodecim legiones angelorum? (Matth. XXVI).

VERS. 3.-- Induam coelos tenebris. Aiunt philosophi non amplius decem stadiis a terra nubes in sublime levari, et solis splendorem abscondi: sic excluso coeli lumine, aer inferior obscuratur. Induam. Sanctos, scilicet ut se cognoscant peccatores.

Et saccum ponam. Ut agant poenitentiam in timore et cilicio.

1294AVERS. 4.-- Dominus dedit, etc. (ID.) Quamvis possint haec referri ad Isaiam, proprie tamen referuntur ad Christum, quasi diceret: Qui olim talia operatus sum, et modo similia operari possum, non est potentia imminuta.

VERS. 9.-- Quasi vestimentum. (ID.) Vestimentum vetustate et tinea consumitur. Judaei quoque quadraginta duobus annis post passionem ad poenitentiam vocati sunt: tandem negligentes contriti sunt advenientibus Romanis, et malae conscientiae tinea devorati.

VERS. 10.-- Quis ex vobis. Qui timet non est perfectus, etc., usque ad hic custodit animas sanctorum integras et pudicas et puras.

Qui ambulavit in tenebris. Gentilis populus in tenebris, etc., usque ad ad poenitentiam voco; nolo 1294Benim mortem peccatoris, sed magis ut convertatur et vivat (Ezech. XVIII).

VERS. 11.-- Ecce omnes, quasi diceret: Nihil prodest exhortatio, nec est spes salutis post scelera: Omnes declinaverunt simul inutiles facti sunt (Psal. XIII).

Accendentes ignem. Civitatis ignem quo a Romanis succensa est accenderunt, etc., usque ad sic peccatoribus diversa peccati materia plus vel minus succenditur.

In doloribus dormietis. Quia nec beneficiis, nec tormentis, Dei Filium suscipere voluerunt, unde, Ite, maledicti, in ignem aeternum (Matth. XXV).

CHAPTER L.

VERS. 1.-- Thus saith the Lord: Who is he? (HIER.) After the calling of the Gentiles, after the taking of the strong one, 1293Cwhose spoil has been divided to the apostles, he speaks to the people of the Jews concerning Sion, which said above: The Lord hath forsaken me, etc.

I have sold. But I am carnal, sold under sin (Rom. VII). He who commits sin is the servant of sin (Joan. VIII). Whence: Let not sin reign in your body [Vulg. Rom 6:12 in vestro mortali corpore, "in your mortal body"] (Rom. VI).

VERS. 2.-- Because I came. (ID.) That is, that you may know that I have not repudiated your mother, but that she withdrew of her own will: after many benefits I took flesh, and no longer through the prophets, but present myself I spoke.

There was not a man. To Herod because of his malice, etc., as far 1293Das concerning all in common: The vision of the four-footed beasts in the desert (Isa. XXX.)

I called. As though he said: Ye rational ones, Incline your ear to the words of my mouth (Psal. LXXVII). He that thirsteth, let him come and drink [Vulg. Jo 7:37 Si quis sitit, veniat ad me et bibat, "If any man thirst, let him come to me and drink"] (Joan. VIII), and the like.

Is it that. (ID.) He inveighs against those who thought, etc., as far as that he may give me more than twelve legions of angels? (Matth. XXVI).

VERS. 3.-- I will clothe the heavens with darkness. The philosophers say that the clouds are lifted on high no more than ten stadia from the earth, and that the brightness of the sun is hidden: thus, when the light of heaven is shut out, the lower air is darkened. I will clothe. The saints, that is, so that they may know themselves sinners.

And I will put sackcloth. That they may do penance in fear and in haircloth.

1294AVERS. 4.-- The Lord hath given, etc. (ID.) Although these things can be referred to Isaias, yet properly they are referred to Christ, as though he said: I who once wrought such things can now also work the like; my power is not diminished.

VERS. 9.-- As a garment. (ID.) A garment is consumed by age and by the moth. The Jews too were called to penance for forty-two years after the Passion: at last, being negligent, they were crushed when the Romans came, and devoured by the moth of an evil conscience.

VERS. 10.-- Who is there among you. He who fears is not perfect, etc., as far as he keeps the souls of the saints whole and chaste and pure.

Who hath walked in darkness. The Gentile people in darkness, etc., as far as I call to penance; for I will not 1294Bthe death of the sinner, but rather that he be converted and live [Vulg. Ezech 33:11 nolo mortem impii, sed ut convertatur impius a via sua, et vivat, "I will not the death of the wicked, but that the wicked turn from his way and live"; Ezech 18:32 nolo mortem morientis] (Ezech. XVIII).

VERS. 11.-- Behold all you, as though he said: Exhortation profits nothing, nor is there hope of salvation after such crimes: They have all gone aside, they are become unprofitable together (Psal. XIII).

Kindling a fire. They kindled the fire of the city by which it was burned by the Romans, etc., as far as so for sinners the diverse matter of their sin is kindled more or less.

You shall sleep in sorrows. Because neither by benefits nor by torments would they receive the Son of God, whence, Go, ye cursed, into everlasting fire [Vulg. Matt 25:41 Discedite a me maledicti, "Depart from me, ye cursed"] (Matth. XXV).

CAPUT LI.

VERS. 1.-- Audite me qui sequimini quod. (HIER.) Attendite ad petram. Christum. De qua, Petra autem 1294Cerat Christus, unde excisi estis (I Cor. X), a Christo Christiani dicti.

Et ad cavernam laci. Propter fossionem lateris Christi, etc., usque ad nascimur per lavacrum baptismi.

VERS. 3.-- Gaudium et laetitia. Nota in Sion quae comparatur paradiso Dei, tantum debet esse gaudium et laetitia et confessio et vox laudis, ut quod in coelo facturi sunt cum angelis, in terra jugiter meditentur.

VERS. 5.-- Justus meus. Omnes justificans, vel justitia eleganter: non ut una gens, sed totus orbis salvetur.

VERS. 6.-- Coeli sicut fumus. (ID.) De sanctis scriptum est, etc., usque ad quidam tamen perire et inveterascere pro abolitione et morte accipiunt.

VERS. 9.-- Consurge. Vox prophetae et fidelis populi 1294Dadventum Christi deprecantis, in quo promissum erat auxilium.

VERS. 12.-- Quis tu ut timeas ab homine, etc., usque ad ut erubescant et poenitentiam agant.

VERS. 17.-- Elevare, elevare. (ID.) Eos hortatur ad poenitentiam qui in passione negaverunt; vel in infidelitate perstiterunt, clamantes, Crucifige, crucifige (Joan. XIX), etc.

Calicem irae. (ID.) Solent medici amarissimum antidotum, etc., usque ad unde non patet quod de ultima captivitate hoc dicitur.

VERS. 22.-- Ecce. (HIER.) Praedixit captivitatem futuram, nunc hortatur ad poenitentiam, ut evadant.

VERS. 23.-- Ponam. Secundum litteram humiliaverunt, 1295Aetc., usque ad et in judicio omnino humiliabuntur.

CHAPTER LI.

VERS. 1.-- Hearken to me, you that follow that which. (HIER.) Look unto the rock. Christ. Of which, And the rock 1294Cwas Christ, out of which you are hewn (I Cor. X), from Christ called Christians.

And to the cavern of the pit. On account of the digging of Christ's side, etc., as far as we are born through the laver of baptism.

VERS. 3.-- Joy and gladness. Note that in Sion, which is compared to the paradise of God, there ought to be so much joy and gladness and thanksgiving and the voice of praise, that what they are to do in heaven with the angels they should meditate on continually upon earth.

VERS. 5.-- My just one. Justifying all, or justice elegantly: not that one nation, but that the whole world may be saved.

VERS. 6.-- The heavens like smoke. (ID.) It is written of the saints, etc., as far as yet some take "to perish" and "to grow old" for abolition and death.

VERS. 9.-- Arise. The voice of the prophet and of the faithful people 1294Dbeseeching the coming of Christ, in whom help had been promised.

VERS. 12.-- Who art thou that thou shouldst fear man, etc., as far as that they may blush and do penance.

VERS. 17.-- Arise, arise. (ID.) He exhorts to penance those who denied him in the Passion; or who persisted in unbelief, crying, Crucify him, crucify him (Joan. XIX), etc.

The chalice of wrath. (ID.) Physicians are wont to give a most bitter antidote, etc., as far as whence it is not clear that this is said of the last captivity.

VERS. 22.-- Behold. (HIER.) He foretold the captivity to come; now he exhorts to penance, that they may escape.

VERS. 23.-- I will put. According to the letter they humbled them, 1295Aetc., as far as and in the judgment they shall be humbled altogether.

CAPUT LII.

VERS. 1.-- Consurge, consurge. Eos hortatur propheta, qui post ascensionem praedicantibus apostolis crediderunt.

Quia non adjiciet. Haec ad coelestem Jerusalem quam nullus immundus ingreditur.

VERS. 2.-- Excutere. Anima polluta sordibus candorem pristinum amittit, etc., usque ad non corruptibilibus argento et auro, sed sanguine agni immaculati.

VERS. 3.-- Gratis. Nec commodum habuistis, nec incommodum effugistis, cum Filium Dei tradidistis.

VERS. 4.-- In Aegyptum. (HIER.) Arguit Judaeos, et ventura praedicit, etc., usque ad ut etiam in terra 1295Bsua opprimat illum, et calumnietur ei?

VERS. 5.-- Quid mihi est hic? (ID.) Nihil hic, ait, apud eos reperio, etc., usque ad quod Redemptorem suum cruci affixerint.

VERS. 7.-- Quam pulchri. (ID.) De eo qui supra dixerat: Ego ipse qui loquebar ecce adsum, testatur propheta quod ipse supra montes Evangelium praedicavit.

Annuntiantis, Christi, per semetipsum, per apostolos, quibus ait: Pacem relinquo vobis, pacem meam do vobis (Joan. XIV). Super quos Dominus sedens praedicabat: Ipsi etiam pedes Christi, praedicationem ejus portando.

VERS. 8.-- Vox speculatorum: (ID.) apostolorum et aliorum qui in specula sunt semper ut omnia praevideant et subjectos promoveant.

VERS. 9.-- 1295CGaudete. Primitivam Ecclesiam consolatur, etc., usque ad multitudine gentium et reparata est et multiplicata est.

VERS. 10.-- Brachium in brachio meo gentes sperabunt (Psal. XCVII). Et alibi: Brachium tuum cum potentia (Psal. LXXXVIII).

VERS. 11.-- Recedite. (ID.) Hoc Judaei ad se referunt, etc., usque ad a Spiritu sancto admoniti.

Exite, in toto orbe Evangelium praedicantes, Ite, docete omnes gentes (Matth. XVIII).

Vasa Domini. (ID.) Templum Domini sanctum est, quod estis vos, vasa scilicet aurea et argentea, unde Paulus vas electionis dicitur; vel vasa Domini, armatura Dei, lorica justitiae, clypeus fidei, galea salutis, et gladius spiritus, quod est verbum Dei.

1295DVERS. 13.-- Ecce intelliget: ne dubitetur quis dixerit, qui loquebar ecce adsum: et quod sit brachium Domini, Pater perspicue docet.

VERS. 14.-- Sicut obstupuerunt. Non servatur ordo historiae. Superius enim loquebatur propheta in persona Patris de ascensione Filii, et subito ad passionem rediit.

CHAPTER LII.

VERS. 1.-- Arise, arise. The prophet exhorts those who believed after the Ascension, when the apostles were preaching.

For he shall no more add. These things to the heavenly Jerusalem, which no unclean person enters.

VERS. 2.-- Shake thyself. A soul defiled with filth loses its former brightness, etc., as far as not with corruptible silver and gold, but with the blood of the immaculate Lamb.

VERS. 3.-- For nothing. You had neither the profit nor did you escape the loss, when you delivered up the Son of God.

VERS. 4.-- Into Egypt. (HIER.) He accuses the Jews, and foretells things to come, etc., as far as that even in his own land 1295Bhe may oppress him, and calumniate him?

VERS. 5.-- What have I here? (ID.) Nothing here, he says, do I find among them, etc., as far as that they fastened their Redeemer to the cross.

VERS. 7.-- How beautiful. (ID.) Of him who had said above: I myself who spoke, behold I am here, the prophet testifies that he himself preached the Gospel upon the mountains.

Of him that announceth, Christ, by himself, by the apostles, to whom he says: Peace I leave with you, my peace I give unto you (Joan. XIV). Sitting upon whom the Lord preached: they are also the feet of Christ, by carrying his preaching.

VERS. 8.-- The voice of the watchmen: (ID.) of the apostles and of the others who are always in the watchtower, that they may foresee all things and advance those subject to them.

VERS. 9.-- 1295CRejoice. He consoles the primitive Church, etc., as far as by the multitude of the Gentiles it has been both repaired and multiplied.

VERS. 10.-- The arm. In my arm shall the Gentiles hope (Psal. XCVII). And elsewhere: Thy arm with power (Psal. LXXXVIII).

VERS. 11.-- Depart. (ID.) This the Jews refer to themselves, etc., as far as being admonished by the Holy Spirit.

Go forth, preaching the Gospel in the whole world, Go ye, teach all nations (Matth. XVIII).

The vessels of the Lord. (ID.) The temple of the Lord is holy, which you are: vessels, that is, of gold and of silver, whence Paul is called a vessel of election; or the vessels of the Lord, the armour of God, the breastplate of justice, the shield of faith, the helmet of salvation, and the sword of the spirit, which is the word of God.

1295DVERS. 13.-- Behold he shall understand: lest it be doubted who said, I who spoke, behold I am here: and that he is the arm of the Lord, the Father plainly teaches.

VERS. 14.-- As they were astonished. The order of the history is not kept. For above the prophet was speaking in the person of the Father concerning the Ascension of the Son, and suddenly he returned to the Passion.

CAPUT LIII.

1296DVERS. 1.-- Quis credidit? (HIER.) Propheta in persona prophetarum conqueritur quod pauci ex Judaeis credunt quod de adventu Filii ab illis audiunt.

Et brachium Domini. (ID.) Pater Filium suum mundo nuntiavit venturum; et ante scandalum crucis, praemisit gloriam resurgentis. Elevabitur et exaltabitur, 1296Aetc. Respondet ergo prophetarum chorus se officium implesse et brachium Domini annuntiasse.

VERS. 2.-- Virgultum. Symmachus, ramus; hominem significans, qui processit de utero virginis.

Sitienti. Aquila, invia, ubi virginitatis privilegium demonstratur, quia sine semine de terra prius invia creatus est.

VERS. 5.-- Livore. (ID.) Perspicuum est, quia sicut corpus laceratum et flagellatum, ita anima vere doluit pro nobis, ne ex parte veritas, ex parte mendacium in Christo credatur.

VERS. 7.-- Non aperuit, etc. Pilato vel Herodi. Pauca enim respondit ne differretur salus humani generis.

Et quasi agnus. (ID.) In cujus figura populus Israel 1296Bagnum in Aegypto comedit (Exod. XII). Hic est agnus qui tollit peccata mundi (Joan. I), qui occisus est ab origine mundi (Apoc. XIII), qui carne et sanguine suo nos pascit, et vellere protectionis nos vestit.

VERS. 8.-- De judicio. Judex omnium judicii non reperit veritatem, sed sine culpa damnatus est. Unde admiratur propheta quod omnium Deus passioni se tradiderit.

VERS. 9.-- Dabit impios. (ID.) Id est gentes, quae sine pietate, Deo Patri, quia mortuus et sepultus eos acquisivit, et divites, Judaeos scilicet divitiis legis et prophetarum gloriantes.

Divites. Scribas et Pharisaeos doctrinae legis divitiis gloriantes.

VERS. 10.-- Semen longaevum. Verbum Evangelii 1296Cscilicet quod non veterascet, sicut Vetus Testamentum, unde: Exiit qui seminavit semen suum (Luc. VIII.)

VERS. 11.-- Saturabitur. Fide gentium, unde esuriens et sitiens supra puteum Jacob emptis cibis uti noluit, quia jam Samaritanae et eorum qui de civitate ad eum exibant, fide satiatus erat.

VERS. 12.-- Et cum sceleratis. Ut sceleratos a peccato redimeret, omnia omnibus factus: Peccata enim nostra portavit in corpore suo (I Petr. II). Cujus imitator Paulus ait: Omnibus omnia factus sum, ut omnes lucrificarem (I Cor. IX).

Rogavit. Pater, ignosce illis. Ad hanc vocem secundum Evangelium Nazareorum multa millia Judaeorum astantium circa crucem crediderunt.

CHAPTER LIII.

1296DVERS. 1.-- Who hath believed? (HIER.) The prophet in the person of the prophets complains that few of the Jews believe what they hear from them concerning the coming of the Son.

And the arm of the Lord. (ID.) The Father announced to the world that his Son would come; and before the scandal of the cross he sent ahead the glory of him rising again. He shall be lifted up and shall be exalted [Vulg. Is 52:13 sets the two verbs the other way about, exaltabitur et elevabitur], 1296Aetc. The choir of the prophets therefore answers that they have fulfilled their office and announced the arm of the Lord.

VERS. 2.-- A shrub. Symmachus, a branch; signifying the man who came forth from the womb of the Virgin.

To a thirsty one. Aquila, pathless, where the privilege of virginity is shown, because without seed he was created from earth that was before pathless.

VERS. 5.-- By his bruises. (ID.) It is plain that, just as his body was torn and scourged, so his soul truly grieved for us, lest in Christ part be believed truth and part falsehood.

VERS. 7.-- He opened not, etc. To Pilate or to Herod. For he answered few things, lest the salvation of the human race be deferred.

And as a lamb. (ID.) In figure of whom the people of Israel 1296Bate the lamb in Egypt (Exod. XII). This is the lamb who taketh away the sins of the world [Vulg. Jo 1:29 peccatum mundi, "the sin of the world," in the singular] (Joan. I), who was slain from the beginning of the world (Apoc. XIII), who feeds us with his flesh and his blood, and clothes us with the fleece of his protection.

VERS. 8.-- From judgment. The judge of all found no truth of judgment, but was condemned without fault. Whence the prophet marvels that the God of all delivered himself over to the Passion.

VERS. 9.-- He shall give the wicked. (ID.) That is, the Gentiles, who are without piety, to God the Father, because dead and buried he acquired them; and the rich, namely the Jews, who glory in the riches of the law and of the prophets.

The rich. The Scribes and Pharisees, glorying in the riches of the doctrine of the law.

VERS. 10.-- A long-lived seed. The word of the Gospel, 1296Cnamely, which shall not grow old, as the Old Testament did, whence: He that sowed went out to sow his seed [Vulg. Lc 8:5 Exiit qui seminat, seminare semen suum, "He that soweth went out to sow his seed" — a present participle where Migne prints a perfect] (Luc. VIII.)

VERS. 11.-- He shall be filled. With the faith of the Gentiles, whence, hungering and thirsting above Jacob's well, he would not use the food that had been bought, because he was already filled with the faith of the Samaritan woman and of those who were coming out to him from the city.

VERS. 12.-- And with the wicked. That he might redeem the wicked from sin, being made all things to all: For he bore our sins in his own body [Vulg. 1 Petr 2:24 peccata nostra ipse pertulit in corpore suo super lignum, "himself bore our sins in his body upon the tree"] (I Petr. II). Whose imitator Paul says: I became all things to all, that I might gain all [Vulg. 1 Cor 9:22 ut omnes facerem salvos, "that I might save all"; lucrifacerem, "gain," stands at 9:19 and 9:22a] (I Cor. IX).

He prayed. Father, forgive them. At this voice, according to the Gospel of the Nazarenes, many thousands of the Jews standing about the cross believed.

CAPUT LIV.

1296DVERS. 1.-- Lauda, sterilis. (HIER.) Hactenus de nativitate, passione, resurrectione, etc., usque ad tam de Judaeis quam de gentilibus congregatam.

Lauda, sterilis. Apostolus, juxta LXX: Laetare, sterilis, quae non paris, erumpe et clama quae non parturis, quia multi filii desertae (Gal. IV), etc. Nos autem fratres secundum Isaac, repromissionis filii sumus.

VERS. 3.-- Ad dexteram enim: ad omnia loca mundi. Vel, nec elata prosperis, nec fracta adversis. Vel, ad bona futurae et praesentis.

VERS. 4.-- Noli timere. (ID.) Cum turris Babel aedificaretur, etc., usque ad qui funiculus.

VERS. 6.-- Quia ut mulierem. (ID.) Qui sequuntur 1297Atropologiam et in locis difficillimis, etc., usque ad stolam osculumque porrigit.

VERS. 9.-- Sicut in diebus Noe, etc. Ut credat sanctorum congregatio misericordiam Domini sempiternam, et ad punctum esse desertos, ut amici Dei foedere aeterno fiant: majorum ponit exempla.

VERS. 14.-- Recede procul. (HIER.) Consolatus est Ecclesiam de gentibus, promittens eis spirituales gratias: nunc docet quid agat, ne adversarios timeat.

VERS. 15.-- Ecce accola. Hoc ad se invertunt Judaei, tanquam sint futuri proselyti ex gentibus tempore Christi sui.

VERS. 16.-- Ecce ego. Non quod sim causa perditionis eorum, sed adversarius pugnans, victis perditionis, victoribus causa est praemii.

1297BVERS. 17.-- Haec est haereditas. Ne justus tribuatus deficiat, addit praemia.

CHAPTER LIV.

1296DVERS. 1.-- Praise, thou barren. (HIER.) Thus far concerning the Nativity, the Passion, the Resurrection, etc., as far as gathered together both from the Jews and from the Gentiles.

Praise, thou barren. The Apostle, according to the Seventy: Rejoice, thou barren, that bearest not, break forth and cry, thou that travailest not, for many are the children of the desolate (Gal. IV), etc. But we, brethren, after Isaac, are the children of the promise.

VERS. 3.-- For to the right hand: to all the places of the world. Or, neither lifted up by prosperity, nor broken by adversity. Or, to the goods of the future life and of the present.

VERS. 4.-- Fear not. (ID.) When the tower of Babel was being built, etc., as far as who a cord.

VERS. 6.-- For as a woman. (ID.) They who follow 1297Atropology, even in the most difficult places, etc., as far as he holds out the robe and the kiss.

VERS. 9.-- As in the days of Noe, etc. That the congregation of the saints may believe the mercy of the Lord to be everlasting, and that they are forsaken but for a moment, that they may become the friends of God by an eternal covenant: he sets forth the examples of the fathers.

VERS. 14.-- Depart far off. (HIER.) He has consoled the Church from among the Gentiles, promising her spiritual graces: now he teaches what she is to do, that she fear not her adversaries.

VERS. 15.-- Behold, an inhabitant. This the Jews turn to themselves, as though there were to be proselytes from among the Gentiles in the time of their own Christ.

VERS. 16. Behold I. Not that I am the cause of their perdition, but the adversary who fights is the cause of perdition to the conquered, of reward to the conquerors.

1297BVERS. 17.-- This is the inheritance. Lest the just man, being afflicted, fail, he adds the rewards.

CAPUT LV.

VERS. 1.-- Omnes sitientes, venite. (HIER.) Quia vas fictum contra Ecclesiam dixerat conterendum, et omnem linguam armatam contra scientiam Dei superandam, provocat credentes ad fluvium Dei, qui repletus est aquis.

Comedite. Audiendo, loquendo, legendo, meditando, operando, corde retinendo. Divina siquidem Scriptura, et potus est, aperta: et cibus, occulta. Ipsa etiam et aqua est et panis qui de coelo descendit.

Quare appenditis argentum. Pensatis sacra eloquia, sed non refectionem. Qui solam speciem litterae custoditis, de spirituali intelligentia pinguedinem 1297Cinternae refectionis amittitis.

VERS. 4.-- Ecce testem. Provocavit Judaeos ad credendum, sed quia sciebat illis non credentibus gentiles esse credituros, dicit se filium misisse qui praecepta ipsius populis nuntiaret.

VERS. 5.-- Dominum Deum tuum. (ID.) Quando Pater dicitur, Pater Christi secundum divinitatem dicitur Pater gloriae: secundum humanitatem, Pater Domini nostri Jesu Christi.

VERS. 6.-- Quaerite Dominum. Haec pertinent ad Judaeos vel ad gentes specialiter, et ad unumquemque generaliter. Propheta ad Judaeos: Quaerite Dominum, quasi diceret: Gentes suscipiunt misericordias David, et vos dedignamini, sed agite poenitentiam, et convertimini dum tempus habetis.

1297DDum prope. (ID.) Ne vestris vitiis recedat, qui appropinquat appropinquantibus sibi, et filio revertenti laetus occurrit.

VERS. 8.-- Non enim. Ne putetis difficile esse quod spondeo: considerate quod inter mea et vestra consilia multa sit differentia: et quanta naturae, tanta voluntatis diversitas.

VERS. 10.-- Et quomodo. (ID.) Alia similitudine ostendit quia multus est ad ignoscendum.

Panem comedenti. Unde Salomon: Mitte panem tuum super aquas, quia in multitudine dierum invenies eum, hoc est, panem doctrinae tuae et gratiam spiritualem cunctis infunde, quia in novissimo praemia recipies

1298AVERS. 11.-- Non revertetur. Sed postquam meam impleverit voluntatem in terris, redibit et sedebit a dextris meis, secundum illud, Sede a dextris meis, etc.

VERS. 12.-- Montes, scilicet animae sanctorum quae pro varietate virtutum montes appellantur et colles.

VERS. 13.-- Pro saliuncula. Saliuncula, aculeis et punctionibus plena. Abies, alta, procera, fabricandis domibus apta, pice fecunda.

Et erit Dominus. Alii. Et erit Dominus in nomen et in signum sempiternum quod non deficiet.

CHAPTER LV.

VERS. 1.-- All you that thirst, come. (HIER.) Because he had said that the vessel fashioned against the Church was to be broken, and every tongue armed against the knowledge of God to be overcome, he calls believers to the river of God, which is filled with waters.

Eat ye. By hearing, by speaking, by reading, by meditating, by working, by keeping in the heart. For the divine Scripture is both drink, when open, and food, when hidden. It is also both the water and the bread that came down from heaven.

Why do you weigh out silver. You weigh the sacred words, but not the refreshment. You who keep the appearance of the letter alone lose, from the spiritual understanding, the fatness 1297Cof the inward refreshment.

VERS. 4.-- Behold, a witness. He challenged the Jews to believe, but because he knew that, when they did not believe, the Gentiles were going to believe, he says that he has sent his Son to announce his precepts to the peoples.

VERS. 5.-- The Lord thy God. (ID.) When he is called Father, the Father of Christ is called, according to the divinity, the Father of glory; according to the humanity, the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ.

VERS. 6.-- Seek ye the Lord. These things pertain to the Jews or to the Gentiles specially, and to each one generally. The prophet to the Jews: Seek ye the Lord, as though he said: The Gentiles receive the mercies of David, and you disdain them; but do penance, and be converted while you have time.

1297DWhilst he is near. (ID.) Lest by your vices he withdraw, who draws near to those who draw near to him, and runs to meet the returning son with gladness.

VERS. 8.-- For not. Do not think that what I promise is hard: consider that between my counsels and yours there is much difference: and as great as is the diversity of nature, so great is that of will.

VERS. 10.-- And how. (ID.) By another likeness he shows that he is abundant in pardoning.

Bread to the eater. Whence Solomon: Cast thy bread upon the waters, for in the multitude of days thou shalt find it [Vulg. Eccle 11:1 Mitte panem tuum super transeuntes aquas, quia post tempora multa invenies illum, "upon the waters that pass by… after many times"], that is, pour out the bread of thy doctrine and spiritual grace upon all, because in the last day thou shalt receive rewards

1298AVERS. 11.-- It shall not return. But after it has fulfilled my will upon earth, it shall return and sit at my right hand, according to that, Sit thou at my right hand, etc.

VERS. 12.-- The mountains, namely the souls of the saints, which for the variety of their virtues are called mountains and hills.

VERS. 13.-- Instead of the nard. Nard, full of prickles and of stings. The fir, tall, lofty, apt for building houses, rich in pitch.

And the Lord shall be. Others. And the Lord shall be for a name and for an everlasting sign which shall not fail.

CAPUT LVI.

VERS. 1.-- Haec dicit Dominus. Custodite judicium. (HIER.) Terminato gentium vaticinio, quae in adventu Christi de saliuncula et urtica in abietem myrtumque mutantur: loquitur ad auditores illius 1298Btemporis, ut se parent ad adventum Salvatoris, qui est misericordia et justitia.

Custodite judicium. (ID.) Non accipientes personam in judicio. Domini enim judicium est, qui judicat judicantes bona.

VERS. 2.-- Custodiens sabbatum. (ID.) Ne sit, scilicet de sex diebus quibus factus est mundus. De quibus apostoli non erant, quibus dicitur: Si de mundo essetis, mundus quod suum erat diligeret, etc. Ego elegi vos de mundo, idcirco odit vos mundus (Joan. XV).

VERS. 3.-- Et non dicat. (ID.) Hoc contra Judaeos, qui sibi tantum adventum Christi vendicabant, et alios desperare cogebant. Sed non est distinctio Judaei et Graeci (Rom. X).

Eunuchus. (ID.) Propter regnum coelorum, qui 1298Cmortificant membra sua super terram, etc., usque ad quod de semine spiritualis operis dicitur, non prolis.

VERS. 4.-- Quia haec dicit Dominus. (ID.) Duo proposuit, proselytos scilicet et eunuchos, etc., usque ad ne se putent subjacere maledictioni legis.

VERS. 6.-- Et filios advenae: post eunuchos ad advenas transit et praemia promittit.

In servos. Qualis Paulus qui de se ait: Paulus servus Christi Jesu, et Moyses famulus Dei.

VERS. 7.-- Holocausta eorum. Mortificate membra vestra, quae sunt super terram (Colos. III). Et Exhibeatis corpora vestra hostiam Deo viventem. Holocaustum totum offertur Deo, et incenditur. Victimae pars sacerdotibus datur. Holocaustum Deo tribuit, qui mundum relinquit, ut semper vacet orationi. Victimam, 1298Dqui Deo servit et quae mundi sunt disponit juste.

Super altari meo. Fidem super quam omnia offeruntur, sine qua impossibile est Deo placere. Unde: Justus autem in fide sua vivet.

VERS. 9.-- Omnes bestiae. Vox prophetae, qui advocat Romanos ad excidium Jerusalem, quibus pene totus orbis serviebat. Vel per bestias, daemones: quibus ad devorandum Judaei traditi sunt.

VERS. 10.-- Caeci. Non habentes Christum verum lumen, vel fidem et alias virtutes.

Canes. (ID.) Sicut canes gregem, sic praelati debent custodire plebem. Canes impudentissimi. Quod est: Sicut canes sine discretione etiam bonos saepe persequuntur, sic et Judaei impudenter clamaverunt, 1299ACrucifige, crucifige eum (Luc. XXIII), nec Domini saturati sunt opprobriis.

VERS. 12.-- Venite sumamus. Sic pervertebant qui corrigere debuerant. Et quia omnes peccaverunt, traditi sunt in manus Romanorum.

CHAPTER LVI.

VERS. 1.-- Thus saith the Lord. Keep ye judgment. (HIER.) The prophecy of the Gentiles being ended, who at the coming of Christ are changed from nard and nettle into fir and myrtle: he speaks to the hearers of that 1298Btime, that they may prepare themselves for the coming of the Saviour, who is mercy and justice.

Keep ye judgment. (ID.) Not accepting the person in judgment. For the judgment is the Lord's, who judges those who judge good things.

VERS. 2.-- That keepeth the sabbath. (ID.) Let it not be, that is, of the six days in which the world was made. Of which the apostles were not, to whom it is said: If you were of the world, the world would love its own, etc. I have chosen you out of the world, therefore the world hateth you (Joan. XV).

VERS. 3.-- And let him not say. (ID.) This against the Jews, who claimed the coming of Christ for themselves alone, and forced others to despair. But there is no distinction of the Jew and the Greek (Rom. X).

The eunuch. (ID.) For the kingdom of heaven, they who 1298Cmortify their members upon earth, etc., as far as which is said of the seed of a spiritual work, not of offspring.

VERS. 4.-- For thus saith the Lord. (ID.) He set forth two, namely proselytes and eunuchs, etc., as far as lest they think themselves subject to the curse of the law.

VERS. 6.-- And the children of the stranger: after the eunuchs he passes to the strangers and promises rewards.

For servants. Such as Paul, who says of himself: Paul, the servant of Christ Jesus; and Moses, the servant of God.

VERS. 7.-- Their holocausts. Mortify your members, which are upon the earth (Colos. III). And That you present your bodies a victim living unto God [Vulg. Rom 12:1 hostiam viventem, sanctam, Deo placentem, "a living victim, holy, pleasing unto God"]. The holocaust is offered whole to God, and is burnt. A part of the victim is given to the priests. He renders a holocaust to God who leaves the world, that he may always be free for prayer. A victim, 1298Dhe who serves God and disposes justly the things that are of the world.

Upon my altar. The faith upon which all things are offered, without which it is impossible to please God. Whence: But the just man shall live in his faith.

VERS. 9.-- All ye beasts. The voice of the prophet, who calls the Romans to the destruction of Jerusalem, whom almost the whole world served. Or, by the beasts, the demons: to whom the Jews were handed over to be devoured.

VERS. 10.-- Blind. Not having Christ the true light, or faith and the other virtues.

Dogs. (ID.) As dogs guard the flock, so ought prelates to guard the people. Most impudent dogs. Which is: As dogs without discernment often pursue even good men, so the Jews too impudently cried out, 1299ACrucify him, crucify him (Luc. XXIII), nor were they sated with reproaches against the Lord.

VERS. 12.-- Come, let us take. Thus did they pervert who ought to have corrected. And because all had sinned, they were handed over into the hands of the Romans.

CAPUT LVII.

VERS. 1.-- Justus perit. (HIER.) Haec referunt Judaei ad Isaiam, qui serra lignea sectus est a Manasse impiissimo rege, et alios prophetas, quos ipse occidit, viros misericordiae. Sed verius hoc de illo dicitur, qui est summe justus.

Et nemo est. Propter speculatores caecos et canes mutos.

Qui recogitet: quia caeci et muti ignorantes sapientiam, non cogitant ea quae Dei sunt.

1299BVERS. 2.-- Veniat pax, requiescat in cubili suo, qui ambulavit in. (ID.) Propheta precatur ut Christus veniat, resurgens a mortuis requiescat in cubili in gloria, scilicet paternae majestatis, vel in Ecclesia, in qua in directione ambulavit.

VERS. 6.-- Libamen. Vinum, oleum et hujusmodi liquores: sacrificium panis, victima et hujusmodi.

VERS. 7.-- Super montem excelsum. Haeretici quoque, etc., usque ad ut conculcentur ab hominibus.

VERS. 8.-- Et post ostium: Quasi diceret: Non solum in montibus idola adorasti, sed etiam post ostium posuisti, ut ingrediens videres et semper adorares.

Quia juxta me discooperuisti. Metaphora adulterae sicut supra meretricis, etc., usque ad omnibus exposita intrantibus.

VERS. 9.-- 1299CMisisti legatos. Ut afferrent longinqua idola, quasi non sufficiant vicina. Et in hoc Babylonios et Aegyptios deos significat.

VERS. 10.-- In multitudine viae tuae laborasti. Qui ambulat via regia non laborat. Una est via veritatis quae dicit: Ego sum via, veritas et vita (Joan. XIV): Multae sunt viae mundanorum, unde, Non sicut viae meae sunt viae vestrae (Isa. XL).

VERS. 13.-- Qui autem fiduciam habet mei. (ID.) Qui idola non coluerunt, vel in captivitatem ducti poenituerunt, sub Zorobabel et aliis ducibus reversi, Deum suum in loco suo adoraverunt secundum Hebraeum.

Possidebit montem sanctum tuum, scilicet legem Veteris et Novi testamenti; utrumque enim in monte 1299Ddatum. Et ad praemiorum altitudinem invitat, vel Christum.

VERS. 14.-- Et dicam ego Christus scilicet apostolis, Viam feci, bene praedicando, praebete iter in exemplum eis bene operando; auferte offendicula per baptismum peccata abluendo, idolorum cultum praedicatione amovendo.

VERS. 15.-- Quia haec dicit. Increpatione contra Jerusalem finita, venit ad eos qui confidunt in Domino, quorum reversioni iter jusserat praeparari. Unde et a laudibus Dei incipit, et quae redeunti populo promiserit, consequenter ostendit.

VERS. 16.-- Non enim in sempiternum litigabo. (ID.) Non in sempiternum servabo iram meam, neque 1300Acausabor cum creatura mea, sed percutiam in praesenti, ut sanem in futuro.

Quia spiritus. Pax, gaudium, vita omnium, etc., usque ad specialiter ad Judaeos.

VERS. 17.-- Abscondi a te, etc., scilicet auxilium meum, ut amplius desideres dicens: Ostende faciem tuam, et salvi erimus (Psal. LXXIX).

Abiit. (ID.) Quod quidam intelligens ait: Tota die contristatus ingrediebar (Psal. XXXVII).

VERS. 19.-- Creavi: quasi diceret: Liberationem a captivitate, quam per Jeremiam post septuaginta annos promisi, opere complevi

Creavi fructum labiorum pacem.

VERS. 20.-- Impii autem. Supra de illis qui in captivitate poenitentiam egerunt, et ideo liberati 1300Bsunt, et de illis qui post ascensionem crediderunt. Hic de illis specialiter loquitur, qui in infidelitate sua remanserunt.

CHAPTER LVII.

VERS. 1.-- The just perisheth. (HIER.) These things the Jews refer to Isaias, who was cut in two with a wooden saw by Manasses, that most impious king, and to the other prophets, whom he himself slew, men of mercy. But more truly this is said of him who is supremely just.

And there is none. On account of the blind watchmen and the dumb dogs.

That considereth: because the blind and the dumb, being ignorant of wisdom, consider not the things that are God's.

1299BVERS. 2.-- Let peace come, let him rest in his bed, who hath walked in. (ID.) The prophet prays that Christ may come, and, rising from the dead, may rest in his bed in glory, namely of the Father's majesty, or in the Church, in which he walked in uprightness.

VERS. 6.-- A libation. Wine, oil and liquids of this kind: a sacrifice of bread, a victim and things of this kind.

VERS. 7.-- Upon a high mountain. Heretics also, etc., as far as that they be trodden down by men.

VERS. 8.-- And behind the door: As though he said: Not only upon the mountains didst thou adore idols, but thou didst also set them behind the door, that going in thou mightest see them and always adore them.

Because thou hast uncovered beside me. The metaphor of an adulteress, as above of a harlot, etc., as far as laid open to all who enter.

VERS. 9.-- 1299CThou hast sent legates. That they might bring far-off idols, as though those near at hand did not suffice. And in this he signifies the gods of the Babylonians and the Egyptians.

VERS. 10.-- Thou hast laboured in the multitude of thy way. He who walks by the king's highway does not labour. There is one way of truth, which says: I am the way, the truth and the life (Joan. XIV): many are the ways of worldly men, whence, My ways are not as your ways [Vulg. Is 55:8 Non enim cogitationes meae cogitationes vestrae, neque viae vestrae viae meae, "For my thoughts are not your thoughts, nor your ways my ways"] (Isa. XL).

VERS. 13.-- But he that hath confidence in me. (ID.) They who did not worship idols, or who, led into captivity, repented, and returning under Zorobabel and the other leaders, adored their God in his own place according to the Hebrew.

He shall possess thy holy mountain, [Vulg. Is 57:13 possidebit montem sanctum meum, "MY holy mountain"; Migne prints tuum] namely the law of the Old and of the New Testament; for both were given 1299Don a mountain. And he invites to the height of the rewards, or to Christ.
