VERS. 14.-- And I will say, I, Christ, that is, to the apostles, I have made a way, by preaching well: furnish the road by way of example to them by working well; take away the stumbling-blocks by washing away sins through baptism, by removing the worship of idols through preaching.

VERS. 15.-- For thus saith. His rebuke against Jerusalem being ended, he comes to those who trust in the Lord, for whose return he had commanded the road to be prepared. Whence he both begins from the praises of God, and shows in order what he has promised to the returning people.

VERS. 16.-- For I will not contend for ever. (ID.) I will not keep my anger for ever, nor 1300Aplead against my creature, but I will strike in the present, that I may heal in the future.

For the spirit. Peace, joy, the life of all, etc., as far as specially to the Jews.

VERS. 17.-- I hid from thee, etc., namely my help, that thou mayest desire it the more, saying: Show thy face, and we shall be saved (Psal. LXXIX).

He went away. (ID.) Which one understanding said: All the day I went sorrowful (Psal. XXXVII).

VERS. 19.-- I created: as though he said: The deliverance from captivity, which through Jeremias I promised after seventy years, I have fulfilled in deed

I created the fruit of the lips, peace.

VERS. 20.-- But the wicked. Above concerning those who did penance in the captivity, and were therefore delivered, and 1300Bconcerning those who believed after the Ascension. Here he speaks specially of those who remained in their unbelief.

CAPUT LVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Clama. (HIER.) Dominus ad prophetas vel ad apostolos: finito scilicet priori capite in quo pax justis, et nulla requies nuntiatur impiis.

Quasi tuba. (ID.) Ubi apostolis, etc., usque ad sic Israel peccato mortuus audiat scelera sua.

Populo meo. Ac si dicat: Videant qualem Patrem perdiderunt, qui etiam peccatores populum suum vocat.

VERS. 2.-- Quasi. Hoc est, quare ego non exaudiam eos sicut illos qui in Ecclesia justitiam faciunt, 1300Cjuste et pie viventes, Christum verum Deum et verum hominem confitentes.

Rogant. (ID.) Alia temeritas Judaeorum, quasi confidentes in conscientia bona judicium justum postulant, secundum illud: Judica me, Domine, quoniam ego in innocentia mea ingressus sum.

VERS. 3.-- Quare jejunavimus. Obliti sunt veteris historiae, quod Dominus non respexit ad munera Cain, eo quod non recte dividebat cum fratre suo, cui charitatem Deo invidebat.

Ecce in die. Jejunium Deo acceptum est, non solum a cibis, sed ab omnibus rebus illicitis abstinere.

Repetitis. Qui repetit non habentem, Deo facit violentiam.

VERS. 4.-- Nolite jejunare sicut usque ad hanc 1300Ddiem. Ne videatur reprobare jejunium, docet quomodo sit jejunandum.

VERS. 5.-- Nunquid tale est jejunium.

VERS. 6.-- Colligationes. (ID.) Peccata intelligimus, etc., usque ad domesticos seminis tui ne despexeris.

VERS. 7.-- Frange esurienti panem. (ID.) Panem esurienti frangimus, etc., usque ad sed animae saturitas.

Et carnem tuam. (ID.) Proximum in necessitate qualibet recognosce similem tibi, considerans te mortalem et passibilem.

VERS. 8.-- Lumen tuum. Sicut matutina lux solvit tenebras, ita lumen veritatis fugat errores.

1301AJustitia. Christus qui omnes justificat, anteibit ad gloriam.

Et gloria Domini colliget te, id est suscipiet ad se, qui dixit: Ubi ego sum, illic et minister meus erit (Joan. XII).

VERS. 9.-- Digitum extendere. Proximos notare, detrahere. Id est non solum non cogites, et non facias haec, sed ne detrahas.

VERS. 11.-- Hortus irruguus, voluptatis paradisus, quem plantaverat Dominus Deus a principio, scilicet Ecclesiam. Fons in medio Christus est, cujus non deficiunt aquae doctrinae spiritualis, quae Ecclesiam irrigant. Et hoc est, Et erit quasi hortus irriguus, o fidelis vel Ecclesia.

Sicut fons. Assimilaberis Christo, capiti tuo.

1301BVERS. 12.-- Aedificabuntur (HIER.) Judaei referunt hoc ad restaurationem quae facta est sub Zorobabel et Jesu Sacerdote magno; vel ad illam quam delirant futuram in ultimo. Melius ad Ecclesiam haec referri possunt.

Fundamenta (ID.) Apostolos, prophetas et similes: Unde, Fundamenta ejus in montibus sanctis (Psal. LXXXVI). Fundamentum autem singulari numero Christus est, unde, Fundamentum aliud nemo potest ponere, praeter id quod positum est, quod est Christus Jesus (I Cor. III).

Iniquitatum, alias in quietem, scilicet semitas erroris in quietem vel veram pacem.

VERS. 14.-- Terrae. Viventium quae sursum non deorsum, unde Loth dicitur: In monte salvum te fac.

1301CHaereditate. Coelesti, quam dedi Jacob cujus filius es per fidem et operationem, vitia atque peccata supplantando. Cujus enim quis imitator fuerit, ejus filius dicitur.

CHAPTER LVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Cry. (HIER.) The Lord to the prophets or to the apostles: the former chapter being ended, in which peace is announced to the just, and no rest to the wicked.

As a trumpet. (ID.) Where to the apostles, etc., as far as so let Israel, dead by sin, hear his own crimes.

To my people. As if he said: Let them see what a Father they have lost, who calls even sinners his people.

VERS. 2.-- As. That is, why should I not hear them as I hear those who do justice in the Church, 1300Cliving justly and piously, confessing Christ true God and true man.

They ask. (ID.) Another rashness of the Jews, as though, trusting in a good conscience, they demand a just judgment, according to that: Judge me, O Lord, for I have walked in my innocence.

VERS. 3.-- Why have we fasted. They have forgotten the ancient history, that the Lord had no regard to the gifts of Cain, because he did not divide rightly with his brother, to whom he grudged charity towards God.

Behold, in the day. A fast acceptable to God is to abstain not only from foods, but from all unlawful things.

You exact. He who exacts from one who has not, does violence to God.

VERS. 4.-- Do not fast as until this 1300Dday. Lest he seem to reprove fasting, he teaches how one ought to fast.

VERS. 5.-- Is this such a fast.

VERS. 6.-- The bundles. (ID.) We understand sins, etc., as far as despise not the household of thy own seed.

VERS. 7.-- Break thy bread to the hungry. (ID.) We break bread to the hungry, etc., as far as but the fullness of the soul.

And thy own flesh. (ID.) Recognize thy neighbour, in whatever need, as like to thyself, considering that thou art mortal and passible.

VERS. 8.-- Thy light. As the morning light dissolves the darkness, so the light of truth puts errors to flight.

1301AJustice. Christ, who justifies all, shall go before unto glory.

And the glory of the Lord shall gather thee up, that is, shall receive thee to himself, who said: Where I am, there also shall my minister be (Joan. XII).

VERS. 9.-- To stretch out the finger. To mark thy neighbours, to disparage them. That is, not only art thou not to think these things and not to do them, but thou art not even to disparage.

VERS. 11.-- A watered garden, the paradise of delight, which the Lord God had planted from the beginning, namely the Church. The fountain in the midst is Christ, whose waters of spiritual doctrine do not fail, which water the Church. And this is, And he shall be as a watered garden [Vulg. Is 58:11 et eris quasi hortus irriguus, "and thou shalt be" — the second person, which the gloss's own address keeps], O faithful one, or O Church.

As a fountain. Thou shalt be made like to Christ, thy head.

1301BVERS. 12.-- They shall be built (HIER.) The Jews refer this to the restoration which was made under Zorobabel and Jesus the high priest; or to that which they rave will come at the last. Better may these things be referred to the Church.

The foundations (ID.) The apostles, the prophets and the like: Whence, The foundations thereof are in the holy mountains (Psal. LXXXVI). But the foundation in the singular number is Christ, whence, Other foundation no man can lay, but that which is laid, which is Christ Jesus (I Cor. III).

Of iniquities, otherwise into rest, namely the paths of error into rest or into true peace.

VERS. 14.-- Of the earth. Of the living, which is above, not below, whence it is said to Lot: Save thyself in the mountain.

1301CWith the inheritance. The heavenly one, which I gave to Jacob, whose son thou art by faith and by working, by supplanting vices and sins. For whosoever has been the imitator of a man is called his son.

CAPUT LIX.

VERS. 2.-- Sed iniquitates vestrae. Ostendit quare non exauditi et in captivitatem ducti.

VERS. 3.-- Manus ejus. (HIER.) Iniquitates per partes exponit, etc., usque ad et tandem in Filium Dei inclamantes: Sanguis ejus super nos et super filios nostros.

VERS. 4.-- Conceperunt, (ID.) Qui exspectant Antichristum. Quem enim multo labore perquirunt in iniquitate suscipient.

VERS. 5.-- Ova. (ID.) Ex ovo fetus, ex corde 1301Dconsilium, ex quo actus. Ova ergo ruperunt, dum corda iniqua et venenosa consilia verbis ostenderunt, dicentes: Reus est mortis (Matth. XXVI).

Aspidum. Aspis ad primam vocem incantatoris alteram aurem terrae infigit, alteram cauda obturat, ne possit incantari: sic Judaei inhaerentes terrenis, ne locum et gentem perderent metuentes, Christi praedicationem repulerunt, venenati venenatorum filii.

Erumpet in regulum. Ex ovis aspidis regulus, qui et basiliscus, ex Judaeis Antichristus. Ex eis enim nascetur, flatu suo, id est sermone venenoso multos occidens volantes ad patriam coelestem. Occidit enim basiliscus flatu suo aves volantes per aera.

VERS. 9.-- Propter hoc (ID.) Propheta in persona 1302Apopuli poenitentis respondet, post accusationem, quid loqui debeant poenitentes.

VERS. 11.-- Rugiemus quasi ursi omnes. Judaeorum meditatio et doctrina in sacra Scriptura rugitui ursi et gemitui columbae comparatur, quasi inutilis et vana, sicut et illa. Contra subjectos quoque saeviunt ut ursi; contra majores vel aequales, gemunt ut columbae.

VERS. 15.-- Veritas in. (ID.) Christus, qui est via, etc., usque ad ut Christi persona monstretur, quam deserentes, secuti sunt mendacium.

VERS. 16.-- Et vidit. (ID.) Ostenso, quia poenituerunt, ostendit non placuisse Domino poenitentiam eorum, quia non puro corde plures poenituerunt.

Quia non est vir. Mente omnes effeminati sunt, 1302Bsicut et illud, Vocavi et non erat qui audiret.

Et salvavit sibi. Pater, scilicet per Filium gentes, Et justitia ejus, vel misericordia secundum LXX confirmabit eum qui voluerit converti: non merito, sed misericordia et justitia. Justum enim est ut poenitenti creaturae Creator parcat, quae meritis suis salvari non poterat.

VERS. 19.-- Et timebunt qui. Praemonstrata vindicta Dei in Judaeos per Romanos, describit fidem gentium, de quatuor partibus mundi venientium, audita praedicatione apostolorum

Quasi fluvius. Quia citissime mundum sua praedicatione et fide implevit.

Quem Spiritus Domini. (ID.). Ad redimendum genus humanum Spiritus sanctus scilicet. Unde: Qui 1302Cconceptus est de Spiritu sancto. Et, Requiescit super eum Spiritus Domini (Isa. XI), etc.

VERS. 21.-- Hoc foedus, ut scilicet eos sanguine Filii redimam, et ipsi praecepta mea faciant, et memoriter teneant et hoc est, Spiritus meus, qui est in te,

CHAPTER LIX.

VERS. 2.-- But your iniquities. He shows why they were not heard and were led into captivity.

VERS. 3.-- His hands. (HIER.) He sets forth the iniquities part by part, etc., as far as and at last crying out against the Son of God: His blood be upon us and upon our children.

VERS. 4.-- They have conceived, (ID.) They who await Antichrist. For him whom they seek out with much labour they shall receive in iniquity.

VERS. 5.-- Eggs. (ID.) From the egg the offspring, from the heart 1301Dthe counsel, from which the act. They have broken the eggs therefore, when they showed forth in words their wicked hearts and their venomous counsels, saying: He is guilty of death (Matth. XXVI).

Of asps. The asp, at the first voice of the charmer, fastens one ear into the earth and stops the other with its tail, that it may not be charmed: so the Jews, cleaving to earthly things, fearing lest they lose their place and their nation, repelled the preaching of Christ, venomous sons of venomous fathers.

It shall break out into a basilisk. From the eggs of the asp the cockatrice, which is also the basilisk; from the Jews, Antichrist. For from them he shall be born, killing by his breath, that is, by his venomous speech, many who are flying to the heavenly country. For the basilisk kills with its breath the birds flying through the air.

VERS. 9.-- For this (ID.) The prophet, in the person 1302Aof the penitent people, answers, after the accusation, what the penitent ought to say.

VERS. 11.-- We shall roar all of us like bears. The meditation and teaching of the Jews in Holy Scripture is compared to the roaring of a bear and the moaning of a dove, as useless and vain, like those. Against their subjects too they rage as bears; against their superiors or their equals, they moan as doves.

VERS. 15.-- Truth in. (ID.) Christ, who is the way, etc., as far as that the person of Christ may be shown, whom, forsaking, they followed a lie.

VERS. 16.-- And he saw. (ID.) Having shown that they repented, he shows that their penance did not please the Lord, because many repented not with a pure heart.

That there is not a man. All are effeminate in mind, 1302Bas also that, I called and there was none that heard.

And he saved for himself. The Father, namely, through the Son, the Gentiles, And his justice, or his mercy according to the Seventy, shall confirm him who has willed to be converted: not by merit, but by mercy and justice. For it is just that the Creator spare the penitent creature, which could not be saved by its own merits.

VERS. 19.-- And they shall fear who. The vengeance of God upon the Jews through the Romans having been shown beforehand, he describes the faith of the Gentiles coming from the four parts of the world, when the preaching of the apostles has been heard

As a river. Because most swiftly he filled the world with his preaching and with faith.

Whom the Spirit of the Lord. (ID.). To redeem the human race, the Holy Spirit, namely. Whence: Who 1302Cwas conceived of the Holy Spirit. And, The Spirit of the Lord resteth upon him [Vulg. Is 11:2 Et requiescet super eum spiritus Domini, "shall rest upon him," a future] (Isa. XI), etc.

VERS. 21.-- This is my covenant, that is, that I may redeem them by the blood of my Son, and that they may do my precepts, and hold me in memory; and this is, My spirit, which is in thee,

CAPUT LX.

VERS. 1.-- Surge, illuminare. (HIER). Haec referunt Judaei ad tempus Christi sui, etc., usque ad ut vel pro his suscipiant Dei Filium.

Surge. (ID.) In Ecclesiis, quae occidisti in synagogis: surge in gentibus, quae cecidisti in Judaeis. Surge per confessionem, per virtutes quae cecidisti per negotiationem, per vitia, et cum surrexeris, illuminare, id est accipe Christi fidem.

Lumen. Tibi longe ante promissum ad redemptionem 1302Dtuam venturum, quod illuminat omnem hominem venientem in hunc mundum, scilicet qui dicit: Ego sum lux mundi (Joan. I, VIII), a quo illuminaberis si fidem Christi susceperis.

Gloria Domini. Ad illuminandum. Filius, scilicet sicut sol ortus illuminat mundum, in cujus figura Moysi et Salomoni, alteri tabernaculum, alteri templum aedificanti ostenditur gloria Domini. Gloria quoque Domini fuit mortui resuscitatio, leprosi mundatio, et hujusmodi.

VERS. 2.-- Terram. (ID.) Judaeos terrena amantes, quibus dicitur: Bona terrae comedetis, quod carnaliter accipiunt.

VERS. 3.-- Ortus tui. (ID.) Quoniam scilicet ex 1303Aaqua et Spiritu sancto nata es, omnes in splendore ortus primitivae Ecclesiae ambulamus, quia eamdem fidem habemus, eodem baptismo renascimur.

VERS. 4.-- Leva in circuitu oculos. Cui simile: Levate oculos vestros et videte regiones, quia jam albae sunt ad messem (Joan. IV).

Filii tui. (HIER.) Quos in fide genuisti: Unde Paulus: Per Evangelium ego vos genui (I Cor. IV). Primi fideles de Judaeis quando Spiritus sanctus datus est, erant in Jerusalem viri religiosi ex omni natione quae sub coelo est (Act. II), vel gentes ex omnibus partibus mundi.

De longe venient. Quia longe erant a Deo per idololatriam, unde: Vos qui eratis longe, facti estis prope (Ephes. II).

1303BDe latere. (ID.) Quidam codices habent, etc., usque ad, neque Hieronymi sensus ad hoc inclinatur.

VERS. 6.-- Camelorum. Camelus animal est gibbosum et oneriferum. Hi sunt divites onus divitiarum et peccatorum gibbum portantes.

Dromedarii. Dromedarius vel dromas, utrumque enim dicitur: est enim animal camelo minus, sed velocius, in quo figurantur gentes velociter ad fidem currentes.

Madian et Epha. Regiones trans Arabiam camelis et dromedariis abundantes, etc., usque ad quae per thus figuratur, Domino obtulerunt.

VERS. 7.-- Omne pecus Cedar congregabitur. (ID.) Simplices qui ante erant tenebrae et moeror, etc., usque ad ubi habitabant Saraceni de illius stirpe progeniti.

1303CArietes. Praedicatores, gregis duces, filii prophetarum qui ea quae prophetae praedixerunt de Christo impleta docuerunt.

Nabaioth, filius Ismaelis, et interpretatur prophetia.

Ministrabunt tibi. Sancti episcopi, presbyteri ministri Ecclesiae, corpus et sanguinem Christi fidelibus administrando.

VERS. 8.-- Qui sunt. Admiratur propheta gentes tam cito ad fidem currentes, vel primitiva Ecclesia:

Ad fenestras. Ad fenestras suas stant sancti custodientes quinque sensus corporis, ne per eos tanquam per fenestras intret mors in animas.

VERS. 9.-- Argentum eorum. Hoc est: Corde creditur ad justitiam, ore autem confessio fit ad salutem (Rom. X).

1303DVERS. 10.-- Filii peregrinorum. Principes gentium qui per orbem Ecclesias aedificaverunt, sicut Constantinus qui Romae duodecim ecclesias in honorem duodecim apostolorum fecit, et alii multi qui multas exstruxerunt ecclesias, et necessaria praebuerunt.

VERS. 13.-- Gloria Libani. Libanus mons est confinis terrae promissionis, etc., usque ad reges et principes qui ad Ecclesiam venerunt.

Abies. (ID.) Superbi, scilicet divites, philosophi. Divitias enim et scientiam sequitur superbia. Cyprianus et Hilarius excelsae in saeculo arbores, Ecclesiam Dei aedificaverunt. Arborum diversitas diversos in Ecclesia ordines significat. Ex quibus omnibus constructa est Ecclesia miro decore distincta.

1304ALocum pedum. Ecclesiam, in qua apostoli, etc., usque ad Ecclesiam sustinent institutis, miraculis et exemplis.

VERS. 14.-- Civitatem Domini. Civitas enim a communione dicitur, etc., usque ad qui sollicite circumiret et auxilium praeberet.

VERS. 15.-- Odio habita. Judaeis, quia fidem Christi praedicabat, legis observantias destruebat; gentibus quia unum Deum afferebat, idola nihil esse astruebat.

Non erat qui per te transiret. Transiret et benediceret tibi, etc., usque ad insuper superbia aliquando mala, aliquando bona:

VERS. 17.-- Pro lignis aes. (ID.) Per ligna et lapides, etc., usque ad alii fortes fide, ut Cyprianus, et alii martyres, sicut ferrum.

1304BVisitationem tuam. (ID.) Christum, vel apostolos pacificos, et praepositos justos, ut recte doceant et judicent.

VERS. 19.-- Non erit. Hoc de die judicii. Hoc capitulo cogimur quaecunque dicta sunt vel dicenda, ad ultimum tempus referre, quoniam coeli terraeque, solis atque lunae nobis cessabit officium, et erit ipse Dominus lumen in perpetuum.

VERS. 21.-- Germen. (ID.) Angeli, omnes electi, qui fidem quam habent operibus implent. Ipsi germen plantationis Domini et opus manuum ejus, quia ab ipso creati, et in Ecclesia per fidem plantati ad glorificandum creatorem suum.

VERS. 22.-- In tempore: adventus Christi. Vel in tempore opportuno vel praefinito. Cum miserit 1304CFilius hominis angelos, et colligent de regno ejus omnia scandala.

CHAPTER LX.

VERS. 1.-- Arise, be enlightened. (HIER). These things the Jews refer to the time of their own Christ, etc., as far as that at least on account of these things they may receive the Son of God.

Arise. (ID.) In the Churches, thou who didst go down in the synagogues: arise among the Gentiles, thou who didst fall among the Jews. Arise by confession, by the virtues, thou who didst fall by trafficking, by vices; and when thou hast arisen, be enlightened, that is, receive the faith of Christ.

Light. Promised to thee long before as about to come for thy redemption, 1302Dwhich enlighteneth every man that cometh into this world, namely he who says: I am the light of the world (Joan. I, VIII), by whom thou shalt be enlightened if thou hast received the faith of Christ.

The glory of the Lord. For enlightening. The Son, namely, just as the risen sun enlightens the world; in figure of which the glory of the Lord was shown to Moses and to Solomon, the one building the tabernacle, the other the temple. The glory of the Lord also was the raising of the dead man, the cleansing of the leper, and things of this kind.

VERS. 2.-- The earth. (ID.) The Jews, who love earthly things, to whom it is said: You shall eat the good things of the land, which they take carnally.

VERS. 3.-- Of thy rising. (ID.) Because, that is, thou wast born of 1303Awater and of the Holy Spirit, we all walk in the brightness of the rising of the primitive Church, because we have the same faith, and are born again by the same baptism.

VERS. 4.-- Lift up thine eyes round about. To which is like: Lift up your eyes and see the countries, for they are already white unto the harvest (Joan. IV).

Thy sons. (HIER.) Whom thou hast begotten in the faith: Whence Paul: Through the Gospel I have begotten you (I Cor. IV). The first believers from among the Jews, when the Holy Spirit was given, there were in Jerusalem religious men out of every nation which is under heaven (Act. II), or the Gentiles from all the parts of the world.

They shall come from afar. Because they were far from God through idolatry, whence: You who were far off, are made nigh (Ephes. II).

1303BFrom the side. (ID.) Certain codices have, etc., as far as, nor does Jerome's sense incline to this.

VERS. 6.-- Of camels. The camel is a humped and burden-bearing animal. These are the rich, carrying the burden of riches and the hump of sins.

Dromedaries. Dromedary or dromas, for it is called both ways: for it is an animal smaller than the camel, but swifter, in which are figured the Gentiles running swiftly to the faith.

Madian and Epha. Regions across Arabia abounding in camels and dromedaries, etc., as far as which is figured by the frankincense, they offered to the Lord.

VERS. 7.-- All the flocks of Cedar shall be gathered together. (ID.) The simple, who before were darkness and mourning, etc., as far as where the Saracens dwelt, begotten of his stock.

1303CThe rams. Preachers, leaders of the flock, sons of the prophets, who taught that the things the prophets foretold of Christ have been fulfilled.

Nabaioth, the son of Ismael, and it is interpreted prophecy.

They shall minister to thee. Holy bishops, priests, ministers of the Church, by administering the body and blood of Christ to the faithful.

VERS. 8.-- Who are these. The prophet marvels at the Gentiles running so swiftly to the faith, or the primitive Church:

To the windows. At their windows stand the saints, guarding the five senses of the body, lest through them, as through windows, death enter into their souls.

VERS. 9.-- Their silver. That is: With the heart we believe unto justice, but with the mouth confession is made unto salvation (Rom. X).

1303DVERS. 10. The sons of strangers. The princes of the Gentiles who throughout the world built churches, as Constantine, who at Rome made twelve churches in honour of the twelve apostles, and many others who raised up many churches, and furnished what was needful.

VERS. 13.-- The glory of Libanus. Libanus is a mountain bordering on the land of promise, etc., as far as the kings and princes who came to the Church.

The fir tree. (ID.) The proud, namely the rich, the philosophers. For pride follows riches and knowledge. Cyprian and Hilary, lofty trees in the world, built up the Church of God. The diversity of the trees signifies the diverse orders in the Church. Of all which the Church is constructed, marked out with a wondrous beauty.

1304AThe place of my feet. The Church, in which the apostles, etc., as far as they sustain the Church by their institutions, their miracles and their examples.

VERS. 14.-- The city of the Lord. For a city is so called from fellowship, etc., as far as who would go about carefully and afford help.

VERS. 15.-- Hated. By the Jews, because she preached the faith of Christ and destroyed the observances of the law; by the Gentiles, because she brought in one God and maintained that idols are nothing.

There was none that passed through thee. Would pass through and bless thee, etc., as far as moreover pride is sometimes evil, sometimes good:

VERS. 17.-- For wood, brass. (ID.) By the wood and the stones, etc., as far as others strong in faith, as Cyprian, and other martyrs, like iron.

1304BThy visitation. (ID.) Christ, or the peaceable apostles, and just overseers, that they may rightly teach and judge.

VERS. 19.-- There shall not be. This of the day of judgment. By this chapter we are compelled to refer whatever things have been said or are to be said to the last time, since the service of the heavens and of the earth, of the sun and of the moon shall cease for us, and the Lord himself shall be our light for ever.

VERS. 21.-- The bud. (ID.) The angels, all the elect, who fulfil in works the faith they hold. They are the bud of the Lord's planting and the work of his hands, because by him they were created, and planted in the Church by faith to glorify their Creator.

VERS. 22.-- In the time: of the coming of Christ. Or, in a fitting or a predetermined time. When 1304Cthe Son of man shall have sent his angels, and they shall gather out of his kingdom all scandals.

CAPUT LXI.

VERS. 1.-- Spiritus Domini (HIER.) Christus secundum humanitatem qui supra dixerat, Ego Dominus, in tempore congregabo eos, etc.

Quod unxerit me: oleo laetitiae, abundantia gratiae. (ID.) Haec unctio in baptismate completa est, quia Spiritus Domini in specie columbae descendit, et mansit in illo.

Ad annuntiandum. Septuaginta: Ad evangelizandum pauperibus.

Apertionem, scilicet resolutionem; sic enim Symmachus 1304Dapertius transtulit.

VERS. 2.-- Diem ultionis: (ID.) damnationis, quae per Romanos, secundum illud: Veniet dies, etc. Et, cum videritis ab exercitu circumdari Jerusalem, etc.

VERS. 3.-- Coronam. Cum scilicet viderint de Judaeis plurimos credidisse, et pro lugubri indumento stolam mundissimam accipere.

Pro cinere. Pro luctu et dolore fructuum suorum, cum scilicet viderint gentes ad fidem confluentes, habebunt oleum gaudii pro luctu, pallium laudis pro spiritu moeroris.

VERS. 5.-- Vinitores. (ID.) Mali cultores vineae, Scribae, et Pharisaei, de quibus dicitur: Malos male perdet, et vineam suam locabit aliis agricolis.

VERS. 6.-- Vos autem. Hoc ad praedicatores de 1305Agentibus, etc., usque ad qui erant sacerdotes in domo Domini.

VERS. 7.-- Partem eorum. Domini. Unde Psalmista: Dominus pars haereditatis meae, etc. Quod nemo veraciter dicit, qui aliam partem quaerit.

VERS. 8.-- Quia ego Dominus: Quasi diceret: Hoc faciet Dominus, qui magis diligit justitiam pauperum et judicium verum quam munera divitum quae saepe de rapina sunt, nec magis quam scorti mercedem vel pretium carnis in sacrificium, vel si quis immolet filium Domino, placent.

In veritate. Ut in veritate comedant agnum, ut faciant circumcisionem cordium, et quae data sunt in figura, omnia in veritate faciant.

Foedus perpetuum. Evangelium aeternum, non ut 1305BVetus Testamentum, cui succedit Evangelium.

VERS. 9.-- Semen eorum. Fideles, quibus apostoli semen verbi commiserunt quod a Domino acceperunt.

VERS. 10.-- Gaudens gaudebo in Domino, non in mundo.

Vestimentis salutis: virtutibus, ipsi Christo, etc., usque ad tamen una in capite et in membris est Ecclesia.

Et quasi sponsam. Alterius comparationis ponit exempla, quorum prius ad sponsum, sequens refertur ad sponsam.

VERS. 11.-- Et laudem: suam, etc., usque ad reddet Domino fructum laudis et bonae operationis.

CHAPTER LXI.

VERS. 1.-- The Spirit of the Lord (HIER.) Christ according to the humanity, who had said above, I the Lord, in the time will gather them, etc.

Because he hath anointed me: with the oil of gladness, with the abundance of grace. (ID.) This anointing was completed in the baptism, because the Spirit of the Lord descended in the likeness of a dove, and remained upon him.

To announce. The Seventy: To preach the Gospel to the poor.

An opening, namely a loosing; for thus Symmachus 1304Dmore openly rendered it.

VERS. 2.-- The day of vengeance: (ID.) of damnation, which came through the Romans, according to that: The day shall come, etc. And, when you shall see Jerusalem compassed about with an army, etc.

VERS. 3.-- A crown. When, that is, they shall have seen that very many of the Jews have believed, and that they receive a most clean robe for a mourning garment.

For ashes. For the grief and sorrow of their own fruits, when, that is, they shall have seen the Gentiles flowing together to the faith, they shall have the oil of joy for mourning, the mantle of praise for the spirit of grief.

VERS. 5.-- Vinedressers. (ID.) The evil tillers of the vineyard, the Scribes and the Pharisees, of whom it is said: He will bring those evil men to an evil end, and will let out his vineyard to other husbandmen.

VERS. 6.-- But you. This to the preachers from among 1305Athe Gentiles, etc., as far as who were priests in the house of the Lord.

VERS. 7.-- Their portion. The Lord's. Whence the Psalmist: The Lord is the portion of my inheritance, etc. Which no man truly says, who seeks another portion.

VERS. 8.-- For I am the Lord: As though he said: This shall the Lord do, who loves rather the justice of the poor and true judgment than the gifts of the rich, which are often from rapine; nor do they please him more than the hire of a harlot, or the price of flesh in sacrifice, or if a man should immolate his son to the Lord.

In truth. That they may eat the lamb in truth, that they may make the circumcision of the heart, and do in truth all the things that were given in figure.

An everlasting covenant. The eternal Gospel, not as 1305Bthe Old Testament, which the Gospel succeeds.

VERS. 9.-- Their seed. The faithful, to whom the apostles committed the seed of the word which they received from the Lord.

VERS. 10.-- Rejoicing I will rejoice in the Lord, not in the world.

With the garments of salvation: with the virtues, with Christ himself, etc., as far as yet the Church is one in the head and in the members.

And as a bride. He sets forth the examples of another comparison, of which the former is referred to the bridegroom, the latter to the bride.

VERS. 11.-- And praise: his own, etc., as far as he shall render to the Lord the fruit of praise and of good working.

CAPUT LXII.

VERS. 1.-- Propter Sion. (HIER.) Propheta in persona sui, impendio exhilaratus ex his omnibus 1305Cquae mundo confert Filius incarnatus. Et hoc est, Propter Sion non tacebo, quamvis loqui prohibeat, quamvis mortem minetur.

Lampas accendatur. Lampas Graece, flamma Latine, unde: Ignem veni mittere in terram, et quid volo nisi ut ardeat? Hoc succensi apostoli loquebantur variis linguis.

VERS. 2.-- Nomen novum. (ID.) Mos est Hebraeis ex novis rebus nova nomina imponere, etc., usque ad similiter prius Simon, postea Petrus.

VERS. 3.-- Corona gloriae. De omnibus partibus mundi, quasi corona venientibus fidelibus.

VERS. 4.-- Non vocaberis. Ecclesiam de utroque populo alloquitur, quasi, non hoc vocabulorum fine contenta eris.

1305DVERS. 5.-- Et gaudebit, etc. Propter hoc relinquet homo patrem et matrem (Gen. II; Matth. XIX). Sacramentum hoc magnum est in Christo et in Ecclesia (Ephes. V).

VERS. 6.-- Super muros tuos. (ID.) Apostolos, praedicatores, qui Ecclesiam muniunt, quorum custodes angeli sunt. Unde: Omnes administratorii spiritus sunt, propter eos missi, qui haereditatem capiunt salutis (Hebr. I). Vel muri Ecclesiarum apostolorum discipuli, quorum custodes ipsi apostoli.

VERS. 7.-- Donec ponat Jerusalem. Quod est, vel ipsa longe lateque diffusa, ubique laudetur, vel ubique laudet semper creatorem suum.

VERS. 8.-- Juravit per Filium scilicet, qui Dei virtus 1306Aest et Dei sapientia. In dextera. In Filio. Unde: Omnia in sapientia fecisti (Psal. CIII), qui et brachium et fortitudo Patris: Unde, Omnia per ipsum facta sunt (Joan. I). Si dedero triticum tuum. Triticum Judaeorum et vinum saepe dedi inimicis eorum, sicut in libro Judicum legitur (Judic. VI): quod in tempore messis Madianitae irruebant et eorum fruges colligebant. Et quando captivi ducti sunt, Samaritani qui eorum messes comederent, substituti sunt. Sed in hoc loco intelligit cibum Ecclesiae, virtutes, et bona opera, corpus Christi et sanguinem. Id est laudes, opera, quae cum idolis impenderas, daemones comedebant. Sed post adventum Filii Dominus delectatus est in eis. Triticum ergo et vinum, cibus (scilicet Judaeorum) corpus Christi et sanguinem significabant. 1306BUnde, Caro mea vere est cibus, et sanguis meus vere est potus (Joan. VI). Hoc hodie non sumitur ab haereticis, paganis, Judaeis, inimicis Ecclesiae.

VERS. 10.-- Per portas. Ministros Ecclesiae, qui alios introducunt. Vel virtutes, quae ducunt in coelum, quasi de virtute in virtutem. Planum facite iter. Auferendo scandala; in baptismo remittendo peccata. Eligite. Vel, projicite secundum Septuaginta, quod est, auferte duritiam infidelitatis a cordibus hominum; vel, reprobos et duros a consortio fidelium.

VERS. 11.-- Dicite filiae Sion. Scilicet primitivae Ecclesiae, quae ex Judaeis primum: unde, Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me (Cant. I). Vel secundum illud: Quotquot autem receperunt eum (Joan. I), etc.

Et vocabunt eum, dicentes: Vos estis populus 1306Csanctus, vos estis redempti a Domino Deo. Sic enim quidam codices habent.

CHAPTER LXII.

VERS. 1.-- For Sion's sake. (HIER.) The prophet in his own person, exceedingly gladdened by all those things 1305Cwhich the incarnate Son confers upon the world. And this is it: For Sion's sake I will not hold my peace, however much he forbid him to speak, however much he threaten death.

Let a lamp be kindled. Lampas in Greek, flamma in Latin; whence: I am come to cast fire upon the earth, and what will I but that it burn? [Vulg. Lc 12:49 nisi ut accendatur, "but that it be kindled"; the lemma's own verb] Set alight by this, the apostles spoke with divers tongues.

VERS. 2.-- A new name. (ID.) It is the custom of the Hebrews to impose new names from new things, etc., as far as likewise first Simon, afterwards Peter.

VERS. 3.-- A crown of glory. Of the faithful coming from all the parts of the world, as it were a crown.

VERS. 4.-- Thou shalt not be called. He addresses the Church gathered out of either people, as if: thou shalt not be content with this end of appellations.

1305DVERS. 5.-- And he shall rejoice, etc. For this cause shall a man leave father and mother (Gen. II; Matth. XIX). This is a great sacrament in Christ and in the Church (Ephes. V) [Vulg. Eph 5:32 Sacramentum hoc magnum est, ego autem dico in Christo et in Ecclesia, "but I speak in Christ and in the Church"].

VERS. 6.-- Upon thy walls. (ID.) The apostles, the preachers, who fortify the Church, whose guardians are the angels. Whence: All are ministering spirits, sent for their sakes who receive the inheritance of salvation (Hebr. I) [Vulg. Hbr 1:14 Nonne omnes sunt administratorii spiritus, in ministerium missi propter eos, qui haereditatem capient salutis?, a question, with in ministerium and the future capient]. Or the walls of the Churches are the disciples of the apostles, whose guardians are the apostles themselves.

VERS. 7.-- Till he establish Jerusalem. Which is, either that she herself, spread far and wide, be praised everywhere, or that she everywhere praise her Creator always.

VERS. 8.-- He hath sworn by the Son, namely, who is the power of God 1306Aand the wisdom of God. In his right hand. In the Son. Whence: Thou hast made all things in wisdom (Psal. CIII), who is both the arm and the strength of the Father: Whence, All things were made by him (Joan. I). If I will give thy wheat. The wheat of the Jews and their wine I have often given to their enemies, as is read in the book of Judges (Judic. VI): that in the time of harvest the Madianites used to rush in and gather their crops. And when they were led away captive, Samaritans were put in their place to eat their harvests. But in this place he understands the food of the Church, the virtues and good works, the body and blood of Christ. That is, the praises and the works which thou hadst spent upon idols, the demons used to eat. But after the coming of the Son the Lord was delighted in them. The wheat, therefore, and the wine, the food (namely of the Jews), signified the body and blood of Christ. 1306BWhence, My flesh is meat indeed, and my blood is drink indeed (Joan. VI). This is not taken today by heretics, pagans, Jews, the enemies of the Church.

VERS. 10.-- Through the gates. The ministers of the Church, who bring others in. Or the virtues, which lead into heaven, as it were from virtue to virtue. Make the way plain. By taking away scandals; by remitting sins in baptism. Choose ye. Or, cast ye forth, according to the Septuagint, which is: take away the hardness of unbelief from the hearts of men; or, the reprobate and the hard, from the fellowship of the faithful.

VERS. 11.-- Say ye to the daughter of Sion. Namely to the primitive Church, which was first out of the Jews: whence, The sons of my mother have fought against me (Cant. I). Or according to that: But as many as received him (Joan. I), etc.

And they shall call him, saying: You are the 1306Choly people, you are the redeemed of the Lord God [Vulg. Is 62:12 Et vocabunt eos, Populus sanctus, redempti a Domino, "they shall call them"]. For so certain codices have it.

CAPUT LXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Quis est iste. Quidam angeli non plene cognoscentes mysterium incarnationis, passionis, resurrectionis, videntes ad coelos ascendere Dominum cum multitudine angelorum et sanctorum hominum propria virtute, non auxilio angelorum, admirantur incarnationis, et passionis, et resurrectionis, et ascensionis mysterium: et quomodo sibi et aliis aperiat regnum coelorum; unde et angelis Dominum comitantibus dicunt: Quis est iste? Et David similiter: Quis est iste rex gloriae? (Psal. XXIII). Videtur tamen Augustus dicere super Genesim quod omnes angeli ab ipsa creatione sua, statim cognoverunt in contemplatione 1306Dsui creatoris, quidquid ipse erat facturus. Hieronymus autem aperte declarat quia quidam angeli, donec perficeretur mysterium incarnationis, ad plenum non cognoverunt.

De Edom. Edom (ex HIER.) ab edulio rufae lentis, etc., usque ad vel de inferno spoliato victor cum fidelibus suis.

Formosus in stola. Formosum calidum. Unde formosus quasi calidus, quia ex colore rubeus et roseus efficitur vultus, et inde speciosus.

VERS. 2.-- Quare ergo. (ID.) Tanquam dicant angeli: Didicimus, etc., usque ad magis tibi clementia convenit quam crudelitas.

VERS. 3.-- Solus. Solus Samson praecisis crinibus 1307APhilisthaeis exponitur, nec angelus nec archangelus vel quilibet coelestis spiritus. Denique humani generis nullus vel Judaeus vel Gentilis auxiliator: solus pugnat, solus superat. Aspersus est. (HIER.) Sanguis meus eorum rancore, etc., usque ad cujus saepe sanguis super vestimenta victoris aspergitur.

VERS. 5.-- Et non fuit, etc. Unde: Omnes amici mei dereliquerunt me, et noti mei quasi alieni recesserunt a me (Job. XIX). Et salvavit. Hieronymus ad Augustinum et Alipium, impietatem Sabellii declinantes, tres personas expressas sub proprietate distinguimus, non ipsum sibi Patrem, non ipsum sibi Filium, non ipsum sibi Spiritum sanctum esse dicentes: sed aliam Patris, aliam Filii, aliam Spiritus sancti personam esse. Non enim nomina tantummodo, 1307Bsed proprietates nominum confitemur.

VERS. 7.-- Miserationum. (HIER.) Propheta in persona Judaici populi poenitentis et misericordiam Domini recognoscentis.

VERS. 9.-- Non est tribulatus. (ID.) Vox quae est, etc., usque ad nemo est adjutor nisi Michael princeps vester, qui astat pro filiis populi mei (Dan. X).

Et angelus. (ID.) Septuaginta. Non legatus nec ipse angelus, sed Dominus salvavit eos.

VERS. 10.-- Spiritum sancti ejus. Id est Moysi: unde, Exacerbaverunt Moysen in castris, et Aaron sanctum Domini. (Psal. CV.) Vel sancti ejus, Christi, cui credere noluerunt. Filius enim Dei qui nos redemit, ipsos de Aegypto et mari Rubro liberavit. Spiritum sanctum, vel, sancti ejus. Quidam codices 1307Chabent, scilicet Spiritum sanctum, quem contristat, qui virum spiritualem contristat: unde, Qui vos tangit, quasi qui tangit pupillam oculi mei. (Zach. II.) Vel, angelum in custodiam missum, quem saepe male faciendo offenderunt.

VERS. 11.-- Moysi. Quia non est alius qui orat pro eis. Ubi est, etc. Propheta videns populi sui abjectionem, miseriam et afflictionem jam imminentem propter Filii Dei negationem, condolendo exclamat, dicens: Ubi est? quasi dicat: Clementia Omnipotentis. Ubi est qui posuit? Moysi scilicet, quia orante Moyse, tulit Dominus de spiritu ejus et dedit septuaginta viris.

VERS. 12.-- Qui scidit aquas ante eos. Vel dicitur. Cum Moyses mare percussisset, et in duodecim vias scidisset et aliis haesitantibus, tribus Juda ingressa 1307Dest, et ob hoc regni dignitatem adepta est.

VERS. 14.-- Spiritus Domini. Angelus, scilicet; unde: Et praecedet te angelus meus (Exod. XIV). Spiritus enim, naturae nomen est, angelus officii.

VERS. 15.-- Attende de coelo et vide. Dominus ubique totus et semper est, unde: Coelum et terram ego impleo (Jer. XXIII). Dicitur tamen sedem habere in coelo, secundum illud: Coelum mihi sedes (Isa. LXVI), quia scilicet in coelestibus angelis et hominibus justis (quorum scilicet conversatio est in coelis) ejus voluntas praecipue fit. Ubi est zelus tuus, etc. Quasi dicat: Quia peccavimus, et nos odisse coepisti, recessit zelus tuus: quo recedente recessit fortitudo, et paternorum viscerum superatur affectus.

1308AVERS. 16.-- Abraham nescivit. (HIER.) Quasi dicat: Quia te offendimus, etc., usque ad non obliviscatur filiorum suorum, ne per ipsos nomen Dei blasphemetur in gentibus.

VERS. 17.-- Quare errare. Paulus ad Corinthios scribens, ad unius scilicet populum civitatis pro varietate habitantium sanctorum scilicet et peccatorum, nunc laudat, nunc reprehendit: sic praesens capitulum, quod ex oratione populi Deum deprecantis contexitur, nunc laudat Dominum, nunc accusat, juxta secundam regulam Ticonii, quae est de corpore Domini.

VERS. 19.-- Quasi in principio. (HIER.) Antequam vocares nos, etc., usque ad non vocabuntur populus Dei, a Christo scilicet Christiani.

CHAPTER LXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Who is this. Certain angels, not fully knowing the mystery of the incarnation, of the passion, of the resurrection, seeing the Lord ascend to the heavens with a multitude of angels and of holy men by his own power, not by the help of angels, marvel at the mystery of the incarnation, and of the passion, and of the resurrection, and of the ascension: and how he opens the kingdom of heaven to himself and to others; whence also they say to the angels accompanying the Lord: Who is this? And David likewise: Who is this King of glory? (Psal. XXIII). Augustus, however, seems to say upon Genesis [Augustus; read Augustinus, "Augustine"] that all the angels from their very creation knew at once, in the contemplation 1306Dof their Creator, whatsoever he himself was about to do. But Jerome plainly declares that certain angels, until the mystery of the incarnation should be accomplished, did not know it to the full.

Of Edom. Edom (ex HIER.) from the eating of red lentils, etc., as far as or, from hell despoiled, victor with his faithful.

Beautiful in his robe. Formosum, warm. Whence formosus as it were calidus, because from its colour the face is made ruddy and rosy, and thence comely.

VERS. 2.-- Why then. (ID.) As though the angels should say: We have learned, etc., as far as clemency befits thee more than cruelty.

VERS. 3.-- Alone. Samson alone, his locks cut off, 1307Ais exposed to the Philistines; neither angel nor archangel nor any heavenly spirit. Finally, of the human race no one, whether Jew or Gentile, is a helper: alone he fights, alone he overcomes. He is sprinkled. (HIER.) My blood by their rancour, etc., as far as whose blood is often sprinkled upon the garments of the victor.

VERS. 5.-- And there was not, etc. Whence: All my friends have forsaken me, and my acquaintance are gone from me as strangers (Job. XIX) [Vulg. Job 19:13 Fratres meos longe fecit a me, et noti mei quasi alieni recesserunt a me; the first half answers rather to Job 19:14 Dereliquerunt me propinqui mei, "my kinsmen have forsaken me"]. And it saved. Jerome, to Augustine and Alipius, shunning the impiety of Sabellius: we distinguish three persons expressed under their property, not saying that the Father is himself to himself, not the Son himself to himself, not the Holy Spirit himself to himself: but that the person of the Father is one, of the Son another, of the Holy Spirit another. For we confess not names only, 1307Bbut the properties of names.

VERS. 7.-- Of the mercies. (HIER.) The prophet in the person of the Jewish people repenting and acknowledging the mercy of the Lord.

VERS. 9.-- He was not afflicted. (ID.) The voice which is, etc., as far as there is no helper but Michael your prince, who stands for the sons of my people (Dan. X) [Vulg. Dn 10:21 nemo est adjutor meus in omnibus his, nisi Michael princeps vester; the closing clause is Dn 12:1 qui stat pro filiis populi tui, "thy people," and is not cited].

And an angel. (ID.) The Septuagint. Not a legate nor an angel himself, but the Lord saved them.

VERS. 10.-- The spirit of his holy one. That is, of Moses: whence, They exasperated Moses in the camp, and Aaron the holy one of the Lord. (Psal. CV.) [Vulg. Ps 105:16 Et irritaverunt Moysen in castris; Aaron, sanctum Domini, "provoked"] Or of his holy one, of Christ, in whom they would not believe. For the Son of God who redeemed us, he it was who delivered them from Egypt and the Red Sea. The Holy Spirit, or, of his holy one. Certain codices 1307Chave, namely, the Holy Spirit, whom he grieves who grieves a spiritual man: whence, He that toucheth you, is as one that toucheth the apple of my eye. (Zach. II.) [Vulg. Zach 2:8 qui enim tetigerit vos, tangit pupillam oculi mei, without quasi: he touches it, rather than is as one who touches it] Or, the angel sent for a guard, whom they often offended by doing ill.

VERS. 11.-- Of Moses. Because there is no other who prays for them. Where is he, etc. The prophet, seeing the casting-off of his people, their misery and their affliction now impending because of the denial of the Son of God, cries out in compassion, saying: Where is he? as if he should say: The clemency of the Almighty. Where is he that put? Of Moses, namely, because at Moses' prayer the Lord took of his spirit and gave it to seventy men.

VERS. 12.-- Who divided the waters before them. Or it is said. When Moses had struck the sea, and had divided it into twelve ways, and while the rest hesitated, the tribe of Juda entered in, 1307Dand for this obtained the dignity of the kingdom.

VERS. 14.-- The spirit of the Lord. The angel, namely; whence: And my angel shall go before thee (Exod. XIV) [Vulg. Ex 23:20 Ecce ego mittam angelum meum, qui praecedat te, and Ex 32:34 angelus meus praecedet te; Exodus XIV as cited reads angelus Dei, qui praecedebat castra Israel]. For spirit is the name of a nature, angel of an office.

VERS. 15.-- Look down from heaven and behold. The Lord is everywhere whole and always, whence: I fill heaven and earth (Jer. XXIII) [Vulg. Jr 23:24 Numquid non caelum et terram ego impleo?, a question]. Yet he is said to have his seat in heaven, according to that: Heaven is my seat (Isa. LXVI) [Vulg. Is 66:1 Caelum sedes mea; Coelum mihi sedes is the wording of Act 7:49, and the verse is quoted in its Isaian form at 1312A], because, namely, in the heavenly angels and in just men (whose manner of life, that is, is in the heavens) his will is chiefly done. Where is thy zeal, etc. As if he should say: Because we have sinned, and thou hast begun to hate us, thy zeal has withdrawn: and when it withdrew strength withdrew, and the affection of a father's bowels is overcome.

1308AVERS. 16.-- Abraham hath not known us. (HIER.) As if he should say: Because we have offended thee, etc., as far as let him not forget his sons, lest through them the name of God be blasphemed among the Gentiles.

VERS. 17.-- Why hast thou made us to err. Paul, writing to the Corinthians, that is, to the people of a single city, on account of the diversity of those dwelling there (of saints, namely, and of sinners), now praises, now reproves: so the present chapter, which is woven out of the prayer of the people beseeching God, now praises the Lord, now accuses, according to the second rule of Ticonius, which is concerning the body of the Lord.

VERS. 19.-- As in the beginning. (HIER.) Before thou didst call us, etc., as far as they shall not be called the people of God, Christians namely from Christ.

CAPUT LXIV.

  • 1308B

VERS. 1.-- Utinam dirumperes. (HIER.) In persona sua, etc., usque ad item David: Inclinavit coelos, et descendit (Psal. XVII).

VERS. 2.-- Aquae arderent. (SYM.) Aquae maris arderent igni. Quod est, populi mundi inflammarentur igne charitatis. Unde: Ignem veni mittere in terram, et quid volo nisi ut ardeat? (Luc. XII.) et sic notum fieret nomen tuum, quod est Christus Jesus, inimicis tuis, scilicet Judaeis.

VERS. 3.-- Non sustinebimus. Nos Judaei, sed blasphemabimus dicentes: In principe daemoniorum Beelzebub ejicit daemones (Matth. XII). Hoc enim dicit propheta in persona infidelium. Descendisti. Tanquam precibus suis exauditis inquit: Descendisti, etc. 1308CMontes. Amissa potentia: Nunc princeps hujus mundi ejicietur foras (Joan. XII). Et Judaeis dicitur: Auferetur a vobis regnum Dei (Matth. XXI), scilicet sancta Scriptura et omnis gloria, et omnis dignitas.

VERS. 4.-- A saeculo. Hinc Paulus ad Corinthios testimonium sumit, non verbum ex verbo, sed sensum exprimens. Oculus. Quia non est corpus vel color. Quod Apostolus ponit in cor hominis non ascendit, quia majus, et cor debet illuc dirigi. Paravit enim seipsum cognitionem sui, cognitionem sanctae Trinitatis; unde: Haec est vita aeterna, ut cognoscant te verum Deum et quem misisti Jesum Christum (Joan. XVII). Hunc nemo potest videre; unde illud: Deum nemo vidit unquam (Joan. I).

VERS. 5.-- Ecce tu, etc. In persona infidelis populi, 1308Dqui volens transire ad veritatem Evangelii, poenitentiam praeferebat ore, quam non habebat in corde.

VERS. 6.-- Menstruatae, etc. Mene Hebraice, Luna Latine; unde menstruatae quae singulis mensibus solent sanguinis fluxum sustinere. Et tunc viri abstinere debent ab eis. Tunc enim concipiuntur membris damnati, caeci, claudi, leprosi et hujusmodi: ut quia parentes non erubuerunt misceri in conclavi, eorum peccata pateant cunctis, et aperte redarguantur in parvulis. Ventus. Commotio aeris, ut ibi, Imperavit ventis et mari (Matth. VIII). Vel Spiritus sanctus: Advenientis Spiritus vehementis (Act I). Hic enim Spiritus pro vento ponitur significatione Spiritus sancti. Vel, tentatio daemonum et malorum 1309Ahominum, ut illud: Venerunt flumina, et flaverunt venti (Matth. VII): et hoc est quasi ventus.

VERS. 8.-- Et nunc, Domine. Ac si dicat: Si consideremus nostra merita, desperandum est; si clementiam tuam, qui flagellas omnem filium quem recipis, audemus preces fundere, tu enim pater noster es. Opera manuum tuarum. Quasi dicat: Respice opus tuum, non merita nostra. Mysterii Trinitatis recta confessio est ignorantia scientiae.

VERS. 9.-- Ne irascaris, Domine. Hoc est, non vitamus iram, nec ultra quaerimus patientiam, per quam thesaurizamus nobis iram in die vindictae: sed quaesumus ne irascaris nobis satis, scilicet, ne tempore ultionis memineris iniquitatis nostrae.

VERS. 10.-- Sion deserta. Sion et Jerusalem in 1309Bhoc loco idem significant, templum, scilicet, et civitatem, ubi quondam specula virtutum et visio pacis. Sed postquam populus Judaeorum negavit regem suum, et addidit illud: Non habemus regem nisi Caesarem (Joan. XIX), Jerusalem deserta est a Deo: unde, Relinquetur vobis domus vestra deserta (Luc. XIII); et illud, Surgite eamus hinc (Joan. XIV). Et in templo, auditae sunt voces (sicut dicit ecclesiastica historia): Transeamus ab his sedibus.

VERS. 11.-- Domus. Allegorice. Domus Dei Ecclesia, et unaquaeque fidelis anima, quae aliquando ignibus luxuriae corrumpitur et avaritiae.

CHAPTER LXIV.

  • 1308B

VERS. 1.-- O that thou wouldst rend. (HIER.) In his own person, etc., as far as likewise David: He bowed the heavens, and came down (Psal. XVII).

VERS. 2.-- The waters should burn. (SYM.) The waters of the sea should burn with fire. Which is, that the peoples of the world should be inflamed with the fire of charity. Whence: I am come to cast fire upon the earth, and what will I but that it burn? (Luc. XII.) [Vulg. Lc 12:49 nisi ut accendatur, "but that it be kindled"] and so thy name should be made known, which is Christ Jesus, to thy enemies, namely to the Jews.

VERS. 3.-- We shall not bear it. We Jews, but we shall blaspheme, saying: By Beelzebub the prince of devils he casteth out devils (Matth. XII) [Vulg. Mt 12:24 Hic non ejicit daemones nisi in Beelzebub principe daemoniorum, a denial: "he casteth not out devils but by Beelzebub"]. For the prophet says this in the person of unbelievers. Thou camest down. As though, his prayers having been heard, he says: Thou camest down, etc. 1308CThe mountains. Their power lost: Now shall the prince of this world be cast out (Joan. XII). And to the Jews it is said: The kingdom of God shall be taken away from you (Matth. XXI), namely Holy Scripture and all glory and all dignity.

VERS. 4.-- From the beginning of the world. Hence Paul, writing to the Corinthians, takes his testimony, not word for word, but expressing the sense. The eye. Because it is not a body or a colour. What the Apostle sets down, it hath not ascended into the heart of man, because it is greater, and the heart ought to be directed thither. For he prepared his very self, the knowledge of himself, the knowledge of the holy Trinity; whence: This is eternal life, that they may know thee the true God, and Jesus Christ whom thou hast sent (Joan. XVII) [Vulg. Jo 17:3 ut cognoscant te, solum Deum verum, "thee, the only true God"]. Him no one can see; whence that: No man hath ever seen God (Joan. I).

VERS. 5.-- Behold thou, etc. In the person of the unbelieving people, 1308Dwho, wishing to pass over to the truth of the Gospel, put forward with the mouth a penance which he had not in the heart.

VERS. 6.-- Of a menstruous woman, etc. Mene in Hebrew, luna in Latin; whence menstruatae, women who each several month are wont to endure a flux of blood. And then men ought to abstain from them. For then are conceived those condemned in their members, the blind, the lame, the leprous and the like: so that, because the parents were not ashamed to be mingled in the bedchamber, their sins may lie open to all, and be openly reproved in their little ones. Wind. A motion of the air, as there, He commanded the winds and the sea (Matth. VIII). Or the Holy Spirit: Of a vehement Spirit coming (Act I). For here Spirit is put for wind, with the signification of the Holy Spirit. Or, the temptation of demons and of evil 1309Amen, as that: The floods came, and the winds blew (Matth. VII): and this is as it were a wind.

VERS. 8.-- And now, O Lord. As if he should say: If we consider our own merits, we must despair; if thy clemency, who scourgest every son whom thou receivest, we dare to pour out prayers, for thou art our father. The works of thy hands. As if he should say: Look upon thy work, not upon our merits. The right confession of the mystery of the Trinity is the ignorance of knowledge.

VERS. 9.-- Be not angry, O Lord. This is, we do not shun wrath, nor do we further seek that patience through which we treasure up to ourselves wrath in the day of vengeance: but we beseech that thou be not angry with us enough, that is, that thou remember not our iniquity in the time of vengeance.

VERS. 10.-- Sion is a desert. Sion and Jerusalem in 1309Bthis place signify the same thing, the temple, namely, and the city, where once was the watchtower of the virtues and the vision of peace. But after the people of the Jews denied their king, and added that: We have no king but Caesar (Joan. XIX), Jerusalem was deserted by God: whence, Your house shall be left to you desolate (Luc. XIII); and that, Arise, let us go hence (Joan. XIV). And in the temple voices were heard (as the ecclesiastical history says): Let us pass over from these seats.

VERS. 11.-- The house. Allegorically. The house of God is the Church, and every faithful soul, which is sometimes corrupted by the fires of luxury and of avarice.

CAPUT LXV.

VERS. 1.-- Quaesierunt, etc. (HIER.) Ad hoc respondet 1309Cfilius, etc., usque ad et Filii alieni mentiti sunt mihi (Psal. XVII).

VERS. 2.-- Expandi manus meas. (ID.) In cruce orans pro eis, etc., usque ad ipsi tamen semper exstiterunt increduli.

VERS. 3.-- Populus qui ad iracundiam. (ID.) Allegorice. Omnis haereticus Deum ad iracundiam procat, etc., usque ad et in quadrum exstruere, et secti in latere pulveris roborare lineis.

VERS. 4.-- In delubris. Delubra a delendo dicta, eo quod ibi putabatur ablui anima. Vel, quia prope erant piscinae ubi et sacerdos lavabatur et hostia. Allegorice. (HIER.) Haeretici in memoriis magistrorum, etc., usque ad ut qui ipsis non fuerit similis, quasi perditum detestetur.

VERS. 5.-- In furore meo. Furorem, oblivionem, iram, poenitudinem, ita in Deo accipere debemus, quomodo manus, pedes, oculos, aures, et caetera membra quae habere dicitur: non quod his pareat perturbationibus qui eas exstinguit in nobis, sed per verba nostra; Dei intelligimus affectum erga nos.

VERS. 6.-- Non tacebo. Qui superius dixit: Tacui, semper silui, patiens fui, modo dicit: Non tacebo, id est, ne paream amplius, neque sustinebo iniquitates eorum.

VERS. 7.-- Iniquitates patrum, etc. Ut scilicet non solum pro peccatis vestris puniamini, sed etiam pro patrum iniquitatibus quos imitati estis. Aliter enim non portat filius iniquitatem patris. Qui sacrificaverunt 1310Asuper montes. Quando in Bethel et in Dan aureos vitulos adoraverunt. Haereticus quoque contemnens simplicitatem Ecclesiae sacrificat in montibus, et exprobrat Deo dum ejus praecepta contemnit.

VERS. 8.-- Quomodo. (HIER.) Quasi dicat: Propter malos non perdam bonos: Sed quomodo, etc. Dixerat enim periturum Israel.

VERS. 9.-- De Jacob semen. Semen Jacob et Judae, apostoli, possidentes montes, alios, scilicet credentes virtutibus eminentes: unde, Nisi Dominus Sabaoth reliquisset nobis semen, quasi Sodoma facti essemus (Rom. IX). Montes sanctos meos. Septuaginta: Montem meum, scilicet Christum, qui est mons montium. Montes enim sunt apostoli, qui de Judaeis possederunt Christum, in quo fundati sunt. Et haereditabunt. 1310B(HIER.) Unde: Semen Abrahae servi ejus: filii Jacob, electi ejus. Quicunque adhuc semen sunt, etc., usque ad non accepistis spiritum servitutis iterum in timore, sed accepistis spiritum adoptionis filiorum Dei (Rom. IX).

VERS. 10.-- Campestria. (ID.) Alii saltus, unde: Dominus revelabit condensa silvarum (Psal. XVIII), ut latrones ibi latitare desistant, et bestiae venenataque animalia in Ecclesiam Dei transeant. In caulas gregum. Alia translatio habet Saronas, quae est regio inter Joppem, et Lyddam atque Jamniam fecunda et pabulosa, sed latronibus infesta: unde interpretatur vallis effundens sanguinem. Et vallis Achor. Ubi Achor filius Charmi de spoliis Jericho furatus cum omni domo periit. Et quia populum turbavit et ibi 1310Cperiit, vocatur locus vallis Achor, id est, turbationis vel tumultus. In quo significatur Gentilitas, sicut et per Saronas, quae interpretatur vallis fundens sanguinem, quae effusione sanguinis plena in tumultu et in turbatione vitiorum manebat. Sed vallis Achor versa est in caulas gregum sive ovilia, et in cubile armentorum, id est, ecclesias, et requiem praedicatorum et aliorum fidelium, qui designantur per armenta et greges ovium. Armentorum. Praedicatorum, qui Ecclesiam armant et defendunt. Armentum enim dicitur ab armando.

VERS. 11.-- Et vos qui dereliquistis, etc. Allegorice. Omnes qui Ecclesiam deserunt, et obliviscuntur montem sanctum Dei, tradentes se spiritibus erroris, vel immundis doctrinis daemoniorum, parant 1310Dfortunae mensam, et nihil ad Deum pertinere credentes, sed vel stellarum cursu vel varietate fortunae omnia gubernari, hi tradentur aeternis suppliciis, ut nullus eorum possit evadere.

Fortunae mensam. (HIER.) Deae Aegyptiorum cui in fine anni parabant mensam, omnibus escis refertam, et poculis mulsa mixtis: vel quasi felicem annum praebuerit, vel quia futurum felicem praebitura sit.

VERS. 12.-- Vocavi. Per prophetas dicens: Convertimini ad me et salvi eritis (Isa. XLV). Et per memetipsum: Poenitentiam agite (Matth. III). Et alibi: Venite ad me, omnes qui laboratis et onerati estis (Ibid. XI).

VERS. 13.-- Ecce servi mei. (HIER.) Quasi dicat: 1311ANon solum corruetis gladio, etc., usque ad et, Satiabor cum apparuerit gloria tua.

VERS. 15.-- Et dimittetis nomen vestrum. Ut aliqui affirmantes dicant: Si aliter fecero, contingat mihi sicut Judaeis Romanorum gladio occisis. Vel ita: Juro per Deum, in quem olim Judaei crediderunt. Vel, quia Deus juramentum eorum erat, quod Christiani modo jurant necessitate compulsi, Vivit Dominus scilicet.

Et interficiet te. Ut vel hunc non habeatis, qui dicit, Ego sum via, veritas et vita (Joan. XIV.) Nec vocemini circumcisio, sed conscisio.

VERS. 16.-- Quia oblivioni. (HIER.) Ut non idolorum meminerint, etc., usque ad Forsitan et in futuro pristinae conversationis memoria omnino delebitur.

1311BVERS. 18.-- Quia ecce. Ex angelis scilicet et hominibus unam Ecclesiam, et exsultabunt et gaudebunt, ut ego qui creavi exsultem, et gaudeam in eis. Exsultationem. Universae terrae, ut quae vetus unigeniti erat gaudium, nova cunctis nationibus laetitia sit. Gaudium. Quod vel post resurrectionem possumus intelligere, vel post baptismum in prima resurrectione. Hoc ad generalem resurrectionem qua omnes in perfecta aetate resurgent. Et hoc est: Non erit, etc.

VERS. 20.-- Puer centum annorum, etc. Ille scilicet, qui etsi diu, tamen pueriliter vixit, serviens vitiis: et hoc exponit sic Gregorius. Puer centum annorum. Et peccator centum annorum. Vita quidem pueri longum trahitur, ut a factis puerilibus corrigatur: sed si a peccati perpetratione, nec temporis 1311Clonginquitate compescitur, haec ipsa vitae longinquitas, quam per misericordiam Dei accepit ei ad cumulum maledictionis crescit.

VERS. 21.-- Domos. (HIER.) Virtutes pro quibus in coelestibus requiescent, etc., usque ad Multi enim sancti aedificant domos, sed non habitant in eis.

VERS. 22.-- Secundum enim. (ID.) Sicut Christus etc., usque ad Lignum vitae est his qui apprehenderunt eam, scilicet, sapientiam, quae est Christus.

VERS. 23.-- Semen benedictorum. Apostoli, et alii doctores in praedicatione, vel in aliis operibus, sicut Judaei, quia aeternam mercedem percipient, quia venient cum exsultatione portantes manipulos suos (Psal. CXXV).

Nepotes. Quos filii eorum in fide genuerunt, 1311Dsicut Marcus Petri discipulus, et Lucas Pauli filius.

VERS. 24.-- Antequam clament. In conturbatione, in dolore mentis, sed in gaudio et laetitia: unde, Quod enim est gaudium nostrum aut corona gloriae, nonne vos (II Thes. II)? etc.

VERS. 25.-- Lupus. Lupus Saulus, id est persecutor, de quo, Benjamin lupus rapax (Gen. XLIX). Agnus. Qui scilicet in candidis vestibus sequuntur agnum quocunque ierit: De quibus Petro dicitur, Pasce agnos meos (Joan. XXI). Et leo et bos comedent. Vir scilicet disertus et potentissimus in saeculo spreta philosophiae eloquentia comedet paleas, id est utilitatem et superficiem historiae cum bobus, id est praedicatoribus Ecclesiae. Haec comedunt leones 1312Adonec multis laboribus veniatur ad profunditatem mysterii, sicut Augustinus et Cyprianus et alii. Neque occident. Deposita feritate, vel in praesenti vel in futuro, et hoc est: In omni monte, etc. Hoc Judaei carnaliter accipiunt, dicentes quod in tempore Christi sui tanta pax et securitas sibi erit, ut lupi et agni, leones et boves, serpentes et homines commorentur simul, et comedant, neque eis noceant, qui in templo, et in monte sancto habitant. Ex quo colligitur, quod omnes alii occidentur. Sed hoc falsum esse ostenditur his verbis: Haec dicit Dominus: Coelum sedes mea, terra autem scabellum pedum meorum, etc., hoc tamen Judaei carnaliter intelligunt.

CHAPTER LXV.

VERS. 1.-- They have sought, etc. (HIER.) To this the Son answers, 1309Cetc., as far as and The children that are strangers have lied to me (Psal. XVII).

VERS. 2.-- I have spread forth my hands. (ID.) Praying on the cross for them, etc., as far as yet they themselves were always unbelieving.

VERS. 3.-- A people that to anger. (ID.) Allegorically. Every heretic solicits God to anger [procat; read provocat, "provokes"], etc., as far as and to build four-square, and to strengthen with lines the dust cut upon the brick.

VERS. 4.-- In the shrines. Delubra are so called from delendo, blotting out, because there the soul was thought to be washed away. Or, because near by were the pools where both the priest and the victim were washed. Allegorically. (HIER.) Heretics in the memorials of their masters, etc., as far as so that whoever has not been like to them they detest as one lost.

VERS. 5.-- In my fury. Fury, forgetfulness, anger, repentance, we ought so to take in God as we take hands, feet, eyes, ears, and the other members which he is said to have: not that he yields to these perturbations who extinguishes them in us, but through our own words; we understand God's disposition toward us.

VERS. 6.-- I will not be silent. He who said above: I have been silent, I have always held my peace, I have been patient, [Vulg. Is 42:14 Tacui semper, silui, patiens fui, "I have always been silent, I have held my peace"; the adverb goes with the first verb] now says: I will not be silent, that is, that I yield no longer, nor will I bear their iniquities.

VERS. 7.-- The iniquities of the fathers, etc. So that, namely, you may be punished not only for your own sins, but also for the iniquities of the fathers whom you have imitated. For otherwise the son does not bear the iniquity of the father. Who have sacrificed 1310Aupon the mountains. When in Bethel and in Dan they adored the golden calves. The heretic also, contemning the simplicity of the Church, sacrifices upon the mountains, and upbraids God while he contemns his precepts.

VERS. 8.-- As. (HIER.) As if he should say: For the sake of the wicked I will not destroy the good: But as, etc. For he had said that Israel should perish.

VERS. 9.-- Out of Jacob a seed. The seed of Jacob and of Juda, the apostles, possessing the mountains, that is, others, namely believers eminent in the virtues: whence, Unless the Lord of Sabaoth had left us a seed, we had been as Sodom (Rom. IX). My holy mountains. [Vulg. Is 65:9 possidentem montes meos, "my mountains," without sanctos] The Septuagint: My mountain, namely Christ, who is the mountain of mountains. For the mountains are the apostles, who out of the Jews possessed Christ, in whom they are founded. And they shall inherit. 1310B(HIER.) Whence: The seed of Abraham his servant: the sons of Jacob, his elect. Whosoever are still seed, etc., as far as you have not received the spirit of bondage again in fear, but you have received the spirit of the adoption of the sons of God (Rom. IX).

VERS. 10.-- The plains. (ID.) Others, the woodlands; whence: The Lord shall discover the thickets of the woods (Psal. XVIII) [Vulg. Ps 28:9 Vox Domini praeparantis cervos: et revelabit condensa, without silvarum, "of the woods," and with the voice of the Lord for subject], that robbers may cease to lurk there, and that beasts and venomous creatures may pass over into the Church of God. Into folds of flocks. Another translation has Saronas, which is a region between Joppe and Lydda and Jamnia, fruitful and full of pasture, but infested by robbers: whence it is interpreted the valley pouring out blood. And the valley of Achor. Where Achor the son of Charmi, having stolen of the spoils of Jericho, perished with all his house. And because he troubled the people and there 1310Cperished, the place is called the valley of Achor, that is, of trouble or of tumult. In which is signified the Gentile world, as also by Saronas, which is interpreted the valley shedding blood, which, full of the shedding of blood, remained in the tumult and in the trouble of vices. But the valley of Achor is turned into folds of flocks or sheepfolds, and into a couching-place of herds, that is, into churches, and into the rest of preachers and of other faithful, who are designated by the herds and the flocks of sheep. Of herds. Of the preachers, who arm and defend the Church. For armentum is so called from armando, arming.

VERS. 11.-- And you that have forsaken, etc. Allegorically. All who desert the Church, and forget the holy mountain of God, delivering themselves over to spirits of error or to the unclean doctrines of demons, prepare 1310Da table for fortune, and believing that nothing pertains to God, but that all things are governed either by the course of the stars or by the changeableness of fortune, these shall be delivered to eternal punishments, so that none of them can escape.

The table of fortune. (HIER.) A goddess of the Egyptians, for whom at the end of the year they used to prepare a table heaped with all manner of food, and with cups mixed with mead: either as though she had furnished a happy year, or because she was about to furnish a happy one to come.

VERS. 12.-- I called. Saying through the prophets: Be converted to me and you shall be saved (Isa. XLV). And through myself: Do penance (Matth. III). And elsewhere: Come to me, all you that labour and are burdened (Ibid. XI).

VERS. 13.-- Behold my servants. (HIER.) As if he should say: 1311ANot only shall you fall by the sword, etc., as far as and, I shall be satisfied when thy glory shall appear.

VERS. 15.-- And you shall leave your name. So that some, affirming, may say: If I do otherwise, may it befall me as it befell the Jews slain by the sword of the Romans. Or thus: I swear by God, in whom the Jews once believed. Or, because God was their oath, which Christians now swear when compelled by necessity, namely The Lord liveth.

And he shall slay thee. That you may not have even him who says, I am the way, the truth and the life (Joan. XIV.) Nor may you be called circumcision, but concision.

VERS. 16.-- Because to oblivion. (HIER.) That they may not remember the idols, etc., as far as Perhaps in the time to come also the memory of their former manner of life shall be altogether blotted out.

1311BVERS. 18.-- For behold. Out of angels, namely, and out of men, one Church; and they shall exult and rejoice, that I who created may exult and rejoice in them. A rejoicing. Of the whole earth, that she who of old was the joy of the Only-begotten may be, made new, a gladness to all nations. A joy. Which we can understand either after the resurrection, or after baptism in the first resurrection. This refers to the general resurrection in which all shall rise again in perfect age. And this is it: There shall not be, etc.

VERS. 20.-- A child of a hundred years, etc. He, namely, who although he lived long, yet lived childishly, serving the vices: and Gregory expounds this thus. A child of a hundred years. And a sinner of a hundred years. The child's life is indeed drawn out long, that he may be corrected from childish deeds: but if he is not restrained from the perpetration of sin even by length 1311Cof time, this very length of life, which he received through the mercy of God, grows for him unto a heap of malediction.

VERS. 21.-- Houses. (HIER.) The virtues, for which they shall rest in the heavenly places, etc., as far as For many saints build houses, but do not dwell in them.

VERS. 22.-- For according to. (ID.) As Christ etc., as far as It is a tree of life to them that have laid hold on her, namely on wisdom, which is Christ.

VERS. 23.-- The seed of the blessed. The apostles, and other teachers in preaching, or in other works, as the Jews, because they shall receive an eternal reward, for they shall come with joyfulness carrying their sheaves (Psal. CXXV).

Grandchildren. Whom their sons have begotten in the faith, 1311Das Mark the disciple of Peter, and Luke the son of Paul.

VERS. 24.-- Before they call. In trouble, in grief of mind, but in joy and gladness: whence, For what is our joy or crown of glory, are not you (II Thes. II)? [Vulg. 1Thes 2:19 Quae est enim nostra spes aut gaudium, aut corona gloriae? nonne vos, with spes, "hope," and cited there to the first epistle] etc.

VERS. 25.-- The wolf. The wolf is Saul, that is, the persecutor, of whom, Benjamin a ravenous wolf (Gen. XLIX). The lamb. Those, namely, who in white garments follow the lamb whithersoever he goes: of whom it is said to Peter, Feed my lambs (Joan. XXI). And the lion and the ox shall eat. A man, namely, eloquent and most powerful in the world, the eloquence of philosophy being despised, shall eat straw, that is, the usefulness and the surface of the history, with the oxen, that is, with the preachers of the Church. These things the lions eat 1312Auntil by many labours one comes to the depth of the mystery, as Augustine and Cyprian and the rest. Neither shall they kill. Their fierceness laid aside, either in the present or in the time to come, and this is it: In all the mountain, etc. This the Jews take carnally, saying that in the time of their own Christ there shall be for them so great peace and security that wolves and lambs, lions and oxen, serpents and men shall dwell together, and shall eat, nor shall they harm those who dwell in the temple and on the holy mountain. From which it is gathered that all the rest shall be killed. But that this is false is shown by these words: Thus saith the Lord: Heaven is my seat, and the earth my footstool, etc.; yet this too the Jews understand carnally.

CAPUT LXVI.

1312BVERS. 1.-- Coelum. Omnes sancti quorum conversatio in coelis est; unde: Anima justi sedes est Dei. Coelum sedes mea. Quasi diceret: Ne putetis haec ad litteram esse complenda. Quae est ista. Quasi diceret: Putatis me localem, et in templo tantum manentem, sed quae est ista domus, etc.

VERS. 2.-- Ad quem, etc. Ablato altari temploque terreno quod humana manus construxit, recte auferentur victimae Judaeorum, ne forte dicerent: Non tam sumus stulti, ut Deum putemus hoc loco posse concludi, sed in separato ad glorificandum Deum loco victimas immolamus sicut in lege praecipitur.

VERS. 3.-- Qui immolat bovem. (HIER.) Quasi: Sacrificium vestrum non placet mihi post immolationem 1312Cveri sacrificii: in cujus umbra illa sacrificia concessi vobis, et ne immolaretis idolis. Canem. De quo per Moysen: Non offeres in domo Dei tui mercedem meretricis, neque pretium canis (Deut. XXIII). Et bene canis et meretrix copulantur, quia similiter libidinantur. Elegerunt in viis suis. Non mea voluntate, ex quo verus agnus immolatus est, Unde: Pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus (I Cor. V).

VERS. 4.-- Et quae timebant. Romanos; unde: Venient Romani, et tollent nostrum locum et Gentem (Joan. XI). Vel famem, pestem, captivitatem.

VERS. 5.-- Odientes vos. Quia de mundo non estis, quia in me creditis, quia nomen meum habetis, a Chrito Christiani dicti: unde, Jam enim conspiraverant Judaei, ut si quis confiteretur Christum, extra Synagogam 1312Dfieret (Joan. IX). Glorificetur. (HIER.) quasi: Appareat gloriosus, etc., usque ad cum potestate magna et majestate et virtute, vel etiam in praesenti.

VERS. 6.-- Vox populi de civitate. Adveniente Romano exercitu, secundum Josephum, divisi sunt Judaei in tres partes de principatu contendentes. Alii sibi alium principem praeponentes Pars autem populi et sacerdotum, tenebant templum, arcem vero et regiam domum pars populi et principum, reliquam vero civitatem tertia pars et maxime vulgus. Pugnabant ergo et extra contra hostes, et intra contra cives. Romanis ergo urbem irrumpentibus audita est vox populi plangentis de civitate, vox sacerdotum, et qui cum ipsis erant de templo. Vox etiam 1313ADomini audita est in Romanis exsultantibus, qui etiam nutu Dei venerunt, reddentis reddentes retributionem inimicis suis, scilicet Judaeis, per Romanos.

VERS. 7.-- Antequam parturiret. (HIER.) Non paulatim, sed nec multo tempore, etc., usque ad et David, inquit, Spes omnium finium terrae et in mari longe (Psal. LXXIV).

Masculum, populum fortem de quo Stephanus, Paulus, qui dicit: Quis nos separabit a charitate Christi (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 8.-- In die una. Apostolus filios suos tanquam una die simul genuit, quia in Christo, qui lux, et dies; et in unitate fidei.

VERS. 9.-- Si ego. Quasi: Ego qui omnes creavi 1313Bhomines in nihilo, creabo mihi ex omnibus gentibus Ecclesiam unam quae aedificatur de vivis lapidibus:

VERS. 10.-- Laetamini, etc. (HIER.) O angeli, o apostoli, tristes de perditione Judaeorum infidelium, laetamini consolati de fide et salute gentium de quibus aedificatur Ecclesia.

VERS. 11.-- Ut sugatis. Fidem simplicium, et opera bona inde delectati. Fides credentium lac et cibus est praedicatorum. Unde et Christus: Ego cibum habeo manducare, quem vos nescitis (Joan. IV). Et repleamini. Puerpera quae genuit masculum non caret abundantia lactis in educatione Gentis, et parvulorum qui simul nati sunt ut praeberet eis duo ubera Veteris et Novi Testamenti. Et notandum quod parvuli lacte consolationis indigent quasi adhuc in carne versantes: qui vero post lac consolationis 1313Cad solidum cibum profecerunt, affluent deliciis veritatis et scientiae omnimodae gloriae. Ut mulgeatis. Sancta Ecclesia in omnibus una, in aliis habet lac, simplicem, scilicet fidem, operationem. In aliis affluit omnimoda gloria, charismatum scilicet abundantia, et divinitatis scientia et humanitatis Christi. Sancti ergo praedicatores in omnibus delectantur tanquam carpentes ab aliis hoc, ab aliis illud. Ab ubere. Ab apostolis et praedicatoribus ad dona Spiritus sancti, vel ad doctrinam Veteris et Novi Testamenti. Quod enim praedicant, operibus monstrant, ut sic auditoribus ducatum praebeant.

VERS. 12.-- Blandientur vobis. (HIER.) Sustentantes, lac dantes, sicut ille qui dicit, Obsecro vos, 1313Dfatres, per misericordiam Dei ut exhibeatis corda vestra (Rom. XII), etc. Et: Obsecro vos tanquam advenas, (I Petr. II). etc.

VERS. 13.-- Quomodo. Omnipotens Deus tanquam misericors et miserator, modo aquilae modo gallinae, modo mari blandienti se comparat, ut vos saltem blanditiis alliciat. Sicut etiam aquila in rupibus inaccessis nidum parat, ne accessus alicui pateat, et in medio amethistum lapidem qui omnia venena fugat, secundum physicos propter serpentes arcendos, quaedam alia secreta naturae collocat: sic ipse in cordibus nostris lapidem, id est fidem suam ponit, scilicet, seipsum, qui est lapis abscisus sine manibus de monte, quo omnia venena diaboli fugantur. 1314AAquila enim plus omnibus bestiis, et jumentis, pecudibus avibusque foetus diligit, ut aiunt qui de animantium scripsere naturis

VERS. 14.-- Videbitis, o apostoli, multitudinem viventium ad fidem. Et gaudebit cor vestrum de salute illorum, et ossa vestra, virtutes, germinabunt in aliorum cordibus, fructus bonae voluntatis et operationis.
